# mrw: findings in detail

Companion to [mrw-improvement-proposals.md](mrw-improvement-proposals.md). This file lists every finding that survived adversarial verification, grouped by the issue it belongs to in that report. The text is the reviewers' and verifiers' own output, unedited. "Severity" shows the reviewer's rating and, after the arrow, the verifier's. "Verifier's correction" is shown where the verifier changed the proposed fix, and it takes precedence over the original proposal.

Repository `fmcgoohan/crispy-rotary-phone` at `4462ea5`. Static review; no code was executed.

## Index

| ID | Issue | Severity | Verdict | Effort | Title |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-1 | A1 | critical | confirmed | S | Shot palettes are clustered from 256 row-band averages, not from the frame's pixels |
| VID-1 | A1 | critical | confirmed | S | Palette is computed from 256 row-band averages, not pixels: pooled (N,1,3) column defeats the ≤256 px downscale |
| ORCH-8 | A2 | high | confirmed | M | Rotation metadata is ignored by the probe, so portrait phone videos are recorded with stored (landscape) dimensions, and the video stage breaks on the same assumption |
| SEC-3 | A2 | high | confirmed | M | ffprobe metadata is trusted for raw-frame geometry: rotated (phone/portrait) videos give scrambled frames, and probe edge cases crash or fail with empty errors |
| VID-4 | A2 | high | confirmed | S | Rotated videos (e.g. portrait iPhone clips) produce garbled representative frames: frames are reshaped with unrotated dims while ffmpeg auto-rotates |
| VID-2 | A3 | high | confirmed | L | Shot, frame and motion times ignore stream start offsets and VFR: not on the unified audio timeline, and motion and shots use different origins |
| ARCH-1 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Freshness ignores upstream changes: downstream documents go silently stale, and `mrw status` hides lyrics/video config drift |
| DET-1 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Re-running an upstream stage never marks or regenerates the documents that depend on it (stems → features → lyrics) |
| FEAT-2 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Features no-op and stale checks key only on cfg.features: re-separated stems (new model/device/threads) or changed code leave audio_features.json 'ok' and `mrw features` a no-op |
| LYR-4 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Lyrics no-op check ignores upstream changes: lyrics.json keeps old vocal-activity data, and its bytes depend on the order stages were run |
| ORCH-1 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Upstream re-runs never invalidate downstream documents, and stale prerequisites are consumed |
| SCH-1 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Dependent documents never go stale: lyrics/features stay 'ok' and no-op after the upstream inputs they consumed change |
| SEC-4 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Staleness never propagates to dependent stages: after re-running stems or features, features and lyrics still report 'up to date' |
| TEST-2 | B1 | high | confirmed | M | Dependent-stage staleness is untested and unimplemented: downstream documents stay `ok` (and re-runs are no-ops) after an upstream stage changes |
| LYR-5 | C1 | high | confirmed | S | Compressed LRC lines with several time tags keep only the first timestamp, so repeated lines are lost |
| SEC-7 | C2 | high | confirmed | S | Lyrics files are decoded as UTF-8 with errors='replace': BOM, UTF-16 and cp1252 files silently corrupt display text and break markup/LRC detection |
| TEST-3 | C5 | high | confirmed | M | The LRC parser test covers one hand-picked format; common real-world .lrc variants produce wrong lyrics.json |
| LYR-1 | D1 | critical → high | confirmed | M | Unhinted alignment takes the best match anywhere in a 400-word lookahead, so a cleaner later chorus captures earlier chorus lines and the rest of the song fails to align |
| LYR-2 | D2 | high | confirmed | M | .lrc-hinted lines ignore the cursor and break ties toward the earliest match: repeated lines reuse the same words even with perfect hints, and offset hints mis-anchor |
| LYR-3 | E1 | high | confirmed | M | Whisper model is looked up online on every run with an unpinned revision: a network call outside the permitted exceptions, and a risk of silent model drift |
| SEC-2 | E1 | high | confirmed | S | Every `mrw lyrics` run calls huggingface.co and silently follows upstream Whisper model revisions |
| TEST-4 | E1 | high | confirmed | M | Nothing in tests or CI enforces the offline-by-default hard constraint; `mrw lyrics` very likely contacts the Hugging Face Hub on every run |
| FEAT-1 | F1 | high | confirmed | M | Onset detection ignores level: near-silent and bleed-only stems produce dense, full-strength onset lists (R-9's 'silent stem → no onsets' only holds for bit-exact zeros) |
| FEAT-3 | F2 | high | confirmed | M | tempo.confidence scores how regular the tracker's output is, and that output is regular by construction; with trim=False the grid also extends through beatless intros and outros at high confidence |
| TEST-1 | I1 | high | confirmed | M | No deterministic CI gate: the tests run only inside the LLM review agent, and the job's status ignores pytest's result |
| ORCH-13 | A3 | medium | plausible | M | Which audio stream gets analysed, and its offset from the video clock, are left to ffmpeg's defaults and never recorded |
| VID-3 | A4 | high → medium | confirmed | S | Motion: the flow pair straddling each cut is counted inside a neighbouring shot, inflating mean and dominating p95 of short shots |
| VID-11 | A5 | medium | confirmed | S | Last shot and video_span are extended to an ffprobe frame estimate that can exceed the real stream, which can abort extraction |
| CRIT-3 | A6 | medium | confirmed | M | HDR/wide-gamut video (HLG or PQ, BT.2020, e.g. iPhone 'HDR Video') is converted to rgb24 with no tone or gamut mapping, so stills, palettes and captions get large colour errors |
| VID-12 | A6 | medium | plausible | M | Frame bytes (hence frame sha256, caption-cache keys and video.json) depend on the unpinned system ffmpeg's YUV→RGB conversion, and its version is not recorded |
| SEC-6 | A8 | high → medium | confirmed | M | The video stage holds entire raw decode outputs in RAM, needing many GB for 4K or long videos (2x transient), and decodes the source three times |
| VID-8 | A8 | medium | confirmed | M | All representative frames are decoded at full resolution into one in-memory buffer (×2 at peak) and held for the whole run |
| ORCH-11 | B2 | medium | confirmed | S | `mrw status` shows stale only for source, stems and features; lyrics and video still show ok after their config changes |
| DET-8 | B3 | medium | confirmed | M | Structural constants are said to 'ride tool_version', but nothing checks tool_version, and output-affecting tool versions (ffmpeg, libraries, host) are never recorded |
| ORCH-3 | B3 | high → medium | confirmed | M | Code and toolchain provenance is not tracked: tool_version is stuck at 'mrw 0.1.0' and never checked, and external tool versions and platform are not recorded |
| ARCH-9 | B4 | medium | confirmed | M | Ingest only ever writes once: new lyrics, titles and ingest-config changes are silently dropped, and the video stage's own remedy message cannot work |
| DET-4 | B4 | medium | confirmed | M | Re-ingesting ignores ingest config changes and new --lyrics/--title arguments: the copy_video remedy the error message gives cannot work, and source shows stale forever |
| LYR-13 | B4 | medium | confirmed | S | Unsupported lyrics extensions are silently treated as txt, and --lyrics on an already-ingested track is silently ignored |
| ORCH-6 | B4 | high → medium | confirmed | M | Re-ingest is always a no-op: new --lyrics or --title, changed ingest config and damaged tracks are ignored, and the video stage's suggested fix cannot work |
| VID-10 | B4 | medium | confirmed | M | OQ-2 escape hatches don't work: the suggested re-ingest is a no-op, and a hash-verified new path given to `mrw video` is ignored |
| DET-3 | B5 | high → medium | confirmed | S | The video stage analyses whatever file is at file.original_path without checking it is the ingested file |
| ORCH-2 | B5 | high → medium | confirmed | S | The video stage analyses whatever file is at original_path (or any source_video.*) without checking it is the ingested bytes |
| VID-5 | B5 | high → medium | confirmed | S | Video stage re-reads original_path (or source_video.*) without checking that it is still the ingested file |
| ORCH-15 | B6 | medium | confirmed | M | Settings that change no output are hashed anyway: budget, retain and copy_video changes force re-runs or a permanent 'stale' |
| LYR-6 | C2 | high → medium | confirmed | S | Lyrics files are decoded with errors="replace" and the BOM is never stripped, silently corrupting the display text |
| ORCH-14 | C2 | medium | confirmed | S | The lyrics file's encoding is never checked, so files with a BOM, UTF-16 or cp1252 are silently mangled |
| LYR-7 | C3 | medium | confirmed | M | LRC metadata tags are emitted as section markup, [offset:] is ignored, timestamp variants stay in display text, and ♪ or unbracketed headers become sung lines |
| SCH-2 | C3 | medium | confirmed | S | supplied_markup records LRC metadata tags as section headers, multi-timestamp LRC lines collapse into one, and its label vocabulary doesn't match structure's |
| LYR-10 | C4 | medium | confirmed | M | Token normalisation lacks NFKC and casefold, strips curly apostrophes and combining marks, and cannot handle CJK or other scripts without spaces |
| CRIT-1 | D3 | high → medium | confirmed | M | Aligned mode drops every transcribed word that doesn't anchor to a supplied line, so abbreviated or incomplete lyric files lose whole sections and report them as untranscribed |

| LYR-11 | D5 | medium | confirmed | S | Unanchored-line interpolation stacks consecutive lines on one span, is not clamped to track duration, and puts every line at 0-4 s when there are no anchors |

## A. Video stage

### A1. Palettes built from averages of whole image rows, not pixels

#### VID-1: Palette is computed from 256 row-band averages, not pixels: pooled (N,1,3) column defeats the ≤256 px downscale

- **Severity:** critical · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:472-474`, `mrw/video.py:487`, `mrw/video.py:109-115`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:72-89`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195`

**Evidence.** run_video pools full-resolution frames into a one-pixel-wide column before calling the palette helper. mrw/video.py:472-474 `pooled = np.concatenate([frames_rgb[i].reshape(-1, 3) for i in shot_indices]).reshape(-1, 1, 3)`, then mrw/video.py:487 `palette=palette_from_rgb(pooled)`. palette_from_rgb was written for an HxWx3 frame. mrw/video.py:109-115 `h, w = rgb.shape[:2]` / `scale = PALETTE_MAX_DIM / max(h, w)` / `if scale < 1.0: rgb = cv2.resize(rgb, (max(1, int(w * scale)), max(1, int(h * scale))), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)`. For one 1920x1080 frame h=2,073,600 and w=1, so dsize=(1,256). Each k-means sample is then the area mean of 8,100 consecutive pixels, i.e. 4.22 full-width image rows (16,200 px for two-frame shots). PLAN.md:286-288 intends pixels pooled from the representative frame(s), downscaled to ≤ 256 px. I reproduced the shape arithmetic with my own numpy script (box-average emulation of INTER_AREA): a 1920x1080 frame that is 70% pure red (left) and 30% pure blue (right) reaches k-means as 256 samples, all in R 169-182, G 0, B 73-86 (13 distinct purples). Downscaling the frame first gives red 0.703 and blue 0.297. The tests cannot catch this. tests/test_m5_smoke.py:72-89 calls palette_from_rgb on an unpooled 100x100 image, so scale ≥ 1 and no resize happens. The stage fixture (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195) uses single solid colours, whose band averages are unchanged.

**Failure scenario.** Affects any shot whose frame has more than one colour region, which is essentially every real music-video shot. A frame that is 70% red neon wall and 30% blue sky yields up to five purple swatches (~#aa0055) whose proportions count bands, not pixels, and no red or blue swatch at all. Small saturated accents, such as a pink sign covering 5% of the frame, are averaged across the full image width and disappear. video.json is written with status ok and no flag.

**Proposal.** Downscale each representative frame to ≤256 px on the long edge as an HxWx3 image (cv2.resize, INTER_AREA) before pooling. Then pool the small frames' pixels (about 37k samples per 16:9 frame), e.g. `samples = np.concatenate([_downscale(frames_rgb[i]).reshape(-1, 3) for i in shot_indices])`. Change palette_from_rgb to take an (N,3) sample array and drop its internal resize, so the shape mistake cannot recur. Add a stage-path test: a textured two-colour 70/30 fixture whose top two swatches must be within ΔE < 5 of the known colours, which is the PLAN §11 M5 criterion. Fixing this changes video.json bytes, so call that out in the PR.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced the call path myself. run_video pools to a one-pixel-wide column: mrw/video.py:472-474 `np.concatenate([frames_rgb[i].reshape(-1, 3) ...]).reshape(-1, 1, 3)`. palette_from_rgb then computes `h, w = rgb.shape[:2]` and `scale = PALETTE_MAX_DIM / max(h, w)` (video.py:109-110), so h=N, w=1, and cv2.resize gets dsize (1, 256) with INTER_AREA (video.py:112-115). I wrote my own emulation of INTER_AREA as an area-weighted box average along the long axis, which is OpenCV's documented downscale behaviour (high confidence): /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/vid-verify/scripts/pool_check.py. On a 1920x1080 frame that is 70% red and 30% blue it reproduces the reviewer's numbers: 256 samples, 13 distinct colours, R 169-182, G 0, B 73-86. Each sample averages 8,100 px, i.e. 4.22 image rows; at the test fixture's 640x480 it is 1.88 rows. No red or blue sample ever reaches k-means. The tests cannot see this. test_palette_known_clusters passes an unpooled 100x100 image (scale 2.56, so no resize), and the stage fixture uses solid colours (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195), whose band averages are unchanged. PLAN.md:286-288 asks for pixels pooled from frames already downscaled to ≤256 px, and nothing records this as a decision. Every real shot with horizontal colour variation gets a wrong palette while the status reads ok. That is silent wrong output in normal use, so critical.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep the proposal: downscale each frame as an HxWx3 image, then pool, and have palette_from_rgb take (N,3) samples, reshaping to (N,1,3) only for cvtColor. Update test_palette_known_clusters to the new signature and add the stage-path 70/30 two-colour fixture with a ΔE<5 check. Land it together with VID-13 and VID-14 so video.json and palette bytes change once, and call that out in the PR.

#### SEC-1: Shot palettes are clustered from 256 row-band averages, not from the frame's pixels

- **Severity:** critical · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S1, T1
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:472`, `mrw/video.py:487`, `mrw/video.py:109`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:72`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:253`

**Evidence.** `run_video` flattens a shot's full-resolution frames into a column one pixel wide before calling the palette helper: `pooled = np.concatenate([frames_rgb[i].reshape(-1, 3) for i in shot_indices]).reshape(-1, 1, 3)` (video.py:472-474), then `palette=palette_from_rgb(pooled)` (487). `palette_from_rgb` was written for a 2-D image: `h, w = rgb.shape[:2]; scale = PALETTE_MAX_DIM / max(h, w)` followed by `cv2.resize(rgb, (max(1, int(w * scale)), max(1, int(h * scale))), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)` (109-115). With h = N pixels and w = 1, the resize target is 1x256. Each of the 256 samples k-means sees is therefore the area-average of N/256 consecutive raster-order pixels: 8,100 px (4.2 full-width rows) for one 1080p frame, 16,200 px (8.4 rows) for a two-frame shot. I emulated that arithmetic in numpy, assuming INTER_AREA with an integer shrink factor is a block mean (high confidence). Input: a 1920x1080 frame, left half red, right half blue. Result: dsize=(1,256), and all 256 samples are purples between (121,0,121) and (134,0,134); no sample is near red or blue. This contradicts the contract: video.schema.json:74 says 'k-means (k=5) in CIELab over the representative frame(s)', with proportions defined as a 'fraction of sampled pixels', and PLAN.md:287 says 'downscaled to <= 256 px'. The tests cannot catch it. `test_palette_known_clusters` passes a 100x100 image, so scale > 1 and the resize never runs. The stage fixture uses solid-colour shots (test_m5_smoke.py:253-256), where any average is the same colour.

**Failure scenario.** Take any real shot with horizontal colour structure, e.g. a singer in a red dress against a blue wall. The palette comes back as five purples with 0% red and 0% blue. A vivid subject in the centre third of the frame gets averaged into its row bands and vanishes from the swatches. video.json still validates and the double-run test still passes. Every palette in every video is silently wrong, and nothing flags it.

**Proposal.** Downscale each representative frame on its own, as a 2-D image, to <=256 px on the long side with INTER_AREA. Then concatenate the downscaled pixels into an (M,3) array and cluster that. Never resize a 1-px column. For example, give `palette_from_rgb` a `frames: list[np.ndarray]` parameter that downscales and pools inside the function. Add a stage-path test with a 1920x1080 two-colour frame asserting both colours come back as swatches at about 0.5 each. Every stored palette will change, so flag it in the PR as a tool_version event and re-run video for existing tracks. Downscaling first also lets each full-res frame be freed immediately (see SEC-6).

**Verifier's notes.** I traced the code path. video.py:472-474 builds `pooled = np.concatenate([frames_rgb[i].reshape(-1, 3) for i in shot_indices]).reshape(-1, 1, 3)`, so the array has shape (H*W*n, 1, 3). palette_from_rgb then runs `h, w = rgb.shape[:2]; scale = PALETTE_MAX_DIM / max(h, w)` (video.py:109-110) with h equal to the pixel count, so cv2.resize gets dsize (1, 256). Every k-means sample is therefore the INTER_AREA mean of N/256 consecutive raster-order pixels: 8,100 px (4.2 full rows) for one 1080p frame, and 1,200 px for the 640x480 test fixture, so the stage test runs this path too. I emulated the arithmetic in numpy for a 1920x1080 frame with the left half red and the right half blue. All 256 samples are purple (R and B between 121 and 134, G = 0). This contradicts PLAN.md:286-287 ('pixels pooled from the representative frame(s) (downscaled to ≤ 256 px') and video.schema.json:74/82 ('fraction of sampled pixels'). test_palette_known_clusters passes a 2-D 100x100 array (scale > 1, so no resize happens), and the stage test uses solid-colour shots, where any average gives the same colour. Nothing in the reviews records this as deliberate. Assumption: OpenCV INTER_AREA downscaling is an area mean (high confidence). Result: every palette of every real video is silently wrong in normal use.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed, with two additions. (1) Move the per-frame 2-D downscale and the pooling inside palette_from_rgb, e.g. `palette_from_frames(frames: list[np.ndarray])`. The fast unit test can then cover the real path with a 1920x1080 half-red/half-blue frame and no video, asserting two swatches at about 0.5 each. (2) Staleness is keyed on config_hash, so existing video.json files will not show as stale after the fix. Note the tool_version change in the PR and re-run video for existing tracks by hand.

### A2. Portrait and rotated videos produce scrambled frames

#### VID-4: Rotated videos (e.g. portrait iPhone clips) produce garbled representative frames: frames are reshaped with unrotated dims while ffmpeg auto-rotates

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:212`, `mrw/video.py:226-228`, `mrw/video.py:233-240`, `mrw/video.py:253-258`

**Evidence.** Frame dimensions come from ffprobe's coded width and height: mrw/video.py:212 `width, height = int(stream["width"]), int(stream["height"])`. Extraction runs ffmpeg with its default autorotation (mrw/video.py:226-228, no -noautorotate), then `frame_bytes = width * height * 3` and `.reshape(height, width, 3)` (mrw/video.py:233-240). The only check is `if len(chunk) < frame_bytes`, and a 90°-rotated frame has the same byte count. I verified this with system ffmpeg 6.1.1 on a synthetic clip carrying display rotation 90. ffprobe reports width=320, height=180 plus side_data rotation 90. ffmpeg's showinfo reports s:180x320 for the selected frame. stdout is exactly 320×180×3 = 172,800 bytes, so no error fires. Reshaping the way video.py does produces a striped, unrecognisable image (rendered in my scratchpad). _motion_pass also scales to coded dims (mrw/video.py:253-258), squashing portrait frames to 320x180.

**Failure scenario.** Affects any input with a ±90/270 display-matrix rotation, which includes every portrait-recorded iPhone or Android video (stored as 1920x1080 plus rotation metadata). Every frames/*.jpg is scrambled. With caption_backend=anthropic, each paid caption describes noise. Palettes survive only by accident, because the pooled pixel multiset is unchanged. Motion is computed on aspect-distorted frames. Status is ok and nothing is flagged.

**Proposal.** In _video_stream_info, read the rotation (side_data_list[].rotation, legacy tags.rotate) and swap (width, height) for ±90/270 in both the RGB extraction and the motion-pass scale. Keep autorotation so frames and captions are upright. More robustly, make the stream self-describing: emit `-c:v ppm -f image2pipe` and take each frame's size from its PPM header, so rotation, SAR or a mid-stream resolution change can never silently mis-shape a frame. Also assert that the total byte count matches exactly. Add a generated fixture made with `-display_rotation 90` and assert the JPEG dimensions are 180x320.

**Verifier's notes.** Reproduced with system ffmpeg 6.1.1 on a clip remuxed with `-display_rotation 90` (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/vid-verify/media/rot.mp4). ffprobe JSON reports width 320, height 180 and side_data rotation 90; these are exactly the coded dims that _video_stream_info reads (video.py:212). The extraction command shape runs with ffmpeg's default autorotate and emits 180x320 frames: the PPM header reads 'P6 180 320', and 320x180 with -noautorotate. The rawvideo output is exactly 172,800 = 320·180·3 bytes, so the `len(chunk) < frame_bytes` guard (238) cannot fire, and `.reshape(height, width, 3)` (240) scrambles the image. _motion_pass derives the scale height from the coded dims (248-257), so it also squashes rotated frames to 320x180. Portrait phone originals carry exactly this metadata. Result: garbled JPEGs, paid captions of noise and distorted motion, all with status ok. That is wrong output on a common input variant, so high.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Preferably emit `-f image2pipe -c:v ppm` and parse each frame's header, so rotation, SAR or a mid-stream size change can never mis-shape a frame. Otherwise swap the dims for ±90/270 in both the extraction and the motion-pass scale. Assert that the total byte count matches exactly, and add a `-display_rotation 90` fixture that expects 180x320 JPEGs.

#### ORCH-8: Rotation metadata is ignored by the probe, so portrait phone videos are recorded with stored (landscape) dimensions, and the video stage breaks on the same assumption

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H2, S1, C1, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:215`, `mrw/video.py:212`, `mrw/video.py:226`, `mrw/video.py:240`

**Evidence.** ingest.py:215-221 builds `MediaVideo(codec=…, width=int(video_stream["width"]), height=int(video_stream["height"]), fps=fps, frame_count=frame_count)` and never reads `side_data_list[*].rotation` (Display Matrix) or the older `tags.rotate`. video.py, which is outside this review's scope but shares the probe assumption, re-probes the same way (`width, height = int(stream["width"]), int(stream["height"])` at video.py:212). It decodes with a plain `ffmpeg … -f rawvideo -pix_fmt rgb24 -` with no -noautorotate (video.py:225-228), and reshapes each chunk with `.reshape(height, width, 3)` (video.py:240). Assumed ffmpeg behaviour (documented; high confidence): ffprobe reports width and height as stored in the stream, before rotation, and the ffmpeg CLI rotates on decode by default, so a 90° or 270° stream decodes as height × width.

**Failure scenario.** An iPhone portrait clip is stored as 1920×1080 with rotation −90. source.json records 1920×1080, so consumers lay it out as landscape. In the video stage, each decoded 1080-wide by 1920-tall frame has the same byte count, so `reshape(1080, 1920, 3)` succeeds but yields a sheared, scrambled image. Palettes are wrong, the JPEG stills are garbage, and with anthropic captions each garbage frame is a paid call that stays cached. The motion pass is scaled to 320×180 using the wrong aspect ratio. No error is raised anywhere.

**Proposal.** Add one probe helper in ingest, reused by video.py, that returns the dimensions as displayed. Read rotation from `side_data_list` (`side_data_type == "Display Matrix"`, `rotation`) or `tags.rotate`, normalise it to 0, 90, 180 or 270, and swap width and height for 90 and 270. Record it in source.json as an additive `media.video.rotation_degrees` (source 1.1.0), and document that width and height are display dimensions. In video.py, reshape using the display dimensions, or pass `-noautorotate` everywhere and rotate in numpy. Test with a generated clip that carries rotation metadata (for example `-display_rotation 90` on recent ffmpeg).

**Verifier's notes.** ingest.py:215-221 records ffprobe's width/height and never reads side_data_list (Display Matrix rotation) or tags.rotate. video.py:212 probes the same way, decodes without -noautorotate (video.py:225-228) and reshapes with those dimensions (video.py:240); `_motion_pass` re-probes them too (video.py:248-254). Assumed ffmpeg behaviour, which is documented and I hold with high confidence: ffprobe reports width/height before rotation, and the CLI autorotates by default. A −90° phone clip therefore decodes as 1080×1920, the byte count is unchanged, and reshape(1080,1920,3) silently succeeds. The JPEG stills, and the paid, cached captions made from them, are scrambled, and motion is computed on a 320×180 frame with the wrong aspect ratio. The palette claim is overstated: k-means pools pixels, so the colour set survives, and only INTER_AREA downscaling blends pixels that are not neighbours in the image. Nothing in the repo or docs mentions rotation. High: silent wrong output on common phone-shot media, and review 007 shows live tracks are in scope.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Use one probe helper that returns rotation and display dimensions, and call it from ingest, `_video_stream_info` and `_motion_pass`. Pick one convention for all three ffmpeg calls: either keep autorotate and use display dimensions everywhere, or pass -noautorotate everywhere and rotate in numpy. Add rotation_degrees to source.json (additive, source 1.1.0). Test with a clip muxed with `-display_rotation 90`.

#### SEC-3: ffprobe metadata is trusted for raw-frame geometry: rotated (phone/portrait) videos give scrambled frames, and probe edge cases crash or fail with empty errors

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H2, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:212`, `mrw/video.py:233`, `mrw/video.py:253`, `mrw/video.py:206`, `mrw/ingest.py:209`, `mrw/ingest.py:215`

**Evidence.** `_video_stream_info` returns the coded dimensions: `width, height = int(stream['width']), int(stream['height'])` (video.py:212). `_extract_frames_rgb` then slices ffmpeg's rgb24 output using `frame_bytes = width * height * 3` and `.reshape(height, width, 3)` (233-240). When a stream has a Display Matrix, ffmpeg autorotates on decode, but ffprobe still reports the pre-rotation width and height. I reproduced this with the system ffmpeg 6.1.1. A 64x32 clip remuxed with `-display_rotation 90` probes as width 64, height 32, side_data Display Matrix 90. The same `select ... -f rawvideo -pix_fmt rgb24` decode returns a 32x64 frame of identical size (6144 bytes), so the `len(chunk) < frame_bytes` guard never fires. Row 0 of mrw's reshaped array equals real rows 0 and 1 side by side. The motion pass derives `height = round(MOTION_WIDTH*h0/w0/2)*2` from the same coded dims (253-254), which squashes portrait frames into a landscape box. Ingest stores the coded dims and no rotation field (ingest.py:215-221). The same blind trust shows up elsewhere. `float(Fraction(...avg_frame_rate...))` (video.py:211, ingest.py:209) raises ZeroDivisionError on ffprobe's '0/0'; in ingest that is not an IngestError, so the user gets a traceback after source_audio.flac is already written. `next(s for s in info['streams'] if ... not attached_pic)` (206-210) raises StopIteration when v:0 is cover art, and since str(StopIteration()) is '', the manifest error and the CLI message are empty. `_motion_pass` never checks the ffprobe return code (248-253). `info['format']['duration']` (214) raises KeyError when both nb_frames and duration are missing.

**Failure scenario.** Take an iPhone concert clip shot in portrait (coded 1920x1080, rotation -90). The stage exits 0, but every JPEG in frames/ is a sheared stripe pattern. Those frames' sha256s key the caption cache, so with the anthropic backend the paid captions describe garbage and are replayed byte-for-byte forever. Motion values come from aspect-distorted frames, and source.json reports a 1920x1080 landscape video.

**Proposal.** Write one probe helper, shared by ingest and video. It should pick the stream once, using ingest's `_first_stream` rule, and return the stream index plus the rotation from side_data_list. Then choose one approach: (a) pass `-noautorotate` to every ffmpeg decode and record `rotation` in source.json/video.json as an additive minor change, so consumers rotate; or (b) keep autorotation and swap W/H for +-90 degrees. Pass `-map 0:<index>` to every decode, and assert `len(raw) == n_frames * frame_bytes` exactly. Parse frame rates defensively: try avg_frame_rate, fall back to r_frame_rate, and raise VideoError/IngestError on 0. Use `next(..., None)` with an explicit error message, and check every probe return code. Add a test that remuxes the cut fixture with `-display_rotation 90` and asserts portrait JPEG dimensions and the expected dominant colour.

**Verifier's notes.** I reproduced the core claim with ffmpeg 6.1.1. A 64x32 testsrc clip remuxed with `-display_rotation 90` probes as width 64, height 32, with side data 'Display Matrix' rotation 90. The same `select='eq(n\,5)' -vsync 0 -f rawvideo -pix_fmt rgb24` decode outputs a 32x64 frame of the same 6,144 bytes. So the `len(chunk) < frame_bytes` guard at video.py:238 cannot fire, and `.reshape(height, width, 3)` (video.py:240) scrambles the rows. The motion pass (video.py:253-257) scales autorotated portrait frames into a box with the coded aspect ratio. iPhone portrait video is coded landscape with rotation metadata, so this is a common media variant, and the result is garbage frames and captions with exit 0. The secondary claims also hold. `Fraction('0/0')` raises ZeroDivisionError (video.py:211, ingest.py:209); in ingest that is not an IngestError, and cli.py:67 catches only IngestError. `next(...)` raises StopIteration with str '' when v:0 is cover art, which leaves the manifest error and CLI message empty. A streamed WebM I generated has no nb_frames and no format.duration, so it raises KeyError at video.py:214. The probe return code is unchecked at video.py:248-253. None of this is recorded.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep the proposal, but prefer option (b): keep ffmpeg's autorotation and swap W/H for ±90°. Frames, palettes and captions then come out upright for consumers. Feed the dimensions and stream index from one shared probe helper, selecting the stream with ingest's `_first_stream` rule. Assert an exact byte count. Recording the rotation in source.json is optional and additive.

### A3. Video times are not on the audio timeline

#### VID-2: Shot, frame and motion times ignore stream start offsets and VFR: not on the unified audio timeline, and motion and shots use different origins

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S4, H1, C2, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:98`, `mrw/video.py:211`, `mrw/video.py:287`, `mrw/video.py:224-227`, `mrw/video.py:256-258`, `mrw/video.py:271`, `mrw/video.py:465`, `mrw/video.py:495-497`, `schemas/video.schema.json:5`, `schemas/video.schema.json:50`

**Evidence.** Times are frame_num/avg_fps with no origin correction. mrw/video.py:211 `fps = float(Fraction(stream["avg_frame_rate"]))`, mrw/video.py:98 `canonical.round_seconds(s / fps)`, mrw/video.py:465 `time_seconds=canonical.round_seconds(frame_idx / fps)`, and mrw/video.py:495-497 `video_span=Interval(start_seconds=0.0, ...)`. The contract says otherwise: schemas/video.schema.json:5 says shots partition 'the video span on the unified audio timeline', and PLAN.md:34-35 says 't = 0 = first sample of the normalized audio decode'. (1) Start offset. With system ffmpeg 6.1.1 I built an MP4 whose video stream starts 0.5 s after the audio (ffprobe: video start_time=0.500000, audio 0.000000). The motion-pass command from mrw/video.py:256-258 then produced 54 frames for 4.0 s of video, and the first 7 were byte-identical: ffmpeg pads to the file start for rawvideo output. So motion pair k (mrw/video.py:271 `(i + 0.5) / MOTION_FPS`) is on the file timeline, while shot times count from the first video frame. (2) VFR. uv.lock pins scenedetect 0.7. I read its sdist, whose sha256 matches uv.lock. VideoStreamCv2.position builds a PTS Timecode from CAP_PROP_POS_MSEC. FrameTimecode.frame_num is documented as 'For VFR video or Timecode-backed objects, this is an approximation based on the average framerate' and computed as `round(self._time.seconds * float(self._rate))`. The 0.7 migration guide adds that frame_num 'is now an approximation based on PTS-derived time rather than a sequential count'. mrw nevertheless uses it as an exact decoded index: mrw/video.py:287 `bounds = [(s.frame_num, e.frame_num) for s, e in scenes]`, which then feeds ffmpeg's select filter on n (mrw/video.py:224-227). schemas/video.schema.json:50 promises a 'Source-video frame index'.

**Failure scenario.** (a) A file whose video stream starts 0.5 s after the audio, e.g. edit lists, MPEG-TS/AVCHD captures, or phone and screen recordings. Tens-of-ms offsets are common from priming and pre-skip. Every cut is reported 0.5 s early against the beat grid, so M6's planned on_beat_cut_ratio (±70 ms window) is wrong for the whole track. Inside the same video.json, the first shot absorbs 6 zero-motion padding pairs and every shot's motion window is shifted by 6 pairs. (b) A phone video recorded at 60 fps for 90 s, then 24 fps in low light for 90 s (average 42 fps). A cut at 90 s gets frame_num = round(90×42) = 3780. Decoded frame 3780 is at 63 s, so the select filter extracts a frame from a different shot 27 s earlier. That frame's JPEG, palette and paid caption all describe the wrong shot. If OpenCV reports r_frame_rate instead, start_seconds becomes 5400/42 = 128.6 s. Nothing flags the VFR source.

**Proposal.** Build one presentation-time table per run, either from sorted packet pts for the chosen stream (no decode) or from frame best_effort_timestamp_time. Map scenedetect's PTS (FrameTimecode.seconds, exact in 0.7) to decoded indices with bisect, and use those indices for start_frame/end_frame and the select filter. Compute every document time as pts − t0, where t0 is the pts of the first decoded audio sample, i.e. the origin of source_audio.flac. Set video_span.start_seconds to the first frame's time. For motion, take pair times from the actual output pts, or remove the padding with setpts=PTS-STARTPTS plus the known offset, so motion and shots share one origin. Record the applied offset and a VFR indicator in the document as an additive minor bump (S4/H1). Add generated fixtures for an itsoffset 0.5 file and for a 30→15 fps concat with fps_mode passthrough.

**Verifier's notes.** Every shot and frame time is frame/avg_fps measured from the first video frame (video.py:98, 211, 465), and video_span starts at a hard 0.0 (495-497). Motion pair times `(i + 0.5) / MOTION_FPS` (271) come from a pass that has no -vsync flag. Start offset: I reproduced this with system ffmpeg 6.1.1, using an MP4 whose video start_time is 0.5 and audio 0.0 (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/vid-verify/media/off.mp4). The motion-pass command shape returns 54 frames whose first 7 are byte-identical, against 48 for the unshifted file. So inside one video.json the motion windows sit 0.5 s away from the shot windows, and every shot time is 0.5 s early relative to t=0 of source_audio.flac. VFR: I extracted the scenedetect 0.7 sdist, whose sha256 c907ac5e… matches uv.lock. VideoStreamCv2.position builds a Timecode from CAP_PROP_POS_MSEC, and FrameTimecode.frame_num returns `round(self._time.seconds * float(self._rate))` for Timecode-backed objects. Its docstring (common.py:263-273) calls this an approximation for VFR. mrw uses it as an exact decoded index for the select filter (video.py:224-227, 287). On VFR input either the extracted frame is wrong or the time is wrong, depending on whether OpenCV's CAP_PROP_FPS is the average or the r_frame_rate, which I cannot check offline. Neither case is handled or flagged. CFR music videos with near-zero offset are unaffected. Phone VFR, MPEG-TS and offset files are plausible real-world inputs, and the unified-timeline contract (schema line 5, PLAN §2) is what M6 relies on, so high.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound but L effort. A cheaper first step: take times from scenedetect's FrameTimecode.seconds, which is PTS-exact in 0.7, and map them to decoded indices through an ffprobe packet-pts table (`-show_entries packet=pts_time`, demux only). Shift all video times by (video start_time − audio start_time). Run the motion pass with `-fps_mode passthrough` (or setpts=PTS-STARTPTS) and compute pair times from the first output pts plus that offset. Record the offset and a vfr indicator additively (minor bump). Then add the fixtures: itsoffset 0.5, and a 30→15 fps concat.

#### ORCH-13: Which audio stream gets analysed, and its offset from the video clock, are left to ffmpeg's defaults and never recorded

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1, H2, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:79`, `mrw/ingest.py:94`, `mrw/ingest.py:124`, `mrw/ingest.py:252`, `PLAN.md:34`

**Evidence.** The audio metadata comes from `_first_stream(streams, "audio")` (ingest.py:79-87, 188, 252-259). Neither ffmpeg call (ingest.py:94-108, 124-141) passes `-map`, so ffmpeg's own stream selection decides what is decoded. Assumed ffmpeg behaviour (medium-high confidence): it ranks audio streams by default disposition first, then channel count. Ingest never reads any stream's `start_time`. The FLAC starts at the first decoded audio sample, while video.py times shots as frame_index/fps on the video clock (video.py:98). PLAN.md:34-37 says every timestamp is measured "from t = 0 = first sample of the normalized audio decode".

**Failure scenario.** (a) An MKV concert rip has stereo LPCM as stream #1 and 5.1 DTS as stream #2, both flagged default (Matroska's default for that flag is 1). ffmpeg decodes a downmix of the 5.1 DTS, while source.json media.audio reports pcm_s16le with 2 channels, so the analysed mix is not the one described. (b) An MP4 whose video starts 0.083 s after its audio (B-frame delay with no edit list), or an MKV with a 200 ms audio delay: video.json cut times are offset from the audio_features beats by that amount. M6's ±70 ms on-beat window (OQ-12) will then misclassify cuts every time, and nothing records the offset.

**Proposal.** Choose the audio stream explicitly from the ffprobe output: the first audio stream with disposition.default, otherwise the first audio stream. Pass `-map 0:<index>` to both ffmpeg calls. Record `media.audio.stream_index` and the number of audio streams (additive, source 1.1.0), and flag files with more than one. Record each stream's `start_time` and an additive `media.video.start_offset_seconds` (video start_time minus audio start_time, 3 dp), so the video stage and M6 can place shots on the shared timeline. Settle and document this convention before M6.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed in code: the audio metadata comes from the first audio stream by index (ingest.py:79-87, 188, 252-259); neither ffmpeg call maps a stream (ingest.py:94-108, 124-141); no stream's start_time is read; and video.py times shots as frame/fps counted from the first decoded frame (video.py:98). The consequences depend on ffmpeg behaviour I cannot verify offline. (a) In ffmpeg 5 and later, automatic selection scores the default disposition above channel count, and Matroska tracks default to FlagDefault=1, so a 5.1 track can be decoded while source.json describes the stereo one. (b) Audio-only raw/FLAC output starts at the first decoded sample with no lead-in padding, so any start offset between the streams shifts video.json against the audio beat grid. How often real files hit either is uncertain, but the ±70 ms M6 window (OQ-12) is sensitive to it, and no doc records a convention. Medium; settle it before M6.

### A4. Motion across a cut is counted inside a shot

#### VID-3: Motion: the flow pair straddling each cut is counted inside a neighbouring shot, inflating mean and dominating p95 of short shots

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H2, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:163`, `mrw/video.py:171-173`, `mrw/video.py:271`, `schemas/video.schema.json:89`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:163 `inside = [v for v, t in zip(pair_values, pair_times) if start <= t < end]` assigns each 12 fps pair to a shot by its midpoint (mrw/video.py:271 `pair_times = [(i + 0.5) / MOTION_FPS for i in range(len(values))]`). The pair whose two samples lie on opposite sides of a cut always has its midpoint inside one of the two shots. So every cut adds one 'flow between two unrelated images' value to a neighbouring shot. p95 uses np.percentile with linear interpolation (mrw/video.py:173). With n≈12 pairs (a 1 s shot), the percentile rank is 0.95×11 = 10.45, which lands 45% of the way to the maximum. With two straddling pairs and n=24, it lands 85% of the way to the second spike. Neither the schema (schemas/video.schema.json:89) nor PLAN.md:292-294 excludes transitions. The cut fixture uses flat solid colours (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195), where Farnebäck returns about 0 flow, so the bias never shows up in tests.

**Failure scenario.** Take a fast-cut music video (1-2 s shots) made of mostly static, textured shots. Assumption, medium confidence (OpenCV could not be run here): Farnebäck with the default 3-level pyramid returns several pixels of mean flow between two unrelated textured 320 px frames, i.e. about 0.005-0.03 after diagonal normalisation. A static 1 s shot then reports p95 ≈ 0.005-0.014 and a mean raised by about 0.001-0.003. On the schema's own scale (<0.002 static) that reads as 'gentle movement' or more. The error grows as shots get shorter, which is exactly the energetic content where the mapping engine relies on motion.

**Proposal.** Keep each pair's exact interval (t_k, t_k+1). Count a pair for a shot only if both samples fall inside it: start <= t_k and t_k+1 <= end, with half a motion frame of tolerance. Shots left with no fully contained pair use the existing H2 0.0/0.0 convention. Update the schema's motion description to say cut-straddling pairs are excluded. Add a slow test that joins two textured static scenes (noise or testsrc patterns) with a hard cut and asserts both shots have p95 < 0.002, while a moving-pattern shot stays above 0.002.

**Verifier's notes.** The mechanism is certain. shot_motion keeps a pair when its midpoint falls in the shot, `if start <= t < end` (video.py:163), with `pair_times = [(i + 0.5) / MOTION_FPS ...]` (271). The 12 fps pair whose two samples straddle a cut always has its midpoint in one of the two neighbouring shots, so each cut adds one cross-cut flow value to a shot. The p95 arithmetic is right for np.percentile's default linear method (rank 0.95·(n−1)): one spike moves p95 about 45% of the way to the spike when n=12. Nothing covers transitions, neither the H2 comment (165-169) nor the schema motion description. How large the spike is depends on what Farnebäck returns for two unrelated frames. I cannot verify that offline, but spurious flow of several pixels on textured content is very likely. The effect therefore lands mainly on short shots (under about 20 pairs, i.e. under about 1.7 s), whose p95 moves from static into the gentle band. Long shots' p95 is unaffected. I downgraded from high because this is a systematic bias in a coarse heuristic score, not a crash or a contract break.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Assign each 12 fps sample to a shot by its own timestamp, using the same [start,end) partition as the shots. Keep pair k only when samples k and k+1 map to the same shot. This needs no tolerance fudge and has no off-by-one when t_{k+1} == end; that sample already shows the next shot. It must use the same origin as the shot times, so land it with or after the VID-2 motion-padding fix, otherwise it excludes the wrong pairs on offset files. Test: two textured static scenes joined by a hard cut, plus a moving-pattern scene.

### A5. Last shot can extend past the real end of the stream

#### VID-11: Last shot and video_span are extended to an ffprobe frame estimate that can exceed the real stream, which can abort extraction

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:213-214`, `mrw/video.py:285-292`, `mrw/video.py:390`, `mrw/video.py:236-239`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:213-214 reads `nb = stream.get("nb_frames")` / `frame_count = int(nb) if nb else int(round(float(info["format"]["duration"]) * fps))`. The container duration is that of the longest stream, often the audio. mrw/video.py:291-292 then stretches the last scene to this count (`if bounds[-1][1] < frame_count: bounds[-1] = (bounds[-1][0], frame_count)`), and the no-cut fallback `[(0, frame_count)]` does the same (mrw/video.py:285-286). Representative indices are clamped only to `e_frame - 1` (mrw/video.py:390), and a missing frame aborts the run (mrw/video.py:236-239, 'decode returned too few frames'). Verified with ffmpeg 6.1.1: an MKV whose video is 4.0 s / 96 frames and whose audio runs to 4.523 s reports nb_frames=N/A and format duration 4.523, giving frame_count = round(4.523×24) = 109.

**Failure scenario.** WebM and MKV files, common for web-sourced music videos, have no nb_frames. When their audio outlasts the video, or for a still-image 'official audio' video whose video stream is shorter than the container, the last shot and video_span.end_seconds are overstated by the overhang. When the overhang exceeds about a third of the real last shot, the representative frames fall past the last decoded frame. Example: a 1 s last shot at frames 72-96 followed by 3 s of audio is extended to [72,168), and its representative frames 104 and 136 do not exist. The stage then fails with exit 1 on every run.

**Proposal.** Stop relying on container metadata for where the stream ends. Call detect(..., start_in_scene=True) and use scenedetect's own last end, which reflects frames actually decoded, or count frames during the extraction or motion decode. Clamp representative indices to the decoded count, and derive video_span from real frame times (see VID-2). Add an MKV fixture with an audio tail.

**Verifier's notes.** Reproduced: an MKV built with system ffmpeg from 96 frames of 24 fps video plus 4.5 s of audio has no nb_frames key in the ffprobe JSON, and format.duration is 4.523. The formula at video.py:213-214 then gives 109 frames; `-count_frames` gives 96. _detect_shots stretches the last scene, and the no-cut fallback, to that count (285-292). Representative indices are clamped only to e_frame−1 (390), and a missing frame raises 'decode returned too few frames' (236-239). One minor correction: the failure threshold is an overhang larger than the real last shot for extended shots under 4 s, or larger than half of it for longer shots (the 2/3-point frame), not 'about a third'. The silent overstatement of the last shot and of video_span is bounded by the audio tail. The hard failure needs a tail that is long relative to the last shot, so this is an edge case and medium is right. scenedetect 0.7's last scene ends at `self._last_pos + 1` (scene_manager.py:366-368), which reflects frames actually decoded, so the proposal is sound.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Alternatively, when nb_frames is absent, use `ffprobe -count_packets -show_entries stream=nb_read_packets` (demux-only and fast), never extend past scenedetect's own end, and call detect(..., start_in_scene=True) so a video with no cuts also gets the decoded end.

### A6. Colour fidelity (HDR, OpenCV Lab, letterbox, pixel aspect, ffmpeg conversion)

#### CRIT-3: HDR/wide-gamut video (HLG or PQ, BT.2020, e.g. iPhone 'HDR Video') is converted to rgb24 with no tone or gamut mapping, so stills, palettes and captions get large colour errors

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:225-228`, `mrw/video.py:116-118`, `mrw/video.py:197-215`, `mrw/video.py:255-258`, `mrw/ingest.py:215-221`, `mrw/models.py:66-71`, `schemas/source.schema.json:67-78`

**Evidence.** Frames are converted with no colour management. mrw/video.py:225-228 runs `["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-i", str(path), "-vf", f"select='{expr}'", "-vsync", "0", "-f", "rawvideo", "-pix_fmt", "rgb24", "-"]`, and the palette treats the resulting bytes as sRGB (video.py:118 `cv2.cvtColor(rgb, cv2.COLOR_RGB2Lab)`). Neither _video_stream_info (video.py:197-215) nor ingest (ingest.py:215-221) reads color_transfer or color_primaries. A grep of mrw/ for color_transfer, color_primaries, tonemap or zscale finds nothing.

Verified with ffmpeg 6.1.1 (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/hdr). I encoded solid sRGB colours as 10-bit HEVC tagged HLG/BT.2020 and extracted them exactly as mrw does (ΔE script: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/deltae.py):

| Source | mrw-style extraction | ΔE76 | Colour-managed (zscale to BT.709) |
|---|---|---|---|
| #d02020 | #733623 | 47.7 (L* 45 to 30) | #d33a2a |
| #20a0a0 | #415b66 | 34.2 | #288f9e |
| #e0a080 | #7e6954 | 31.5 | #d69d7c |

Assumption: swscale converts only the YUV matrix, not the transfer function or primaries. This is verified on 6.1.1 but not on newer FFmpeg; if newer versions behave differently, HDR stills also differ between CI and the Mac, which extends VID-12. Error sizes on real HLG footage depend on mastering (diffuse white sits at about 75% HLG signal); PQ is worse.

**Failure scenario.** An iPhone 12+ performance video recorded with the default 'HDR Video' setting (HEVC Main10, HLG BT.2020):
- Every shot's palette is darker and desaturated; a red stage light is reported as brown.
- The JPEG stills look dim and grey, and the captions describe muted lighting.
- Nothing in source.json or video.json says the source was HDR.

**Proposal.** - In _video_stream_info, read color_transfer, color_primaries, color_space and any Dolby Vision side data; record them in source.json media.video (additive minor).
- When the transfer is arib-std-b67 or smpte2084, or the primaries are bt2020, insert a fixed tone-map and gamut chain before rgb24 in _extract_frames_rgb. Example: `zscale=t=linear:npl=203,format=gbrpf32le,zscale=p=bt709,tonemap=hable:desat=0,zscale=t=bt709:m=bt709:r=pc,format=rgb24`, or libplacebo.
- Record the conversion in video.json.
- Fail with a clear message when the system ffmpeg lacks zimg.
- Test with a synthetic HLG fixture like the one above: the top swatch must be within ΔE < 10 of the SDR source colour.

**Verifier's notes.** video.py:225-228 converts frames with '-pix_fmt rgb24' and no colour filter. Nothing in mrw/ reads color_transfer or color_primaries (grep finds nothing), and the issue is not recorded anywhere.

I encoded solid sRGB colours as 10-bit HEVC HLG/BT.2020 with BT.2408 reference white (SDR white at HLG signal 0.749) and extracted them exactly as mrw does (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit-verify/scripts/hdr_check.py):
- #d02020 became #96432f (ΔE76 36.6)
- #20a0a0 became #628b8d (19.6)
- #e0a080 became #a7927b (21.4)
- grey: ΔE 3.9

With a hotter mapping (white at 63%), the errors were 16–46, which matches the finding. Medium stands: the error is silent, but HDR is the less common input for music videos (although it is the iPhone default).

The proposed fix is wrong. On the BT.2408 fixtures, the chain zscale=t=linear:npl=203,…,tonemap=hable:desat=0,… gave ΔE 15–31 and darkened neutral grey from L* 53.6 to 38.2, which is worse than mrw's current extraction for neutrals. A BT.2408-style inverse with no hable, zscale=t=linear:npl=203,format=gbrpf32le,zscale=p=bt709:t=iec61966-2-1:m=gbr:r=full:npl=203, reproduced the colours within ΔE ≤ 1.2. That result is partly by construction for these fixtures, but it is the standard HLG-to-SDR mapping.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Step 1 (S):
- Read color_transfer, color_primaries and color_space in _video_stream_info.
- Record them in source.json media.video (additive).
- State in video.json whether colours were converted, so consumers are told (H1).

Step 2 (M):
- HLG (arib-std-b67): linearise at npl=203, convert to BT.709 primaries with clipping, apply the sRGB/BT.709 transfer. No hable.
- PQ (smpte2084): use a tone map that preserves mid-tones (e.g. libplacebo bt.2390, or tonemap=mobius), tuned by fixtures.
- Fail with a clear message when zimg is absent.
- Keep a ΔE<10 fixture test at two mastering levels, plus a double-run byte test, since this adds a float pixel path that may differ between x86 and ARM.

#### VID-13: k-means runs in OpenCV 8-bit Lab, where L is stretched ×2.55 relative to a/b, so the 'perceptual' palette over-splits lightness and merges hues

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:116-120`, `PLAN.md:286-291`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:116-118 says '# cv2 uint8 Lab is uint8-scaled (L*255/100, a/b offset +128); keep the clustering AND the reverse conversion in that same space.' and then runs `lab = cv2.cvtColor(rgb, cv2.COLOR_RGB2Lab).reshape(-1, 3).astype("float64")` followed by `KMeans(...).fit(lab)` (mrw/video.py:120). The round trip back to hex is consistent, and RGB order is used throughout. But Euclidean distance in this space is sqrt((2.55ΔL)² + Δa² + Δb²), not ΔE76, so lightness differences count 6.5× more in the squared k-means objective. PLAN.md:289-291 justifies Lab on the grounds that 'perceptual distance is what dominant color means'.

**Failure scenario.** Two shades of one grey-blue 10 L* apart sit 25.5 units apart in the clustering space, although their true ΔE is 10. Red and orange at equal lightness (Δa=15, Δb=10) sit 18.0 units apart, matching their true ΔE of 18. With k=5, the clustering therefore tends to split a dark background into lightness steps while merging red and orange into one muddy swatch, the reverse of perceptual priority.

**Proposal.** Cluster in true CIELab. Either convert with `cv2.cvtColor(rgb.astype(np.float32) / 255, cv2.COLOR_RGB2Lab)` (L 0..100, signed a/b) and convert centres back through the float Lab2RGB path before rounding to hex, or scale column 0 by 100/255 before fitting and undo it afterwards. Determinism (D5): today's integer-valued features make sklearn's thread-local centre sums exact. Non-integer features could pick up order-dependent ulp jitter in its OpenMP reduction. So wrap the fit in threadpoolctl.threadpool_limits(1), or quantise the features, and rely on the existing double-run test.

**Verifier's notes.** OpenCV's 8-bit Lab stores L as L*·255/100 and a/b offset by +128; the comment at video.py:116-117 says so itself. KMeans runs on those values directly (118-120), so Euclidean distance weights ΔL by 2.55 against Δa/Δb, which contradicts the 'Lab because perceptual distance' rationale (PLAN.md:289-291, OQ-9). The scenario arithmetic is right: 25.5 against 18.0. The determinism caveat is also sound: today's integer-valued features keep sklearn's per-thread centre sums exact, and non-integer features would not. Still, this is a second-order quality issue: any k-means palette is a heuristic, and CIEDE2000 itself down-weights lightness in many regions. VID-1 dominates palette correctness, so I rate it low and would bundle it with the VID-1 fix so palettes change only once.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Scale the L column by 100/255 before fitting and undo it before the uint8 round trip back to RGB, or use the float32 Lab path. Wrap the fit in threadpool_limits(1), or quantise the features to dyadic steps so the sums stay exact. Ship it in the same PR as VID-1.

#### CRIT-8: Letterbox/pillarbox bars are treated as picture content: a ~26% black swatch in every shot of a 2.39:1-in-16:9 video, and motion diluted by the same fraction

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** C2, S4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:472-474`, `mrw/video.py:102-137`, `mrw/video.py:140-156`, `mrw/video.py:244-272`, `schemas/video.schema.json`

**Evidence.** The palette clusters every pooled pixel of the representative frames (video.py:472-474, palette_from_rgb at :102-137). Motion averages flow over every pixel and normalises by the full-frame diagonal (video.py:146 `diagonal = float(np.hypot(width, height))`, :154 `np.linalg.norm(flow, axis=2).mean()`). Nothing detects or crops black borders; there is no cropdetect anywhere.

The schema calls the palette 'Dominant colors' and the motion score 'comparable across videos' (video.schema.json shots.palette / shots.motion descriptions). For 2.39:1 picture inside 1920x1080, the active area is 803 rows, so the bars are 25.6% of the pixels.

**Failure scenario.** A letterboxed cinematic music video, once VID-1 is fixed:
- Every shot's palette carries a near-black swatch of about 0.26, which is the top swatch in darker scenes. An LLM consumer reads 'dominant colour: black' throughout.
- Motion mean and p95 are scaled by about 0.74 compared with the same footage without bars.

**Proposal.** - After fixing VID-1, detect constant borders once per video: ffmpeg cropdetect with fixed limit/round over sampled frames, or a border-variance check on the extracted frames.
- Crop stills, palette and motion to the active picture.
- Record the crop rectangle in video.json as a params block (additive, S4).
- Test with a synthetic letterboxed fixture.

**Verifier's notes.** The described state is accurate:
- The palette pools every pixel of the representative frames (video.py:472-474, then palette_from_rgb).
- Motion averages the whole flow field and divides by the full-frame diagonal (video.py:146, :154).
- Nothing detects borders.

Even with today's column pooling, the black bar rows survive the INTER_AREA averaging intact. For 2.39:1 inside 1920x1080 (803 active rows), a black swatch of about 0.26 appears and motion is scaled by about 0.74. That undercuts the schema's 'comparable across videos' claim for motion.

This is a design improvement worth doing at low priority; letterboxing is common in music videos.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Detect bars deterministically from frames already decoded: rows or columns whose maximum luma stays below a fixed threshold in every representative frame of the video. Requiring this across all frames avoids cropping dark scenes.
- Exclude bar pixels from the palette, and from motion (add a crop to the motion-pass ffmpeg command, or slice the arrays).
- Decide explicitly whether stills are cropped.
- Record the active rectangle in video.json (additive, S4).
- Test with a synthetic letterboxed fixture.

#### CRIT-7: Non-square-pixel (anamorphic DVD/HDV) video is analysed and stored at storage aspect: stills are squeezed and source.json reports the wrong shape

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** C2, H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:212`, `mrw/video.py:226-228`, `mrw/video.py:240`, `mrw/video.py:253-257`, `mrw/video.py:184-187`, `mrw/ingest.py:215-221`, `schemas/source.schema.json:67-78`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:212 `width, height = int(stream["width"]), int(stream["height"])` takes the coded dimensions. video.py:226-228 extracts rawvideo with no scaling, and video.py:240 reshapes to (height, width, 3). The motion pass derives its height from the same stored aspect (video.py:253-254 `height = max(2, round(MOTION_WIDTH * h0 / w0 / 2) * 2)`). Ingest records only width and height (ingest.py:215-221). No code reads sample_aspect_ratio, display_aspect_ratio or field_order (grep).

Verified with ffmpeg 6.1.1 (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/sar): a 720x480 SAR 32:27 (DAR 16:9) stream probes as width 720 / height 480. mrw's select+rawvideo extraction returns 1,036,800 bytes = 720x480x3, which is the storage aspect.

This is separate from the rotation findings (ORCH-8/VID-4/SEC-3): frames are not garbled, the geometry is just silently wrong.

**Failure scenario.** A DVD-sourced 16:9 music video (720x480, SAR 32:27):
- Stills are written at 720x480 (3:2), squeezed horizontally to 84% of their display width.
- source.json says 720x480, so the renderer and the LLM infer 3:2.

HDV 1440x1080 (SAR 4:3) squeezes to 75%. Interlaced sources, often the same DVD/broadcast material, also show combing in the stills.

**Proposal.** - Read sample_aspect_ratio and field_order in _video_stream_info.
- Extract with `scale=trunc(iw*sar/2)*2:ih,setsar=1`, plus `bwdif=mode=send_frame` for interlaced sources, and reshape with the display dimensions.
- Apply the same correction in _motion_pass.
- Record sample_aspect_ratio (or display dimensions) and field_order in source.json media.video (additive).
- Test with the synthetic SAR 32:27 fixture.

**Verifier's notes.** I verified this with ffmpeg 6.1.1. A 720x480 stream with SAR 32:27 (DAR 16:9) probes as width 720, height 480. mrw's select+rawvideo extraction returns 1,036,800 bytes, which is 720x480x3. video.py:212 and :240 reshape at storage size, and no code reads sample_aspect_ratio or field_order. Stills are therefore written at 3:2, and source.json reports 720x480. Low stands.

The proposed ffmpeg `scale=trunc(iw*sar/2)*2:ih` has two problems:
- It breaks the D5 invariant in video.py:12-15: 'Pillow does all resizing'.
- It upscales 720 to 853, against 'NEVER upscaled'.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Read sample_aspect_ratio (and field_order) in _video_stream_info.
- Keep the raw decode at storage size and correct the aspect in encode_jpeg with Pillow, shrinking the other dimension: 720x480 at SAR 32:27 becomes 720x405; 1440x1080 at SAR 4:3 becomes 1440x810, then 1280x720. This keeps both the never-upscale and the single-resizer invariants.
- In _motion_pass, compute the height from w0 × SAR.
- Record SAR or display dimensions in source.json media.video (additive).
- Treat deinterlacing (bwdif) as a separate, optional step with its own determinism check.

#### VID-12: Frame bytes (hence frame sha256, caption-cache keys and video.json) depend on the unpinned system ffmpeg's YUV→RGB conversion, and its version is not recorded

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:9-15`, `mrw/video.py:226-228`, `mrw/video.py:465-467`, `mrw/captions.py:41-43`, `mrw/models.py:305-310`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:34-35`

**Evidence.** The module docstring calls JPEG byte stability 'load-bearing' (mrw/video.py:9-15) and pins the Pillow encoder. The pixels themselves, though, come from the system ffmpeg's auto-inserted swscale conversion with default flags (mrw/video.py:226-228, `-pix_fmt rgb24`; no -sws_flags, colour matrix or range given). Frame sha256s are written into video.json (mrw/video.py:465-467) and key the caption cache (mrw/captions.py:41-43). RunMetadata records only the mrw version (mrw/models.py:305-310), not the ffmpeg or Pillow/libjpeg versions. CI uses Ubuntu's apt ffmpeg (.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:34-35; 6.1 on x86), while the target platform is macOS arm64 with Homebrew ffmpeg, which updates often. Assumption, medium confidence: swscale's default SIMD YUV→RGB paths are not guaranteed bit-identical across versions and architectures, which is why FFmpeg's own FATE tests pass +bitexact/+accurate_rnd.

**Failure scenario.** After `brew upgrade ffmpeg`, the video stage re-runs, for example because of an unrelated video config edit. Some RGB values shift by ±1, so every JPEG sha256 changes. video.json now differs for the same input and config, and nothing in the manifest explains why. Every caption becomes a cache miss, so the whole track is captioned and paid for again; the budget gate shows a full-price estimate with no reason given. Caption caches also fail to replay when a library moves to another machine.

**Proposal.** Pin the conversion with an explicit scale step: `-sws_flags accurate_rnd+bitexact+full_chroma_int` plus explicit in_color_matrix and in_range/out_range. Record the first line of `ffmpeg -version` and the Pillow/libjpeg-turbo versions in run metadata (additive manifest minor bump). State in PLAN §7 that frame byte-identity is scoped to a fixed ffmpeg build. Optionally, to let captions survive decoder changes, key the cache on (source sha256, frame indices, prompt_version, model, JPEG settings) instead of the JPEG bytes.

**Verifier's notes.** The code facts are confirmed. The rgb24 conversion has no -sws_flags or colour options (video.py:226-228). The frame sha256 keys the caption cache (captions.py:41-43) and is written into video.json (465-467). RunMetadata records only the mrw version (models.py:305-310). CI uses Ubuntu's apt ffmpeg (claude-review.yml:34-35). Whether frame bytes actually differ across ffmpeg versions or CPU architectures depends on swscale internals I cannot run here. My reading of FFmpeg is that the default unscaled yuv420p→RGB path is taken only without SWS_ACCURATE_RND and dispatches to arch-specific SIMD code that is not guaranteed bit-exact, so plausible. The impact is real but bounded: a one-time re-captioning spend that the budget gate still estimates, and video.json drift that nothing explains. Medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Add `-sws_flags accurate_rnd+bitexact+full_chroma_int`, which applies to the auto-inserted scalers. Record the first line of `ffmpeg -version` and the Pillow/libjpeg-turbo versions in run metadata (additive manifest bump). Scope PLAN §7's JPEG identity to a fixed ffmpeg build. Re-keying the caption cache away from frame sha256 would reverse OQ-10's recorded design and needs an OQ-10 resolution note (P2), so treat it as optional.

### A7. Motion precision and unrounded detector params

#### VID-14: Motion rounded to 3 dp leaves about one significant digit in the schema's own static/gentle range; the normative example uses 4 dp

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D3, S5, C2 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:171-173`, `schemas/video.schema.json:89`, `schemas/examples/video.json:41-42`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:171-173 rounds mean and p95 with canonical.round_ratio, i.e. 3 dp. The schema's scale (schemas/video.schema.json:89) is '<0.002 static, 0.002–0.01 gentle movement, >0.03 fast action'. At 3 dp, 'static' has two representable values (0.000, 0.001) and 'gentle' has nine. schemas/examples/video.json:41-42 shows `"mean": 0.0038, "p95": 0.0091` (also 0.0112/0.0247 and 0.0284/0.0561), values the code cannot emit; it would write 0.004/0.009. Review 002 made the examples normative. The same example's shot times are not frame-derived the way the code derives them: shot 0 ends at 3.104, but end_frame 74 / 23.976 gives 3.086. Its representative times are exact fractions of the span rather than snapped to frames. The example still validates against the schema; both checks were made with my own script.

**Failure scenario.** Two shots with true mean motion 0.0015 (static) and 0.0024 (gentle) both serialise as 0.002. A mapping engine thresholding at the documented 0.002 boundary cannot tell them apart. A Swift or LLM consumer that treats the example as the contract expects values and precision the producer never emits. The 3 dp ratio rule (OQ-14) was set before it was clear that motion lives in the [0, 0.03] range.

**Proposal.** Give motion its own precision class, e.g. 5 dp or 3 significant digits. It stays a JSON number, so the change is compatible; document it in the schema and the precision contract. Alternatively, express motion in a well-scaled unit such as percent-of-diagonal per second, which is a breaking change and needs a major bump. Regenerate examples/video.json from a real run of the fixture pipeline so that times, frame indices and precision match the implementation (S5).

**Verifier's notes.** I checked with my own script (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/vid-verify/scripts/example_check.py). schemas/examples/video.json validates with 0 errors, yet its motion values have 4 dp (0.0038/0.0091, 0.0112/0.0247, 0.0284/0.0561), which round_ratio cannot produce (video.py:171-173). Its times are not frame-derived either: shot 0 ends at 3.104 where 74/23.976 gives 3.086, and the representative time is 1.552 where frame snapping gives 1.543. The 3 dp choice is recorded as deliberate (video.py:16-17), but the contradicting normative example and the schema's own 0.002 boundary are a genuinely new argument. The practical impact is small: steps of 0.001 on a scale of about 0-0.06, mattering mainly at the static/gentle boundary. The example drift is a docs issue (S5). Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Use 4 dp for motion. It matches the normative example, gives 20 levels below the 0.002 'static' boundary, and keeps the JSON type. Document it in the schema description and in the precision contract, with an OQ-14 note per P2. Regenerate examples/video.json from a real fixture run, and bundle this with VID-1/VID-13 so video.json bytes change once.

#### DET-14: Motion is rounded to 3 dp, coarser than the normative example's 4 dp and the schema's own documented scale

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D3, S5, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:159-174`, `mrw/video.py:16-17`, `schemas/examples/video.json`, `schemas/video.schema.json:89`, `docs/reviews/002-schema-revisions.md:29`

**Evidence.** shot_motion rounds with canonical.round_ratio, i.e. 3 dp (video.py:171-173; the module docstring says 'proportions/motion 3 dp'). The example, which review 002:29 declares normative ('Treat these examples as normative going forward'), shows 4-dp motion values: `{"mean": 0.0038, "p95": 0.0091}`, `{"mean": 0.0112, "p95": 0.0247}`, `{"mean": 0.0284, "p95": 0.0561}`. My scan of all six examples (det/check_examples.py) found this to be the only precision mismatch; all six examples validate against their schemas. The schema documents the scale as '<0.002 static, 0.002–0.01 gentle movement, >0.03 fast'. At 3 dp, the static band has only two representable values (0.000 and 0.001), and the gentle band eight.

**Failure scenario.** On the 320-px motion pass (diagonal about 367 px), a slow pan of about 0.9 px per frame (≈0.0024) and one of about 1.3 px per frame (≈0.0035) are reported as 0.002 and 0.004. That is roughly ±20% quantization exactly where the mapping engine has to separate static shots from gentle ones. Consumers built from the example expect 4-dp values that never appear.

**Proposal.** Choose the precision explicitly and make code, contract and example agree. Recommended: add 'normalized motion: 4 dp' to the precision contract in schemas/README.md and CLAUDE.md, add a canonical.round_motion helper, and use it in shot_motion. Finer rounding is still a JSON number, so video 1.0.0 consumers are unaffected and the example stays normative. The alternative is to keep 3 dp and update both the example and the scale guidance.

**Verifier's notes.** shot_motion rounds with canonical.round_ratio, i.e. 3 dp (video.py:171-173), as the module docstring says: 'proportions/motion 3 dp' (video.py:16-17). schemas/examples/video.json shows 4-dp motion values (0.0038/0.0091, 0.0112/0.0247, 0.0284/0.0561). Review 002 (docs/reviews/002-schema-revisions.md:29) declares the examples normative, and S5 requires them to be updated alongside the code. The quantization point holds: below the schema's 0.002 'static' bound (video.schema.json:89) only 0.000 and 0.001 are representable. All examples still validate, so this is S5 drift. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Either choice is fine. Moving to 4 dp changes video.json for new runs under an unchanged config_hash, while existing documents stay 'ok' at 3 dp (the DET-8 gap), so pair it with a video algorithm-version bump. Updating the example to 3 dp is the zero-churn alternative.

#### SCH-13: video.json precision: motion's 3 dp is unstated and coarse against the schema's own scale, and detector params are written unrounded

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D3, C2, S5 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:160-174`, `mrw/video.py:16-17`, `schemas/video.schema.json:87-95`, `mrw/video.py:498-502`, `schemas/README.md:30-31`

**Evidence.** shot_motion uses the ratio rounder: video.py:172-173 `mean=canonical.round_ratio(float(arr.mean()))` and `p95=canonical.round_ratio(...)`. The docstring at line 17 records '3 dp' as a deliberate choice. The schema's motion description gives the scale '<0.002 static, 0.002–0.01 gentle movement, >0.03 fast action' (video.schema.json:89) and states no precision. At 3 dp the whole 'static' band is just {0.000, 0.001}, and values from 0.0015 to 0.0019 round up into the 'gentle' band. The worked example uses 4 dp (0.0038, 0.0091, ...), which the code cannot emit. The detector block embeds config values unrounded: video.py:500-501 `threshold=vcfg.detector_threshold, min_shot_seconds=vcfg.min_shot_seconds`. That is the same D3 issue review 006 round 1 fixed for vocal_activity.params. The new point here is the conflict with the schema's own scale and the example; the 3 dp choice itself is known.

**Failure scenario.** Two near-static shots with true motion 0.0004 and 0.0014 report 0.000 and 0.001. A shot at 0.0016 reports 0.002, and a consumer applying the schema's '<0.002 static' rule classifies it as moving. `[video] min_shot_seconds = 0.3333333` is written into video.json unrounded.

**Proposal.** Either declare motion's precision explicitly and choose one that can resolve the documented scale (e.g. 5 dp, listed in schemas/README.md's precision contract, as video 1.1.0 with a note since outputs change), or restate the scale in 0.001 steps and fix the example. Round the detector params on the way in: round_seconds for min_shot_seconds and a stated precision for threshold. Run detection from the rounded values, as features does.

**Verifier's notes.** shot_motion rounds with round_ratio, 3 dp (video.py:172-173). That is deliberate per the docstring at video.py:16-17, and video.schema.json:89's 'Rough scale' (<0.002 static) then resolves to {0.000, 0.001} with a half-quantum boundary effect. The example's 4 dp values (0.0038, ...) can't be emitted. The D3 part is new and concrete: video.py:500-501 echoes `vcfg.detector_threshold` and `vcfg.min_shot_seconds` unrounded. Review 006 round 1 fixed this exact pattern for vocal_activity.params, and nothing records detector params as an exception (no M5 review record exists). The motion-precision argument is weak: the scale is explicitly rough and a precision change would churn the frozen video outputs.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Narrow the fix. Route min_shot_seconds through round_seconds and give threshold a stated precision (e.g. 1 dp), and run detection from the rounded values, as features does (features.py:233-236). Keep motion at 3 dp, restate the scale at that resolution (e.g. '≤0.001 static, 0.002-0.009 gentle, ≥0.030 fast'), and fix the example to 3 dp (with SCH-8). Do not change motion precision.

### A8. Video memory use

#### SEC-6: The video stage holds entire raw decode outputs in RAM, needing many GB for 4K or long videos (2x transient), and decodes the source three times

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** performance · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:225`, `mrw/video.py:234`, `mrw/video.py:255`, `mrw/video.py:393`, `mrw/video.py:472`

**Evidence.** `_extract_frames_rgb` runs ffmpeg with `capture_output=True` and keeps `raw = proc.stdout` (225-234). The `frames` dict holds numpy views into that buffer, which keeps it alive through the whole shot loop because palettes need full-res frames (393, 472-474). `_motion_pass` likewise captures the entire 12 fps grayscale stream (255-264). CPython's communicate() collects 32 KiB chunks and then joins them with `b''.join`, so peak memory is about 2x the output size. Raw rgb24 is 6.2 MB per 1080p frame and 24.9 MB per 4K frame, with 1-2 frames per shot. Estimates by case:
- 4-minute 4K music video, 150 shots (~165 frames): ~4.1 GB held, ~8 GB peak.
- 4K video with fast cutting (300 shots): ~8 GB held, ~16 GB peak.
- 1-hour 1080p concert (~1,500 frames): ~9.3 GB held, ~19 GB peak, plus 2.5 GB (5 GB peak) for the motion stream.
The stage also decodes the whole source three times: once in PySceneDetect (280), once in the extraction pass (224-228), and once in the motion pass. The extraction pass decodes every frame to keep about one per shot and evaluates an N-term `select` expression on each frame.
