PLAN.md:152 budgets 'at most ~2 sequential decodes plus a handful of precise seeks'. `--estimate` pays the same memory cost, because extraction (393) runs before the estimate.

**Failure scenario.** Running `mrw video T`, or even just `mrw video T --estimate`, on a 4-minute, fast-cut 4K music video pushes a 16 GB Mac into heavy swap or the out-of-memory dialog. A 1-hour 1080p concert needs about 19 GB at peak, and each full software decode of an hour of 4K takes tens of minutes.

**Proposal.** Stream both passes. Use Popen with stderr going to a temp file, and `readinto` exactly `frame_bytes` per selected frame. Process each frame as soon as it arrives: encode the JPEG, take the <=256 px palette sample (per SEC-1), record the sha256, then drop the full-res buffer. Memory becomes O(1 frame). Feed motion frames to `motion_stats` straight from the pipe. Merge extraction and motion into a single ffmpeg decode using a `split` filter with two outputs (select on one branch; fps=12, scale and gray on the other), which removes one full decode. Assert exact byte totals. The decode and encode paths don't change, so the existing double-run byte-identity test should keep passing.

**Verifier's notes.** video.py:225-234 uses capture_output, so the whole rgb24 stream is buffered; communicate() joins 32 KiB chunks, about 2x transiently. The frames dict then keeps every selected full-res frame through the shot loop for the palette step (video.py:393, 472-474). One correction to the mechanism: `raw[a:b]` copies bytes, so the frames are copies, not views keeping `raw` alive. The held (≈1x) and peak (≈2x) figures still hold. A 4K frame is 24.9 MB, so 150-300 frames means 3.7-7.5 GB held and 7.5-15 GB peak. `--estimate` with the anthropic backend extracts frames first (line 393 runs before 420). The third full decode is deliberate: video.py:9-11 says 'exact source FRAME INDEX (ffmpeg select filter on frame number, single sequential decode), never time-seek'. So PLAN.md:151-160's '2 decodes + seeks' wording is stale; the code is not wrong there. The N-term select cost is negligible. A typical 1080p video stays at about 1-2 GB peak. Downgraded to medium: this is scalability on 4K or fast-cut inputs, with no wrong output.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Stream the extraction with Popen, stderr to a temp file, and `readinto` exactly frame_bytes per selected frame. For each frame as it arrives: take the ≤256 px palette sample (SEC-1 fix), encode the JPEG, compute the sha, then drop the full-res buffer. Stream the motion pass the same way. Do not merge the two decodes with a split filter: two concurrent raw pipes bring back the SEC-5 deadlock class, and the sequential frame-index decode is a deliberate determinism choice. Update PLAN §4 stage 1 to describe the implemented three passes.

#### VID-8: All representative frames are decoded at full resolution into one in-memory buffer (×2 at peak) and held for the whole run

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** performance · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:225-241`, `mrw/video.py:393-394`, `mrw/video.py:472-474`, `mrw/video.py:255-270`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:225-230 runs `subprocess.run([... '-f', 'rawvideo', '-pix_fmt', 'rgb24', '-'], capture_output=True)`. communicate() gathers stdout in chunks and then joins them, so the full stdout exists twice at peak. mrw/video.py:233-241 builds frames as views onto that bytes object. frames_rgb (mrw/video.py:393-394) stays alive until the per-shot loop ends, because mrw/video.py:472-474 reuses it for palettes. The motion pass also buffers the entire 320 px gray stream (mrw/video.py:255-270). One frame costs width×height×3 bytes: 6.2 MB at 1080p, 24.9 MB at 4K.

**Failure scenario.** A 10-minute 4K (3840x2160) concert film with about 300 shots needs about 400 representative frames: roughly 10 GB in one bytes object and about 20 GB at peak during the join, before any JPEG work. On a 16 GB Mac the run swaps heavily or is OOM-killed. Even a typical 4-minute 4K music video (about 120 frames) peaks around 6 GB just to pick its stills. PLAN §9 budgets the whole video stage at 1-2 minutes.

**Proposal.** Stream instead of buffering. Popen the select-filter decode and read exactly frame_bytes per selected frame (or parse PPM frames, see VID-4). From each frame, immediately produce the ≤1280 px JPEG and the ≤256 px palette sample, keep only those, and drop the full-resolution frame. Feed motion_stats, which already accepts an iterator, from the motion pipe the same way. Peak memory becomes about one frame, whatever the length or resolution.

**Verifier's notes.** `subprocess.run(..., capture_output=True)` (video.py:225-230) goes through CPython's communicate(), which collects chunks and then b''.join()s them, so the full stdout exists twice for a moment. Each frame is an np.frombuffer view (240) that keeps the whole buffer alive through the per-shot palette pooling (472-474). The per-frame sizes are right: 6.2 MB at 1080p, 24.9 MB at 4K. A typical 4-minute 1080p video peaks at roughly 0.6-1.2 GB, which is acceptable. The problem bites only on 4K or long inputs, so it is a medium robustness gap.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: Popen, then read exactly frame_bytes per frame (or PPM frames, VID-4) and keep only the JPEG and the ≤256 px palette sample. Send stderr to a temp file or DEVNULL, or drain it in a thread, so the pipe cannot deadlock, and check returncode after draining.

## B. Up-to-date checks, provenance and input identity

### B1. Upstream changes never invalidate dependent documents

#### DET-1: Re-running an upstream stage never marks or regenerates the documents that depend on it (stems → features → lyrics)

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:298-305`, `mrw/lyrics.py:463-470`, `mrw/lyrics.py:486-493`, `mrw/lyrics.py:507`, `mrw/lyrics.py:568-572`, `mrw/models.py:313-320`, `mrw/cli.py:278-283`, `mrw/cli.py:304-305`, `mrw/config.py:111-117`, `PLAN.md:390-391`, `mrw.toml`

**Evidence.** Each stage's no-op check looks only at its own config subset. features.py:300-305 is `prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / "audio_features.json").is_file()`, and lyrics.py:465-470 is the same check with `lyrics_hash`. DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) has no field for upstream or input identity, and the manifest stems block stores no content digest. Lyrics reads audio_features.json at run time: `region_tuples = [(r["start_seconds"], r["end_seconds"]) for r in features_doc["vocal_activity"]["regions"]]` (lyrics.py:487-490). Those regions drive language detection, clip_timestamps (lyrics.py:190-199), the `outside_vocal_activity` flag, `untranscribed_regions` and the coverage ratios (`_coverage_and_untranscribed(lines, features_doc["vocal_activity"]["regions"], ...)`, lyrics.py:568-572). `_vocals_rms_lookup(features_doc)` (lyrics.py:507) drives `overlapping_vocals`. `mrw status` derives stale only from config hashes, and it prints the raw status for two stages, `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"` and `f"video={docs.video.status}"` (cli.py:304-305), even though STAGE_SECTION maps both (config.py:111-117). PLAN.md:390-391 promises that 'changing a video threshold marks only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale'. The checked-in operator mrw.toml (`[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`) shows this kind of tuning already happens in practice. I found no review, OQ entry or deferred item that covers propagation to dependents.

**Failure scenario.** Track T already has lyrics.json. The operator sets `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`, as in the repo's mrw.toml, and runs `mrw features T`, which merges the vocal_activity.regions. `mrw lyrics T` then prints 'lyrics up to date: T — no-op' and `mrw status` shows `lyrics=ok`. But lyrics.json's untranscribed_regions, vocal_activity_covered_ratio and outside_vocal_activity flags were all computed against the old regions, so the two documents contradict each other. The lyrics schema defines untranscribed_regions as vocal-activity regions from audio_features.json minus word coverage. Changing `[stems] model` or `cpu_threads` behaves the same way: stems are re-separated, but features and lyrics stay 'ok' even though they were derived from stems that no longer exist. Editing any `[lyrics]` or `[video]` key never shows as stale in `mrw status`. M6 structure will fuse these inconsistent inputs.

**Proposal.** (1) Give stems an identity: add `content_sha256` to StemsState, computed as the sha256 over the four stem files' sha256s in STEM_NAMES order. Compute it in run_stems before the tmp→stems rename, and keep it even when retain=false. (2) Add `inputs_sha256` (16 hex) to DocumentEntry. Each stage computes it from the identities it consumed: features from stems.content_sha256 (+ source); lyrics from source + audio_features content_sha256 + the stems digest; video from source (+ the verified video sha, see DET-3); M6 from features + lyrics + video. (3) The no-op checks (features.py:300-305, lyrics.py:465-470, video.py:331-337) require both config_hash and inputs_sha256 to match. (4) In cli.status, route every stage through one `_display(name, entry)` that reports stale when either hash differs, computing the current inputs from the manifest alone. This also replaces the raw prints at cli.py:304-305. (5) Tests: change `vocal_enter_db`, re-run features, then assert `mrw lyrics` is not a no-op and status shows lyrics=stale; stub `_separate` to write different PCM and assert features goes stale. Ship as additive manifest 1.4.0. Entries without inputs_sha256 will show stale once; note that in the PR, as the config-evolution policy does.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced. The features no-op test is only `prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / "audio_features.json").is_file()` (mrw/features.py:300-305). Lyrics uses the same test with lyrics_hash (mrw/lyrics.py:465-470). Each stage's `_record` writes only its own entry (features.py:310-312, stems.py:172-174), and DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) carries no upstream identity. Lyrics consumes the features regions for clip_timestamps, outside_vocal_activity, overlapping_vocals, untranscribed_regions and coverage (lyrics.py:487-507, 568-572). So after `mrw features T` with the repo mrw.toml's `vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`, `mrw lyrics T` returns already_done and prints 'lyrics up to date: … — no-op' (cli.py:166) over a lyrics.json computed from the old regions. Separately, cli.py:304-305 prints `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"` and `f"video={docs.video.status}"` raw although STAGE_SECTION maps both (config.py:111-117). A changed [lyrics] or [video] config therefore never displays as stale; only features=stale is tested (tests/test_m3_followups.py:51). PLAN.md:390-391 promises dependents go stale, and no OQ or review defers this. High stands: the normal calibration workflow (OQ-5, operator mrw.toml) silently produces mutually inconsistent documents labelled ok.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Split out the cli.py:304-305 fix (route lyrics and video through _display, plus a status test) as an immediate S change; it is an independent bug. For propagation, record the identities each stage actually consumed, not whole upstream document hashes. Features ← stems digest (+ source audio_stream_sha256). Lyrics ← audio_features content_sha256 + stems digest + source.json lyrics_input.sha256. Video ← verified file.sha256 (DET-3). Keying lyrics on source.json's content_sha256 would cascade a minutes-long Whisper re-run whenever only file.original_path changes, e.g. on re-ingest from a new location (DET-4). Interim S alternative: on a successful upstream run, flip dependents' `ok` entries to `stale` in the same manifest write, so their no-op checks fail.

#### ARCH-1: Freshness ignores upstream changes: downstream documents go silently stale, and `mrw status` hides lyrics/video config drift

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:298`, `mrw/lyrics.py:463`, `mrw/video.py:329`, `mrw/stems.py:146`, `mrw/cli.py:295`, `mrw/models.py:313`, `mrw/config.py:111`, `PLAN.md:390`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:390-391 promises: 'Per-stage config hashing means changing a video threshold marks only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale.' No code implements the 'dependents' part. Each stage's no-op check looks only at its own config hash and whether its file exists: features.py:298-305 (`prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / "audio_features.json").is_file()` then `return FeaturesResult(track_id, 0.0, -1, already_done=True)`), lyrics.py:463-470 (the same with lyrics_hash), video.py:329-337 and stems.py:146-165. Upstream checks look at status only: features.py:285 `stems_state.status != "ok"`, lyrics.py:443 `manifest.documents.audio_features.status != "ok"`. Yet lyrics depends heavily on features output. The vocal regions read at lyrics.py:487-490 drive the clip windows (196-199), the `outside_vocal_activity` flags (316-320), and untranscribed_regions and coverage (568-572). Features reads the stem FLACs (features.py:322-323). Nothing records what an entry was computed from: DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) holds only status/path/schema_version/content_sha256/config_hash/run/error, and StemsState (models.py:331-337) has no content fingerprint. A repo-wide grep for upstream/dependents/propagate finds only that PLAN sentence. Separately, `mrw status` derives `stale` only for source, features and stems. cli.py:304-305 prints `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"` and `f"video={docs.video.status}"` as stored, although config.STAGE_SECTION maps both stages (config.py:111-117). The only stale-display test covers features (tests/test_m3_followups.py:30-63).

**Failure scenario.** A track has been through stems, features and lyrics. The operator changes `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`, which is exactly what the committed mrw.toml:3-4 does. `mrw status` shows features=stale and lyrics=ok. `mrw features T` rewrites vocal_activity with merged regions, then `mrw lyrics T` answers 'lyrics up to date — no-op'. lyrics.json's untranscribed_regions, coverage ratios and outside_vocal_activity flags now refer to regions that no longer exist in audio_features.json, and no status, document or manifest field says so. The same happens with `[stems] model = "htdemucs_ft"` (the opt-in in PLAN §10): stems are separated again while audio_features.json and lyrics.json keep describing the old stems. Separately, after a change to `[lyrics] confidence_threshold` or `[video] detector_threshold`, status still shows lyrics=ok or video=ok, so an operator using status to find pending work misses them. M6's structure.json reads three upstream documents and would be the worst case.

**Proposal.** Make freshness one function that both the stage no-op checks and `mrw status` call. (1) Declare the stage dependency graph once, for example a STAGES registry: audio_features requires source and stems; lyrics requires source, stems and audio_features; video requires source; structure requires audio_features, lyrics, and video when applicable. (2) When a stage writes, record an `inputs` block in its manifest entry (an additive manifest 1.4.0 change). It holds the upstream `content_sha256` values, a stems fingerprint and the stage algorithm version (ARCH-6). For the stems fingerprint, add `content_sha256` to StemsState: a sha256 over the four FLAC hashes, computed when the stems are committed. (3) `freshness(manifest, stage, cfg) -> (state, reason)` returns stale when either the config hash or the recorded inputs differ from the current upstream entries. Stages use it for the no-op check, and status prints `stale(config)` or `stale(upstream: audio_features)`. Consumers such as the iPad app can then check cross-document consistency themselves by comparing `lyrics.inputs.audio_features` with `audio_features.content_sha256`. (4) Migration: show entries without `inputs` as `ok?` and refresh them on their next run, rather than marking everything stale at once. (5) A small fix that can land now (S): build the status line by iterating STAGE_SECTION, so lyrics, video and later structure all go through `_display`, and parametrize the stale-display test over every stage. The fast-test fixtures write the stems entry by hand (tests/test_m3_smoke.py:107-110, tests/test_m4_smoke.py:96-100); give them a library helper so the fingerprint gets filled in.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced. Every no-op check compares only the stage's own config_hash and file existence: features.py:300-305, lyrics.py:465-470, video.py:331-337, stems.py:151-159. Upstream checks look at status only (features.py:285, lyrics.py:443). Lyrics consumes features' vocal_activity regions (lyrics.py:487-490). Those regions drive the clip windows (:196-199), outside_vocal_activity (:316-320) and untranscribed/coverage (:568-572). DocumentEntry and StemsState (models.py:313-337) record no upstream fingerprint. A grep for upstream/dependents finds only PLAN.md:391, which promises dependent staleness. Status prints lyrics and video as stored (cli.py:304-305), and the only stale-display test covers features (test_m3_followups.py:30-65). The scenario plays out exactly as described: the committed mrw.toml:3-4 changes the features hash; after `mrw features`, `mrw lyrics` no-ops, and its regions/coverage/flags now refer to an audio_features.json that no longer contains them. No status flags it. This is not recorded as deferred anywhere. The manifest schema's 'stale' definition covers only a stage's own config, but nothing records a decision to ignore upstream changes. The result is silent cross-document inconsistency in the operator's normal re-run workflow, a contract break for consumers who join lyrics.json with audio_features.json.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound; sequence it as follows. (1) First, an S PR: build the status line from STAGE_SECTION so lyrics and video go through _display, and parametrize the stale-display test. (2) An optional cheap interim guard: make each downstream no-op check also compare the upstream sections' config hashes (lyrics: stems+features+lyrics). This catches the config-driven scenario with no schema change. (3) For the full `inputs` fingerprint, fingerprint source by file sha256/audio_stream_sha256 plus the ingest hash, not by source.json's content_sha256. That hash embeds original_path, so a relink (ARCH-9) would otherwise stale everything downstream. (4) Update manifest.schema.json's 'stale' description in the same 1.4.0 bump.

#### FEAT-2: Features no-op and stale checks key only on cfg.features: re-separated stems (new model/device/threads) or changed code leave audio_features.json 'ok' and `mrw features` a no-op

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3, T2, P2
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:298-305`, `mrw/cli.py:278-283`, `mrw/stems.py:223-235`, `mrw/__init__.py:3-5`, `mrw/features.py:58-62`, `mrw/features.py:175-177`, `mrw/lyrics.py:464-470`, `PLAN.md:387-391`

**Evidence.** run_features returns already_done when `prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / "audio_features.json").is_file()`, where `features_hash = config.stage_hash(cfg.features)` (features.py:298-305). The stems the document was computed from are not part of that key. A successful `mrw stems` rewrites only `manifest.stems` (stems.py:223-235) and never touches `documents.audio_features`. `mrw status` derives 'stale' from the same per-section hash (`stored_hash != config.stage_hash(getattr(cfg, section))`, cli.py:281). PLAN.md:390-391 promises that a config change marks a document 'and its dependents' stale. The comments that classify N_FFT, the silence floor and the 4.0 confidence multiplier as 'riding tool_version' (features.py:58-62, 175-177) have no mechanism behind them. `run.tool_version` is written (features.py:356) but no code reads it, and TOOL_VERSION is still 'mrw 0.1.0' at M5 (mrw/__init__.py:3-5). Review 007 F-1 noted that changing such constants 'would silently alter outputs under an unchanged config hash'. It did not note that tool_version is never compared, or that upstream stems are not part of the key.

**Failure scenario.** The user sets `[stems] model = "htdemucs_ft"` (the opt-in quality model PLAN §10 names) and runs `mrw stems <id>`; new stems are written and stems.config_hash changes. `mrw features <id>` then prints 'features up to date: <id> — no-op', and `mrw status` shows features=ok. audio_features.json, including every stems.* series and vocal_activity, still describes the old separation. Lyrics has the same blind spot in its no-op check (lyrics.py:464-470), so it keeps clipping Whisper to the stale regions. The same happens after changing a constant documented as 'riding tool_version' or upgrading librosa: every existing document stays ok.

**Proposal.** Make the stage's no-op key include its inputs as well as its own config section. One option is a helper such as `config.stage_hash(cfg.features, upstream={'stems': manifest.stems.config_hash, 'tool_version': TOOL_VERSION})`, used both in the no-op check in run_features and in cli.status so the derived stale display agrees. The other option implements PLAN §5 literally: when stems (or features) complete, set dependent `ok` entries (audio_features, lyrics, structure) to `stale`, which Status already supports, and treat `stale` as not done. Bump __version__ whenever a constant documented as 'riding tool_version' changes, and say so next to the constants. Test: stub `_separate`, change stems.model, re-run stems, then assert `mrw features` is not a no-op and status shows features=stale.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced. The no-op key in run_features is only `features_hash = config.stage_hash(cfg.features)` (features.py:298-305). A successful `mrw stems` writes only `manifest.stems` (stems.py:223-235). `mrw status` derives stale from the same per-section hash (cli.py:281), and lyrics uses the identical key (lyrics.py:464-470). So with `[stems] model = "htdemucs_ft"`, or a change of device, cpu_threads or retain, `mrw stems` re-separates, then `mrw features` prints 'features up to date … no-op' and status shows features=ok. The same gap exists one level down. Changing a vocal_* knob re-runs features (the operator's own mrw.toml:3-4 already overrides vocal_min_gap_seconds), but `mrw lyrics` stays a no-op. It keeps clipping Whisper to the old regions (lyrics.py:190-197, 487-492) and measuring coverage and untranscribed spans against them. Worse, cli.py:304-305 prints lyrics and video statuses raw (`f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"`), so their stale state is never derived even for their own config. PLAN.md:390-391 promises that dependents go stale, and nothing defers that. The tool_version sub-point adds nothing new: review 007 F-1 explicitly accepted that changing structural constants 'would silently alter outputs under an unchanged config hash … the stated classification is what makes that a conscious decision'. The upstream-dependency gap alone justifies high: silently stale output in an ordinary re-tune-and-re-run workflow.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do not fold upstream state into config_hash. manifest.schema.json:66 defines it as the hash of THIS stage's config subset, and stage_hash is also used by status. Either: (a) when stems or features complete successfully, set the dependent `ok` entries (audio_features, lyrics, structure) to `stale` in the same manifest write; the no-op checks already require status == 'ok', so a stale entry re-runs. Or: (b) record upstream fingerprints in each DocumentEntry (additive manifest 1.4.0 field, e.g. `inputs: {stems_config_hash, audio_features_content_sha256}`) and compare them in the no-op checks and in `status`. Also route lyrics and video through `_display` in cli.status. Drop the TOOL_VERSION part. Test: fabricate a track and run features, then change manifest.stems.config_hash to simulate re-separation. Assert that `mrw features` is not a no-op and that status shows features=stale. Do the same for features → lyrics.

#### LYR-4: Lyrics no-op check ignores upstream changes: lyrics.json keeps old vocal-activity data, and its bytes depend on the order stages were run

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:463-470`, `mrw/lyrics.py:487-493`, `mrw/lyrics.py:568-572`, `mrw/models.py:313-320`, `mrw/cli.py:304`, `mrw/config.py:115`, `PLAN.md:390-391`

**Evidence.** The no-op condition at lyrics.py:463-470 is `prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == lyrics_hash and (track_dir / "lyrics.json").is_file()`, and `lyrics_hash = config.stage_hash(cfg.lyrics)` covers only the [lyrics] section. Yet the document is built from upstream data: features `vocal_activity.regions` (clip windows, the outside_vocal_activity flag, untranscribed_regions, coverage at lyrics.py:487-493 and 568-572), the vocal-stem `rms_db` series (lyrics.py:273-287), and `stems/vocals.flac`. DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) has no field recording which upstream inputs were consumed. PLAN.md:390-391 promises that a config change marks a document '(and its dependents) stale'. `mrw status` doesn't even show lyrics as stale after a change to the [lyrics] config: cli.py:304 prints `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"` without the `_display` hash comparison it uses for source, features and stems, even though config.py:115 maps 'lyrics'. This repo's own mrw.toml already overrides `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`.

**Failure scenario.** A track's lyrics are produced with the default min gap of 0.2. The operator sets vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0 and re-runs `mrw features`, which changes the regions. `mrw lyrics` then reports 'up to date — no-op'. lyrics.json keeps untranscribed_regions, outside_vocal_activity flags, coverage ratios and clip windows computed from regions that no longer exist in audio_features.json, and `mrw status` shows lyrics=ok. A library that ran features at 1.0 before lyrics holds different lyrics.json bytes for the same input and the same final config, which breaks D1. Re-separated stems (a new vocals.flac) are likewise never re-transcribed. After the review-007 clip-fix mass-stale, `status` still showed lyrics=ok for every track.

**Proposal.** Record the upstream identity each stage consumed, for example `inputs_sha256` on DocumentEntry (manifest 1.4.0, additive): a hash over the audio_features content_sha256, the stems config_hash or run identity, and source.json's lyrics_input sha. Require it to match in the no-op check. In `mrw status`, apply `_display` to lyrics and video and also compare input digests. Apply the same rule to features with respect to stems. Add a test: change the features config, re-run features, then check that `mrw lyrics` re-runs and that status showed lyrics as stale.

**Verifier's notes.** The no-op check at lyrics.py:463-470 compares only `config.stage_hash(cfg.lyrics)`. The document consumes features vocal_activity.regions (:487-490, :568-572), the vocals rms_db series (:273-287) and stems/vocals.flac. DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) carries no upstream identity, and features.py:298-305 has the same gap with respect to stems. In status, cli.py:304-306 prints the raw lyrics/video/structure status with no `_display` hash comparison, even though STAGE_SECTION maps 'lyrics' and 'video'. The only staleness test (test_m3_followups.py:30-64) covers features. PLAN.md:390-391 promises that dependents go stale, and no review defers this. The repo's own mrw.toml tunes vocal_min_gap_seconds, and OQ-5 expects threshold calibration, so the scenario (re-run features, lyrics no-ops, status says ok) is a normal operator workflow. It also makes lyrics.json bytes depend on the order stages were run.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Two notes. First, digest the bytes actually consumed: audio_features content_sha256 is already in the manifest; for the vocal stem use a vocals.flac digest or a stems run identity, because MPS re-separation can change stem bytes under an unchanged stems config_hash. Second, split out the S-sized fix now: apply `_display` to lyrics and video in `mrw status`, and extend the staleness test to cover them.

#### ORCH-1: Upstream re-runs never invalidate downstream documents, and stale prerequisites are consumed

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, D1, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:298`, `mrw/lyrics.py:463`, `mrw/features.py:285`, `mrw/lyrics.py:443`, `mrw/stems.py:223`, `mrw/models.py:331`, `mrw/cli.py:278`, `PLAN.md:390`

**Evidence.** Features decides it is up to date by looking only at its own config subset: `if (prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / "audio_features.json").is_file()): return FeaturesResult(track_id, 0.0, -1, already_done=True)` (features.py:298-305). Lyrics uses the same pattern with `lyrics_hash` (lyrics.py:463-470). The prerequisite checks look only at status: `if stems_state is None or stems_state.status != "ok"` (features.py:285) and `manifest.documents.audio_features.status != "ok"` (lyrics.py:443). They never ask whether the upstream is stale under the current config or was regenerated after this document was made. A successful stems run only does `manifest.stems = state` (stems.py:172-174, 223-235), and StemsState stores no content hash (models.py:331-337). Nothing can therefore detect that the stems a features document used have been replaced. `status` derives stale only from each document's own stage hash (cli.py:278-283). PLAN.md:390-391 promises that changing a video threshold "marks only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale". Lyrics uses the features vocal_activity regions for its clip windows, untranscribed_regions, coverage and outside_vocal_activity flags (lyrics.py:487-493, 568-572).

**Failure scenario.** A track has been processed end to end with default config. The operator sets `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0` (the value now in the committed mrw.toml) and runs `mrw features T`, which writes new merged regions. `mrw lyrics T` then prints 'lyrics up to date: T — no-op', and `mrw status` shows features=ok lyrics=ok. But lyrics.json's untranscribed_regions, vocal_activity_covered_ratio, outside_vocal_activity flags and Whisper clip windows were all computed from regions that no longer exist in audio_features.json. The same happens after switching `[stems] device = "cpu"` as the README advises: stems re-separate, while features and lyrics stay 'ok' as no-ops computed from the old MPS stems. A fresh library given the same input and config would produce different bytes, so D1 is broken during normal tuning and nothing shows it.

**Proposal.** Record the identity of each stage's inputs in its manifest entry, and use it in the no-op, staleness and prerequisite checks. (1) StemsState gets `content_sha256`: a sha256 over the four stem files' sha256 values in STEM_NAMES order, computed before the swap. (2) DocumentEntry gets an additive `inputs_sha256` (manifest 1.4.0): a hash of the upstream digests the stage consumed. For features that is stems digest + source content_sha256; for lyrics, stems digest + audio_features content_sha256 + source content_sha256; for video, source content_sha256. (3) Features and lyrics are a no-op only when both config_hash and inputs_sha256 match. (4) `status` shows `stale` when the stored inputs differ from the current upstream digests. (5) A stage refuses with exit 2 when its prerequisite is stale under the current config, and names the command to run (H3). Add a fast test: features → lyrics → change features config → features; lyrics must then not be a no-op, and status must show lyrics=stale before it runs.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced every path cited. Features is a no-op when its own config_hash matches and the file exists (mrw/features.py:298-305); lyrics does the same (mrw/lyrics.py:463-470). Prerequisite checks look only at status and file existence (features.py:285-296, lyrics.py:443-461). StemsState carries no content digest (models.py:331-337), and a stems run only replaces manifest.stems (stems.py:223-235). A grep shows `content_sha256` is written in four places and never read anywhere in mrw/. status `_display` compares only each document's own section hash (cli.py:278-283). Lyrics consumes the features regions for its clip windows (lyrics.py:487-493), for outside_vocal_activity (lyrics.py:316-320) and for coverage/untranscribed_regions (lyrics.py:568-572), and the vocals RMS for overlapping_vocals (lyrics.py:507). So after `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0` and `mrw features T`, `mrw lyrics T` returns already_done and status shows lyrics=ok. The committed mrw.toml (device="cpu", vocal_min_gap_seconds=1.0) shows the operator has made exactly these upstream changes. PLAN.md:390-391 promises that dependents go stale, and no doc defers this. There is no --force; the only workaround is deleting the downstream file. Kept at high rather than critical: each document is still a deterministic output of some input/config pair; what fails is staleness tracking, and it is recoverable.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Two refinements. (a) Decide and document what legacy entries without inputs_sha256 show: displaying `stale` once is the honest choice. (b) Cheapest first step: record in the lyrics entry the manifest.documents.audio_features.content_sha256 it consumed (already available), and record in the features entry a stems digest computed in run_stems before the rename. Compare both in the no-op checks and in `status`, and refuse (exit 2) when a prerequisite is itself stale under the current config.

#### SCH-1: Dependent documents never go stale: lyrics/features stay 'ok' and no-op after the upstream inputs they consumed change

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H3, S4, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:463-470`, `mrw/features.py:298-305`, `mrw/cli.py:295-307`, `mrw/ingest.py:176-183`, `mrw/models.py:313-320`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:58-62`, `PLAN.md:387-391`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:390-391 says 'Per-stage config hashing means changing a video threshold marks only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale', and manifest.schema.json:61 defines 'stale = present but produced under a different config_hash than current config'. Nothing implements the 'dependents' part, and no code ever writes `stale` (grep: it appears only in the Literal at models.py:28 and the display helper in cli.py). Each stage's no-op check looks only at its own config hash. lyrics.py:463-470 reads `lyrics_hash = config.stage_hash(cfg.lyrics)` / `if (prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == lyrics_hash and (track_dir / "lyrics.json").is_file()): return LyricsResult(track_id, "?", -1, -1, already_done=True)`. features.py:298-305 has the same check and ignores the stems run it consumed. DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) stores only status/path/schema_version/content_sha256/config_hash/run/error, with no record of upstream inputs, so neither mrw nor a consumer can detect the mismatch. `mrw status` works out `stale` only for source, stems and features. cli.py:304-305 print `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"` and `f"video={docs.video.status}"` as stored, even though config.STAGE_SECTION (config.py:111-117) has entries for both. No stage command has a --force flag. ingest.py:176-183 skips the run whenever source is ok and ignores the ingest config_hash, but `mrw status` compares that hash (cli.py:296-297), so it can show `source=stale` for a document no command can refresh. Known decision: `mrw status` works out staleness itself and never rewrites the manifest (test_m3_followups.py:47-53). This finding is about the unimplemented dependency staleness and the lack of any refresh path.

**Failure scenario.** The operator calibrates vocal activity (OQ-5; the repo's mrw.toml already sets `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`) and re-runs `mrw features T`, so audio_features.json gets new vocal_activity.regions. `mrw lyrics T` prints 'lyrics up to date — no-op' because the lyrics config did not change. lyrics.json keeps untranscribed_regions, coverage.vocal_activity_covered_ratio, the outside_vocal_activity flags and the transcription clip windows from the OLD regions, while both the manifest and `mrw status` say lyrics=ok. An LLM that joins lyrics.untranscribed_regions with audio_features.vocal_activity (the documented honesty ledger) gets spans that match no region, and nothing is flagged. Changing `stems.model` has the same effect: stems re-run, but features stays ok and skips, keeping per-stem features from the old separation. Changing `[lyrics] model` leaves `mrw status` showing lyrics=ok.

**Proposal.** (1) cli.py:304-305: pass lyrics and video through `_display` as features already is. (2) Add a fixed-key `inputs` object to DocumentEntry (additive, manifest 1.4.0), e.g. optional `stems_config_hash`, `audio_features_sha256`, `lyrics_sha256`, `video_sha256`. Each stage fills it with the config_hash/content_sha256 of the upstream entries it actually read. Compare it in every no-op check (features: stems; lyrics: stems and audio_features; structure: audio_features, lyrics and video) and in `mrw status`. (3) When a stage rewrites its document, set its dependents' entries to `status: "stale"` in the same manifest write, so the Swift app sees staleness without running mrw. Update the `status` description to say when `stale` is stored and when it is only worked out for display. (4) Add `--force` to the stage commands. Either let `mrw ingest` re-run when the ingest config changes, or drop source from the stale display. (5) Test: re-running features under a new vocal config makes lyrics show stale, and `mrw lyrics` then recomputes.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced every path. Each stage's no-op check compares only its own subset hash: lyrics.py:463-470 (`prior.config_hash == lyrics_hash`) and features.py:298-305 (`prior.config_hash == features_hash`). DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) records no upstream identity. grep finds no code that writes 'stale'; the only hits are the Literal at models.py:28 and the display helper at cli.py:278-283. cli.py:304-305 print `lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}` and `video={docs.video.status}` raw. Even through `_display`, lyrics would still show ok after a features change, because only the lyrics subset is compared. The dependency is real: lyrics consumes VA regions for clip windows (lyrics.py:190-199), the language-detection window (186), untranscribed spans and coverage (568-572), and the outside_vocal_activity flag. After a features re-run under the operator's own calibration (mrw.toml already sets `vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`), lyrics.json stays ok and `mrw lyrics` no-ops. The library then holds a lyrics.json that the current config would not produce, contradicting PLAN.md:390-391 ('and its dependents' stale) and PLAN §2's 'pure functions of (input bytes, config)'. That is a missing safety net for the determinism constraint, so high fits the rubric. Not a duplicate: review 006 note 2 only decided that `mrw status` derives staleness without rewriting the manifest. New supporting evidence for point (4): ingest.py:176-183 no-ops whenever source is ok, so the remedy printed by video.py:306-310, 're-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true', silently does nothing for an existing track. Toggling the documented copy_video escape hatch also makes every track display source=stale, and no command can clear that. No CLI command offers --force; the only workaround is deleting the document by hand.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Proposal is sound, with three additions. (a) Record upstream identity in `inputs`: the stems config_hash, since stem files are not byte-stable on MPS, and the upstream document content_sha256, which also captures tool_version-riding changes. (b) Make ingest act when the stored ingest config_hash differs: at minimum perform the copy_video copy, or fix the video.py:306-310 message, because today's documented remedy is a no-op. (c) Add `--force` (S) now as the cheap stopgap, before the M-sized inputs/propagation work.

#### SEC-4: Staleness never propagates to dependent stages: after re-running stems or features, features and lyrics still report 'up to date'

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:289`, `mrw/features.py:300`, `mrw/lyrics.py:465`, `mrw/cli.py:304`, `mrw/ingest.py:177`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:390-391 says 'changing a video threshold marks only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale.' The code, however, keys every no-op on the stage's own config hash and nothing else. The features stage only checks that stems status is 'ok' and the files exist (features.py:284-296). It then treats `prior.status == 'ok' and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / 'audio_features.json').is_file()` as a no-op (300-305). The lyrics stage does the same against `lyrics_hash` (465-470). It never looks at the audio_features content it consumes, even though the vocal regions drive its clip windows, flags, coverage and untranscribed_regions. `mrw status` derives 'stale' only for source, features and stems. The f-strings for lyrics and video (`lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}`, `video={docs.video.status}`, cli.py:304-305) bypass `_display`, which contradicts the command's own docstring (271-274). Re-ingest is a no-op whenever source is ok (ingest.py:177-183), even if the ingest hash has changed or a new `--lyrics` is passed.

**Failure scenario.** Take the documented OQ-5 calibration workflow. Set `[features] vocal_enter_db = -30` and run `mrw features T`, which recomputes the regions. Then `mrw lyrics T` prints 'lyrics up to date — no-op'. lyrics.json keeps clip windows, untranscribed_regions and coverage computed from the old regions, and `mrw status` shows features=ok, lyrics=ok. The same happens upstream: change `[stems] model` to htdemucs_ft, run `mrw stems T`, and `mrw features T` is a no-op over the old stems. Editing `[lyrics] language` also leaves status at lyrics=ok. The planned M6 structure stage, which fuses features, lyrics and video, will inherit the inconsistency.

**Proposal.** Record upstream fingerprints in each manifest entry. Add `inputs: {source: <content_sha256>, stems: <digest>, audio_features: <content_sha256>}` to DocumentEntry as an additive manifest minor version. Give StemsState a content digest: a sha256 over the four FLAC hashes, which is stable on CPU. Change the no-op test to require both the stage's own config hash and the recorded inputs to match the current upstream entries. Make `status` derive staleness transitively, and route lyrics, video and structure through `_display`. For ingest, add `--force`, or re-ingest automatically when the ingest hash or the lyrics file differs. That lets a stale source, or lyrics added later, be refreshed without deleting the track and its expensive stems. Test: change a features threshold, then check that lyrics shows stale and re-runs.

**Verifier's notes.** The features no-op (features.py:298-305) and the lyrics no-op (lyrics.py:463-470) compare only their own config_hash and check that their output file exists; neither looks at the upstream document it consumes. So after `mrw features T` runs under a new vocal_* threshold, `mrw lyrics T` returns already_done. lyrics.json then keeps clip windows, flags and untranscribed_regions computed from vocal_activity regions that no longer exist in audio_features.json. That breaks the cross-document meaning the schema defines (uncovered vocal activity). PLAN.md:390-391 says a change marks 'only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale'. cli.py:304-305 prints `lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}` and `video={docs.video.status}` without `_display`, contradicting the docstring at cli.py:271-274. As a result, the lyrics mass-stale after the review-007 clip-fix config change never appeared in `mrw status`. ingest.py:177-183 returns already_ingested whatever the ingest hash or a newly passed --lyrics, and the CLI prints a plain no-op. Lyrics therefore cannot be added later without deleting the track and its stems. Only the plain re-ingest no-op is deliberate (M0 smoke). The scenario is a routine, documented workflow (OQ-5 calibration).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep the proposal. Ship the S-effort part first: route lyrics, video and structure through `_display`. Then record upstream content hashes in each entry (`inputs`, as a manifest minor version) and require them to match for a no-op. For ingest, warn loudly when --lyrics or the ingest hash differ on an already-ingested track, and offer `--force`.

#### TEST-2: Dependent-stage staleness is untested and unimplemented: downstream documents stay `ok` (and re-runs are no-ops) after an upstream stage changes

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3, T4
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:389-391`, `mrw/features.py:298-305`, `mrw/lyrics.py:463-470`, `mrw/lyrics.py:487-490`, `mrw/cli.py:295-313`, `mrw/models.py:313-320`, `tests/test_m3_followups.py:30-65`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:390-391: "Per-stage config hashing means changing a video threshold marks only `video.json` (and its dependents) stale." The features no-op test (features.py:300-305) is `prior.status == "ok" and prior.config_hash == features_hash and (track_dir / "audio_features.json").is_file()`. It covers only the features config subset and ignores the stems the document was computed from. lyrics.py:465-470 is the same, covering only the lyrics subset, even though the stage consumes `features_doc["vocal_activity"]["regions"]` (L487-490) for clip windows, flags, coverage and untranscribed regions. DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) stores no upstream content hashes, so staleness can't be derived later either. `status` derives stale only for source, stems and features: cli.py:304-305 prints `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"` and `f"video={docs.video.status}"` without `_display()`, although config.STAGE_SECTION maps both. The only staleness test, test_m3_followups.py:30-65, exercises features' own config knob.

**Failure scenario.** OQ-5 calibration, which is planned work. Set `[features] vocal_enter_db = -40` and run `mrw features`, which regenerates the vocal regions. Then `mrw lyrics` prints 'lyrics up to date — no-op'. lyrics.json keeps untranscribed_regions, vocal_activity_covered_ratio, outside_vocal_activity flags and clip windows computed from the old regions, contradicting audio_features.json in the same track dir, and `mrw status` shows `lyrics=ok`. Similarly, changing `[stems] cpu_threads` or `device` and re-running `mrw stems` overwrites stems/, but `mrw features` is a no-op, so audio_features.json now describes stems that are no longer on disk.

**Proposal.** Record upstream identity in each entry: an additive `inputs` block holding the upstream documents' content_sha256 and the stems config_hash (manifest minor bump). Include it in each stage's no-op condition and in `status`'s stale derivation, and apply `_display` to lyrics and video too. Add fast tests with stubbed engines: (1) change the features config and re-run features; status must show lyrics=stale and `mrw lyrics` must regenerate. (2) re-run stems with a stubbed `_separate` under a new stems config; features must show stale and regenerate. (3) status must show lyrics/video stale after a change to their own config.

**Verifier's notes.** I traced both no-op checks. run_features (features.py:298-305) compares only `stage_hash(cfg.features)` plus file existence. run_lyrics (lyrics.py:463-470) compares only the lyrics subset. Yet the lyrics stage consumes `features_doc['vocal_activity']['regions']` (L487-490, L568-571) and the vocal stem (L450). Neither DocumentEntry (models.py:313-320) nor StemsState (models.py:331-337) records any upstream identity. Scenario: `[features] vocal_enter_db` changes and `mrw features` regenerates. `mrw lyrics` then returns already_done, so lyrics.json keeps the clip windows, flags and untranscribed_regions computed from the old regions. Two libraries with an identical full config can therefore end with different lyrics.json depending on run order, which is a D1 problem. Changing `stems.model` and re-running stems likewise leaves features and lyrics as no-ops over replaced stems. Separately, cli.py:304-305 prints the lyrics and video statuses raw, so `mrw status` shows lyrics=ok even after the lyrics config itself changes. That contradicts the command's own docstring (cli.py:271-274), even though STAGE_SECTION maps both stages (config.py:111-117). Nothing in the reviews or OQs defers this, and PLAN.md:390-391 promises dependent staleness. There is also no `--force`: the slow tests hand-edit manifest.json to force a re-run (test_m4_smoke.py:493-496, 554-557).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do it in two steps. Now (S): wrap lyrics and video in `_display()` at cli.py:304-305 and add a fast status test. Then (M): a stems config_hash is not enough as upstream identity, because the same stems config can produce different stem bytes (MPS jitter, the MPS-to-CPU fallback at stems.py:190-201, cpu_threads > 1). Record a per-stem or combined sha256 in StemsState (additive manifest minor bump). Add an additive `inputs` block to the features and lyrics entries: the stem sha256s, plus audio_features' content_sha256 for lyrics. Use it in each stage's no-op test and in status' stale derivation. Add a `--force` flag to the stage commands so neither users nor tests have to edit manifest.json.

### B2. mrw status never shows lyrics/video as stale

#### ORCH-11: `mrw status` shows stale only for source, stems and features; lyrics and video still show ok after their config changes

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/cli.py:295`, `mrw/cli.py:304`, `mrw/config.py:111`, `mrw/cli.py:269`

**Evidence.** cli.py:295-307 passes source, stems and audio_features through `_display(...)`, but prints the raw stored status for the rest: `f"lyrics={docs.lyrics.status}"`, `f"video={docs.video.status}"`, `f"structure={docs.structure.status}"`. Yet STAGE_SECTION maps "lyrics" and "video" (config.py:111-117), and the docstring promises "A document recorded `ok` under a different config than the current one is displayed as `stale`" (cli.py:269-275).

**Failure scenario.** The committed mrw.toml pins `[lyrics] language = "en"`. Tracks whose lyrics ran before the pin show `lyrics=ok` in `mrw status`, yet `mrw lyrics T` is not a no-op and transcribes again. The same applies to changes to `[video] detector_threshold` or `caption_backend`: status still says video=ok, so a batch operator cannot see what needs re-running.

**Proposal.** Build the parts list by looping over `config.STAGE_SECTION`, so every hashed stage goes through `_display`; stages without a config section yet are printed raw. Once ORCH-1 lands, `_display` should also compare the input digests. Extend the staleness test in test_m3_followups to cover lyrics and video settings.

**Verifier's notes.** cli.py:295-307 sends source, features and stems through `_display`, but prints lyrics, video and structure raw (cli.py:304-306). Yet STAGE_SECTION maps lyrics and video (config.py:111-117), and the docstring (cli.py:269-275) promises a stale display. Because run_lyrics and run_video re-run on a hash mismatch (lyrics.py:463-470, video.py:329-337), status shows ok for documents the next invocation will regenerate. With the committed `[lyrics] language = "en"` pin, this already applies to tracks transcribed before the pin. It is not recorded anywhere. Medium: an operator-facing correctness bug on the two stages most often tuned.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Iterate over STAGE_SECTION with a label map ('audio_features' → 'features'), keep stems optional, and print structure raw until it gets a section. Extend test_m3_followups to toggle one lyrics field and one video field.

### B3. Code and toolchain provenance is missing

#### ORCH-3: Code and toolchain provenance is not tracked: tool_version is stuck at 'mrw 0.1.0' and never checked, and external tool versions and platform are not recorded

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5, P2
- **Locations:** `mrw/__init__.py:3`, `pyproject.toml:3`, `mrw/models.py:305`, `mrw/features.py:58`, `mrw/lyrics.py:60`, `mrw/align.py:27`, `docs/reviews/006-m3-features.md:35`, `CLAUDE.md:21`

**Evidence.** `__version__ = "0.1.0"` (mrw/__init__.py:3, pyproject.toml:3) has not changed from M0 through M5. TOOL_VERSION is written by every stage (ingest.py:273, stems.py:206, features.py:356, lyrics.py:598, video.py:547,570) but nothing reads it: no no-op check or status derivation looks at it. The tunables policy nevertheless relies on it for structural constants. features.py:58-62 says "riding tool_version rather than config"; features.py:175 says "rides tool_version, not config"; lyrics.py:60 says "Structural (rides tool_version)"; align.py:27-33 and reviews 006 F-1 and 007 F-1 say the same. RunMetadata (models.py:305-310) holds only started_at, duration_seconds, tool_version, device and api_usage. It records no versions for ffmpeg, torch, demucs, librosa, faster-whisper, ctranslate2, scenedetect, opencv or sklearn, and no platform or architecture. Yet CLAUDE.md:21 lists "host, device, tool versions" as the volatile data the manifest exists to hold, and review 007 already saw separation output differ by architecture (Apple Silicon vs x86 CI).

**Failure scenario.** A PR changes one of the constants that 'ride tool_version' (for example lyrics._OVERLAP_FRACTION or align.LOOKAHEAD_WORDS), or fixes a features bug, without touching config. After `git pull`, `mrw features` and `mrw lyrics` report 'up to date' for every existing track, while new tracks get the new behaviour. Both sets of manifests say tool_version 'mrw 0.1.0', so the library quietly mixes two algorithms and there is no way to tell which documents came from which. The same happens after `brew upgrade ffmpeg`, where resampler or AAC decoder changes alter source_audio.flac and everything downstream, or after a `uv sync` that moves librosa. In both cases an M7 determinism diff between two machines fails with no recorded cause.

**Proposal.** (1) Give each stage an algorithm version constant (for example FEATURES_ALGO, LYRICS_ALGO, STEMS_ALGO, VIDEO_ALGO, INGEST_ALGO) that a PR bumps whenever document bytes can change under unchanged config. Store it in the manifest entry (additive field) and include it in the no-op comparison and the derived stale display. This makes the 'rides tool_version' mechanism actually work, per stage. (2) Record toolchain provenance in RunMetadata as an array of objects with fixed keys, which Swift can decode without dynamic keys: `tools: [{name, version}]`, filled from `importlib.metadata.version(...)` for the stage's packages and from the first line of `ffmpeg -version`, plus `platform` (for example `darwin-arm64`). (3) Add a CHECKLIST rule: an output-affecting code change must bump that stage's algorithm version.

**Verifier's notes.** Verified that `__version__ = "0.1.0"` (mrw/__init__.py:3, pyproject.toml:3). TOOL_VERSION is written at ingest.py:273, stems.py:206, features.py:356, lyrics.py:598 and video.py:547/570, but no code reads tool_version or the per-entry schema_version, in any no-op check or in status. features.py:58-62 and 175-177, lyrics.py:60-62 and align.py:27-33 hand structural constants off to tool_version. RunMetadata (models.py:305-310) records no ffmpeg, torch, librosa or ctranslate2 versions and no platform. Part of this is known: reviews 006 F-1 and 007 F-1 accepted that changing such constants "would silently alter outputs under an unchanged config hash". The new argument holds: the version is never bumped and never consulted, so handing constants to tool_version protects nothing, and the external toolchain an M7 cross-machine determinism diff needs is not recorded. Downgraded to medium: this is a latent provenance and staleness gap; no wrong output has been shown yet.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Store the per-stage algorithm version as an additive manifest field and compare it in the no-op checks and in `status`. Do not fold it into stage_hash: that would mass-stale every stage at once, including a full Demucs re-separation of every track. Treat an absent value as the initial version so introducing the field causes no churn. Record toolchain versions with importlib.metadata.version (no heavy import, so T5-safe) and, for ingest/video, the first line of `ffmpeg -version`, as `tools: [{name, version}]` plus `platform`. Add the CHECKLIST bump rule.

#### DET-8: Structural constants are said to 'ride tool_version', but nothing checks tool_version, and output-affecting tool versions (ffmpeg, libraries, host) are never recorded

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/__init__.py:3-5`, `mrw/models.py:305-310`, `mrw/stems.py:150-159`, `mrw/features.py:299-305`, `mrw/lyrics.py:464-470`, `mrw/video.py:330-337`, `mrw/features.py:58-62`, `mrw/lyrics.py:60-62`, `mrw/align.py:27-33`, `mrw/ingest.py:94-141`, `mrw/video.py:224-229`, `mrw/video.py:255-258`

**Evidence.** Reviews 006 F-1 and 007 F-1, and the code comments at features.py:58-62 and 175-177, lyrics.py:60-62 and align.py:27-33, classify structural constants as 'riding tool_version'. But TOOL_VERSION is `f"mrw {__version__}"` with `__version__ = "0.1.0"` (__init__.py:3-5), still the Phase-0 example value at M5, and no code compares it. The no-op checks (stems.py:150-159, features.py:299-305, lyrics.py:464-470, video.py:330-337) and `status` (cli.py:278-283) look only at status, config_hash and whether the file exists; schema_version is never compared either. RunMetadata (models.py:305-310) records no host or architecture and no versions of the tools that determine output. ffmpeg is a system binary outside uv.lock (README: `brew install ffmpeg`). It produces source_audio.flac and audio_stream_sha256 (ingest.py:94-141), including 48→44.1 kHz resampling of the AAC audio typical in music videos. It also produces the rgb24 frames and the scaled motion frames (video.py:224-229, 255-258), and the frames' JPEG sha256s key the paid caption cache.

**Failure scenario.** (1) A maintainer changes `_OVERLAP_FRACTION` from 0.8 to 0.7, or changes JPEG_QUALITY. Every existing lyrics.json and video.json stays 'ok', `mrw lyrics` and `mrw video` report no-op, and new tracks get the new behaviour. Both old and new runs record tool_version 'mrw 0.1.0', so the library silently mixes algorithms. (2) Homebrew upgrades ffmpeg, or the library moves between an Intel and an Apple Silicon Mac. Assumption, high confidence: lossy decoding, swresample, and swscale's yuv→rgb24 are not bit-exact across versions or architectures without bitexact flags; FFmpeg's own FATE suite compares these with tolerances. Re-ingested audio hashes, frame JPEGs and their sha256s therefore change, which causes caption cache misses and re-spend, and every downstream document changes, with no recorded version to explain why. Review 003's cross-platform identity result used a 44.1 kHz PCM WAV, which exercises neither decoding nor resampling.

**Proposal.** (1) Give the policy a mechanism. Add per-stage integer ALGORITHM_VERSION constants (stems, features, lyrics/align, video) and fold them into stage_hash, e.g. hash {'algorithm': N, 'config': subset}. Bump the constant in the same PR that changes a structural constant, so the config-evolution policy also covers code changes. Also compare the stored schema_version in the no-op checks, and bump __version__ per milestone. (2) Record provenance in RunMetadata (manifest-only, additive): platform/arch, the first line of `ffmpeg -version`, and importlib.metadata versions of librosa, numpy, torch, demucs, faster-whisper, ctranslate2, opencv-python-headless, scikit-learn, pillow, scenedetect and pyloudnorm. (3) Harden the ffmpeg calls: add `-sws_flags accurate_rnd+bitexact+full_chroma_int` to the rgb24 extraction and the motion scale, and `-fflags +bitexact -flags:a +bitexact` to the FLAC encode. (4) State the scope in PLAN §7 and CLAUDE.md: byte identity holds on the same architecture, ffmpeg build and uv.lock.

**Verifier's notes.** TOOL_VERSION = f"mrw {__version__}" with __version__ = "0.1.0" (mrw/__init__.py:3-5, pyproject.toml:3) is only written into RunMetadata. No no-op check (stems.py:150-159, features.py:299-305, lyrics.py:464-470, video.py:330-337) and not cli status compares tool_version or schema_version. Reviews 006 F-1 and 007 F-1, and the comments at features.py:58-62 and 175-177, lyrics.py:60-62 and align.py:27-33, classify constants as 'riding tool_version'. 007 F-1 accepted silent output changes as a 'conscious decision' but presumed a carrier that does not exist; that is the genuinely new argument. RunMetadata (models.py:305-310) records no host, ffmpeg or library versions, although CLAUDE.md names host and tool versions as manifest data. Review 003's cross-platform identity used a 44.1 kHz s16 WAV (tests/test_m0_smoke.py:33-46), which exercises no lossy decode or resample. The ffmpeg cross-version bit-exactness concern is plausible (FATE's tolerance-based comparisons) but not verified here. Medium: a maintainability and provenance gap, not a defect in normal use.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Sound, with one caveat. The `-sws_flags accurate_rnd+bitexact+full_chroma_int` hardening changes frame pixels, and with them the JPEG sha256s, the caption-cache keys and video.json, all under an unchanged config. Ship it in the same PR as the video ALGORITHM_VERSION bump, and state that paid captions will be re-requested. The FLAC `-fflags +bitexact` change only affects source_audio.flac bytes (it drops the Lavf encoder tag), not documents.

#### ARCH-6: Provenance cannot detect or explain output changes caused by code or environment: TOOL_VERSION is never read, is defined twice and has never been bumped, and there is no `--force`

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5, H3 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/__init__.py:3`, `pyproject.toml:3`, `mrw/models.py:305`, `mrw/features.py:58`, `mrw/lyrics.py:60`, `mrw/align.py:27`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:495`

**Evidence.** The version is defined in two places, pyproject.toml:3 (`version = "0.1.0"`) and mrw/__init__.py:3-5 (`__version__ = "0.1.0"` and `TOOL_VERSION = f"mrw {__version__}"`), and is still 0.1.0 after M0-M5. tool_version is written at ingest.py:273, stems.py:206, features.py:356, lyrics.py:598, video.py:547 and video.py:570, and never read: every freshness check compares only config_hash (stems.py:151-159, features.py:300-305, lyrics.py:465-470, video.py:331-337). Yet the code's stated policy for constants that affect output is that they 'ride tool_version': features.py:58-62, features.py:175-177 (the 4.0 confidence multiplier), lyrics.py:60-62 (`_OVERLAP_FRACTION`) and align.py:27-33 (LOOKAHEAD_WORDS, the hint windows, the interpolation bounds). That policy is already recorded in reviews 006 F-1 and 007 F-1. What is new here is that it has no effect, because tool_version is never compared and never bumped. Since no stage can be forced to re-run, the slow tests edit manifest.json by hand to re-run lyrics: tests/test_m4_smoke.py:495-496 and :556-557 set `manifest["documents"]["lyrics"] = {"status": "pending"}` and rewrite the file with json.dumps. RunMetadata (models.py:305-310) records nothing about the environment. Outputs nevertheless depend on the system ffmpeg (ingest decode and FLAC, frame decode and rgb24 conversion), on PyAV's bundled FFmpeg (faster-whisper decodes the vocal stem; `av` is a faster-whisper dependency in uv.lock), and on numpy, scipy, librosa, soxr, torch, demucs, ctranslate2, OpenCV, scikit-learn, Pillow/libjpeg-turbo and scenedetect.

**Failure scenario.** (a) A PR changes `_OVERLAP_FRACTION` from 0.8 to 0.7. Every existing lyrics.json keeps the old flags, status says ok, and `mrw lyrics T` answers 'up to date — no-op'. The only way to recompute is to delete lyrics.json or edit the manifest by hand. (b) `brew upgrade ffmpeg`, or a lock upgrade that moves Pillow/libjpeg-turbo, changes the frame JPEG bytes. That changes each frame's sha256 and therefore the caption cache keys (captions.py:41-43), so the next video run misses every cached caption and pays for them again. Neither the manifest nor `mrw costs` can say why. (c) When a determinism check fails across two machines, the manifests contain nothing to compare except timestamps.

**Proposal.** (1) Keep the version in one place: `dynamic = ["version"]` with `[tool.hatch.version] path = "mrw/__init__.py"`, or `__version__ = importlib.metadata.version("mrw")`. (2) Give each stage module an integer ALGORITHM_VERSION, record it in the manifest entry and include it in the ARCH-1 freshness fingerprint. Add a CHECKLIST D-item: 'a PR that changes a stage's output without a config change bumps that stage's ALGORITHM_VERSION'. This makes 'rides tool_version' enforceable, and affected documents show as `stale(code)` in status. (3) Record a `run.environment` block in the manifest (an additive 1.4.0 change; this is volatile data, so per D2 it belongs only in the manifest). It holds the Python version, the versions of numpy, scipy, librosa, soxr, torch, demucs, faster-whisper, ctranslate2, av, opencv, scikit-learn, pillow, scenedetect and pyloudnorm, and the first line of `ffmpeg -version`. (4) Add `--force` to every stage command; it skips only the no-op check. Use it in the tests instead of editing manifest.json.

**Verifier's notes.** Accurate. The version is duplicated (pyproject.toml:3, mrw/__init__.py:3-5) and is still 0.1.0. tool_version is written in six places and a grep finds no reads; freshness compares only config_hash. Tests reset manifests by hand (test_m4_smoke.py:495-496, 556-557). RunMetadata (models.py:305-310) holds no environment data. Already recorded: 006 F-1 and 007 F-1 explicitly accepted that changing structural constants alters outputs under an unchanged config hash and called that 'a conscious decision'. The new argument is valid: 'rides tool_version' has no mechanism behind it, because tool_version is never bumped or compared. --force and environment provenance are also new. Low, because the core trade-off was consciously accepted and a workaround exists: deleting the document defeats the no-op check (e.g. lyrics.py:468). The environment-drift scenario (JPEG bytes changing, cache misses) is plausible but unverified.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Cheapest form: a per-stage integer ALGORITHM_VERSION folded into the stage's hashed payload, or recorded as an additive DocumentEntry field and compared in the no-op check and in status, plus the proposed CHECKLIST D-item. This reuses the existing stale display without new freshness machinery. Single-source the version and add --force to the stage commands. The run.environment block is optional.

#### LYR-15: 'Structural constants ride tool_version' does not work because TOOL_VERSION never changes; decode parameters are hard-coded and unclassified

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** maintainability · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D1
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:27-36`, `mrw/lyrics.py:60-62`, `mrw/lyrics.py:160-170`, `mrw/lyrics.py:91`, `mrw/__init__.py:3-5`, `PLAN.md:244-246`

**Evidence.** align.py:27-33 states that LOOKAHEAD_WORDS and the HINT_* constants 'are STRUCTURAL search bounds … and ride tool_version', and `_OVERLAP_FRACTION` likewise 'rides tool_version' (lyrics.py:60-62). But `__version__ = "0.1.0"` and `TOOL_VERSION = f"mrw {__version__}"` (mrw/__init__.py:3-5, and pyproject.toml) are still 0.1.0 after M0-M5. The decode parameters are hard-coded with no classification comment: `compute_type="int8"`, `cpu_threads=1`, `beam_size=1`, `best_of=1`, `temperature=0.0`, `condition_on_previous_text=False` (lyrics.py:160-170), and the 30 s detection window (lyrics.py:91). PLAN.md:244-246 says 'exact decode params are config'.

**Failure scenario.** A later commit tunes LOOKAHEAD_WORDS, `_OVERLAP_FRACTION` or beam_size. New runs produce different lyrics.json while both config_hash and tool_version ('mrw 0.1.0') stay the same. Neither the no-op check nor `mrw status` can tell old documents from new ones: the silent drift that the review 006 F-1 and 007 F-1 classification was supposed to prevent.

**Proposal.** Mix a per-stage `ALGORITHM_VERSION` constant into the stage hash, or bump `__version__` on every change that affects output and add a CHECKLIST item requiring it. Classify the decode parameters in a comment like align.py's, or promote beam_size and compute_type into LyricsConfig, accepting the deliberate mass-stale.

**Verifier's notes.** `__version__` is "0.1.0" (mrw/__init__.py:3-5, pyproject.toml) after M0-M5. A grep shows TOOL_VERSION is only ever written into RunMetadata; no no-op check and no status display ever compares it. 'Rides tool_version' (align.py:27-33, lyrics.py:60-62) therefore gives no signal at all. The classification itself is recorded (review 006 F-1, review 007 F-1), but the argument that the version is never bumped and never read is new. The decode parameters at lyrics.py:160-170 are hard-coded, while PLAN.md:244-246 says 'exact decode params are config'; that drift is real.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** A per-stage ALGORITHM_VERSION constant mixed into stage_hash gives both the no-op check and the stale display in `mrw status` with no extra code. Note in the PR that introducing it mass-stales every stage once, which is consistent with the config-evolution policy, and add a CHECKLIST item requiring a bump on any change that affects output.

### B4. Ingest only ever runs once per track

#### ORCH-6: Re-ingest is always a no-op: new --lyrics or --title, changed ingest config and damaged tracks are ignored, and the video stage's suggested fix cannot work

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:176`, `mrw/cli.py:71`, `mrw/video.py:306`, `mrw/stems.py:181`, `mrw/cli.py:296`

**Evidence.** ingest.py:176-183: `existing = library.read_manifest(track_id)` then `if existing is not None and existing.documents.source.status == "ok": return IngestResult(..., already_ingested=True)`. This returns before looking at lyrics_path, title, cfg.ingest, or whether source.json and source_audio.flac still exist. cli.py:71-72 prints only 'already ingested: … — no-op'. video.py:306-310 tells the user to "restore it there, or re-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true", and stems.py:181-182 reports "missing source_audio.flac — library is damaged?". Meanwhile `status` shows the source as stale whenever the cfg.ingest hash differs (cli.py:278-283, 296-297), and nothing can ever clear that state.

**Failure scenario.** (1) The user runs `mrw ingest song.mp4`, then stems, features, lyrics (transcribed) and video. Later they find song.lrc and run `mrw ingest song.mp4 --lyrics song.lrc`. It prints 'already ingested … — no-op' and exits 0, the lyrics are never registered, and `mrw lyrics` stays in transcribed mode. The only workaround is deleting library/T, which also destroys the stems (the most expensive artifact) and the paid caption cache stored in .cache/captions. (2) The original video is moved, and `mrw video T` says to re-ingest with copy_video = true. The user does that from the new location, but it is the same bytes, so the same track_id and a no-op. No copy is made and the same error repeats forever. (3) If source_audio.flac is deleted, stems fails with 'library is damaged?' and re-ingest cannot repair it.

**Proposal.** Make re-ingest reconcile the existing track instead of returning early. (a) Check that source.json and source_audio.flac exist, and repair them if not, keeping the other manifest entries. (b) If `--lyrics` is given and its sha256 differs from source.json lyrics_input (or none is recorded), copy and register it, rewrite source.json, and reset documents.lyrics to pending, since the mode changes. (c) `--title` updates manifest.title only. (d) If copy_video is now true and no verified copy exists, copy atomically from the given path (its bytes match by construction) and update source.json original_path. (e) Otherwise, report exactly which inputs were ignored. An alternative is an explicit `mrw ingest --update`, with the no-op message listing the ignored options. Update the video.py remedy text to match. Add tests for (b) and (d).

**Verifier's notes.** ingest.py:176-183 returns already_ingested before it looks at lyrics_path, title or cfg.ingest, or checks that source.json and source_audio.flac exist; cli.py:71-72 prints only '— no-op'. Traced three consequences. (1) `mrw ingest same.mp4 --lyrics x.lrc` silently drops the lyrics (an H1 omission). (2) The remedy in video.py:306-310, 're-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true', can never work: the same bytes give the same track_id, which returns before the copy at ingest.py:228. `mrw video <new path>` does not help either, because resolve_track_id hashes the file back to T and `_resolve_video_path` still uses the stored original_path, so after a move only restoring the original path works. (3) A changed ingest config leaves source=stale forever (cli.py:296-297). No doc defines re-ingest beyond M0's 'second run reports no-op'. Medium rather than high: the tool says no-op or fails loudly, with no wrong analysis output, but this is a real workflow dead end and the error message gives a wrong remedy.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep a plain re-ingest a no-op (the M0 contract), but list each ignored option: --lyrics with a different sha, --title, a changed copy_video. Add an explicit `mrw ingest --update` that (a) registers new lyrics, rewrites source.json and its manifest config_hash, and resets lyrics (and later structure) to pending; (b) updates the title; (c) makes a verified, atomic copy when copy_video is now true and updates original_path; (d) regenerates a missing source_audio.flac. Change the video.py message to name `--update`. Add tests for the lyrics and copy cases.

#### ARCH-9: Ingest only ever writes once: new lyrics, titles and ingest-config changes are silently dropped, and the video stage's own remedy message cannot work

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, T4 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:176`, `mrw/video.py:306`, `mrw/cli.py:71`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:426`

**Evidence.** ingest.py:176-183 returns early: `existing = library.read_manifest(track_id)` / `if existing is not None and existing.documents.source.status == "ok": return IngestResult(..., already_ingested=True)`. The return comes before lyrics registration (:223), the copy_video copy (:228-231) and title resolution (:267-269), and the ingest config hash is never compared. cli.py:71-72 prints 'already ingested: ... — no-op' even when `--lyrics` or `--title` was passed. video.py:306-310 tells the user 'original video not found at {original} ... — restore it there, or re-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true', and tests/test_m5_smoke.py:426 asserts that remedy text ('names the remedy'). status derives `source=stale` from the ingest config hash (cli.py:279-283; STAGE_SECTION maps 'source' to 'ingest' at config.py:112), but no command can clear it. Lyrics mode is fixed by source.json's lyrics_input (lyrics.py:476-477). Already recorded: re-ingesting the same file as a no-op is the deliberate M0 contract (PLAN §11 M0 smoke). New here: the no-op also throws away new inputs, and the code's own remedy relies on a re-ingest that cannot happen.

**Failure scenario.** (a) A user ingests song.mp4 and runs stems, features and lyrics (transcribed mode), then finds an .lrc file. `mrw ingest song.mp4 --lyrics song.lrc` prints 'no-op' and lyrics stay transcribed. The only way to get aligned mode is to delete the track directory and redo 5-10 minutes of separation. (b) The user moves the video folder. `mrw video T` says to re-ingest with copy_video = true. `mrw ingest /new/path/song.mp4 --config copy.toml` hashes to the same track_id and does nothing: no copy is made and original_path is unchanged. `mrw video T` fails again, and status now shows source=stale permanently.

**Proposal.** Make ingest idempotent but able to update an existing track. (1) If `--lyrics` is given and its sha256 differs from source.json's lyrics_input, register it again and rewrite source.json; that must mark lyrics (and later structure) stale through ARCH-1's upstream fingerprint. (2) If `--title` is given, update the manifest title. (3) If `ingest.copy_video = true` and the given path hashes to the track, copy the video. (4) Add `mrw relink <track> <path>`, which checks the sha256 and updates the original path (source.json byte identity is already scoped per path, PLAN §7). (5) If the ingest config hash has changed (sample_rate or channels), require `--force` and do a full re-ingest. Fix the message in video.py to name a command that works, and keep a second run with identical arguments a no-op (M0 smoke).

**Verifier's notes.** ingest.py:176-183 returns before lyrics registration (:223), the copy_video copy (:228-231) and title resolution (:267-269), and cli.py:71-72 prints a bare 'no-op' even when --lyrics or --title is passed. The remedy in video.py:306-310, which test_m5_smoke.py:426 asserts, cannot work: re-ingest hashes to the same track_id and no-ops. Because copy_video is an IngestConfig field (config.py:23), following the remedy makes status show source=stale permanently (cli.py:279-283). Lyrics mode is fixed by source.json (lyrics.py:476-477), so adding an .lrc later means deleting the track and redoing the 5-10 minute separation. The identical-arguments no-op is the recorded M0 contract (PLAN §11). The discarded new inputs and the broken remedy are new.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** S first: change the video.py message to name remedies that work today: restore the file at original_path, or copy it into the track dir as source_video<ext>, which _resolve_video_path (video.py:300-302) already prefers. Update the test to match. Have ingest warn when --lyrics, --title or copy_video would change an existing track, instead of printing a silent no-op.
