For relink and update-ingest, make sure the downstream freshness fingerprint uses the file/audio-stream sha and not source.json's bytes, so a path change does not stale every downstream document.

#### DET-4: Re-ingesting ignores ingest config changes and new --lyrics/--title arguments: the copy_video remedy the error message gives cannot work, and source shows stale forever

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, D1
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:176-183`, `mrw/ingest.py:223`, `mrw/ingest.py:228-231`, `mrw/video.py:306-310`, `mrw/cli.py:71-72`, `mrw/cli.py:278-283`

**Evidence.** `if existing is not None and existing.documents.source.status == "ok": return IngestResult(... already_ingested=True)` (ingest.py:176-183) runs before `_lyrics_input(...)` (ingest.py:223) and before the copy_video copy (ingest.py:228-231). It does not compare `config.stage_hash(cfg.ingest)`, the lyrics file or --title. The video stage's error tells the user to '... re-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true' (video.py:306-310). The CLI prints only 'already ingested: … — no-op' (cli.py:71-72). Meanwhile `mrw status` reports `source=stale` from the changed ingest hash (cli.py:278-283, 296-297), and no command can clear it.

**Failure scenario.** (1) The original video was moved. `mrw video T` fails with 'original video not found … re-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true'. The user sets that option and runs `mrw ingest /new/place/video.mp4`. The track_id is the same, so ingest reports 'no-op': no copy is made, source.json still points to the old path, `mrw video T` fails again, and `mrw status` now shows source=stale permanently. (2) A user ingested without lyrics and later obtains them. `mrw ingest song.mp4 --lyrics song.lrc` reports 'no-op', the track stays in transcribed mode, and nothing says --lyrics was dropped. A corrected .lrc, or a changed --title, is dropped the same way.

**Proposal.** When the manifest exists and source is ok, stop returning early unconditionally. (a) If --lyrics is given and its sha256 differs from source.json's lyrics_input (or none is recorded): copy it, rewrite source.json through canonical.write, update the source entry's content_sha256, and reset lyrics to pending. If that is too much for now, exit 2 with an explicit message instead. (b) If `stage_hash(cfg.ingest)` differs: perform the config-dependent side effects (copy_video from the hash-verified media path) and update config_hash, leaving the other entries alone. (c) Apply a differing --title to the manifest. (d) Whenever an explicit argument is not applied, say so on stderr. Align the video.py:306-310 message with whatever is implemented. Add one test per branch.

**Verifier's notes.** `if existing is not None and existing.documents.source.status == "ok": return IngestResult(..., already_ingested=True)` (mrw/ingest.py:176-183) runs before `_lyrics_input` (ingest.py:223), the copy_video copy (ingest.py:228-231) and title resolution. It never compares `config.stage_hash(cfg.ingest)`. The remedy printed at video.py:306-310 ('re-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true') therefore cannot work. Identical bytes give the same track_id, ingest prints 'already ingested: … — no-op' (cli.py:71-72), no copy is made, and source.json keeps the old path. `mrw status` then shows source=stale (cli.py:278-283, 296-297) with no command that clears it. A --lyrics or --title supplied later is dropped silently, so a track ingested without lyrics can never switch to aligned mode. The M0 no-op contract (tests/test_m0_smoke.py:91-99) covers identical re-ingest only, not new arguments or config.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Simpler than per-field partial updates: when --lyrics or --title differ from what is recorded, or stage_hash(cfg.ingest) differs from the stored config_hash, re-run ingest in full (a few seconds). Rewrite source.json and the source entry and keep the other entries. Invalidate dependents through DET-1's consumed identities (lyrics_input.sha256 for lyrics, audio_stream_sha256 for stems), so a path-only change from re-ingesting a moved file does not cascade. Fix the video.py:306-310 message in the same PR, and add one test per branch.

#### VID-10: OQ-2 escape hatches don't work: the suggested re-ingest is a no-op, and a hash-verified new path given to `mrw video` is ignored

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:306-310`, `mrw/ingest.py:176-183`, `mrw/ingest.py:228-231`, `mrw/library.py:63-67`, `mrw/video.py:300`, `mrw/video.py:325-326`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:414-426`

**Evidence.** The error message names a remedy (mrw/video.py:306-310): 'restore it there, or re-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true'. But ingest() returns early for a registered track (mrw/ingest.py:176-183 `if existing is not None and existing.documents.source.status == "ok": return IngestResult(... already_ingested=True)`). It therefore neither copies the video (mrw/ingest.py:228-231) nor updates original_path. `mrw video /new/path.mp4` resolves the track by hash (mrw/library.py:63-67), but run_video throws that verified path away and looks only at source.json (mrw/video.py:325-326). Separately, an input without an extension is copied as plain `source_video` (mrw/ingest.py:230), which `glob("source_video.*")` (mrw/video.py:300) never matches. tests/test_m5_smoke.py:414-426 only checks that the message mentions copy_video. OQ-2/R-7 accepted that a moved original fails loudly; that the stated remedy does not work is a new issue.

**Failure scenario.** A user moves their video folder. `mrw video T` fails and suggests a remedy. `mrw ingest /new/loc/clip.mp4 --config copy.toml` prints 'already ingested ... no-op' and copies nothing. `mrw video /new/loc/clip.mp4` still fails with 'original video not found at /old/loc/clip.mp4'. The only real options are moving the file back or deleting the track directory, which discards stems, features and lyrics that take minutes of Demucs and Whisper time per track to rebuild.

**Proposal.** (a) When the track argument is a path that hash-resolves to the track, use it as the video source; it has already been verified. (b) Have ingest on an existing ok track honour copy_video=true by copying after a sha256 check, without touching the documents. Or add `mrw relink <track> <path>`, which verifies the file and rewrites file.original_path; the source.json churn is within what the PLAN §7 scoping allows. (c) Glob `source_video*`, or record the copy's name in the manifest. (d) Test the remedy end to end: move the file, run with the new path or relink, and expect exit 0.

**Verifier's notes.** ingest() returns early for an ok track (ingest.py:176-183), before the copy at 228-231. The remedy named at video.py:306-310, 're-ingest with ingest.copy_video = true', therefore prints 'already ingested … no-op' and copies nothing. run_video throws away the hash-verified path from resolve_track_id (library.py:63-67) and uses source.json only (video.py:316, 325-326). An extensionless input is copied as 'source_video' (ingest.py:230), which glob('source_video.*') (video.py:300) never matches; this case is rare. The test checks only the message text (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:414-426). OQ-2/R-7 accepted loud failure for moved files, but a documented escape hatch that does not work is new. 'Restore it there' still works, so this is friction, not data loss. Medium is defensible; part (a) is the cheap, high-value piece and also strengthens VID-5.

#### LYR-13: Unsupported lyrics extensions are silently treated as txt, and --lyrics on an already-ingested track is silently ignored

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:144-156`, `mrw/ingest.py:176-183`, `mrw/cli.py:45-47`, `mrw/cli.py:71-72`

**Evidence.** ingest.py:145 sets `fmt = "lrc" if lyrics_path.suffix.lower() == ".lrc" else "txt"`. The CLI help says '(.lrc or .txt)' (cli.py:46), but nothing enforces it. ingest.py:176-183 returns `already_ingested=True` before looking at `lyrics_path`, and the CLI prints 'already ingested: … — no-op' (cli.py:71-72). source.schema.json says lyrics_input is present 'only when a lyrics file was supplied at ingest', and there is no other way to add lyrics later.

**Failure scenario.** (1) `mrw ingest video.mp4 --lyrics video.srt` registers the file as txt. Every SRT index line ('1') and timing line ('00:00:12,500 --> 00:00:15,000') becomes a sung line, so two-thirds of lines[] are unaligned garbage, with exit 0. A .docx or .rtf produces lines of binary garbage. (2) A user who ingested without lyrics re-runs `mrw ingest song.wav --lyrics song.lrc`, gets 'no-op', and the lyrics are dropped. The only workaround is deleting the track directory, which throws away stems that took minutes to compute.

**Proposal.** In the CLI, accept only .lrc and .txt (optionally parse .srt/.vtt into timed lines) and exit 2 for anything else, with a check that the content is text. When --lyrics is passed for an already-ingested track and its sha differs, either register the new lyrics_input (rewrite source.json and reset lyrics to pending) or exit 2 with a message naming the supported path. Never print 'no-op' while ignoring an explicit argument. Test both behaviours.

**Verifier's notes.** ingest.py:145 maps any suffix other than .lrc to 'txt', while the help text at cli.py:46 says '(.lrc or .txt)' but nothing enforces it. Parsed as txt, an .srt's index lines and '-->' timing lines become sung lines; tokens such as '000012500' never anchor, so they come out as unaligned garbage. ingest.py:176-183 returns already_ingested before it looks at lyrics_path, and cli.py:71-72 prints 'no-op'. No other command registers lyrics afterwards (source.schema.json:83: 'Present only when a lyrics file was supplied at ingest'). Nothing records this. Medium rather than higher because the CLI at least says 'no-op'.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. When re-registering lyrics on an existing track, also update the manifest's source content_sha256, reset lyrics (and later structure) to pending, and remove the previous lyrics_input.* file when the format changes.

### B5. Video stage never checks it is analysing the ingested file

#### DET-3: The video stage analyses whatever file is at file.original_path without checking it is the ingested file

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:299-310`, `mrw/video.py:325-326`, `mrw/ingest.py:233-240`, `mrw/library.py:57-72`

**Evidence.** `_resolve_video_path` returns `copies[0]` when a `source_video.*` copy exists, otherwise `original` whenever `original.is_file()` (video.py:300-305). It never compares the bytes with `source_doc["file"]["sha256"]`, which ingest recorded (`sha256=file_sha`, ingest.py:239). PLAN §7 defines identity as a function of file bytes. Only the path form of the CLI re-hashes the file (library.py:63-71). The documented `mrw video <track_id>` form does not. With the default copy_video=false, every video run re-reads a path outside the library.

**Failure scenario.** The user ingests ~/Videos/song.mp4 (track 3f9a…) and later re-exports a new cut to the same path. `mrw video 3f9a…`, or a re-run after tuning detector_threshold, finds a file at original_path and analyses the new cut. video.json's shots, frames, palettes and captions now describe bytes that don't hash to file.sha256. source.json's fps and frame_count no longer match. The video timeline no longer matches source_audio.flac, which audio_features and lyrics were computed from. The manifest records status ok. M6's cut/beat alignment would compute on mismatched timelines. The same input track_id and config produce different documents, so the determinism contract is broken too.

**Proposal.** In `_resolve_video_path`, hash the candidate with `hashing.sha256_file` and compare it with `source_doc['file']['sha256']`, for both the `source_video.*` copy and original_path. On mismatch, raise PrerequisiteError (exit 2, nothing recorded) naming the path and both hashes: 'the file at <path> is not the ingested video — restore it or ingest it as a new track'. To avoid re-hashing large files on every run, keep (path, size, mtime_ns, sha256) in the manifest entry and re-hash only when size or mtime change. Fold the verified sha into video's inputs_sha256 (DET-1). Test: ingest the cut fixture, overwrite the same path with a re-cut fixture, run `mrw video <track_id>`, and assert exit 2 with no video.json or frames/.

**Verifier's notes.** `_resolve_video_path` returns `copies[0]` or `original` purely on existence (mrw/video.py:300-305). run_video never compares those bytes with source_doc['file']['sha256'] (written at ingest.py:239). It also never compares the probed fps and frame_count (video.py:373) with source.json's media.video. Only the path form of the argument re-hashes (library.py:63-71); the documented `mrw video <track_id>` form does not. PLAN §4 stage 1 promises that re-analysis 'fails loudly if the original path has gone stale'. OQ-2 accepted moved originals, not replaced ones, so in-place replacement is analysed silently and recorded ok. Rated medium rather than high: it requires the user to overwrite or re-encode the original at the same path, an edge case even though it is silent when it happens.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Hash unconditionally instead of caching (path, size, mtime_ns) in the manifest. hashlib SHA-256 runs at roughly GB/s, which is negligible next to the stage's 1–2 min of decode passes, and an mtime cache adds manifest schema and its own staleness logic for no real gain. Run the check only when the stage will execute: move `_resolve_video_path` (video.py:326) after the no-op check (video.py:331-337). That also stops an up-to-date track whose original was moved from failing with exit 2. On mismatch, raise PrerequisiteError naming the path and both hashes.

#### ORCH-2: The video stage analyses whatever file is at original_path (or any source_video.*) without checking it is the ingested bytes

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:299`, `mrw/ingest.py:228`, `mrw/ingest.py:235`, `PLAN.md:143`

**Evidence.** `_resolve_video_path` (video.py:299-310): `copies = sorted(track_dir.glob("source_video.*")); if copies: return copies[0]; original = Path(source_doc["file"]["original_path"]); if original.is_file(): return original`. source.json records `size_bytes` and `sha256` (ingest.py:235-240), but nothing compares them with the file before analysis. The copy is written straight to its final name with no temp-and-rename: `shutil.copyfile(media_path, track_dir / f"source_video{media_path.suffix.lower()}")` (ingest.py:228-231). PLAN §4 stage 1 (PLAN.md:143-146) says: "Without the copy, video re-analysis fails loudly if the original path has gone stale." (_resolve_video_path is in video.py; it is included here because it enforces the referenced-media identity contract that ingest sets up.)

**Failure scenario.** (a) A user ingests ~/Videos/song.mp4 and later exports a different cut under the same filename. `mrw video T` analyses the new file without any warning. The shots, frames, palettes and paid captions now describe bytes whose sha256 does not match track_id. The video-clock times no longer line up with source_audio.flac, and source.json media.video (fps, frame_count) disagrees with video.json. Everything is recorded as ok. (b) Ingest with copy_video=true runs out of disk space (ENOSPC) part-way through copying a multi-GB video, raises OSError and leaves a truncated source_video.mp4. The user re-ingests with copy_video=false to save space, which succeeds. The video stage then prefers the truncated copy and video.json covers only the start of the video, with no error.

**Proposal.** In `_resolve_video_path`, check the candidate against source.json before using it. First compare `stat().st_size` with `file.size_bytes` (cheap). Then compare a streamed sha256 with `file.sha256`; the stage decodes the whole file anyway, so the extra read is affordable. On a mismatch, raise PrerequisiteError naming the mismatch and the remedy. At ingest, write the copy to `.source_video.tmp` and `os.replace` it into place, removing the temp file on failure. Add a fast test: ingest an mp4, overwrite the original with different bytes, run `mrw video`, and expect exit 2 with the manifest untouched.

**Verifier's notes.** `_resolve_video_path` (mrw/video.py:299-310) returns any `source_video.*` or whatever file is at original_path. It never compares the file with source.json `file.size_bytes`/`file.sha256` (recorded at ingest.py:235-240), and run_video never compares the re-probed fps/frame_count with source.json either. The copy is written in place, with no temp file, before any manifest exists (ingest.py:228-231). A failed copy therefore survives a later successful ingest with copy_video=false, and the video stage prefers it. source.schema.json:17 says of original_path "nothing re-reads it", and PLAN.md:143-146 promises a loud failure on a stale path, so a replaced file silently breaks a stated contract. Downgraded to medium because the trigger is an edge case, not normal use: different bytes at the same path, or ENOSPC followed by a re-ingest with a changed config. Caveat on (b): a truncated MP4 with its moov atom at the end makes ffprobe fail loudly (exit 1). A silent partial analysis needs a faststart MP4 or an MKV.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Check the size first, then a streamed sha256, and raise PrerequisiteError naming the expected and actual values. Apply the same check to `source_video.*` copies so leftovers from failed ingests are rejected. Make the copy atomic (temp file + os.replace); ORCH-12's staging directory removes the partial-copy path at its source.

#### VID-5: Video stage re-reads original_path (or source_video.*) without checking that it is still the ingested file

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:299-305`, `mrw/models.py:39-43`, `schemas/source.schema.json:17`

**Evidence.** mrw/video.py:299-305 reads: `copies = sorted(track_dir.glob("source_video.*"))` / `if copies: return copies[0]` / `original = Path(source_doc["file"]["original_path"])` / `if original.is_file(): return original`. source.json already records size_bytes and sha256 (mrw/models.py:39-43), but neither is compared. Track identity is defined as the hash of the ingested bytes (CLAUDE.md, D1). schemas/source.schema.json:17 even describes original_path as 'Informational; may go stale — nothing re-reads it.' The video stage does re-read it.

**Failure scenario.** A user ingests ~/Downloads/video.mp4 as track A. Later a different or re-cut video is saved to the same path, or the file is re-encoded in place. `mrw video A`, or a re-run after a config edit, analyses the new file. It writes video B's shots, frames, palettes, motion and paid captions into track A's video.json, alongside A's audio features and lyrics. The manifest records ok, and M6 would align B's cuts to A's beats. A truncated source_video.* copy is analysed just as silently.

**Proposal.** In _resolve_video_path, first compare stat().st_size with file.size_bytes, which costs nothing. Then verify `hashing.sha256_file(path) == file.sha256`, about 1 s per GB; the result can be cached by (size, mtime_ns) under .cache/. Apply this to both the copy and the original. On mismatch, raise PrerequisiteError("file at <path> no longer matches track <id> (sha256 differs)"). Fix the source.schema.json description. Test: ingest, overwrite the file with a different generated clip, run `mrw video`, and expect exit 2 with no document written.

**Verifier's notes.** _resolve_video_path (video.py:299-305) returns the source_video.* copy or file.original_path without comparing it to the size_bytes/sha256 recorded at ingest (models.py:39-43; ingest.py:235-240). source.schema.json:17 still says original_path is 'Informational; may go stale — nothing re-reads it', which is now false. OQ-2/R-7 and PLAN §4 accepted that a moved original 'fails loudly'. A replaced original, same path with different bytes, instead silently writes file B's shots, palettes and paid captions into track A's video.json. That is a genuinely new argument and not a recorded decision. The truncated-copy sub-scenario is essentially unreachable: the copy is made during ingest before the manifest is written, and an interrupted ingest re-runs from scratch. The main scenario needs a file re-saved at the same path plus a later video run for the old track, for example after a config change or in M7 batch mode. It is silent but an edge case, so I downgraded to medium. The fix is cheap.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Compare size first, then sha256. Hashing costs about 1 s per GB and the stage already decodes the file three times, so the (size, mtime) cache is optional. On mismatch raise PrerequisiteError (exit 2, nothing recorded). When the CLI argument is a path, reuse the hash that resolve_track_id already computed (see VID-10a). Fix the source.schema.json description.

### B6. Settings that change no output are hashed

#### ORCH-15: Settings that change no output are hashed anyway: budget, retain and copy_video changes force re-runs or a permanent 'stale'

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, P2 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:98`, `mrw/config.py:27`, `mrw/config.py:23`, `mrw/config.py:131`, `mrw/video.py:431`, `mrw/stems.py:150`, `CLAUDE.md:53`

**Evidence.** `stage_hash` hashes the whole config section (config.py:131-134). So `caption_budget_usd_per_run` (config.py:98), `retain` (config.py:27) and `copy_video` (config.py:23) are part of the video, stems and ingest config_hashes, even though they change no document bytes. BudgetExceeded tells the user to "raise video.caption_budget_usd_per_run in mrw.toml to proceed" (video.py:431-438). The stems no-op check requires the hashes to be equal (stems.py:150-159), and the retain=false branch then deletes what it just produced (stems.py:213-221). The source stale display compares against the cfg.ingest hash (cli.py:296-297), while re-ingest is a no-op (ORCH-6). CLAUDE.md:53-56 records the policy of hashing the full subset as it applies to *adding* fields. Already known as a policy; the new argument is that these settings impose a cost on every change, not just once, and that cost was not part of that decision.

**Failure scenario.** Raising the caption budget for one long video changes the video hash for every track. Each `mrw video T` then re-decodes the video, re-detects shots and re-runs the motion pass, taking minutes per track. With anthropic captions it also replays from cache and overwrites the ledger with $0 (ORCH-7). Setting `retain = false` to free disk space runs a full 5–10 minute CPU Demucs separation per track, only to delete the result. Toggling `copy_video` makes every track show `source=stale` permanently.

**Proposal.** Split each section into output-affecting fields, which are hashed, and operational fields, which `stage_hash` leaves out. This could be a marker such as `Field(json_schema_extra={"hashed": False})` that stage_hash respects, or a separate `[run]` or `[budget]` section. Move caption_budget_usd_per_run, retain and copy_video there. Handle a retain true→false change by deleting stems/ and updating `retained`, without separating again. Record which fields are which in CLAUDE.md next to the config-evolution policy. The one-time mass-stale this causes is consistent with that policy.

**Verifier's notes.** stage_hash covers the whole section (config.py:131-134), so caption_budget_usd_per_run (config.py:98), retain (config.py:27) and copy_video (config.py:23) are hashed, although none of them changes document bytes; copy_video is not even recorded in source.json. Traced the costs. Raising the budget, as the BudgetExceeded message instructs (video.py:431-438), makes every track's video stage re-run in full. Flipping retain true→false fails the stems no-op check (stems.py:150-159), so a full Demucs separation runs and is then deleted (stems.py:213-221); test_m2_smoke.py:164 exercises exactly this. Toggling copy_video leaves source=stale permanently because re-ingest is a no-op. The full-subset policy is known (CLAUDE.md:53-56, review 006 note 1), but it was argued only for added fields, and review 001 wanted unrelated edits not to stale anything. The new argument holds.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do not remove these fields from the hashed subset. That changes every stage hash once, which means re-separating every track for stems and a permanent source=stale for ingest (ORCH-6). Instead, hash each section with its operational fields reset to their defaults. I verified this leaves every default-config hash byte-identical while making budget, retain and copy_video changes hash-neutral. Then handle retain transitions explicitly in run_stems. true→false: delete stems/ and set retained=false without separating. false→true: separate again, because once retain stops affecting the hash, the existing no-op guard `not prior.retained` would return already_done. Document the two field classes next to the config-evolution policy.

## C. Lyrics input parsing

### C1. Compressed LRC lines lose their repeats

#### LYR-5: Compressed LRC lines with several time tags keep only the first timestamp, so repeated lines are lost

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:249-254`, `mrw/lyrics.py:56`

**Evidence.** lyrics.py:249 `match = _LRC_TS.match(stripped)` takes only the first tag as the hint, and lyrics.py:254 `text = _LRC_TS.sub("", stripped).strip()` removes all tags, so each physical line produces exactly one SuppliedLine. I applied the repo's own regexes, read as data via ast, to `[00:45.00][01:30.00][02:40.00]Neon reverie, don't wake me up`. The result is a single line with hint 45.0; the 01:30 and 02:40 occurrences disappear. Several time tags on one line is a standard LRC feature, typically used to compress choruses. Multi-tag markup lines behave the same way.

**Failure scenario.** With a compressed .lrc, each chorus line appears once in lines[]. The second and third sung occurrences are never aligned, so their audio is reported in untranscribed_regions ('vocals here, content unknown') even though the supplied file says exactly what is sung there. vocal_activity_covered_ratio drops, and line counts no longer match the performance. M6's lyric-repetition evidence ('a line cluster that recurs ≥ 2×', PLAN §4 stage 6) never sees the repetition, which is exactly the chorus evidence the structure stage depends on.

**Proposal.** Parse the leading run of time tags, `^(?:\[\d{1,3}:\d{2}(?:[.:]\d{1,3})?\])+`, and emit one SuppliedLine per tag, sharing the text and source_line_index. Do the same for markup. When any hints exist, stable-sort lines by (hint, source_line_index) before alignment so the cursor sees them in timeline order. Add parser tests for multi-tag lines. Ingest's `line_count` can stay a count of physical lines.

**Verifier's notes.** lyrics.py:249 takes only the first tag via `_LRC_TS.match`, and :254 strips every tag with `_LRC_TS.sub`, so each physical line yields exactly one SuppliedLine. I read the repo's own regexes via ast and applied them: `[00:45.00][01:30.00][02:40.00]Neon reverie, don't wake me up` gives one line with hint 45.0. Several time tags on one line is a core part of the LRC format, not an exotic extension. The 01:30 and 02:40 occurrences, which the supplied file specifies exactly, are reported as untranscribed 'content unknown', coverage drops, and M6 loses its chorus-repetition evidence. This is not recorded anywhere. The schema's wording 'lines mirror the supplied file's line breaks' (lyrics.schema.json:36) literally permits the current behaviour, so the contract text has to change along with the code.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Additionally: (1) update the lines[] description so that a multi-tag LRC line yields one entry per tag, and pair this with LYR-9's source_line_index so the duplicates stay traceable to their file line; (2) land it together with, or after, LYR-2's consumed-word exclusion, because expanded identical lines less than 5 s apart would otherwise collapse onto the earliest occurrence.

### C2. Lyrics decoding silently corrupts text

#### SEC-7: Lyrics files are decoded as UTF-8 with errors='replace': BOM, UTF-16 and cp1252 files silently corrupt display text and break markup/LRC detection

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H2, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:148`, `mrw/lyrics.py:513`, `mrw/lyrics.py:245`

**Evidence.** Both readers use `read_text(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace')` (ingest.py:148, lyrics.py:513-515). `parse_supplied_lyrics` relies on `raw.strip()` plus the anchored `_LRC_TS.match` and `_MARKUP.fullmatch` (lyrics.py:245-257), and str.strip() does not remove U+FEFF. I ran the same regexes and loop on one sample saved in three encodings:
- UTF-8 with BOM: the first-line header `[Verse 1]` becomes a sung line, 'U+FEFF[Verse 1]', instead of markup.
- UTF-16 (Windows Notepad's 'Unicode'): every line comes out with NUL (U+0000) between characters, all markup and LRC hints are lost, and an extra line containing only NUL appears.
- cp1252: 'Café au lait' becomes 'Caf' + U+FFFD + ' au lait'.
Because `normalize()` strips non-word characters, UTF-16 tokens can still anchor against the transcript. The NUL-riddled `text` is then written to lyrics.json as the authoritative display text. None of this is flagged (H1), and ingest's `has_timestamps` is computed from the same garbage.

**Failure scenario.** A .txt that Windows Notepad saved as UTF-8 with BOM, starting with `[Verse 1]`, gets that header aligned as a sung line. It ends up unaligned and low_confidence and inflates lines_flagged_ratio, which is exactly the failure R-3 was meant to prevent. A UTF-16 .lrc loses every timestamp hint and sends NUL-interleaved strings to the Swift consumer. A cp1252 file with accented letters silently gets U+FFFD in their place.

**Proposal.** Decode once, at ingest. Sniff BOMs (utf-8-sig, utf-16/32), then try strict UTF-8. If that fails, either refuse with exit 2 and a clear message, or fall back to cp1252, record `lyrics_input.encoding` (additive source minor) and set a flag. Store a normalized UTF-8 copy (NFC, BOM and C0 controls stripped) next to the verbatim file, hash both, and have the lyrics stage read only the normalized copy. Add tests for BOM, UTF-16 and cp1252 inputs.

**Verifier's notes.** I ran the repo's own regexes and loop (lyrics.py:56-57, 244-269), read from source, on one sample saved in four encodings and decoded as utf-8 with errors='replace'. With a UTF-8 BOM, the first line becomes the sung line '﻿[Verse 1]'; str.strip() does not remove U+FEFF ('﻿'.isspace() is False). With UTF-16, every line is NUL-interleaved, all markup and LRC hints are lost, and extra lines containing only '\x00' appear. With cp1252, the output is 'Caf� au lait, don�t stop' (the curly apostrophe is lost too). ingest.py:148 uses the same decode for line_count and has_timestamps. Nothing is recorded about encodings. BOM-prefixed LRC/TXT files, which often start with [ti:...] or [Verse 1], are a common variant and reproduce exactly the R-3 failure review 001 required fixing. The cp1252 case silently corrupts the authoritative display text with no flag.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Use one shared decode helper in both ingest and the lyrics stage. Sniff the BOM (utf-8-sig, utf-16, utf-32); otherwise decode as strict UTF-8. If strict decoding fails, refuse at ingest with exit 2, name the file and suggest `iconv`. Do not fall back to cp1252: cp1252 maps nearly every byte, so GBK, Shift-JIS and Latin-2 lyrics would be silently mojibaked, which is the very failure being fixed. Strip U+FEFF and C0 controls and NFC-normalize. No second stored copy is needed, because decoding the verbatim bytes is deterministic. Add tests for BOM, UTF-16 and invalid-byte inputs.

#### LYR-6: Lyrics files are decoded with errors="replace" and the BOM is never stripped, silently corrupting the display text

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:513-515`, `mrw/lyrics.py:245`, `mrw/lyrics.py:249`, `mrw/lyrics.py:257`, `mrw/ingest.py:148`

**Evidence.** lyrics.py:513-515 reads the file with `read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")`, and ingest.py:148 does the same; nothing handles a BOM. `raw.strip()` (lyrics.py:245) does not remove U+FEFF, since `'﻿'.isspace()` is False. `_LRC_TS.match` and `_MARKUP.fullmatch` are anchored at the first character. Checked with the repo's regexes: '﻿[Verse 1]\n[00:12.50]City lights' yields lines ['﻿[Verse 1]', 'City lights'] and markup []; '﻿[00:12.50]City lights' yields the line '﻿City lights' with hint None. cp1252 bytes b"don\x92t caf\xe9" decode to 'don�t caf�'. The schema calls supplied text the 'display truth' (lyrics.schema.json:14).

**Failure scenario.** (1) A BOM-prefixed file, as written by pre-2019 Windows Notepad 'UTF-8' and by many LRC editors. A first-line '[Verse 1]' header becomes a sung line, can't anchor, and appears as an `unaligned` line with invented timing. It inflates lines_flagged_ratio and is missing from supplied_markup. If the first line is a timestamped lyric instead, it loses its hint and its display text starts with an invisible U+FEFF. (2) cp1252, Latin-1, GBK or Shift-JIS files: every typographic apostrophe or accented character becomes U+FFFD in lyrics.json with exit 0 and no flag. For GBK or Shift-JIS, nearly the whole text is garbage.

**Proposal.** At ingest, decode the lyrics bytes strictly as `utf-8-sig`. On UnicodeDecodeError, exit 2 with an actionable 're-save as UTF-8' message, or implement an explicit, recorded fallback: try cp1252 and record `encoding` in source.json lyrics_input (an additive change). In the lyrics stage, read with `utf-8-sig` (strict, since ingest already validated it) and NFC-normalize display text. Add tests for BOM, CRLF and cp1252 input.

**Verifier's notes.** lyrics.py:513-515 and ingest.py:148 both decode with `encoding='utf-8', errors='replace'`. Python's utf-8 codec keeps U+FEFF, and `'﻿'.isspace()` is False, so `raw.strip()` at :245 leaves it in place. Applying the repo regexes: a BOM followed by '[Verse 1]' gives the sung line '﻿[Verse 1]' and no markup. A BOM followed by '[00:12.50]City lights' gives the text '﻿City lights' with hint None, and ingest's has_timestamps also misses that first line. cp1252 bytes such as b'don\x92t caf\xe9' decode silently to 'don�t caf�'. I downgrade to medium. The BOM damage is confined to the first line and mostly surfaces as an `unaligned` flag. Non-UTF-8 lyrics files are uncommon on the macOS target. The cp1252 corruption is silent in the display text, but normalize() strips U+FFFD, so alignment mostly survives.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Two implementation notes: the CLI maps IngestError to exit 1 (cli.py:67-69), so the 're-save as UTF-8' failure needs its own error type to exit 2. Also, use one shared strict `utf-8-sig` decode helper in both ingest (for line_count and has_timestamps) and the lyrics stage, so the two never disagree.

#### ORCH-14: The lyrics file's encoding is never checked, so files with a BOM, UTF-16 or cp1252 are silently mangled

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H2, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:144`, `mrw/ingest.py:148`, `mrw/ingest.py:155`, `mrw/lyrics.py:513`

**Evidence.** `_lyrics_input` copies the file unchanged, then runs `text = dest.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")` (ingest.py:147-148) and computes `has_timestamps=any(_LRC_TIMESTAMP.match(ln) for ln in lines)` on lines that have not been stripped (ingest.py:149-155). The lyrics stage re-reads the copy the same way (lyrics.py:513-515). The format is chosen from the file suffix alone (ingest.py:145). I verified three cases: `"﻿[Verse 1]".strip()` keeps the BOM; a UTF-16 .lrc decodes to text with NUL bytes between the characters; and cp1252 'café' becomes 'caf�'.

**Failure scenario.** A lyrics.txt saved on Windows as UTF-8 with a BOM starts with '[Verse 1]'. Because of the BOM, the header is not recognised as markup and becomes a sung line: it pollutes lines[] and coverage, and the R-3 structure evidence is lost. A UTF-16 .lrc gives has_timestamps=false and line text full of NULs, so every line ends up unaligned. A cp1252 file puts U+FFFD replacement characters into lyrics.json, whose text is meant to be the display truth. Ingest reports success in all three cases.

**Proposal.** Decide the encoding at ingest. If there is a BOM, honour it (utf-8-sig or utf-16); otherwise decode strictly as UTF-8. On UnicodeDecodeError, exit 2 with 'lyrics file is not UTF-8; convert it'. Compute line_count and has_timestamps on the decoded text with the BOM removed and lines stripped. Either record the encoding in source.json (additive `lyrics_input.encoding`) or store a normalised UTF-8 copy next to the original, and make lyrics.py decode accordingly. Optionally reject non-text files (containing NUL bytes) passed via --lyrics.

**Verifier's notes.** Verified: ingest copies the file, then reads it with read_text(utf-8, errors='replace') (ingest.py:147-148), and computes has_timestamps on lines that are not stripped (ingest.py:149-155). lyrics.py:513-515 re-reads it the same way, and parse_supplied_lyrics strips each line but keeps U+FEFF (lyrics.py:244-257). Reproduced: '﻿[Verse 1]'.strip() keeps the BOM and fails `_MARKUP`, so the header becomes a sung line. The same happens to an LRC's leading `[ti:…]` tag. A UTF-16 LRC decodes to lines laced with NULs and has_timestamps=False, and cp1252 'café' becomes 'caf�'. Nothing in the docs mentions encoding. Medium: unaligned flags partly surface the BOM and UTF-16 damage, but cp1252 replacement characters land silently in the display text.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Decode once at ingest: honour a BOM with utf-8-sig or utf-16, otherwise decode strict UTF-8, and exit 2 with a 'convert to UTF-8' message if that fails. Record the encoding as an additive field and have lyrics.py decode with it, or store a normalised UTF-8 copy. Compute has_timestamps on stripped lines.

### C3. LRC metadata and format variants

#### LYR-7: LRC metadata tags are emitted as section markup, [offset:] is ignored, timestamp variants stay in display text, and ♪ or unbracketed headers become sung lines

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:56-58`, `mrw/lyrics.py:244-270`, `mrw/ingest.py:38`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:84-87`

**Evidence.** Only `_LRC_TS = \[(\d{1,3}):(\d{2})(?:\.(\d{1,3}))?\]` and `_MARKUP = ^\[[^\]]+\]$` are recognised (lyrics.py:56-57). I applied these regexes from the repo to common variants: (1) ID tags `[ar:…] [ti:…] [al:…] [by:…] [offset:+1500] [length: 03:45]` become 6 supplied_markup entries with label 'other'. The schema (lyrics.schema.json:86) defines supplied_markup as section headers that 'feed structure.json section labeling'. (2) `[offset:]` is never applied. (3) `[00:12:50]City lights…` keeps '[00:12:50]City…' as display text with hint None, while ingest's has_timestamps regex `^\[\d{1,3}:\d{2}` (ingest.py:38) counts it as timestamped. (4) Enhanced word tags `<00:12.00>City <00:12.40>lights` stay in the display text, and normalize() turns '<00:12.00>City' into '001200city', so the line rarely anchors. (5) `[01:10.00]♪`, the instrumental marker in many synced-lyrics exports, becomes a sung line. (6) 'Chorus:', '(Chorus)' and 'Verse 1' become sung lines, and '[Chorus] Neon reverie' keeps the header in the text. (7) `_LABELS` maps 'Hook' and 'Refrain' to 'other'.

**Failure scenario.** A typical real .lrc with an ID-tag header produces supplied_markup full of 'other' pseudo-headers at source lines 0-5. Those are what M6 is told to treat as the dominant section-label evidence. Every hint is shifted by the ignored offset. Instrumental ♪ lines and header-only lines land in lines[] as unaligned lines with interpolated timing, which inflates lines_flagged_ratio. Files in the mm:ss:xx or enhanced format put raw timestamps into lyrics.json display text and mostly fail to anchor.

**Proposal.** Recognise LRC ID tags, case-insensitively: `^\[(ar|ti|al|au|by|re|ve|length|offset|la|#)\s*:.*\]$`. Record them or skip them, but never as markup. Apply `[offset:±ms]` to every hint (LRC convention: positive offset means lyrics appear earlier, so use hint − offset/1000, clamped at 0). Accept `mm:ss:xx` and `mm:ss,xx`. Strip `<mm:ss.xx>` word tags from the text, optionally keeping them as word hints. Treat lines with no word characters (♪, …, —) as markup rather than sung lines. Recognise a small grammar of unbracketed headers, e.g. `^\(?(intro|verse|pre-?chorus|chorus|hook|refrain|bridge|outro)\b[^)]*\)?:?$` case-insensitive, and map hook and refrain to chorus. Add a parser unit test for each variant.

**Verifier's notes.** I checked every variant against the repo's `_LRC_TS`, `_MARKUP` and `_LABELS` (read via ast) with my own re-implementation of the parse loop:
- [ar:], [ti:], [al:], [by:], [offset:+1500] and [length: 03:45] become six supplied_markup entries labelled 'other'.
- `[00:12:50]City…` keeps the tag in its text with hint None, yet ingest's `^\[\d{1,3}:\d{2}` reports has_timestamps=true.
- Enhanced `<00:12.00>City` stays in the text, and normalize() turns it into '001200city'.
- `[01:10.00]♪` becomes a sung line: normalize('♪')=='' gives empty tokens, so the line is emitted unanchored and flagged.
- 'Chorus:', '(Chorus)' and 'Verse 1' become sung lines, and '[Chorus] Neon reverie' keeps the header in its text.
- Hook, Refrain and Pre-Chorus map to 'other'.
One overstatement: [offset:] tags are usually absent or 0, so 'every hint is shifted' is rarely true. The common real-world impact is ID-tag pollution of supplied_markup.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Mapping hook and refrain to chorus is a vocabulary decision under OQ-11 (closed label set), so record it with a resolution note in OPEN_QUESTIONS.md (P2) rather than folding it into a parser fix. For symbol-only lines such as ♪, keep them as markup entries with hint_seconds rather than silently dropping them, because they are useful instrumental evidence for M6.

#### SCH-2: supplied_markup records LRC metadata tags as section headers, multi-timestamp LRC lines collapse into one, and its label vocabulary doesn't match structure's

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S3, H1, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:56-58`, `mrw/lyrics.py:234-270`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:84-107`, `schemas/structure.schema.json:18-22`

**Evidence.** lyrics.py:57 `_MARKUP = re.compile(r"^\[[^\]]+\]$")` treats any line that is entirely in brackets as a section header, and `_LRC_TS` (lyrics.py:56) only recognises [mm:ss(.xx)] timestamps. I ran the patterns, copied verbatim, on sample lines in a scratch check. `[ar:The Hypothetical]`, `[ti:Neon Reverie]`, `[length: 04:02]` and `[offset:+250]` all became markup with label 'other' via lyrics.py:259 `label = next((l for l in _LABELS if inner.startswith(l)), "other")`. The compressed-LRC line `[00:45.50][01:45.20][03:10.00]Neon reverie...` became a single SuppliedLine: `match = _LRC_TS.match(stripped)` (249) keeps only the first hint and `_LRC_TS.sub("", stripped)` (254) deletes the others. The schema defines supplied_markup as 'section-header lines like [Verse 1] or [Chorus]' that feed structure.json as evidence which 'dominates label assignment'. Its label enum (intro/verse/chorus/bridge/outro/other) lacks structure's `instrumental`. The startswith rule also maps [Pre-Chorus], [Hook], [Refrain], [Instrumental] and [Solo] to 'other'. This overlaps the lyrics stage's scope but breaks the cross-document contract.

**Failure scenario.** A typical .lrc from a lyrics site starts with [ti:], [ar:], [al:], [by:] and [length:] tags. lyrics.json's supplied_markup then gets five entries like {"text": "[ar:...]", "label": "other", "source_line_index": n}, which an LLM and M6 read as section headers. A compressed LRC that lists the chorus once with three timestamps yields one supplied line instead of three. The 2nd and 3rd choruses' Whisper words then anchor to nothing and never appear in lines[]. Those chorus spans are reported in untranscribed_regions as 'vocals here, content unknown' even though the file contains the text, and M6 loses the lyric-repetition evidence for the chorus.

**Proposal.** In parse_supplied_lyrics, skip LRC ID tags (case-insensitive ar/al/ti/au/by/length/offset/re/ve/tool/# followed by ':') and apply [offset:±ms] to the hints. Expand a multi-timestamp line into one SuppliedLine per timestamp, with the same source_line_index and distinct hint_seconds. Before M6, align the vocabularies: add `instrumental` (and decide on pre_chorus/hook) to the lyrics label enum as an additive 1.2.0 change, with an explicit mapping table (hook/refrain→chorus, solo/interlude→instrumental). Keep the lyrics labels a subset of structure's labels plus 'other'. Add a fixture LRC with ID tags and a compressed line to test_m4.

**Verifier's notes.** I reproduced it with the verbatim regexes and loop from lyrics.py:56-58 and 244-270 (lrc_probe.py). [ti:], [ar:], [al:], [by:], [length: 04:02] and [offset:+250] all become SuppliedMarkup with label 'other'. `[00:45.50][01:45.20][03:10.00]Neon reverie...` becomes one SuppliedLine with hint 45.5: `_LRC_TS.match` keeps the first stamp and `_LRC_TS.sub` deletes the rest. In aligned mode only supplied lines carry words (lyrics.py:518-536), and coverage counts only lines' words (369-371). The 2nd and 3rd chorus repeats are therefore absent from lines[] and reported as uncovered (fragments of 1 s or more) although the file contains their text. [Instrumental] and [Guitar Solo] map to 'other' although 'instrumental' is in structure's label enum, contradicting the lyrics schema's own description ('other' only for headers that don't map to the structure vocabulary). ID tags are standard in .lrc files from lyric sites, and multi-timestamp lines are a documented LRC feature. Nothing about either is recorded in the reviews. The impact is honest (flagged as untranscribed, label 'other') but pollutes supplied_markup and M6 evidence, so medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Before markup detection, skip LRC ID tags matching `^\[(ar|al|ti|au|by|length|offset|re|ve|tool|#)\s*:` (case-insensitive). Applying [offset:] is unnecessary: hints only open -5/+20 s search windows (align.py:35-36). Expand a multi-timestamp line into one SuppliedLine per stamp, and sort the expanded lines by hint before align_lines. Add 'instrumental' to the lyrics label enum (additive 1.2.0) when M6's mapping table is designed. Leave the pre_chorus/hook/refrain question to OQ-11. Add the ID-tag and compressed-line fixture to test_m4.

### C4. Token normalisation is too narrow

#### LYR-10: Token normalisation lacks NFKC and casefold, strips curly apostrophes and combining marks, and cannot handle CJK or other scripts without spaces

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:25`, `mrw/align.py:68-69`, `mrw/align.py:106-107`, `mrw/align.py:125`

**Evidence.** align.py:25 defines `_PUNCT = re.compile(r"[^\w']+", re.UNICODE)`, and normalize() is `_PUNCT.sub("", token.lower())` (align.py:68-69). Results from the repo's own pattern: 'don’t' becomes 'dont' while Whisper's "don't" stays "don't", a ratio of 0.889. 'café' (NFD) becomes 'cafe' while 'café' (NFC) stays 'café'. Full-width 'Ｈｅｌｌｏ' is not folded. Devanagari 'नमस्ते' becomes 'नमसत', because Python's \w excludes combining marks; this happens on both sides and collapses distinct words. A lone "'" survives as a token. Supplied words come from `line.text.split()` (align.py:106), and window lengths are capped at `k + 3` observed words (align.py:125). I'm assuming, with high confidence, that faster-whisper splits zh/ja/th/lo/my/yue word streams on Unicode token boundaries, giving roughly one character per word. A 9-character Chinese line is then one supplied token compared against windows of at most 4 characters; in my simulation it never anchors.

**Failure scenario.** (1) Lyrics copied from sources that use typographic apostrophes (Apple Music, Spotify, Word) lose about 11% on every contraction. On short lines, combined with real mishearings, that can push the score below the 0.6 threshold or the confidence below 0.5. (2) Any aligned run in Chinese, Japanese or Thai leaves every line unaligned with interpolated timing and no word timing at all. The flags are honest, but the aligned path does not work for these languages.

**Proposal.** Normalize as follows: NFKC, then casefold, then map apostrophe variants (’ ‘ ʼ ´ `) to ' and drop apostrophes entirely on both sides (so don't matches dont), then strip only Unicode P* and S* categories and keep M*. For scripts without spaces (detected from the language or unicodedata script), tokenize supplied lines into characters, or compare at character level with window sizes measured in characters. Add tests for curly quotes, NFD and CJK.

**Verifier's notes.** I applied the repo's `_PUNCT` pattern: 'don’t'→'dont' while "don't" stays, NFD 'café'→'cafe', full-width text is lowercased but not folded, and Devanagari 'नमस्ते'→'नमसत' because combining marks are not \w. In faster-whisper v1.2.1, tokenizer.split_to_word_tokens uses unicode-point splitting for zh/ja/th/lo/my/yue (I read this in the upstream source). Simulating a 9-character Chinese line: with 1-character words the best score is 0.429 and the line is unanchored. With 2-character words it anchors at exactly 0.6 to a partial window with conf 0.364 (flagged low_confidence). The apostrophe impact is overstated, though: two curly-quote contractions in a 6-word line still score 0.962, and a lone 'I’m' scores 0.8, so curly quotes rarely decide anchoring. The consequential part is that the aligned path does not work for scripts written without spaces. It is flagged honestly, hence medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Prioritise character-level tokenization for scripts without spaces, choosing it from the language or the script. Apostrophe and NFKC folding are cheap but low value. Normalisation changes alter outputs, so ship them under LYR-15's algorithm-version mechanism.

### C5. The parser is tested on one hand-picked format

#### TEST-3: The LRC parser test covers one hand-picked format; common real-world .lrc variants produce wrong lyrics.json

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:56-57`, `mrw/lyrics.py:244-270`, `mrw/lyrics.py:513-515`, `mrw/lyrics.py:369-371`, `mrw/ingest.py:38`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:199-222`, `PLAN.md:231-236`

**Evidence.** The only parser test, test_m4_smoke.py:199-222, uses `[Verse 1]`, `[00:12.50]text`, `[Weird Header]` and a plain line. In the parser, lyrics.py:249 `match = _LRC_TS.match(stripped)` reads only the first tag, L254 `text = _LRC_TS.sub("", stripped).strip()` removes all of them, and L257 `if _MARKUP.fullmatch(text):` classifies bracket-only text as markup. I ran the verbatim regexes from lyrics.py:56-57 and ingest.py:38 in a scratch script. (a) `[ti:Neon Reverie]`, `[ar:Some Artist]`, `[offset:+250]` and `[length: 03:30]` all come out as MARKUP with label "other", so they land in supplied_markup, which stage 6 is meant to consume as gold-standard structure evidence (PLAN.md:231-236, R-3). (b) `[00:12.50][01:30.00]Chorus line sung twice` gives one line with hint 12.5; the 1:30 repetition never becomes a line. (c) `[00:20:50]text` keeps '[00:20:50]' in the display text while ingest's `_LRC_TIMESTAMP` reports has_timestamps=True. (d) Enhanced-LRC `<00:25.40>word` tags stay in the text. (e) The file is decoded with `encoding="utf-8"`, not utf-8-sig (L513-515), and `'﻿[Verse 1]'.strip()` keeps the BOM. A BOM-prefixed `[Verse 1]` therefore becomes a sung line, counted in lines[] and in coverage, contrary to R-3; a BOM-prefixed first timestamp loses its hint and leaves U+FEFF in the text. Coverage counts only words inside lines (L369-371: `for line in lines for w in line.words`).

**Failure scenario.** Aligned mode with a typical downloaded .lrc that starts with [ti:]/[ar:]/[al:]/[by:] tags and writes its chorus as `[00:45.10][01:52.30]Chorus...`. lyrics.json gains four bogus 'other' supplied_markup entries at source lines 0–3, and the second chorus has no line. Whisper transcribed it, but coverage only counts line words, so that vocal region is listed in untranscribed_regions ('vocals here, content unknown') although the text is known. M6 also loses the repetition evidence for that chorus. A Windows-saved file (with BOM) that starts with [Verse 1] gets a fake unaligned lyric line.

**Proposal.** Parser changes: treat `[a-z]+:...` ID tags as metadata (apply `[offset:±ms]` to hints, drop the rest, never emit them as markup); expand multi-timestamp lines into one SuppliedLine per tag; accept `[mm:ss:xx]`; strip `<mm:ss.xx>` word tags; decode with utf-8-sig; share one timestamp regex between ingest.py and lyrics.py. Tests: a fast parametrized `parse_supplied_lyrics` table with one case per variant above, plus a fast aligned-document test with a stub engine asserting that a double-stamped chorus yields two lines and no untranscribed region over the second occurrence.

**Verifier's notes.** I re-implemented parse_supplied_lyrics (lyrics.py:244-270) with the verbatim regexes from lyrics.py:56-57 and ingest.py:38, extracted via ast (scratchpad lrc_check.py). Results:
- `[ti:..]`, `[ar:..]`, `[al:..]`, `[by:..]`, `[offset:+250]`, `[length: 03:30]` and `[re:..]` all become supplied_markup with label 'other'. The schema defines that field as section headers that feed structure labeling (lyrics.schema.json:84-96).
- `[00:12.50][01:30.00]Chorus...` produces a single line with hint 12.5. The second occurrence is lost, its words are dropped by align_lines, and its region lands in untranscribed_regions although the text is known.
- `[00:20:50]text` keeps the tag in the display text while ingest reports has_timestamps=True.
- `<mm:ss.xx>` word tags survive into the text.
- `'﻿'.isspace()` is False. With the utf-8 (not utf-8-sig) decode at lyrics.py:513-515, a BOM-prefixed `[Verse 1]` becomes a sung line. That line is unaligned and flagged, so it inflates lines_flagged_ratio. It adds no words, so vocal_activity_covered_ratio is unaffected; the evidence slightly overstates 'counted in coverage'.
ID-tag headers are close to universal in downloaded .lrc files. No doc or OQ records these variants, and only one hand-picked format is tested (test_m4_smoke.py:199-222).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Use an explicit whitelist of the standard LRC ID tags (ti, ar, al, au, by, re, ve, length, offset, tool, #) rather than a generic `[a-z]+:` pattern. The generic form would swallow real lowercase headers such as `[chorus: x2]` or `[verse: artist]`. Ignoring `offset` is acceptable, because hints are only -5/+20 s search windows (align.py:35-36). When expanding multi-timestamp lines, stable-sort the expanded SuppliedLines by hint before align_lines, so the cursor logic (align.py:153) sees timeline order. Decode with utf-8-sig in both lyrics.py:513 and ingest.py:148. Share one timestamp regex between the two modules and make it accept `[mm:ss:xx]`. Add the tests as proposed.

## D. Lyrics alignment

### D1. Greedy alignment misplaces repeated sections

#### LYR-1: Unhinted alignment takes the best match anywhere in a 400-word lookahead, so a cleaner later chorus captures earlier chorus lines and the rest of the song fails to align

- **Severity:** critical → high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:120-131`, `mrw/align.py:153`, `mrw/align.py:75`, `mrw/align.py:8-15`, `PLAN.md:228-230`

**Evidence.** For lines with no .lrc hint, align.py:120-121 searches `span = (cursor, min(len(words), cursor + LOOKAHEAD_WORDS))` and align.py:124-130 keeps the highest score: `if best is None or score > best[0]: best = (score, start, length)`. Nothing in the score accounts for distance from the cursor. A tie keeps the earliest window, but any later window that scores strictly higher wins. After the anchor, align.py:153 runs `cursor = max(cursor, start + length)`, so every word between the old cursor and the chosen window becomes unreachable for the remaining lines. `_score` uses `fuzz.token_sort_ratio` (align.py:75), which ignores word order. PLAN.md:228-230 promised 'fuzzy sequence alignment (rapidfuzz, Needleman-Wunsch style over normalized tokens)', but align.py:8 implements greedy 'sequential fuzzy anchoring'. No review or open question records how this behaves on repeated sections. I checked the numbers with my own re-implementation of the search, using an LCS-based equivalent of token_sort_ratio and no repo code. For the line 'Neon reverie, don't wake me up', the correct chorus-1 window as Whisper heard it ('neon river he don't wake me up') scores 0.881, well above min_anchor_score 0.6. The clean chorus-2 window scores 1.000, so it wins.

**Failure scenario.** Plain .txt lyrics with no hints, song structure V1 C1 V2 C2. Whisper mishears one word in C1 and transcribes C2 cleanly, about 60 words later and well inside the lookahead. In the simulation, C1 line 1 anchors at 110.0 s (C2's time) and C1 line 2 at 114 s, and the cursor jumps past all remaining words. The V2 and C2 lines become `unaligned`. `_interpolate_unanchored` then gives all of them the same start, prev_end = 116.75 s. After the timeline sort the document reads: V1 at 16 s; C1 at 110-117 s with confidence 1.0 and flags []; V2 and C2 stacked at 116.75 s. The audio actually sung at 46-52 s (C1) and 80-83 s (V2) is reported in untranscribed_regions as 'content unknown', although Whisper transcribed it. The mis-placed chorus lines carry full confidence and no flag. This happens whenever an earlier occurrence of a repeated line is transcribed less cleanly than a later one, which is common on sung material.

**Proposal.** Replace the per-line argmax with a global monotone alignment at line granularity, the Needleman-Wunsch/DTW approach PLAN §4 describes. Use dynamic programming over (line i, word index j): each line either anchors a window starting at or after the end of the previous anchored window (gain = score − τ) or is skipped (penalty). Precompute window scores with rapidfuzz `process.cdist` or a cached `_score`; the cost is about lines × words × 6, which is trivial. As a minimal interim patch: within the lookahead, pick the earliest window whose score is at least max(min_anchor_score, best − ε), with ε ≈ 0.1, or subtract a penalty proportional to (start − cursor). Also add `low_confidence` when an anchor skips more than N unconsumed words or T seconds of vocal activity. Add aligner unit tests with two chorus repetitions where repetition 1 has a one-word error, asserting that C1 lines anchor to repetition 1 and V2 still anchors.

**Verifier's notes.** I traced align.py:120-131 and :153. For unhinted lines the search is a global argmax over `span = (cursor, min(len(words), cursor + LOOKAHEAD_WORDS))` with a strict `score > best[0]`. The score has no distance term, and `cursor = max(cursor, start + length)` then makes every skipped word unreachable. I re-implemented align_lines myself, using an LCS-equivalent of rapidfuzz ratio/token_sort_ratio and no repo code, under scratchpad/lyr-verify/. It reproduces the scenario exactly. The misheard C1 window scores 0.881 at 46.0 s and the clean C2 window scores 1.0. Both C1 lines anchor at 110.0 and 114.0 s with conf 1.0 and flags []. The cursor then reaches the end of the stream, so the V2 and C2 lines get best=None and are all emitted unaligned at 116.9-120.9 s. Nothing records this as a decision: OQ-6's 'known weakness' covers only untranscribable passages, and PLAN.md:228-230 promised Needleman-Wunsch-style alignment. I downgrade from critical to high. This is wrong output on very common input (plain .txt lyrics with repeated choruses), but it is not silent corruption. The confidently emitted text/time pairs are true (that chorus text IS sung at 110 s). The collateral damage shows up as unaligned/timing_interpolated flags, a higher lines_flagged_ratio, and untranscribed_regions over 46-52 s and 80-86 s.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposed interim patch does not fix the finding's own example. 'Earliest window with score >= best − ε, ε≈0.1' gives a threshold of 1.0 − 0.1 = 0.9, which is above 0.881, so C2 still wins. Go straight to the monotone line-level DP: the gain for anchoring is score − τ, skipping has a penalty, consumed words are never reused, hints enter as a cost term, and ties break deterministically toward the earliest window. If an interim patch is needed, use a per-skipped-word penalty or ε ≥ 0.15, plus a check that the next supplied line also matches right after the candidate window. Add the two-chorus unit test as proposed.

### D2. Lines with timestamp hints ignore the cursor

#### LYR-2: .lrc-hinted lines ignore the cursor and break ties toward the earliest match: repeated lines reuse the same words even with perfect hints, and offset hints mis-anchor

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:111-119`, `mrw/align.py:125-130`, `mrw/align.py:35-36`, `mrw/align.py:150-153`, `mrw/lyrics.py:56`

**Evidence.** For hinted lines, align.py:111-119 searches only `lo_t <= w.start <= hi_t` with `lo_t = hint - HINT_BEFORE_SECONDS` (5.0) and `hi_t = hint + HINT_AFTER_SECONDS` (20.0). `cursor` is never consulted, and already-anchored words are not excluded. Ties keep the earliest window (`score > best[0]`). Window starts are limited to the candidate indices, but `length` in `range(max(1, k - 2), k + 4)` (align.py:125) can run past hi_t, so a window can span a long gap. There is no fallback to the cursor search when the hint window finds nothing, no estimate of a global hint offset, and `[offset:]` tags are never parsed: the `_LRC_TS` regex at lyrics.py:56 only matches numeric tags. Results from my own re-implementation: (a) 'Give it to me' sung at 40.0 s and 43.0 s, with exact hints 40.0 and 43.0. Line 2's window [38, 63] contains both occurrences, the tie goes to the earliest, and both lines anchor to 40.0-41.55 at score 1.0. (c) An .lrc synced to the album version, used with a music video whose song starts 25 s later. Verse 1 ends up unaligned. The chorus line anchors to a window that starts at verse 1's last word 'pane' (43.8 s) and ends at 73.35 s. Positional pairing gives per-word confidences 0.5, 0.182, 0.0, 1, 1, 1, a line confidence of 0.614, and no flags.

**Failure scenario.** (1) A hook line repeated back-to-back, a few seconds apart, in a correctly synced .lrc: the second line silently gets the first occurrence's timing at confidence 1.0. The second sung occurrence then counts as uncovered and can appear in untranscribed_regions. Word times across the document are no longer monotone, which violates the PLAN §11 M4 criterion. (2) The project's own target input is a music video plus album-synced lyrics. Hints that are more than 5 s late leave the true occurrence outside the window. Hints that are 5-20 s early let earlier repeated lines win. Hints more than 20 s early produce unaligned lines or 30-second lines that span the gap, unflagged, as in scenario (c).

**Proposal.** (1) For hinted lines, never reuse words already consumed by another anchored line, and prefer start ≥ cursor; allow earlier starts only when the hint is itself earlier than the previous anchor, i.e. a genuinely out-of-order file. (2) Break ties and near-ties by |words[start].start − hint|. (3) Require the whole window to lie inside [lo_t, hi_t], and reject windows with an internal inter-word gap larger than about 3 s. (4) Estimate a global hint offset before the hinted pass: take the median of (anchor − hint) over confident anchors of unique lines from an unhinted pass, or grid-search an offset, and also apply `[offset:]` (see LYR-7). (5) Fall back to the cursor search when the hint window yields nothing at or above min_anchor_score. (6) Tests: identical consecutive lines with exact hints must anchor to distinct occurrences; shifting all hints by −25 s or +10 s must give the same anchors as exact hints.

**Verifier's notes.** align.py:111-119 builds the hinted span from candidates within [hint−5, hint+20]. It never consults `cursor` or excludes words already anchored, ties keep the earliest window (`>` at :129), and `length` (range at :125) can run past hi_t. When nothing reaches min_anchor_score there is no fallback. My re-implementation shows all three effects. (a) With exact hints 40.0 and 43.0 on back-to-back 'Give it to me', both lines anchor to 40.0-41.9 at conf 1.0 with flags []. The same lines without hints anchor correctly (40.0 and 43.0), so hints make the result worse. (c) With a +25 s video offset, the verse lines come out unaligned at their pre-intro hint times. The chorus line anchors to 48.0-73.9 s because its window starts at the previous line's last word. Its per-word confidences are 0.5, 0.182, 0.0, 1, 1, 1, giving line conf 0.614 with flags []. The next line is unaligned at score 0.411. OQ-6 expects hints to be 'frequently offset', but the code tolerates only −5/+20 s, and the repeated-line collapse is not recorded anywhere.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound, with three refinements. (1) Apply the consumed-word exclusion and the long-gap rule to unhinted windows too: the same window overrun happens there. (2) Do not hard-reject any internal gap over 3 s. Sustained notes and short breaks can legitimately produce gaps, so use a larger cap (for example 8 s) or a flag. (3) Estimate the global hint offset from the LYR-1 DP: the median of (anchor − hint) over unique, confidently anchored lines.

### D3. Aligned mode drops unmatched transcript words

#### CRIT-1: Aligned mode drops every transcribed word that doesn't anchor to a supplied line, so abbreviated or incomplete lyric files lose whole sections and report them as untranscribed

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, C2, T1
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:508`, `mrw/lyrics.py:512-536`, `mrw/lyrics.py:568-572`, `mrw/lyrics.py:369-371`, `mrw/lyrics.py:57`, `mrw/lyrics.py:257-268`, `mrw/align.py:132-165`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:5`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:111`, `PLAN.md:325-328`

**Evidence.** The transcript is used only as alignment input. mrw/lyrics.py:508 `all_words = [w for seg in segments for w in seg.words]` is passed to lyrics.py:518-520 `anchored = align_lines(supplied_lines, all_words, cfg.lyrics.min_anchor_score)`. Aligned-mode lines are then built only from `for a in anchored:` (lyrics.py:521-536), one per supplied line. Every Whisper word that no supplied line anchors to is discarded and appears nowhere in the document.

Coverage counts only words inside those lines: lyrics.py:369-371 `spans = sorted((w.start_seconds, w.end_seconds) for line in lines for w in line.words)`, called at 568-572. A passage Whisper did transcribe but the file omitted is therefore emitted in untranscribed_regions. The schema defines that field as 'sung passages the system could not read' (lyrics.schema.json:5) and tells LLM consumers to say 'vocals here, content unknown' (:111).

parse_supplied_lyrics has no concept of repeats: only a line that is entirely '[...]' is markup (lyrics.py:57 `_MARKUP`, :257 `if _MARKUP.fullmatch(text):`). I applied the repo's own regexes (read as data via ast; script /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/abbrev_lyrics.py) to a typical abbreviated .txt. The second '[Chorus]' became a markup entry with no lines; '(Repeat chorus x2)' and 'Chorus:' became sung lines to align.

PLAN.md:325-328 makes repeated lyric lines the M6 chorus evidence. LYR-5 covers the compressed-LRC form (multi-timestamp lines). This finding is the general mechanism: the .txt forms (bare repeated headers, 'Repeat chorus', 'x2', omitted ad-libs and backing vocals) and the wrong diagnosis in the coverage ledger.

**Failure scenario.** A .txt file reads '[Verse 1] ... [Chorus] Hold on, hold on tonight / Hold on until the light ... [Verse 2] ... [Chorus] (Repeat chorus x2)'. Whisper transcribes all three chorus passes.
- lines[] holds the chorus text once, anchored to whichever pass scores best (cf. LYR-1).
- The other two passes' words are dropped. Their vocal-activity regions are listed in untranscribed_regions, and vocal_activity_covered_ratio falls (for example from about 0.95 to about 0.6).
- '(Repeat chorus x2)' is emitted as an 'unaligned' line with interpolated timing.
- M6 sees the chorus once and gets no repetition evidence.

Ad-libs and backing vocals, which lyric files usually leave out, get the same treatment: they are reported as unreadable singing even though Whisper read them.

**Proposal.** 1. After align_lines, collect the transcript words that no anchored line consumed.
   - Count them toward coverage, so untranscribed_regions keeps its 'could not read' meaning.
   - Emit them either as extra lines carrying a new additive flag (e.g. 'not_in_supplied_lyrics') or in a separate 'unmatched_transcript' array (lyrics schema 1.2.0, additive).
2. In parse_supplied_lyrics, recognise repeat directives and expand them by copying the earlier labelled block before alignment. Cover: a bare '[Chorus]' or '[Chorus xN]' header with no lines before the next header, '(Repeat chorus)', 'Chorus 2x', and 'Label:' lines. Record provenance, e.g. 'repeat_of_line_index'.
3. Add stubbed-engine tests:
   - An abbreviated .txt whose transcript contains three chorus passes must align all three, with no untranscribed regions.
   - Ad-libs left out of the file must be reported as unmatched, not as untranscribed.

**Verifier's notes.** I traced the code path. In aligned mode, lines are built only from align_lines output, one per supplied line (mrw/lyrics.py:518-536). Any Whisper word in all_words (lyrics.py:508) that no anchor consumes is dropped. Coverage and untranscribed_regions use only the words inside lines (lyrics.py:369-371, called at :568-572).

I read the repo's _MARKUP, _LRC_TS and _LABELS regexes as data via ast and ran them on an abbreviated file (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit-verify/scripts/parse_check.py):
- The second '[Chorus]' becomes markup with no lines.
- '[Chorus x2]' becomes markup with label chorus; the x2 is lost.
- '(Repeat chorus x2)', 'Chorus:' and 'Chorus 2x' all become sung lines. They cannot anchor, so they come out as unaligned with interpolated timing (align.py:154-165, :175-198).

This is not recorded anywhere. OQ-6's 'known weakness' covers the reverse direction: supplied lines with no transcript.

Downgraded from high to medium. The document still meets its own operational definition: untranscribed = vocal activity minus word-covered spans (lyrics.schema.json:111). The directive line carries unaligned, low_confidence and timing_interpolated, and nothing is fabricated. The harm is:
- lost timing for repeats and ad-libs;
- a misleading 'could not read' diagnosis and an understated coverage ratio;
- lost repetition evidence for M6, which is not built yet.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** 1. Keep lines[] equal to the supplied lines. Add an additive array (e.g. unmatched_words, lyrics 1.2.0) holding the Whisper words that no anchor consumed, and count them in coverage so untranscribed_regions keeps its 'could not read' meaning.
2. In parse_supplied_lyrics, classify whole-line repeat directives as markup with a repeat count, never as sung lines: bracketed or parenthesised 'repeat …', '<label> xN' / '<label> Nx', or a lone '<label>:', using the _LABELS vocabulary only so ordinary lyrics with colons are not caught.
3. Many files simply omit repeats with no directive at all. So after sequential anchoring, add a repeat-aware pass: re-try already-anchored labelled blocks against transcript spans nothing consumed, and emit matches as extra lines with provenance (e.g. repeat_of_line_index, additive).
4. Add stubbed-engine tests for directive, bare-header and omitted-repeat files.

### D4. Word timings assigned by position

#### LYR-8: _pair_words maps supplied words to Whisper words by position, inventing word timings without the timing_interpolated flag

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:78-93`, `mrw/align.py:139-149`, `mrw/align.py:162`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:62`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:66`

**Evidence.** align.py:89 computes `j = 0 if n_sup == 1 else round(i * (n_obs - 1) / (n_sup - 1))`. That mapping is purely positional, and each word's confidence is just `fuzz.ratio` against its positional partner. Anchored lines are created with `flags=[]` (align.py:146). The schema defines `timing_interpolated: word times inferred from neighbors rather than observed` (lyrics.schema.json:62), but the code sets it only on unanchored lines (align.py:162), which have no words. My own simulation: supplied 'I really really love you' against Whisper 'i really love you' picks a 4-word window at score 0.829. The second 'really' then gets the observed timing of 'love' (10.8 s, confidence 0.2) and 'love' gets the same 10.8 s span. Line confidence is 0.84, so no flag fires. If Whisper drops a leading word ('oh'), every following supplied word shifts to a later observed word's timing.

**Failure scenario.** Whisper's word count on a sung line often differs from the supplied text: it drops, merges or inserts words. Each time, aligned-mode words[] carries timings that look observed but are positional guesses. Two words share an identical span, or a word carries its neighbour's time. The line is unflagged whenever the mean confidence stays above 0.5. A karaoke-style renderer would highlight the wrong word, and the align.py docstring's promise to 'never fake word-level precision' is broken for anchored lines. Per-word confidence partly reveals the problem, but the line flag that exists for exactly this case is never set.

**Proposal.** Align tokens within the window by content rather than position: use rapidfuzz.distance.Levenshtein.opcodes or difflib.SequenceMatcher over the normalized token lists, treating tokens with ratio ≥ 0.8 as equal. Matched supplied tokens take their observed word's timing. Unmatched supplied tokens get times interpolated between matched neighbours (split by character share), and the line gets `timing_interpolated`. Never let two supplied words share one observed span without that flag. Add unit tests for dropped, merged and inserted words.

**Verifier's notes.** align.py:89 maps words by position: `j = round(i * (n_obs - 1) / (n_sup - 1))`. Anchored lines are built with `flags=[]` (:146), and timing_interpolated is set only for unanchored lines (:162). In my re-implementation, supplied 'I really really love you' against heard 'i really love you' gives window score 0.829. The second 'really' receives 'love''s span (10.8-11.2, conf 0.2), 'love' gets the same span, and the line has conf 0.84 with flags []. The _pair_words docstring documents positional mapping as the design, but no review weighs its honesty consequences against the schema's timing_interpolated definition (lyrics.schema.json:62), so the argument is new. I keep it at medium rather than higher because the per-word confidence already partly reveals the mispairing.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Because this changes output under an unchanged config, ship it under the algorithm-version mechanism proposed in LYR-15.

### D5. Unanchored lines stack and run past the end of the audio

#### LYR-11: Unanchored-line interpolation stacks consecutive lines on one span, is not clamped to track duration, and puts every line at 0-4 s when there are no anchors

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:175-198`, `mrw/lyrics.py:496-506`, `mrw/align.py:158`

**Evidence.** In align.py:182-197, every unanchored line without a hint gets `start = prev_end`, the same previous anchored end for each one, and `end = min(next_start, start + 10.0)` or `start + 4.0`. Hinted lines take `start = line.start` straight from the file. Nothing clamps these values to the track duration; run_lyrics clamps only Whisper segment and word times (lyrics.py:496-506, 'timeline validity is ours', PR #9). With zero observed words, every unhinted line gets [0.0, 4.0]. Review 007 says interpolation 'bounds its guesses', but that applies to the length of the guess, not to the timeline.

**Failure scenario.** (1) Three consecutive lines Whisper missed, between anchors at 10 s and 40 s, are all emitted at [10, 20]. (2) An .lrc for a longer album version used with a shorter radio edit or video: trailing lines are hinted past the end of the audio and emitted with start_seconds > duration_seconds, contrary to the stage's own timeline-validity policy. (3) Whisper yields no words (heavy vocoder, wrong pinned language): a 40-line document in which every line spans 0.000-4.000 s. This degenerate case has no documented convention (H2).

**Proposal.** Spread k consecutive unanchored lines evenly, by line index or character count, across [prev_end, next_start], with a per-line cap. Clamp interpolated and hinted start and end to [0, duration], either by passing duration into align_lines or by clamping in run_lyrics as is done for Whisper times. Define and document the zero-anchor convention in the schema, e.g. hints only, otherwise spread over vocal-activity regions, and test it.

**Verifier's notes.** _interpolate_unanchored (align.py:182-197) looks only at anchored neighbours. Every run of unhinted unanchored lines therefore gets the same [prev_end, min(next_start, prev_end + 10)] span: in my simulation, three lines all land at 9.6-19.6 s.
