With no words at all, every line gets 0.0-4.0 s. Hinted trailing lines are used as-is with no clamp: on a 180 s track they were emitted at 195-199 and 200-204 s. The [0, duration] clamp at lyrics.py:500-506 applies only to Whisper times, which contradicts the stage's own 'timeline validity is ours' policy. Review 007's 'bounds its guesses' covers the length of each guess, not stacking or the timeline domain. Every affected line carries unaligned and timing_interpolated, which keeps this at medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Clamp inside align_lines by passing duration in, so the pure unit tests also cover it. Give stacked lines distinct, ordered sub-spans of the gap, and state the zero-anchor convention in the schema (H2).

#### SCH-3: Documents can contain times outside [0, duration]: unaligned lyric lines and final vocal regions run past the end of the timeline

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, C1, C2, T1
- **Locations:** `mrw/align.py:186-198`, `mrw/lyrics.py:496-506`, `mrw/lyrics.py:521-534`, `mrw/features.py:252-253`, `mrw/features.py:264-268`, `schemas/common.schema.json:20-24`, `schemas/README.md:63-72`, `PLAN.md:525`

**Evidence.** lyrics.py:496-499 states the policy: 'Times outside the unified timeline domain are clamped to [0, duration]; content honesty is the flags' job, timeline validity is ours (PR #9)'. But it clamps only segments and words BEFORE alignment (lyrics.py:500-506). Unanchored lines get their span afterwards in align.py:190-198: `if line.start >= 0.0:  # had an .lrc hint — keep it as the start` / `start = line.start` ... `end = min(next_start, start + 10.0)`, otherwise `end = start + 4.0`. lyrics.py:527-528 then writes `start_seconds=canonical.round_seconds(a.start)` and `end_seconds=canonical.round_seconds(a.end)` without clamping. In features, a region still active at the last frame is closed with `spans.append([start, len(vocal_rms_db)])` (features.py:253) and written as `end_seconds=canonical.round_seconds(e * HOP_SECONDS)` (267). I assume librosa 0.11's center=True framing gives len(values) = 1 + floor(n_samples/441) (high confidence), so that end = duration + (0, 0.01]. The schema never states an upper bound: common.schema.json `seconds` has only `minimum: 0`. The series-length convention is also documented wrongly. README.md:71 says '4-minute track ⇒ 24,250 points' for 242.5 s, but the actual count is 24,251. PLAN.md:525's smoke criterion 'RMS series length = ⌈duration × 100⌉' is one short whenever duration×100 is a whole number (as for the 20.0 s and 8.0 s fixtures), and no test checks the length.

**Failure scenario.** Take a music video (the primary input) whose audio is the shorter 4:02 video edit, with the album .lrc whose last lines carry hints [04:08.00]-[04:18.00]. No Whisper words fall in those hint windows, so the lines stay unanchored. lyrics.json then contains lines with start_seconds 248.0-258.0 and end_seconds up to 262.0, against audio_features.duration_seconds 242.5. A renderer scheduling lines by start_seconds, or Swift code indexing the 100 Hz series at that time, runs past the data. Separately, a 20.0 s clip with vocals up to the final frame gets a vocal_activity region ending at 20.01 s. lyrics copies that region verbatim into untranscribed_regions when no words cover it.

**Proposal.** In lyrics.py, clamp after alignment and before building LyricsLine: `a.start = min(max(a.start, 0.0), duration)` and `a.end = min(max(a.end, a.start), duration)`. Alternatively pass duration into _interpolate_unanchored and drop or clamp hints beyond duration when parsing. In features.py, clamp region ends to the sample-accurate duration: `round_seconds(min(e * HOP_SECONDS, len(mix_mono) / SAMPLE_RATE))`. State the invariant in common.schema.json `seconds` and the README: every time lies in [0, audio_features.duration_seconds], and for video in [0, video_span.end_seconds]. Also document the exact series length, `len(values) == floor(n_samples / 441) + 1`. Fix README.md:71 and PLAN.md:525. Add a cross-document invariant check to the M3/M4 smoke tests.

**Verifier's notes.** align.py:190-197 keeps an unclamped hint as start and sets `end = min(next_start, start + 10.0)` or `start + 4.0`. lyrics.py:527-528 writes `round_seconds(a.start/a.end)` with no clamp. The clamp at lyrics.py:500-506 runs only on segments and words before alignment. A more common trigger than the finding's hint case: a trailing unaligned line with no hint (any .txt file) gets start = prev_end and end = prev_end + 4.0. An anchored line ending at 241.0 on a 242.5 s track therefore yields end_seconds 245.0. Outro ad-libs are exactly the lines likely to be unaligned. This breaks the stage's own declared invariant (lyrics.py:496-499: 'clamped to [0, duration] ... timeline validity is ours'), which test_m4_smoke.py:482 asserts for the transcribed path only. Review 007 checked only the lower bound ('floors at 0.0'). For features: librosa 0.11 rms pads frame_length//2 on both sides (spectral.py text read), giving len = 1 + n//441. A region still open at the end is closed at index len (features.py:253), so end = duration + (0, 0.01]: a 20.0 s clip has 2001 frames and end 20.01. README.md:71 (24,250 vs 24,251) and PLAN.md:525 (⌈dur×100⌉ = 2000 vs 2001) are off by one, and no test checks the length.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Clamp after align_lines returns: `a.start = min(max(a.start, 0.0), duration)` and `a.end = min(max(a.end, a.start), duration)`. The clamp is monotone, so the timeline sort still holds. Pass the duration into _vocal_activity and clamp `e * HOP_SECONDS` to `len(mix_mono) / SAMPLE_RATE`. This changes outputs for regions open at the end without a config-hash event; note that in the PR. Document the invariants and the exact series length `floor(n_samples/441) + 1`, fix README.md:71 and PLAN.md:525, and add the cross-document bounds assertion to the M3 and M4 smoke tests.

### D6. Short regions vanish from untranscribed_regions

#### CRIT-5: untranscribed_regions drops short vocal regions that contain only a zero-length (or tiny) word, breaking the guarantee that a zero-coverage region is always emitted

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1, H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:341-360`, `mrw/lyrics.py:501-506`, `mrw/lyrics.py:379-385`, `mrw/features.py:252-253`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:111`

**Evidence.** mrw/lyrics.py:357 `whole_region_uncovered = len(gaps) == 1 and gaps[0] == (r_start, r_end)` and :358-360 `if whole_region_uncovered or e - s >= min_seconds:`. A region is emitted whole only when no word span touches it at all. Any touching span, even one with start == end, splits the region into fragments, and each fragment must then pass the 1.0 s minimum.

Zero-length words do reach this code. The clamp at lyrics.py:505-506 (`w.start = min(max(w.start, 0.0), duration)`; `w.end = min(max(w.end, w.start), duration)`) collapses past-the-end hallucinations, which review 007 observed on CI, to (duration, duration). A vocal region that is active at the end of the track also extends past duration (features.py:252-253, FEAT-7).

Simulation with my own re-implementation (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/uncovered_sim.py):
- Region (10.0, 11.5), no words: untranscribed = [(10.0, 11.5)].
- Same region with one word (10.7, 10.7): covered 0.000 s, untranscribed = [].
- Region (14.6, 15.01) with a clamped word at 15.0: untranscribed = [].

The schema promises 'A region with zero word coverage is always emitted whole regardless of length' (lyrics.schema.json:111). Assumption (medium confidence): faster-whisper's DTW word timing can also emit words with start == end without any clamping.

**Failure scenario.** A 1.5 s backing-vocal 'ooh' region contains one zero-length word 'you' from Whisper. Coverage counts 0 s for the region, but untranscribed_regions omits it. The honesty ledger and the coverage ratio then disagree, and the LLM consumer hears nothing about that singing. If the word lasts 20 ms instead, the region is 98.7% uncovered and still vanishes.

**Proposal.** - Drop spans with end <= start before merging (lyrics.py:336 and :369).
- Decide 'whole region uncovered' from the covered length (e.g. covered < one hop, or covered/len below a small fraction) instead of tuple equality.
- Consider emitting every fragment of a region whose covered fraction is below about 0.5.
- Add these cases to the existing pure uncovered_spans tests.

**Verifier's notes.** I checked uncovered_spans (lyrics.py:324-361) with my own re-implementation (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit-verify/scripts/uncovered_reimpl.py):
- Region (10.0, 11.5) with word (10.7, 10.7): covered 0.000 s, untranscribed = [].
- Region (14.6, 15.01) with word (15.0, 15.0): untranscribed = [].

The whole-region test at :357 fails because the zero-length span splits the gaps.

Zero-length words are reachable in two ways:
- The clamp at :505-506 turns any word past the end into (duration, duration). Regions active at the end always end past duration (features.py:252-253: (1+floor(n/441))/100 > n/44100).
- faster-whisper 1.2.1's add_word_timestamps itself drops zero durations before taking the median (word_durations[word_durations.nonzero()]), so Whisper does emit such words.

This breaks the explicit guarantee in lyrics.schema.json:111, the same guarantee PR #10 [major S2] was about. That is a new argument, not a duplicate. Only regions shorter than 2 × uncovered_min_seconds are affected.

The 20 ms-word sub-case is not a bug: filtering fragments of partially covered regions is the recorded PR #10 design, so the broader 'covered fraction' proposal would change a decision.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - In uncovered_spans (lyrics.py:336), drop spans with end - start <= 0 before merging (optionally also spans shorter than one 10 ms hop). Coverage is unchanged, since these spans contribute 0.
- Add the interior zero-length case and the clamped-at-duration case to test_uncovered_spans_localize_partial_misses.
- Treat any change for tiny but non-zero words as a separate decision, recorded against the PR #10 resolution in 007.

### D7. No source_line_index on aligned lines

#### LYR-9: Aligned lines[] carry no source_line_index, so supplied_markup headers cannot be tied to timed lines (contract gap for M6)

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** C1, C2, S2, S5
- **Locations:** `mrw/models.py:254-260`, `mrw/lyrics.py:521-536`, `mrw/align.py:50-53`, `mrw/align.py:168-171`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:84-107`, `PLAN.md:330-332`

**Evidence.** SuppliedLine has `source_line_index` (align.py:50-53), but run_lyrics drops it when it builds LyricsLine (lyrics.py:521-536), and the LyricsLine model and schema (models.py:254-260) have no such field. Markup entries carry `source_line_index` and only optionally `hint_seconds`, which is present only for timestamped .lrc headers (lyrics.schema.json:102-105). lines[] is re-sorted by time (align.py:168-171), so file order is not guaranteed. PLAN.md:330-332 makes supplied_markup the dominant evidence for section labels. Review 001 R-6 fixed the same kind of positional-linkage gap in structure.json by adding per_section[].section_index.

**Failure scenario.** Take a Genius-style .txt with [Verse 1] and [Chorus] headers and no timestamps, the most common way section markup arrives. supplied_markup holds entries like {text:'[Chorus]', label:'chorus', source_line_index:10} with no time, and lines[] has no file index. M6 cannot tell which timed lines follow which header. Matching by text is ambiguous exactly for choruses, whose lines repeat. Re-parsing lyrics_input.txt would duplicate the parser and break after the timeline re-sort or the multi-tag expansion proposed in LYR-5.

**Proposal.** Add an optional `source_line_index` (integer ≥ 0) to lines[] items in aligned mode, as an additive change to lyrics schema 1.2.0. Update the model in field-order lockstep, the worked example and the tests. Optionally add a derived `start_seconds` to each markup entry, taken from the first anchored line that follows it. Document the linkage in the supplied_markup description.

**Verifier's notes.** SuppliedLine carries source_line_index (align.py:50-53), but run_lyrics drops it when building LyricsLine (lyrics.py:521-536). Neither the model (models.py:254-260) nor the schema's lines[] items has a file index, and lines[] is re-sorted by time (align.py:168-171). Markup entries have hint_seconds only when the header itself was timestamped (lyrics.schema.json:102-105). Review 001 R-3 asked for headers 'with their positions', but those positions use a numbering that lyrics.json never exposes for lines. For the common untimed .txt case the header-to-line linkage therefore cannot be recovered from the document: an LLM consumer cannot do it at all. M6 could only re-parse the file and assume file order equals time order, which LYR-1 and LYR-2 show is not guaranteed. The fix is cheap and additive, and should land before M6.

### D8. Calibration and example drift

#### CRIT-10: The overlapping_vocals flag is an uncalibrated absolute-loudness test (vocal stem above -15 dBFS for 80% of a line) that no test exercises

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:62`, `mrw/lyrics.py:60-62`, `mrw/lyrics.py:273-287`, `mrw/lyrics.py:311-312`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:62`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:107-112`, `docs/reviews/007-m4-lyrics.md:193-196`

**Evidence.** The flag fires when at least 80% of a line's vocal-stem frames exceed -15 dBFS:
- mrw/config.py:62 `overlap_rms_db: float = -15.0  # overlapping_vocals: sustained loud stem`
- lyrics.py:62 `_OVERLAP_FRACTION = 0.8`
- lyrics.py:311-312 applies the test.

The schema describes the flag as 'sustained dense vocal-stem energy suggests harmonies; words may be merged' (lyrics.schema.json:62). Its only test mention keeps fixtures below the threshold so it never fires (tests/test_m4_smoke.py:108-112); no test asserts that it fires.

Review 007's field data puts vocal regions around a -32 dB mean (007-m4-lyrics.md:193-196) and attributes the flagged ratios to possibly_non_lexical. On those tracks the flag almost certainly never fired. An absolute dBFS threshold moves with master loudness and with downmix (CRIT-6), not with the number of voices.

**Failure scenario.** - Track A: three-part stacked harmonies mixed at -22 dBFS RMS get no overlapping_vocals flag, although Whisper merges their words.
- Track B: a single compressed lead in a -7 LUFS master sits at -13 dBFS. Every line is flagged overlapping_vocals, which inflates lines_flagged_ratio and tells the consumer there are harmonies where there are none.

**Proposal.** Calibrate the flag the way review 007 calibrated possibly_non_lexical. Either:
- make the level test relative to the track's own vocal-stem level (e.g. frames above its 90th-percentile active RMS) and combine it with a signal tied to multiple voices, such as duplicate or overlapping word timings within the line, or spectral spread of the vocal stem; or
- until it is calibrated, describe it honestly in the schema ('loud sustained vocal stem') and record its calibration status in OPEN_QUESTIONS.

Add one test where it fires and one where it does not.

**Verifier's notes.** I verified the threshold settings and their use: config.py:62 (−15 dBFS), lyrics.py:62 (0.8), applied at lyrics.py:311-312. The tests only keep the flag from firing (the _bursty_vocals docstring at tests/test_m4_smoke.py:107-112; the aligned test asserts flags == []), and no test makes it fire.

Review 007's field data has vocal regions at about −32 dB mean (007-m4-lyrics.md:192-196), so on typical material the flag effectively never fires. A heavily compressed lead in a very loud master could sit above −15 dBFS for most of a line and trigger it with a single voice.

Neither OQ-5 (about the vocal-activity thresholds) nor 007 finding 5 (about possibly_non_lexical) covers this. The premise is accurate and the cheap part of the fix is proportionate. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do the S-sized part now:
- Add one stubbed-features test in which the flag fires.
- Reword the schema description to say what is actually measured: 'vocal stem above overlap_rms_db for ≥80% of the line — an uncalibrated loudness proxy for stacked voices'.
- Record its calibration status next to 007 finding 5.

Defer a relative threshold or a multi-voice signal to the same multi-track calibration pass as possibly_non_lexical.

#### LYR-14: The normative lyrics example disagrees with the implementation: line confidence is not the mean of word confidences, and an unaligned line shows a confidence

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** docs · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S5, C1
- **Locations:** `schemas/examples/lyrics.json:18`, `schemas/examples/lyrics.json:36`, `schemas/examples/lyrics.json:52-54`, `mrw/align.py:161`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:44-47`

**Evidence.** The example validates against the schema (checked with jsonschema), but: line 1 has `"confidence": 0.912` while its word confidences average 0.914; line 2 has `0.641` while they average 0.64; the unaligned bridge line has `"confidence": 0.18` with `"words": []`. The implementation omits confidence for unanchored lines (`confidence=None` at align.py:161, dropped by exclude_none), and the schema defines confidence as 'Mean word confidence.' (lyrics.schema.json:44-47). Review 002 declared the worked examples normative.

**Failure scenario.** A Swift consumer modelled on the normative example declares `confidence: Double` as non-optional and then fails to decode every real aligned document that contains an unaligned line. An LLM reader is led to believe unaligned lines carry an anchoring score.

**Proposal.** Correct the two numbers (0.914 and 0.64) and remove `confidence` from the unaligned example line, or document a different meaning for it in the schema. State in the schema that confidence is absent when words is empty. Add an example-consistency test (line confidence equals mean word confidence to within 0.001).

**Verifier's notes.** I computed the means from schemas/examples/lyrics.json. Line 1's word confidences average 0.91375, which rounds to 0.914, but the example says 0.912. Line 2 averages 0.64, but the example says 0.641. The unaligned bridge line shows confidence 0.18 with words [], whereas align.py:161 emits None, which exclude_none drops. The example passes jsonschema validation, but it breaks S5's internal-consistency rule, and review 002 declared the examples normative. The Swift-decoding risk is weak because the schema already marks confidence as optional, hence low.

## E. Offline guarantee and external services

### E1. Whisper contacts Hugging Face on every run, revision unpinned

#### LYR-3: Whisper model is looked up online on every run with an unpinned revision: a network call outside the permitted exceptions, and a risk of silent model drift

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:160-162`, `mrw/lyrics.py:227-231`, `mrw/config.py:51`, `mrw/lyrics.py:577-581`, `CLAUDE.md:13-18`

**Evidence.** lyrics.py:160-162 creates the model with `WhisperModel(cfg.model, device="cpu", compute_type="int8", cpu_threads=1, num_workers=1)`, passing no `local_files_only`, `revision` or `download_root`. `fetch_whisper_model` (lyrics.py:227-231) calls `download_model(model_name)`, also unpinned. The only model identity recorded anywhere is `engine.model = "small"` (lyrics.py:579), plus tool_version 'mrw 0.1.0'. CLAUDE.md:13-18 permits only first-use weight downloads, so that batch runs 'never surprise-download'. Assumed library behaviour (high confidence) for faster-whisper 1.2.1 and huggingface-hub 1.23.0, the versions in uv.lock: a size name is resolved through `download_model` → `huggingface_hub.snapshot_download('Systran/faster-whisper-small', revision='main', local_files_only=False)`. When online, that makes a Hub API `repo_info` call to resolve main's current commit on every call, and downloads any files missing for that commit. Only when offline, or with HF_HUB_OFFLINE set, does it fall back to the cached snapshot. By contrast, the Demucs weights in stems.py come from torch.hub URLs that embed a content hash, so they are pinned and reused offline.

**Failure scenario.** (1) Every `mrw lyrics` run makes an HTTPS request to huggingface.co, which is not first use. (2) If the upstream repo's `main` ref ever moves (a re-conversion, or a tokenizer or config update), the next run in a batch silently downloads about 480 MB and transcribes with different weights. lyrics.json then changes while config_hash, engine.model and tool_version all stay the same, so the D1 break cannot even be seen in the manifest. Two machines that fetched at different times produce different documents. (3) Lower confidence: behind a proxy that returns an error instead of refusing the connection, the stage may fail with status `failed` (exit 1) even though the model is cached, depending on which exceptions huggingface_hub treats as offline.

**Proposal.** Add `LyricsConfig.model_revision`, defaulting to a pinned commit SHA for `small`. It joins the config_hash, so this is a deliberate mass-stale. Pass `revision=` to `download_model` in fetch and to `WhisperModel`. In `_transcribe`, construct the model with `local_files_only=True`. Check for the cached model before any heavy work (e.g. `snapshot_download(..., local_files_only=True)` or `huggingface_hub.try_to_load_from_cache`) and raise PrerequisiteError('run `mrw models fetch`', exit 2) on a miss. Record the resolved snapshot commit and the faster-whisper and ctranslate2 versions in the manifest run metadata (D2). Run the slow lyrics tests with HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 after a fetch step to prove offline operation.

**Verifier's notes.** I read the upstream sources for the uv.lock versions. In faster-whisper v1.2.1, WhisperModel.__init__ defaults to local_files_only=False and revision=None and calls download_model(...), which calls huggingface_hub.snapshot_download(repo_id, local_files_only=False, revision=None) with no fallback of its own. In huggingface_hub v1.23.0, snapshot_download sets revision to 'main' and calls `api.repo_info(...)` whenever local_files_only is False. It fetches the tree and files for any new commit. It re-raises `httpx.ProxyError`, so a blocking proxy fails the stage even with a warm cache. It falls back to the cache only on ConnectError, Timeout or OfflineModeIsEnabled. mrw passes neither option (lyrics.py:160-162, 227-231), nothing sets HF_HUB_OFFLINE, and only engine.model='small' is recorded. README:39-41 and CLAUDE.md promise that `mrw models fetch` prevents surprise downloads. Nothing records this. I rate it high (a missing safety net for the offline and determinism constraints) rather than critical: no media or content is acquired, and a truly offline machine still runs from the cache.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Pin per model, not with one default. A SHA pinned for `small` is invalid if a user sets model = 'medium'. Keep a code table {model_name: commit_sha} that is part of the lyrics hash, plus an optional config override. In _transcribe, resolve the directory with download_model(name, local_files_only=True, revision=pinned) and pass that path to WhisperModel; this skips the Hub call entirely. On a cache miss, decide explicitly: either raise PrerequisiteError (exit 2, 'run mrw models fetch'), or perform the first-use download, which CLAUDE.md permits. `models fetch` must use the same pinned revision. Record the resolved commit, plus faster-whisper/ctranslate2 versions, in manifest run metadata, and run the slow lyrics tests with HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1.

#### SEC-2: Every `mrw lyrics` run calls huggingface.co and silently follows upstream Whisper model revisions

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:160`, `mrw/lyrics.py:227`, `mrw/lyrics.py:577`, `README.md:40`

**Evidence.** `_transcribe` builds `WhisperModel(cfg.model, device='cpu', compute_type='int8', cpu_threads=1, num_workers=1)` (lyrics.py:160-162) with no `local_files_only` and no `revision`. `fetch_whisper_model` calls `download_model(model_name)` without a revision either (227-231). The engine block records only `model=cfg.lyrics.model`, i.e. 'small' (577-580). I read the third-party sources at the locked versions. faster-whisper 1.2.1: `WhisperModel.__init__(..., local_files_only: bool = False, ...)` (transcribe.py:630) calls `download_model(...)` (681), which calls `huggingface_hub.snapshot_download(repo_id, local_files_only=..., revision=None)` (utils.py:100-116). huggingface_hub 1.23.0, `_snapshot_download.py:254-258`: `if not local_files_only: ... repo_info = api.repo_info(repo_id=repo_id, repo_type=repo_type, revision=revision)`. It then sets `commit_hash = repo_info.sha` and fetches that commit's files (372-373). Also, `except httpx.ProxyError: raise` (259-261) bypasses the cache fallback. CLAUDE.md allows only first-use weight downloads ('pre-fetch with `mrw models fetch` so batch runs never surprise-download'), and README.md:40-41 makes the same promise.

**Failure scenario.** (1) When online, every `mrw lyrics T` sends an HTTPS request to huggingface.co (IP address, model id, timing), even with the weights cached. That breaks offline-by-default in normal use. (2) If Systran/faster-whisper-small main moves to a new commit, the next run silently downloads it. Transcriptions then change while the lyrics config_hash stays the same, and the manifest records only 'small', so the determinism break can't be seen or audited. (3) Behind a proxy that refuses huggingface.co (corporate or captive network), httpx.ProxyError is re-raised and `mrw lyrics` fails even though the model is fully cached.

**Proposal.** Load with `WhisperModel(..., local_files_only=True, revision=<pinned sha>)`, and map the not-cached error to a PrerequisiteError telling the user to run `mrw models fetch`. Pin a commit revision per model size, either as a code constant or as `lyrics.model_revision` so it joins the config hash, and use the same pin in `models fetch` and in the stage. Record the resolved snapshot commit in the manifest run metadata. Reject non-official repo ids (anything containing '/') unless an explicit `lyrics.allow_custom_model` is set. Optionally set HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 for analysis commands (not for `models fetch`). Add a fast test that stubs WhisperModel and asserts `local_files_only=True`.

**Verifier's notes.** lyrics.py:160-162 builds `WhisperModel(cfg.model, device="cpu", compute_type="int8", cpu_threads=1, num_workers=1)` with no local_files_only and no revision. fetch_whisper_model (lyrics.py:227-231) calls `download_model(model_name)` unpinned, and the engine block records only the model name (lyrics.py:577-580). I read the third-party code from source copies of faster-whisper 1.2.1 and huggingface_hub 1.23.0. WhisperModel.__init__ calls download_model(local_files_only=False, revision=None), which calls huggingface_hub.snapshot_download. When local_files_only is False, snapshot_download always calls `api.repo_info(...)`, resolves `main` to the current commit sha and fetches that commit's files. It also does `except httpx.ProxyError: raise`, which skips the cache fallback. Demucs is different: its weights load through torch.hub with check_hash=True, are pinned by hash and download only when missing. CLAUDE.md allows only first-use weight downloads and promises 'batch runs never surprise-download'; README.md says the same. None of this is recorded in OPEN_QUESTIONS or the reviews. So when online, every lyrics run makes a network request, and an upstream model change would alter lyrics.json under an unchanged config_hash. That is a missing safety net for both the offline and the determinism constraints.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Load with `local_files_only=True` and a pinned `revision=<commit sha>` per model size. Use the same pin in fetch_whisper_model. On LocalEntryNotFoundError, either raise a PrerequisiteError ('run `mrw models fetch`'), or do a single pinned first-use download to keep CLAUDE.md's exception. Record the snapshot commit in the manifest run metadata. Add a fast test that stubs WhisperModel and asserts the kwargs. Drop the 'reject custom repo ids' part: the config is user-controlled and CTranslate2 weights do not execute code, so that is scope creep.

#### DET-9: The Whisper model version is unpinned: faster-whisper re-resolves the Hugging Face 'main' branch on every lyrics run

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D2, D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:155-162`, `mrw/lyrics.py:227-231`, `mrw/lyrics.py:577-581`, `mrw/config.py:49-51`

**Evidence.** `WhisperModel(cfg.model, device="cpu", compute_type="int8", cpu_threads=1, num_workers=1)` (lyrics.py:160-162) passes neither `revision` nor `local_files_only`. In the locked faster-whisper 1.2.1, `WhisperModel.__init__` defaults to `local_files_only=False, revision=None`. When the model argument is not a directory, it calls `download_model`, which returns `huggingface_hub.snapshot_download(repo_id, local_files_only=False, revision=None, ...)`; I verified this in the v1.2.1 source. Assumption, high confidence: when online, snapshot_download asks the Hub API for the current commit of Systran/faster-whisper-<model> on every call and downloads any changed files. `fetch_whisper_model` (lyrics.py:227-231) is just as unpinned. The lyrics config_hash covers only `model: "small"`, and lyrics.json records `model=cfg.lyrics.model` (lyrics.py:579).

**Failure scenario.** If the Hub repo's main branch moves (a re-conversion or a tokenizer fix), the next `mrw lyrics` downloads and uses different weights without saying so. This happens on any re-run after a config change and on every new track. lyrics.json changes under an unchanged config_hash and engine block, and old and new tracks in one library come from different models. A batch run also contacts huggingface.co for every track, a possible surprise download outside `mrw models fetch` (CLAUDE.md exception 2). Separately, a user who stays offline with `[lyrics] model = "/Users/<name>/models/whisper-small-ct2"` gets that absolute, user-specific path written into lyrics.json's engine.model (a D2 leak).

**Proposal.** Add `LyricsConfig.model_revision`, a Hub commit sha that is part of config_hash. Construct `WhisperModel(cfg.model, revision=cfg.model_revision, local_files_only=True, ...)` so stage runs never touch the network, and pass the same revision in fetch_whisper_model so `mrw models fetch` is the only download path. Record the resolved snapshot (commit or model.bin sha256) in the manifest run metadata. When model is a local directory, write a path-free identity into engine.model, such as the directory name or a model.bin hash. As defence in depth, optionally set HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 while the stage runs. Include the revision in the CI Whisper cache key.

**Verifier's notes.** I checked the upstream sources. In faster-whisper v1.2.1, WhisperModel.__init__ has `local_files_only: bool = False` and `revision: Optional[str] = None`, and calls download_model(...) when the argument is not a directory. download_model forwards both to huggingface_hub.snapshot_download, with no try/except in 1.2.1. In the locked huggingface_hub 1.23.0, snapshot_download with local_files_only=False calls `api.repo_info(repo_id=..., revision=revision)` (revision defaults to main) on every call, before consulting the cache. It falls back to the cache only on connection or HTTP errors. lyrics.py:160-162 and fetch_whisper_model (lyrics.py:227-231) pass neither argument. So every online `mrw lyrics` run contacts huggingface.co, and a moved main would swap weights under an unchanged config_hash and engine block (lyrics.py:577-581). Demucs is unaffected: torch hub reuses its cached checkpoint. Medium: the traffic is metadata to a permitted distribution host and the repo is effectively static, but the 'batch runs never surprise-download' safety net is missing. The engine.model local-path point is a portability/privacy nit; it is config-derived and deterministic, so not a D2 volatility violation.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do not make local_files_only=True unconditional. That breaks the documented first-use download (README 'Model weights', CLAUDE.md exception 2) and the slow CI tests on a cold cache. Try `WhisperModel(name, revision=PINNED_SHA, local_files_only=True, ...)` first. Only if that snapshot is absent, download the pinned revision, or fail with 'run mrw models fetch'. Pass the same revision in fetch_whisper_model, record the resolved commit in the manifest run metadata, and fold the revision into the CI cache key.

#### TEST-4: Nothing in tests or CI enforces the offline-by-default hard constraint; `mrw lyrics` very likely contacts the Hugging Face Hub on every run

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D5
- **Locations:** `CLAUDE.md:12-18`, `mrw/lyrics.py:155-162`, `mrw/lyrics.py:227-231`, `tests/conftest.py:17-19`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:320-343`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:51-55`

**Evidence.** CLAUDE.md:12-18 allows exactly two network exceptions, and model weights are first-use downloads to be pre-fetched with `mrw models fetch` "so batch runs never surprise-download". A grep finds no network guard anywhere: no socket patching, no pytest-socket, no HF_HUB_OFFLINE, no local_files_only, no ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL. CI runs with full network access. lyrics.py:160-162 constructs `WhisperModel(cfg.model, device="cpu", compute_type="int8", cpu_threads=1, num_workers=1)` with no `local_files_only=True` and no pinned `revision`. Assumed library behaviour (faster-whisper 1.2.1 and huggingface-hub 1.23 per uv.lock; fairly sure): WhisperModel(name) calls download_model, which calls huggingface_hub.snapshot_download(local_files_only=False, revision=None). That resolves `main` against the Hub API on every construction; offline it logs 'An error occured while synchronizing the model ... Trying to load the model directly from the local cache'. If main has moved, it downloads the changed files. Separately, test_m5_smoke.py:337-343 runs the real AnthropicBackend with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=test-key-never-used. Its 'zero calls' outcome depends on cache hits, so a cache-key regression would send a real request from CI instead of failing deterministically.

**Failure scenario.** A batch `mrw lyrics` run on a workstation that is supposed to be offline makes one HTTPS request to huggingface.co per track. If Systran/faster-whisper-small's main branch is updated, the next run silently downloads new weights, and lyrics.json changes under an unchanged config (D1). CI runs online with a cached model and passes either way.

**Proposal.** (1) Add a session-scoped autouse guard in conftest that patches socket.socket.connect/connect_ex to raise for non-loopback addresses unless a test is marked `network`. (2) In the CI slow job, run `uv run mrw models fetch` first with network allowed, then run pytest with the guard active. The Whisper tests will fail until lyrics.py passes `local_files_only=True` (raising a PrerequisiteError that says 'run `mrw models fetch`') and pins `revision=` to a commit recorded in config or tool_version. (3) Optionally run the slow job under `unshare -n` so ffmpeg subprocesses are covered too.

**Verifier's notes.** I confirmed statically that no network guard exists. A grep for socket, HF_HUB_OFFLINE, local_files_only and ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL finds only the workflow's HF cache path and the README. lyrics.py:160-162 builds `WhisperModel(cfg.model, ...)` with neither local_files_only nor revision. Assumption (high confidence; faster-whisper 1.2.1 and huggingface-hub 1.23.0 per uv.lock): a bare model name goes through faster_whisper.utils.download_model to huggingface_hub.snapshot_download(local_files_only=False, revision=None). When online, that resolves `main` through the Hub API on every construction, and downloads changed files if the ref has moved. The local_files_only parameter exists to avoid exactly this. Demucs is different: torch.hub reuses a cached checkpoint without network. So every `mrw lyrics` run contacts huggingface.co. That is not the 'first-use download' exception in CLAUDE.md:12-18, and a moved upstream ref would surprise-download new weights and change lyrics.json under an unchanged config. The test-gap half holds regardless of library behaviour: nothing guards a hard constraint. The m5 cache-replay test (L337-343) would, after a cache-key regression, send a real request with a fake key from CI. It would then fail with a 401, but non-deterministically and over the network.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep the permitted first-use download instead of turning it into a hard error. In _transcribe, construct WhisperModel(..., local_files_only=True) first. Only on the hub's not-cached error, fall back to a networked load, or raise a PrerequisiteError pointing to `mrw models fetch` if you prefer strict offline. Pin `revision` to a commit stored in LyricsConfig, so a model change becomes a config_hash event. Pass the same revision in fetch_whisper_model (lyrics.py:227-231), and record the resolved snapshot commit in the manifest run block. The conftest socket guard must allow loopback and AF_UNIX, which TEST-7's stub server needs. Make it session-scoped and autouse, with a `network` marker to opt out. In CI, run `mrw models fetch` before pytest.

### E2. Caption backend is not ready for paid batches

#### ARCH-12: M7 caption-backend hardening: the paid code path is untested, reply parsing is fragile, PLAN's temperature-0 promise can no longer be met, and the SDK version is unbounded

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D5, T1, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/captions.py:96`, `mrw/captions.py:112`, `mrw/captions.py:133`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:386`, `PLAN.md:307`, `pyproject.toml:21`, `mrw/costs.py:21`, `mrw/video.py:347`

**Evidence.** No test executes AnthropicBackend.caption. The replay test pre-fills the cache (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:321-357), and the partial-spend test replaces make_backend with a fake (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:386-389). Nothing mocks `anthropic.Anthropic` or `messages.create`, even though PLAN.md:548-550 makes 'a mock HTTP layer' the M7 smoke test. Parsing is fragile: captions.py:133-134 does `text_block = next(b.text for b in response.content if b.type == "text")` then `parsed = json.loads(text_block)`. A reply wrapped in a ```json code fence raises JSONDecodeError, and a reply with no text block (for example a refusal) raises a StopIteration with an empty message (ARCH-8). Either one fails the whole stage. A new client is created on every call (captions.py:112, `client = anthropic.Anthropic()`). captions.py:96 accepts only ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, although the SDK also resolves ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN and `ant auth login` profiles. PLAN.md:307-310 promises 'temperature 0', and captions.py:125-129 correctly sends none. Per Anthropic's current API reference, sampling parameters return a 400 error on Opus 4.7/4.8 and Sonnet 5, which are three of the five models in costs.py:24-30, and on every model under API version 2026-10-21, which newer SDK major versions send. `anthropic>=0.116.0` (pyproject.toml:21) has no upper bound, although later SDK major versions are breaking (1.x moves to httpx2). PRICES_PER_MTOK is 'As of 2026-07' (costs.py:21) and lacks the current model generation (for example claude-haiku-5-5, claude-sonnet-5-5, claude-opus-5-5). `_accrued_usage` (video.py:347-362) prices 'actual' spend from the same table, so the ledger's estimate-vs-actual comparison only measures error in the token estimate, not in prices.

**Failure scenario.** On the first paid run, the model wraps its JSON in a code fence. Shot 1 is billed, json.loads raises, and the video stage records failed. That shot never reaches the cache, so every re-run pays for the same call again and fails at the same point. Separately, a later `uv lock --upgrade` moves anthropic to a new major version; no test exercises the request path, so the breakage first shows up on a paid run. And a contributor who 'fixes' the code to match PLAN's temperature 0 breaks every Opus 4.7/4.8 and Sonnet 5 configuration with a 400 error.

**Proposal.** (1) Add the M7 mocked-transport test. Either pass the client into AnthropicBackend as a constructor parameter (created once), or point `anthropic.Anthropic(base_url=..., api_key="test")` at a local stub. Cover the request shape, usage accounting, the cache write, fenced-JSON and refusal replies, and the two-run replay that makes zero calls. (2) Request structured output (an output_config JSON schema for {text, tags}) where the pinned SDK and model support it. Otherwise strip code fences and validate with `ShotCaption.model_validate_json`. Decide in OPEN_QUESTIONS whether a shot whose reply cannot be parsed, or is refused, fails the stage or omits its caption with a recorded reason. (3) Remove 'temperature 0' from PLAN §4 stage 5, and state that caption determinism rests solely on the cache (OQ-10). (4) Bound `anthropic<1` until the test in (1) exists, then upgrade deliberately. (5) Either let the SDK resolve credentials and map AuthenticationError to a MissingCredential usage error (exit 2), or document the key-only policy. (6) Validate caption_model against the price table at config load, and refresh the table and its as-of date whenever models are added.

**Verifier's notes.** A grep finds no test that touches AnthropicBackend.caption or anthropic.Anthropic. The replay test pre-fills the cache, and the partial-spend test replaces make_backend. captions.py:133-134 parses the raw text with json.loads and caches only after success. A fenced or prose reply therefore fails the stage after the call is billed, and the next run bills that shot again. How often the model fences its JSON cannot be checked offline; it is plausible. Checked against the bundled Claude API reference: temperature and top_p return 400 on claude-opus-4-7, claude-opus-4-8 and claude-sonnet-5 (3 of the 5 models in costs.py:24-30). They also return 400 on every model under API version 2026-10-21, which Python anthropic 2.0.0 sends by default, and 1.x moves to httpx2. claude-haiku-5-5, claude-sonnet-5-5 and claude-opus-5-5 exist but are missing from PRICES_PER_MTOK. The reference lists Sonnet 5 at $2/$10, while costs.py:27 deliberately uses $3/$15. _accrued_usage prices 'actual' spend from that same table (video.py:352-355), so it is not a true actual. The ANTHROPIC_API_KEY-only rule is documented policy (CLAUDE.md:14-15), not a defect.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed, with adjustments. If you bound the SDK, use `anthropic<2`, which targets the API-version switch. mrw sends no sampling parameters and uses a plain messages.create, so 1.x likely works. uv.lock already prevents silent upgrades, so the mocked-transport test is the real guard. Structured outputs (output_config.format) are documented for haiku-4-5, sonnet-5 and opus-4-8 but not for opus-4-7 or sonnet-4-6, so keep a fallback that strips code fences and validates with ShotCaption.model_validate_json. Remove 'temperature 0' from PLAN §4.

#### VID-6: Anthropic backend: one unparseable, refused or truncated response fails the whole stage (repeatedly); no stop_reason handling or structured output

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, D5, S2
- **Locations:** `mrw/captions.py:112`, `mrw/captions.py:125-138`, `mrw/video.py:557-579`, `mrw/costs.py:27`, `mrw/config.py:95`

**Evidence.** mrw/captions.py:125-129 calls `client.messages.create(model=self.model, max_tokens=self.max_output_tokens, messages=[...])` with no structured-output format and no thinking setting. The response is then read with captions.py:133 `text_block = next(b.text for b in response.content if b.type == "text")`, captions.py:134 `parsed = json.loads(text_block)` and captions.py:137 `tags=[str(tag) for tag in parsed.get("tags", [])]`. stop_reason is never checked. Any exception reaches mrw/video.py:557-579, which fails the stage with `error=str(exc)[:500]`. For a missing text block the exception is StopIteration, whose str() is '', so both the manifest error and the CLI line 'error: video failed: ' are empty. A new `anthropic.Anthropic()`, with a fresh HTTP pool, is created for every call (captions.py:112). Per Anthropic's current API reference: claude-sonnet-5, which mrw/costs.py:27 prices and therefore accepts, runs adaptive thinking when `thinking` is omitted, so thinking tokens draw on max_tokens=300 (config.py:95). Structured outputs (output_config.format) are supported on the default claude-haiku-4-5, and `refusal` is a documented stop_reason. Each call is billed and counted (captions.py:130-132) before parsing.

**Failure scenario.** (1) The model wraps the JSON in ```json fences or adds a sentence. json.loads raises and the run fails at that shot after paying for it. The shot is never cached, so a re-run pays again with no guarantee of success. (2) A frame triggers a refusal (e.g. violence or nudity in a music video). There is no text block, so StopIteration fails the run with an empty error message. Every re-run fails at the same shot, so the track can never get a captioned video.json even though all other shots are cached. (3) Tags returned as a string, e.g. 'night, street', become a list of single characters. (4) With caption_model=claude-sonnet-5, thinking can consume the 300-token budget, leaving stop_reason max_tokens with missing or truncated text.

**Proposal.** Request JSON through structured outputs, e.g. output_config={'format': {'type': 'json_schema', 'schema': {...}}} with a text string and up to 8 tag strings, on models that support it. Elsewhere, extract tolerantly: strip code fences and take the first {...}. Check stop_reason before reading content, and turn refusal and max_tokens into descriptive errors carrying the shot index, stop_reason and request id. For models that think by default, set thinking/effort explicitly or raise max_tokens, and keep the estimate in sync. Create the client once, lazily. Retry parse failures a bounded number of times. For a shot that keeps failing, add an additive per-shot marker (e.g. flags ['caption_refused'], minor schema bump) so the rest of the document is still written, per H1. Normalise tags to lowercase snake_case.

**Verifier's notes.** The code facts check out. There is no stop_reason check. captions.py:133 reads `next(b.text for b in response.content if b.type == "text")` and 134 calls json.loads on the raw text. 137 does `[str(tag) for tag in parsed.get("tags", [])]`, so a string becomes characters. 112 creates a new client per call. 130-132 count usage before parsing, which is good for the ledger, but the failing shot is never cached, so every re-run pays for it again, and a shot that keeps failing blocks the whole document. If there is no text block, next() raises StopIteration; it is not in a generator context, so PEP 479 does not convert it. run_video's `except Exception` (video.py:557-579) then records error="" and the CLI prints 'error: video failed: '. I checked the API claims against the claude-api skill reference (cached 2026-10-06). `refusal` is a documented stop_reason, and its content can be empty (pre-output) or partial. output_config.format structured outputs list Claude Haiku 4.5 and Sonnet 5 as supported. Sonnet 5 runs adaptive thinking when `thinking` is omitted, and thinking counts against max_tokens=300. Medium fits: this is robustness on an opt-in paid path.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed, with specifics. Use `output_config={"format": {"type": "json_schema", "schema": {...additionalProperties: false}}}` on models the docs list (Haiku 4.5, Sonnet 5, Opus 4.8+). Use a fence-stripping tolerant parser for the others in PRICES_PER_MTOK (claude-sonnet-4-6 and claude-opus-4-7 are not in the current structured-output list). Check stop_reason first; on refusal or max_tokens, raise an error that carries the shot index, stop_reason and request id. On Sonnet 5 send thinking={"type":"disabled"}, which it accepts. Bump caption_prompt_version when the request shape changes. Count retries in the budget and the ledger. The per-shot refusal marker needs a video schema minor bump (S2).

#### SEC-11: AnthropicBackend hardening gaps: fragile JSON parsing after payment, no temperature 0, API key required for zero-call cache replay, prompt_version detached from the actual prompt

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D5, T2, H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/captions.py:41`, `mrw/captions.py:96`, `mrw/captions.py:112`, `mrw/captions.py:125`, `mrw/captions.py:133`, `mrw/config.py:94`

**Evidence.** Cache key and prompt versioning: the key is `f'{combined}.{prompt_version}.{model}'` (captions.py:41-43). It leaves out the prompt text: PROMPT_TEMPLATE lives in code (28-35), while `caption_prompt_version: str = 'v1'` is free-form user config (config.py:94). The key is also used unvalidated as a file name (69-70).

API key: `__init__` raises unless ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is set (96-100), even when every shot is already cached. The replay test has to inject a dummy key to pass (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:337).

Request: a new `anthropic.Anthropic()` client is built for each uncached call (112). `messages.create(model=..., max_tokens=..., messages=...)` (125-129) passes no temperature, so the API default of 1.0 applies, while PLAN.md:308 specifies 'temperature 0'.

Parsing: the reply goes through `next(b.text for b ...)` and a bare `json.loads(text_block)` (133-134), with no stop_reason check. A reply wrapped in ```json fences, cut off at max_tokens, using a different key (`parsed['text']` raises KeyError), or with `tags` as a string (iterated into single characters) either fails or is cached after the call has been paid. Assumption: Claude models sometimes wrap JSON in fences despite 'STRICT JSON' instructions; medium confidence on how often.

**Failure scenario.** (a) On a machine without the key, or in the planned M7 warm-cache determinism CI, `mrw video T` fails with 'requires ANTHROPIC_API_KEY' even though every shot is cached and zero calls would be made. (b) One shot's reply comes back fenced. The stage fails after paying for that call, and since failed calls are never cached, every re-run pays for it again. (c) A developer edits PROMPT_TEMPLATE without bumping the config default, so old captions replay under the new prompt's label. Conversely, setting `caption_prompt_version = 'v2'` in mrw.toml re-pays for an identical prompt and records a version that names nothing. (d) A prompt_version containing '/' makes `cache.put` fail with ENOENT after the call is paid.

**Proposal.** Bind versions to templates in code, e.g. `PROMPTS = {'v1': TEMPLATE}`, with a test pinning each version's sha256. Include sha256(template)[:12] and max_output_tokens in the cache key. Validate model and prompt_version against `^[A-Za-z0-9._@-]+$` at config load. Build a single client lazily on the first cache miss, require the key only at that point, and set explicit `timeout`/`max_retries`. Pass `temperature=0`. Request structured output (tool use with an input_schema, or JSON output mode). Failing that, strip fences, validate with a pydantic model (3-8 snake_case tags) and check `stop_reason == 'end_turn'` before caching. These belong in the M7 hardening work and its mocked-HTTP double-run test.

**Verifier's notes.** The code facts hold. The key check in __init__ (captions.py:96-100) runs even for a fully cached replay; the replay test injects a dummy key (test_m5_smoke.py:337). A new client is built per call (captions.py:112). There is no stop_reason check, the reply goes through a bare json.loads, a missing 'text' raises KeyError, and a string 'tags' is iterated into characters (captions.py:133-138). The cache key embeds the free-form config prompt_version (captions.py:41-43, config.py:94) unvalidated as a filename. The temperature remedy is wrong, though. Per current Anthropic API docs, temperature, top_p and top_k return a 400 on claude-opus-4-7, claude-opus-4-8 and claude-sonnet-5, all of which are in costs.PRICES_PER_MTOK, and on every model under API version 2026-10-21, which anthropic SDK 2.0+ sends by default. temperature 0 never guaranteed identical output, and the contract is deterministic-given-cache anyway, so PLAN.md:308 is the stale part, not the code. The consequential item (fenced or invalid JSON after a paid call means the stage fails, the reply isn't cached, and the shot is re-paid on re-run) depends on model behaviour I cannot verify offline. Rated low because the backend is opt-in, the failure is loud with spend recorded (video.py:561), and M7 already plans backend hardening.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do NOT add temperature=0; correct PLAN.md:308 instead. Request structured output: `output_config.format` with a JSON schema (supported on claude-haiku-4-5) or one tool with an input_schema. Before caching, check `stop_reason == 'end_turn'` (handle 'max_tokens' and 'refusal') and validate the reply with a pydantic model (3-8 snake_case tags). Build a single client lazily on the first cache miss and require the key only then. Bind prompt versions to templates in code (`PROMPTS = {'v1': ...}`) with a test pinning each template's sha256. Validate prompt_version and model against a filename-safe regex at config load.

#### CRIT-12: Caption replies are coerced instead of validated, then cached for good: a string 'tags' becomes one tag per character, and the documented snake_case vocabulary is never enforced

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, C1, C2, D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/captions.py:28-35`, `mrw/captions.py:133-139`, `mrw/captions.py:72-80`, `schemas/video.schema.json`

**Evidence.** mrw/captions.py:133-139:
- `text_block = next(b.text for b in response.content if b.type == "text")`
- `parsed = json.loads(text_block)`
- `caption = ShotCaption(text=str(parsed["text"]), tags=[str(tag) for tag in parsed.get("tags", [])])`
- `self.cache.put(key, caption)`

If 'tags' is a string, the comprehension iterates over its characters. A non-string 'text' is passed through str(). The prompt's '3-8 lowercase snake_case tags' (captions.py:33-34) and the schema's 'Lowercase snake_case tags' (video.schema.json caption.tags) are never enforced. The cached result is replayed on every later run (captions.py:72-76, OQ-10).

VID-6, SEC-11 and ARCH-12 cover replies that fail to parse and stop the stage. This finding is the other path: a reply that parses but has the wrong shape.

**Failure scenario.** A reply `{"text": "A singer on a neon stage.", "tags": "close_up, neon_lighting"}` produces caption.tags ['c','l','o','s','e','_','u','p',',',' ',...] in video.json and in the cache, and every later run replays it byte for byte. A reply with tags ['Neon Lighting', 'Close-Up'] silently breaks the documented vocabulary.

**Proposal.** - Validate the reply with a strict pydantic model: text is a non-empty str with a length cap; tags is a list[str] of 1–8 items.
- Normalise tags (lowercase, non-alphanumerics to '_', dedupe) before caching.
- Treat a shape failure as a retry or a per-shot flag instead of caching it (alongside VID-6's structured-output proposal).
- Unit-test with stubbed responses.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed in the code:
- captions.py:133-139 coerces 'text' with str() and iterates parsed.get('tags', []); a string iterates into single characters.
- ShotCaption (models.py:210-212) has no constraints.
- The result is cached and replayed indefinitely (captions.py:72-76, :139).

The snake_case vocabulary in the prompt (captions.py:33-34) and the schema is never enforced, and no test drives AnthropicBackend.caption with a stubbed reply.

With an explicit JSON prompt, a reply that parses but has the wrong shape is uncommon. Caching makes it permanent when it happens. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Validate the reply with a strict pydantic model: text a non-empty str with a length cap; tags a list[str] of 1–8 items.
- Normalise tags deterministically before cache.put: lowercase, non-alphanumerics to '_', dedupe.
- Never cache a reply with the wrong shape: retry once or fail the shot.
- Add unit tests with stubbed responses: string tags, non-string text, wrong-case tags.

#### DET-15: The caption cache key omits the prompt text and max_output_tokens, and the plan's 'temperature 0' is neither implemented nor still achievable

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D5
- **Locations:** `mrw/captions.py:28-43`, `mrw/captions.py:102-140`, `mrw/config.py:93-95`, `PLAN.md:305-317`

**Evidence.** `cache_key(frame_sha256s, prompt_version, model)` (captions.py:41-43) omits `caption_max_output_tokens` (config.py:95), which is part of the video config hash, and omits the PROMPT_TEMPLATE text (captions.py:28-35). `client.messages.create(model=..., max_tokens=..., messages=...)` (captions.py:125-129) sends no sampling parameters, although PLAN.md:308 specifies 'temperature 0'. According to the Claude API reference I consulted, API version 2026-10-21 (the default from anthropic-python 2.0.0) rejects temperature, top_p and top_k on every model. The lock has 0.116.0, but pyproject only requires `anthropic>=0.116.0`, so the next `uv lock --upgrade` can reach 2.x. 'temperature 0' is therefore not a durable control, and the cache is the only determinism mechanism. config.py:93 calls `"claude-haiku-4-5"` 'pinned', but that is the alias for the dated snapshot claude-haiku-4-5-20251001, and the resolved `response.model` is not recorded anywhere.

**Failure scenario.** (1) Someone edits PROMPT_TEMPLATE, for example to add a tag rule, without bumping caption_prompt_version. Every cached caption from the old prompt is replayed, and video.json labels them prompt_version v1 even though the current prompt never produced them. (2) Lowering caption_max_output_tokens changes the video config_hash and re-runs the stage, yet captions generated under the 300-token limit are replayed. (3) When the cache is cold (a new machine, frame shas changed per DET-8, or the cache deleted), captions are re-sampled at the default temperature and can differ from the originals, and nothing records which model snapshot produced them.

**Proposal.** (1) Extend cache_key with max_output_tokens and a short sha256 of PROMPT_TEMPLATE, keeping prompt_version for readability. (2) Store `response.model` in each cache file and in the manifest api_usage block. If 'pinned' is the intent, use the dated id in config. (3) Update PLAN §4 stage 5 to drop 'temperature 0', or note that it depends on the API version, and do not add `temperature=0` now because it would return 400 after the SDK upgrade. Treat `.cache/captions` as precious: never clean it together with frames, and consider a library-level cache so moving tracks keeps it.

**Verifier's notes.** The facts check out. cache_key covers only the frame shas, prompt_version and model (captions.py:41-43), and messages.create sends no sampling parameters (captions.py:125-129), although PLAN.md:308 says 'temperature 0'. Per the Claude API reference, on API version 2026-10-21, the default from anthropic-python 2.0.0, temperature, top_p and top_k return 400 on every model. pyproject has no upper bound (`anthropic>=0.116.0`). `claude-haiku-4-5` is an alias whose full id is claude-haiku-4-5-20251001, and response.model is recorded nowhere. However, the key composition is OQ-10's recorded design, tested at tests/test_m5_smoke.py:126-139. So the template-hash suggestion hardens against a forgotten prompt_version bump; it is not a bug. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do not add max_output_tokens to the cache key. It is an output ceiling, not a content parameter: keying on it forces paid re-captioning on every budget tweak, while captions cached under a higher ceiling remain valid. If config switches to the dated model id, add that id to costs.PRICES_PER_MTOK first. costs.estimate raises UnknownModelError for unknown ids, and _accrued_usage indexes the table by the configured id (video.py:352), so pinning would otherwise break the budget gate. Fix the PLAN §4 stage 5 wording, and consider `anthropic<2` until the 2026-10-21 migration is reviewed.

### E3. Determinism contract overstated for MPS and across devices

#### DET-2: Rounding cannot absorb upstream jitter at document scale, so the promise of identical documents across devices (default device=auto → MPS) does not hold

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D5, C2, T2 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `schemas/manifest.schema.json:79`, `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:129-142`, `PLAN.md:420-436`, `mrw/features.py:24-29`, `mrw/config.py:29-34`, `mrw/stems.py:47-57`, `mrw/stems.py:77-86`, `mrw/stems.py:188-201`, `README.md`

**Evidence.** The contract is stated in four places. manifest.schema.json:79: 'Documents are byte-identical across devices; stem FILES are guaranteed identical only on cpu with stems.cpu_threads = 1'. OQ-13: 'contract = document byte-identity on both devices'. PLAN.md:434-436: 'Demucs on MPS may produce ±1-sample-level differences that survive into stem files (not documents, since features are rounded)'. features.py:24-29: 'Last-ulp float differences ... sit far below the coarsest rounding step ... so no thread pinning is needed'. The defaults put normal use on this path: `device: str = "auto"` (config.py:29) resolves to mps on Apple Silicon (stems.py:53-56); there are no MPS determinism controls (threads are pinned only `if device == "cpu"`, stems.py:78-86); and the F-1 fallback (stems.py:190-201) can turn the same config into a CPU run. The rounding argument is statistically wrong. A 4-minute audio_features.json holds about 24k values × 10 series. Rounding only protects values that are not within the jitter of a rounding boundary, and at this scale many are. I simulated librosa-equivalent float32 RMS and centroid (n_fft 2048, hop 441, Hann window, center=True with constant padding) on an int16 'stem' (scratchpad det/sim_rounding.py). Perturbing 0.1% of samples by ±1 LSB changed 10.5% of spectral_centroid_hz values (1 dp) and 0.03% of rms_db values (2 dp); at 1% of samples, 38% of centroid values changed. Even pure 1-ulp jitter in the float32 |STFT| flipped 0.23% of centroid values (sim_ulp.py), about 280 per 4-minute document. This is an already-decided item (OQ-13). The new argument is the quantitative one: the premise is false, not merely optimistic.

**Failure scenario.** Default config on the target Mac. Run A separates on MPS. Run B differs in one of these ways: it runs on another Mac, MPS hits the F-1 runtime failure and retries on CPU, or the user raises cpu_threads to 4. The two stem sets then differ by ±1 LSB in a fraction of samples. `mrw features` writes audio_features.json files that differ in hundreds to thousands of spectral_centroid_hz values plus some rms_db and onset values, and lyrics word confidences and timings can shift too. All of these documents carry identical stems/features/lyrics config_hashes, so nothing in the manifest explains the difference, while the schema tells consumers it cannot happen. PLAN §7 itself assumes MPS produces ±1-sample jitter, which makes this a run-to-run violation of the determinism hard constraint under the default config.

**Proposal.** (1) Correct the contract wherever it is stated: manifest.schema.json:79, an OQ-13 resolution note, PLAN §7 items 1 and 3, the README 'Model weights' paragraph, and features.py:24-29. The new wording: documents are byte-identical only when their stems are (stems on cpu with cpu_threads = 1, same machine, same uv.lock and ffmpeg), and rounding is a precision contract, not a jitter absorber. (2) Make the dependency auditable through the stems digest and inputs_sha256 from DET-1, so documents with equal config_hash but different stems can be told apart. Record a manifest warning when stems ran on mps or with cpu_threads > 1. (3) Decide the default deliberately: either use device = 'cpu' where identity matters, or keep 'auto' and print a one-line determinism caveat whenever mps or cpu_threads > 1 is used. (4) Replace the features D5 waiver with an actual policy. Either wrap features and video computation in `threadpoolctl.threadpool_limits(1)` (threadpoolctl already ships with scikit-learn), or justify the waiver with a subprocess double run at different thread counts. (5) Add a fast test that perturbs a fabricated vocal stem by ±1 LSB on 0.1% of samples and asserts that audio_features.json changes, to pin the corrected wording.

**Verifier's notes.** The contract is stated unconditionally: manifest.schema.json:79 says 'Documents are byte-identical across devices'; OQ-13 sets 'contract = document byte-identity on both devices'; PLAN.md:434-436 says 'not documents, since features are rounded'; features.py:24-29 says the same. Rounding cannot deliver this once stems differ. It flips every rounded value that lies within the perturbation of a boundary, and a document holds ~10^5 values. I ran an independent simulation (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/det-verify/sim_stems.py: librosa-style float32 RMS and centroid, n_fft 2048, hop 441, center=True, on a synthetic vibrato voice quantized to int16). ±1 LSB on 0.1% of samples changed 24.8% of the 1-dp centroid values and 0.05% of the 2-dp rms_db values; at 1% of samples, 71% and 0.85%. The repo's own record already shows cross-environment divergence downstream of separation: tests/test_m4_smoke.py:462 says 'different CPU arch → slightly different separation output → different decode', and docs/reviews/007-m4-lyrics.md:91 records 2 vs 3 hallucinations. Defaults reach this path: device='auto' resolves to mps (config.py:29, stems.py:53-56), with the CPU retry at stems.py:190-201. OQ-13 is an already-known item, but the false-premise argument is genuinely new. Medium rather than high: stems.run.device and stems.warning already record the device and the fallback, PLAN §7 labels MPS best-effort, same-machine MPS run-to-run jitter is unverifiable offline, and no consumer depends on cross-device identity. The defect is a false contract statement plus missing attribution.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep (1), correcting the wording in the schema, OQ-13 (with a P2 resolution note), PLAN §7 and features.py; README does not claim document identity. Keep (2), attribution through DET-1's stems digest. Keep device='auto': OQ-13 deliberately rejected CPU-by-default on cost. Print and record the caveat only when mps or cpu_threads > 1 actually ran. Drop (5): a test asserting that documents DO change under a ±1 LSB perturbation is signal-dependent and pins nothing a consumer needs. Treat (4), threadpoolctl, as optional: same-machine BLAS with a fixed thread count is already deterministic, and cross-machine identity is lost earlier through stems and ffmpeg (DET-8).

#### FEAT-9: The D5 claim that rounding absorbs BLAS jitter does not hold numerically; byte identity actually depends on multithreaded float32 matrix multiplication being bit-reproducible, which is only tested on Linux

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D5, D1, T2 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:24-29`, `mrw/features.py:82-89`, `mrw/lyrics.py:157-162`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml`

**Evidence.** features.py:24-29 says: 'Last-ulp float differences from BLAS thread ordering in librosa/numpy/pyloudnorm sit far below the coarsest rounding step (0.001 s / 0.01 dB / 0.1 Hz), so no thread pinning is needed'. Rounding absorbs a perturbation δ only when no value lies within δ of a rounding boundary. The chance of a flip is about δ/step per value, and a 4-minute document has about 2.4×10^5 rounded values (10 dense series × ~24k, plus onsets). The stage is float32 end to end (`sf.read(path, dtype="float32", always_2d=True)`, features.py:86). librosa's mel projection is `np.einsum("...ft,mf->...mt", S, mel_basis, optimize=True)` (pinned feature/spectral.py:2150), a multithreaded float32 matrix multiply (sgemm) that feeds every onset envelope. At float32 precision (about 1e-7 relative), any genuine run-to-run jitter would flip about half an onset strength per document on average. It can also flip discrete decisions: peak-pick equality and `>= avg + delta`, the beat DP and tempo argmax, and the phase-fit argmax. That is the same near-threshold mechanism the lyrics stage pins threads against ('near-threshold decodes on quiet audio can flip with thread order', lyrics.py:157-159). The only evidence is a double run inside one process on ubuntu CI (OpenBLAS). The target platform's numpy (Accelerate on Apple Silicon) is never tested. I could not observe jitter here; the claim is that the stated argument does not hold and the safety net covers only one platform. PR #6 round 2 recorded 'rounding IS the policy'.

**Failure scenario.** On the target Mac, two `mrw features` runs over identical stems differ in one onset strength, or in one onset whose envelope sits at the delta threshold. That breaks 'same input + same config ⇒ byte-identical analysis documents', and no CI job would notice.

**Proposal.** Make the policy hold by construction. Run the numeric part of run_features under `threadpoolctl.threadpool_limits(1)`; threadpoolctl 3.6.0 is already locked as a scikit-learn dependency, and the mel matrix multiply is small, so the cost is negligible. Optionally compute in float64. Reword the comment to 'determinism comes from single-threaded, fixed-order computation; rounding is a second layer'. Include a macOS double run in the M7 determinism CI.

**Verifier's notes.** The comment's reasoning (features.py:24-29) is incomplete: rounding absorbs a perturbation only away from rounding boundaries, so 'rounding IS the policy' does not by itself guarantee byte identity. The practical risk is small, though. The only threaded numeric call in this stage is the mel projection `np.einsum(..., optimize=True)` (librosa feature/spectral.py:2150, BLAS sgemm). FFTs use scipy.fft single-threaded (core/fft.py:14), rms and centroid are plain numpy reductions, and pyloudnorm uses lfilter. OpenBLAS level-3 threading partitions M and N, not K, so results are run-to-run reproducible. CI's 8 s double run (test_m3_smoke.py:214-222) already performs a 128×1025×801 sgemm, far above OpenBLAS's threading threshold, and passes. Accelerate (numpy 2.4.6's macosx_14_0_arm64 wheel, uv.lock:1216) is not in CI, but the suite is evidently run locally on Apple Silicon (review 007 cites Apple Silicon vs CI results). There is no demonstrated failure. The recorded policy (PR #6 round 2) stands; its stated rationale needs correcting.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposed `threadpoolctl.threadpool_limits(1)` would not pin the target platform. As far as I know threadpoolctl has controllers for OpenBLAS, MKL, BLIS, FlexiBLAS and OpenMP, but none for Apple Accelerate, which macOS ≥14 arm64 numpy links. Instead: reword the D5 comment to state the real basis (fixed reduction order in the only BLAS call; rounding is a second layer). If pinning is wanted, use `os.environ.setdefault` for VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS, OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS and OMP_NUM_THREADS = 1 at CLI start, before numpy loads. In M7, run the features double-run on a macOS arm64 runner with a longer fixture (≥60 s).

#### TEST-5: Determinism evidence covers only Linux x86 CPU at one thread, in one process; the target default (Apple Silicon, device=auto → MPS) has none, and rounding cannot deliver OQ-13's document identity

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** ci · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** D1, D5, T2 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:129-142`, `PLAN.md:430-438`, `mrw/config.py:29`, `mrw/stems.py:53-56`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:17`, `tests/test_m2_smoke.py:64-67`

**Evidence.** OQ-13 (OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:133-135) promises "document byte-identity on both devices; stem-file byte-identity on CPU only". PLAN.md:436-438 says MPS 'may produce ±1-sample-level differences that survive into stem files (not documents, since features are rounded)'. The default is config.py:29 `device: str = "auto"`, and stems.py:56 `return "mps" if torch.backends.mps.is_available() else "cpu"`. CI is claude-review.yml:17 `runs-on: ubuntu-latest` only, every neural test forces CPU (test_m2_smoke.py:66, test_m3_smoke.py:251, test_m4_smoke.py:440), and every double-run happens in one process via CliRunner. The rounding argument: a perturbation δ of a value flips its rounded form with probability about δ/step. audio_features.json holds about 10 dense series at 100 values/s, roughly 240k rounded values for a 4-min track (schemas/README.md:71). So even δ ≈ 1e-5·step gives about 2 expected byte flips per document, and a slightly different vocal stem can change Whisper's output wholesale. Rounding does absorb last-ulp BLAS jitter (δ/step ~1e-12, the argument in features.py:24-29), but not sample-level stem differences. Platform divergence has already been observed: '2 on Apple Silicon vs 3 on CI' (docs/reviews/007-m4-lyrics.md:91). CLAUDE.md's unscoped 'same input + same config ⇒ byte-identical' has never been re-scoped since.

**Failure scenario.** On an M-series Mac with the default config, two `mrw stems` + `mrw features` runs where MPS Demucs differs by a few LSBs in some samples produce audio_features.json files that differ in some rms_db or centroid values. No test or CI job can observe this, while OQ-13 promises identity.

**Proposal.** (1) Re-scope OQ-13, PLAN §7.3 and CLAUDE.md: document identity is guaranteed when the stems are byte-identical (CPU, cpu_threads = 1, same platform); MPS, multi-thread and cross-platform runs are best-effort at document level too. Alternatively, offer an explicit deterministic mode that forces CPU. (2) Add a macos-14/15 arm64 job running the fast suite plus the CPU slow subset, to catch platform drift (Accelerate vs OpenBLAS numerics, Homebrew ffmpeg). (3) MPS evidence needs Apple GPU access. As far as I know, GitHub-hosted macOS arm64 VMs don't expose MPS to PyTorch (moderate confidence), so add a scripted `scripts/determinism_check.py --device mps` run on the dev Mac or a self-hosted runner and record its result at each milestone (P1).

**Verifier's notes.** The facts check out. OQ-13 (OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:133-135) promises document byte-identity on both devices. PLAN.md:434-438 says MPS stem differences do not reach documents 'since features are rounded'. The default device is auto, which resolves to mps (config.py:29, stems.py:51-56). CI is ubuntu-latest only, and every neural test forces cpu (test_m2_smoke.py:66, test_m3_smoke.py:251, test_m4_smoke.py:440). The rounding argument is correct and new. Rounding absorbs perturbations that are tiny relative to the step. A +/-1-LSB change in many stem samples, though, moves per-frame RMS and centroid by a non-negligible fraction of the 0.01 dB / 0.1 Hz step. With about 242k rounded values per audio_features.json (10 series x 24,251), some flips are near-certain, and Whisper output can change wholesale. PLAN's premise therefore contradicts its own conclusion. The MPS-to-CPU fallback (stems.py:190-201) also makes even same-device runs history-dependent. OQ-13 already records the decision, but no review disputes the rounding premise, so this is a genuinely new argument. I lowered severity to medium for three reasons: no MPS divergence has been observed, the operator's mrw.toml:1-2 already pins cpu, and the main action is re-scoping the contract.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Lead with the S-sized docs fix. Re-scope CLAUDE.md:19-23, PLAN §7.3 and OQ-13 to: 'document identity is guaranteed when the stems are byte-identical (cpu, cpu_threads = 1, same platform and locked dependency set); mps, multi-thread, fallback and cross-platform runs are best-effort for documents too'. Add an OQ-13 resolution note (P2). Add a macos-14 arm64 job running the fast suite to catch platform drift; minutes are free on a public repo. Treat the MPS double-run as a scripted manual check on the dev Mac, recorded at each milestone, because hosted macOS VMs very likely do not expose MPS (moderate confidence).

#### DET-10: The determinism tests run both 'independent' runs in one process, and there is no full-pipeline double run or merge-blocking CI job

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T2, T1 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `tests/test_m0_smoke.py:102`, `tests/test_m2_smoke.py:138-144`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:214-222`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:241-269`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:370-381`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:488-503`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:271-288`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml`, `PLAN.md:440-441`

**Evidence.** Every double-run test drives the CLI through typer's in-process CliRunner (`runner.invoke(app, ...)`). Both runs therefore share one interpreter: the same PYTHONHASHSEED, the same BLAS/OpenMP pools, and the process-global `torch.set_num_threads` left behind by the stems stage (stems.py:80-86). What is covered: source.json (M0/M1); CPU stems (test_m2_smoke.py:138-144); features only on fabricated stems (test_m3_smoke.py:214-222, while test_features_on_real_separation runs once); lyrics with a stubbed engine (test_m4_smoke.py:370-381) plus a same-library re-run of the real engine (488-503); and video plus frames with the null backend (test_m5_smoke.py:271-288). What is missing: an ingest→stems→features→lyrics→video double run with `diff -r` (PLAN.md:440 says 'once implemented'; it is an M7 item, and `mrw analyze` doesn't exist yet), a byte comparison of captioned video.json, and any run at different thread counts. The only workflow, claude-review.yml, runs pytest as an instruction inside the LLM review action, so no job fails mechanically on a red determinism test. The full-chain check is a known M7 deferral. The in-process and CI-gating gaps are new.

**Failure scenario.** Suppose a change writes flags via `list(set(flags))`, or introduces a reduction whose result depends on the BLAS/OpenMP thread count.
