Bytes then differ across processes but are identical in both in-process runs, so every double-run test stays green. A regression that appears only when features consumes real Demucs output, or only across the whole chain, is never compared at all. Even a red test does not block a merge.

**Proposal.** (1) Add a subprocess double-run helper (`subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'mrw.cli', ...])`) and use it in every double-run test. Give the two runs different PYTHONHASHSEED and OMP_NUM_THREADS / OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS / VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS values. (2) Implement the M7 check now as a slow test: run ingest→stems(cpu)→features→lyrics→video on an A/V fixture into two library roots, compare every file except manifest.json, and assert the two manifests' content_sha256 values are equal. (3) Turn test_features_on_real_separation into a double run. (4) Byte-compare video.json across two cache-replay runs of the anthropic backend. (5) Add a plain CI job (fast suite on every push, slow suite nightly) whose failure blocks merge, independent of the LLM reviewer.

**Verifier's notes.** The described state is accurate. Every double-run test drives typer's in-process CliRunner (tests/test_m0_smoke.py:102-106, test_m2_smoke.py:139-144, test_m3_smoke.py:214-222, test_m4_smoke.py:370-381 and 488-503, test_m5_smoke.py:271-288), and test_features_on_real_separation runs once (test_m3_smoke.py:241-269). Today no output path iterates an unordered set (the only set() uses are sorted, video.py:224 and 236), so the cross-process point is preventive. The CI point is concrete. .github/workflows/claude-review.yml has no test step of its own: pytest runs only as a prompt instruction inside claude-code-action, whose result does not depend on test outcomes. docs/reviews/005-m2-stems.md:37 records a run that hit the turn cap 'without posting anything', and 006:90 another that failed at the cap. The full-chain double run is a known M7 deferral (PLAN.md:440-441, §11 M7). Medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Prioritize (5): a separate plain workflow running `uv run pytest -m 'not slow'` on every PR and push to main, with the slow suite nightly or on milestone PRs, made a required check. Land it on main between review cycles (review 005 note 1, CHECKLIST P3); it does not touch claude-review.yml's anti-tampering constraint. The subprocess double-run helper in (1) is cheap insurance, and `python -m mrw.cli` already works (cli.py:337-338).

## F. Audio features and stems

### F1. Onset detection ignores signal level

#### FEAT-1: Onset detection ignores level: near-silent and bleed-only stems produce dense, full-strength onset lists (R-9's 'silent stem → no onsets' only holds for bit-exact zeros)

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H2, S4, S2, S5, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:120-140`, `mrw/features.py:109-111`, `mrw/features.py:152-159`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:121-131`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:97-105`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:167-175`

**Evidence.** `_onsets` runs `env = librosa.onset.onset_strength(...)` and `frames = librosa.onset.onset_detect(onset_envelope=env, ..., backtrack=False)` with no level gate. Its only empty path is `if len(frames) == 0: return Onsets(times=[], strengths=[], strength_reference=1.0)` (features.py:124-129). The R-9 silent predicate `silent = rms_db <= SILENCE_FLOOR_DB + 1e-9; if silent.all(): return np.zeros_like(cent)` (109-111) is applied to the centroid only. I read the pinned librosa 0.11.0 wheel (sha256 matches uv.lock). onset_strength converts mel power with `S = core.power_to_db(S)` (onset.py:584). Its defaults are ref=1.0, amin=1e-10 and top_db=80, so the floor sits 80 dB below the channel's own maximum (core/spectrum.py:1820), and it then averages positive dB differences. That is a ratio measure, so it does not change with gain. onset_detect then min-max normalises the envelope (onset.py:165-169) before peak-picking with a relative delta of 0.07 (onset.py:188). Neither the onset count nor the p98-normalised strengths depend on the channel's level. I re-implemented these code paths in numpy; the re-implementation reproduces review 006's click-track results (120.0 BPM, beats within one hop, correct downbeat). Results: (a) a stem of ±0.7–1.5 LSB dither has rms_db = −80.0 in every frame, so its centroid takes the R-9 all-zeros path, yet it yields 148–180 onsets in 20 s with median strength 0.93–0.96. (b) A stem holding only drum bleed at −55 dB (rms max −70.3 dBFS) reports 42 onsets, against 40 for the full-level drum stem, with median strength 1.00. (c) The click track 60 dB down gives the same 40 onsets. The R-9 test plants bit-exact zero stems (`stems.get(name, np.zeros_like(mix))`, test_m3_smoke.py:98), which is the only input whose envelope is exactly 0. Real separations are not bit-exact silence: test_m4_smoke.py:461-462 attributes environment-dependent Whisper hallucinations on the no-vocals stem to 'slightly different separation output'.

**Failure scenario.** An instrumental track, or any song's vocal stem during instrumental passages: the Demucs vocals stem carries low-level bleed or dither. audio_features.json then shows stems.vocals.rms_db at or near −80, spectral_centroid_hz all 0.0 (R-9 'silent throughout') and vocal_activity.regions = []. At the same time stems.vocals.onsets lists hundreds of onsets at strengths ≈0.9–1.0: drum hits that leaked into the vocal stem appear as full-strength vocal onsets. A mapping rule of the kind the schema itself uses as an example ('strength > 0.6') fires several times a second on a silent stem. An LLM reader gets contradictory signals about one channel.

**Proposal.** Gate onsets by the channel's own level, consistently with R-9. (1) In `_channel_features`, pass the already-computed rms series into `_onsets`. If `silent.all()`, return the R-9 sentinel so all three series agree. (2) Drop detected onsets whose frame rms_db is below a new `FeaturesConfig.onset_gate_db`, for example −60.0. It changes output, so it belongs in config and the config_hash per review 006 F-1. Embed it next to the data per S4 as an additive field (audio_features schema 1.1.0, example updated). Reword the strengths/strength_reference descriptions to say onsets are only reported above the gate. Tests: dither-only and −55 dB bleed-only stems must give empty onsets, and the drums stem must be unchanged.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced mrw/features.py:120-140. `_onsets(y)` gets no level information, and its only empty path is `if len(frames) == 0` (128-129). The R-9 floor predicate (109-111) is applied to the centroid only. In the pinned librosa 0.11.0 source (the wheel sha256 0b6415c4… matches uv.lock:901), onset_strength_multi runs `S = core.power_to_db(S)`: ref=1.0, amin=1e-10, top_db=80 below the channel's own maximum. It then takes the mean of positive dB differences. onset_detect min-max normalises the envelope (`normalize=True`) before peak_pick(delta=0.07). Both steps are gain-invariant. I wrote an independent numpy replica from that source (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/feat-verify/lr.py, e1.py). It reproduces the M3 fixture (120.0 BPM, beats within 10 ms, correct downbeat). Results: (a) Gaussian noise of σ=0.7–1.5 LSB after 16-bit quantisation gives rms_db −80.0 in every frame, so the centroid takes the R-9 zeros path, yet there are 117–159 onsets in 20 s with median strength 0.94–0.97. (b) The click track at −40/−55/−60/−70 dB gives 40 onsets each, the same as at full level. (c) −55 dB bleed plus 1 LSB noise gives 41 onsets with median strength 1.000. Real stems are not bit-exact zeros: tests/test_m2_smoke.py:203 only asserts 'near-silent' stems from silent input, and test_m4_smoke.py:461-462 notes environment-dependent separation output. The effect is not limited to vocals. The drums stem in drumless intros or breakdowns carries bleed, which becomes phantom drum onsets at ~1.0, and the R-1 example rule 'drum strength > 0.6' fires on them. Nothing in docs/reviews or OPEN_QUESTIONS covers this; R-9 only assumed that 'e.g. a silent stem' has no onsets. High stands: wrong output on common material, consumed directly by the primary visual-mapping use. The proposed absolute gate is incomplete, though (see fix).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** (1) Cheap consistency fix: round the rms series first (this also fixes FEAT-13), then drop onsets whose frame has a published rms_db at the −80.0 floor. If the channel is at the floor throughout, write the R-9 sentinel, so rms, centroid and onsets agree. (2) Separation bleed typically sits around −40 to −55 dBFS, above any sensible absolute gate. An absolute `onset_gate_db = −60` would leave it in place at full strength. Gate (or flag) onsets relative to the mix instead: drop an onset when stem_rms_db − mix_rms_db at that frame is below a FeaturesConfig threshold (e.g. −30 dB). Keep an absolute floor gate as well if wanted. Put the threshold(s) in FeaturesConfig so they join the config_hash (review 006 F-1). Embed them in an onsets params block (S4, audio_features 1.1.0, example updated), and reword the strengths, strength_reference and R-9 text. Tests: a dither-only stem gives empty onsets plus the sentinel; −55 dB bleed under a full-level mix is gated; the drums stem is unchanged; every document goes through `_validate`.

### F2. tempo.confidence does not measure confidence; trim=False

#### FEAT-3: tempo.confidence scores how regular the tracker's output is, and that output is regular by construction; with trim=False the grid also extends through beatless intros and outros at high confidence

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1, C2, T3, P2
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:167-169`, `mrw/features.py:173-183`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:21-24`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:144-148`

**Evidence.** The call is `bpm, beat_frames = librosa.beat.beat_track(onset_envelope=env, sr=SAMPLE_RATE, hop_length=HOP, trim=False)` (features.py:167-169). Confidence is `max(0.0, min(1.0, 1.0 - 4.0 * cv))`, where cv is the coefficient of variation of the inter-beat intervals (178-181). The comment's premise is 'A metronomic grid scores ~1.0; an erratic one → 0' (173-174). librosa's tracker (`__beat_track_dp` in the pinned beat.py, default tightness 100) penalises any deviation from the single global period, so its output is near-metronomic whether or not onsets support it. trim=False sets the trim threshold to 0.0 (beat.py:629-632), so only beats over an exactly-zero local score are removed. Nothing in the repo explains trim=False; it appears only at features.py:168. I re-implemented librosa 0.11's tempo and beat code paths (checked against the pinned source). Results: a sustained drone scores 0.777, pink noise 0.803, white noise 0.792 and irregular noise bursts 0.532, all above the schema's 0.5 'advisory' line. A 174 BPM click track is tracked at half tempo (87.0 BPM) with confidence 0.988. A 12 s ambient pad, then 16 s of 120 BPM clicks, then a 12 s pad gives 82 beats, 50 of them inside the 24 s of beatless pad, with confidence 0.935; with trim=True it gives 32 beats and none in the pads. Real click tracks score 0.92–0.99, so the field barely separates rhythmic from beatless audio.

**Failure scenario.** An ambient piece, or a song with a 30 s rubato piano intro: beats.times holds an evenly spaced grid through the intro, so every bar index there is invented, and tempo.confidence is about 0.8–0.9. Per the schema ('below ~0.5 treat the whole grid as advisory') a consumer treats the grid as reliable. M6 will snap section boundaries to these downbeats and compute cuts_per_bar and on_beat_cut_ratio against them.

**Proposal.** (1) Replace the CV heuristic with a measure of how strongly the onsets support the beats. One option is beat salience: the mean onset envelope within ±w of each beat divided by the mean at the half-beat midpoints, which is about 1 for beatless audio and maps to 0. Another is the prominence of the prior-weighted tempogram peak over the tempogram median. Document the formula in the schema description (C2). (2) Use trim=True, librosa's default. If a continuous grid is wanted for rendering, record which span was actually tracked (additive `beats.tracked_interval`, or per-beat strengths) so extrapolated beats can be told apart (H1). (3) State in the schema that confidence cannot detect half- or double-tempo errors. Tests: a drone or pink-noise mix gives confidence < 0.5; an ambient-intro fixture has no beats in the pad, or they are marked; the click track stays ≥ 0.8. Record the decision in OPEN_QUESTIONS (P2).

**Verifier's notes.** Traced features.py:167-183 together with the pinned beat.py. __beat_track_dp (tightness 100) penalises log-deviation from one global period, so its output is near-metronomic by construction. In __trim_beats, trim=False sets `threshold = 0.0`, so only beats over an exactly-zero local score are removed. trim=False appears nowhere else in the repo and has no rationale. My replica (e2.py) gives: drone 0.816, steady chord 0.823, white noise 0.746, pink noise 0.795, irregular bursts 0.491. A metronomic 100 BPM click scores only 0.877, and a 174 BPM click is tracked at 87.0 BPM with confidence 0.988. For 12 s pad | 16 s of 120 BPM clicks | 12 s pad: trim=False gives 82 beats, 49 of them inside the beatless pads, at confidence 0.937; trim=True gives 32 beats, none in the pads. These match the finding's numbers. The schema says 'below ~0.5 treat the whole grid as advisory' (audio_features.schema.json:23), but beatless material scores about 0.75–0.94. Beat grids are invented through rubato or ambient intros and fade-outs. M6 snaps section boundaries to these downbeats (PLAN §4 stage 6). Nothing in OPEN_QUESTIONS or the reviews records this.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposed beat-vs-half-beat-midpoint salience ratio would misfire on strongly rhythmic offbeat material. With 8th-note hats or shakers, offbeat house hats or a reggae skank, the midpoints carry onsets too, so the ratio goes to about 1 ("beatless"). A pure 8th-note click train tracked at 120 BPM would score as beatless. Use instead the beat-aligned onset energy relative to the envelope's overall mean, or to the grid shifted by 1/3 and 2/3 of a beat, or the prominence of the prior-weighted tempogram peak over the tempogram median. Prefer per-beat salience values (or a tracked span) to trim=True alone: trim only removes leading and trailing beats, so interior breakdowns stay filled. Both changes alter output. Make them FeaturesConfig members (e.g. `beat_trim`, confidence method) per review 006 F-1 so the config_hash moves. Document the formula and the half/double-tempo blind spot in the schema (C2), and add an OPEN_QUESTIONS note. Tests: pink noise and a drone score below 0.5, an ambient-intro fixture has no beats in the pad (or they are marked), and the click track stays at or above 0.8.

### F3. Degenerate inputs (silent mix, <400 ms)

#### FEAT-8: Degenerate inputs: a digitally silent mix writes bpm_global 0.0 (fails the schema, recorded ok), and inputs under 400 ms fail the whole stage inside pyloudnorm

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, S2, S5, T3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:166-171`, `mrw/features.py:202-206`, `mrw/features.py:216-223`, `mrw/features.py:366-376`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:16-20`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:54-57`, `mrw/models.py:109-111`

**Evidence.** librosa's beat_track returns `(0.0, np.array([], dtype=int))` when `not onset_envelope.any()` (pinned beat.py:227-229). features.py:170 turns that into 0.0, and line 204 writes `bpm_global: 0.0`. The schema declares `"exclusiveMinimum": 0` (audio_features.schema.json:16-18), and jsonschema rejects such a document ('0.0 is less than or equal to the minimum of 0'; checked with a script). The pydantic field `Tempo.bpm_global: float` (models.py:109-111) does not enforce this, and nothing validates before `library.write_document`, so an invalid document is recorded ok. Separately, pyloudnorm 0.2.0's `valid_audio` raises `ValueError("Audio must have length greater than the block size.")` when `data.shape[0] < block_size * rate` (util.py:32-33), i.e. under 17,640 samples. features.py:220 hits it, and the generic handler (366-376) fails the whole stage even though every other feature can be computed. The −70.0 clamp for a silent programme (features.py:221-223) is documented only in the module docstring, not in the schema's integrated_lufs description.

**Failure scenario.** (a) A video whose audio track is digital silence, or a silent render: stems separate to silence (M2 handles that) and `mrw features` exits 0. audio_features.json has bpm_global 0.0, beats.times [] and downbeat_offset 0, so the document fails its own schema, and a consumer computing 60 / bpm_global divides by zero. (b) A 0.3 s sting: `mrw features` exits 1 with 'Audio must have length greater than the block size.', and lyrics can never run.

**Proposal.** Define and enforce the conventions (H2). For an empty beat grid, either relax bpm_global to `minimum: 0` with '0.0 = no tempo detected; beats.times empty, confidence 0.0' (schema bump, example and README updated), or keep the schema and raise a specific FeaturesError ('mix has no onset energy') before writing. For inputs shorter than one block, measure loudness over a single block spanning the signal (`pyloudnorm.Meter(SAMPLE_RATE, block_size=n / SAMPLE_RATE)`) and document it. Move the −70.0 silent-programme convention into the schema description. In tests, validate the silent-mix and 0.3 s documents against the schema.

**Verifier's notes.** (a) With digital silence the mel power is 0, so power_to_db is a flat −100 dB, the envelope is exactly 0, and the pinned beat.py:227-229 returns `(0.0, np.array([], dtype=int))`. features.py:170/204 then writes `bpm_global: 0.0`. I checked with jsonschema (e7.py): '0.0 is less than or equal to the minimum of 0'. `Tempo.bpm_global: float` (models.py:110) has no constraint, and library.write_document (library.py:50-55) does not validate, so the invalid document is recorded ok. Ingest has no silence or duration guard, and stems deliberately supports silent input (stems.py:103-111). (b) pyloudnorm 0.2.0 `valid_audio` raises 'Audio must have length greater than the block size.' for fewer than 17,640 samples (util.py:32-33). The generic handler (features.py:368-376) then fails the whole stage, and lyrics is refused as a result. Both inputs are edge cases, but H2 requires a defined convention and (a) ships a contract-violating document, so medium stands.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. If bpm_global is relaxed to `minimum: 0`, treat it as a contract change for consumers that divide by it: bump the schema with the documented convention ('0.0 = no tempo detected; beats.times empty, confidence 0.0'). Alternatively, keep the schema and raise a specific FeaturesError. Move the −70.0 silent-programme convention into the integrated_lufs description, and validate the silent-mix and 0.3 s documents in tests.

#### DET-11: A silent mix produces bpm_global 0.0, which violates the schema's exclusiveMinimum: 0, and there is no defined convention for this case

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, S1
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:162-170`, `mrw/features.py:202-206`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json`

**Evidence.** The code calls `bpm, beat_frames = librosa.beat.beat_track(onset_envelope=env, ...)` and then `bpm = float(np.atleast_1d(bpm)[0])` (features.py:167-170). Verified in the 0.11.0 source: librosa (locked 0.11.0) returns `(0.0, np.array([], dtype=int))` when `not onset_envelope.any()`. That is exactly the digital-silence case: power_to_db of an all-zero mel spectrogram is constant, so the onset envelope is all zeros. `Tempo(bpm_global=canonical.round_bpm(bpm), ...)` (features.py:203-206) then writes 0.0. The schema requires `"exclusiveMinimum": 0` for tempo.bpm_global, and validating the example with bpm_global=0.0 reports '0.0 is less than or equal to the minimum of 0' (det/probe_nan_validate.py). Every other field in this document has a defined silence convention (LUFS clamped to -70, all-zero centroid, the strength_reference sentinel, confidence 0.0); tempo does not.

**Failure scenario.** The user ingests a file whose audio track is digital silence: a muted screen recording or video export, a silent interlude track, or the existing M2 silent fixture. They run stems and then features. The stage exits 0, audio_features.json contains `"bpm_global": 0.0` and fails schema validation, and a consumer computing the beat period as 60/bpm divides by zero.

**Proposal.** Define the degenerate-case convention explicitly, following the R-9 precedent. When the mix has no onset evidence (beat_frames is empty), relax the schema to `minimum: 0` and document: 'bpm_global 0.0 = no beat evidence (silent or beatless mix); beats.times is empty and confidence is 0.0; consumers must not divide by it'. Ship it as audio_features 1.1.0 and state the convention in the field description. Add a features test on a fabricated all-silent mix that validates the resulting document.

**Verifier's notes.** I checked the librosa 0.11.0 source of beat_track: `if not onset_envelope.any():` returns `(0.0, np.array([], dtype=int))` when sparse. For exact digital silence the mel power is constant after power_to_db, so the onset envelope is all zeros. features.py:167-170 then yields bpm 0.0, and `Tempo(bpm_global=canonical.round_bpm(bpm), ...)` (features.py:203-206) writes it; the pydantic Tempo model has no bound. Validating the example with that tempo block gives '0.0 is less than or equal to the minimum of 0' (exclusiveMinimum at audio_features.schema.json:18). The check is in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/det-verify/schema_checks.py. Silent input separates fine (tests/test_m2_smoke.py:184-203), so the chain reaches features, and no test runs features on a silent mix. Only exact digital silence triggers it; dither or noise produces a positive tempo. Medium: an edge case, but a document that fails its own schema with no H2 convention.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Relaxing exclusiveMinimum to minimum admits a value consumers were promised could not occur. Argue the bump level explicitly, following review 007's finding-3 dispute precedent; 'minor' rests on 'no external consumers yet'. The contract-preserving alternative is to fail the features stage loudly on a digitally silent mix (H3: refuse rather than improvise). Either way, add the silent-mix features test with schema validation.

#### SCH-9: Degenerate beat grids break the schema and leave structure's beat indices undefined

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H2, C1, S3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:166-213`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:12-48`, `schemas/structure.schema.json:30-37`, `schemas/structure.schema.json:79-93`

**Evidence.** audio_features.schema.json:16-19 requires `bpm_global` with `exclusiveMinimum: 0`, but features.py:204 writes `bpm_global=canonical.round_bpm(bpm)` straight from librosa. I assume, with about 75% confidence, that librosa 0.11's beat_track returns early with tempo 0 and no beats when the onset envelope is all zeros, giving `bpm_global: 0.0` for a digitally silent mix. With fewer than 4 beats, features.py:192-200 sets `downbeat_offset = 0` but still records `downbeat_method="assumed_4_4_phase_fit"` (211), although no fit ran. With zero beats, 'Index into times of the first downbeat' (schema line 41) points into an empty array. structure.schema.json (M6 is next) requires `start_beat_index` and `start_bar_index` (30-37) and per-cut `nearest_beat_index` (84-87) as integers ≥ 0, with no rule for an empty grid. It also doesn't say which beat a section starting before the first beat maps to: the example's intro starts at 0.0 but beat 0 is at 0.612. Nor does it define the bar count behind `cuts_per_bar`; the example only works with fractional bars of beats/4. No M3 test uses a silent or very short mix.

**Failure scenario.** A silent interlude or hidden-track file, or a clip under 2 s, run through `mrw features` gets bpm_global 0.0. The resulting audio_features.json is schema-invalid but recorded as ok, with `beats: {times: [], downbeat_offset: 0}`. A Swift consumer doing `beats.times[beats.downbeatOffset]` crashes with index out of range. M6 then has no valid value for the required beat indices of that track's sections.

**Proposal.** Define the H2 conventions now. Either relax bpm_global to `minimum: 0` with '0.0 = no tempo detected (beats.times empty)' (a relaxation, so a minor bump), or add `downbeat_method: "none"` for grids shorter than beats_per_bar. Document that downbeat_offset is meaningful only when len(times) > downbeat_offset. For structure, which has no compatibility cost yet: make the beat/bar indices optional and absent when the grid is empty; define start_beat_index as 'first beat with time >= start_seconds' and state the clamp rule; define cuts_per_bar's denominator. Add a silent-mix fixture to test_m3 that validates against the schema.

**Verifier's notes.** I read librosa 0.11.0's source (the uv.lock pin) as text. beat.py:227-229: `if not onset_envelope.any(): if sparse: return (0.0, np.array([], dtype=int))`. onset_strength of digital silence is all zeros: power_to_db gives a constant, then diff, then max(0, ·) (onset.py:584-609). features.py:170/204 therefore writes bpm_global 0.0, which fails `exclusiveMinimum: 0` (verified with jsonschema), and the stage records ok. With fewer than 4 beats, features.py:192-200 sets downbeat_offset 0 under method 'assumed_4_4_phase_fit' with no fit. With zero beats the offset indexes an empty array, and no convention is documented (H2). Structure (M6 next) requires the beat/bar indices with no empty-grid rule, no rule for sections before beat 0, and no cuts_per_bar denominator. No M3 test uses a silent mix (only silent stems). Correction: the 'clip under 2 s' trigger is wrong. A non-silent short clip has a non-zero envelope and gets a non-zero tempo; only an all-zero envelope yields 0.0. A silent mix is a rare edge case, so medium per the rubric.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Relaxing `exclusiveMinimum` to `minimum` weakens a consumer guarantee: consumers computing 60/bpm would divide by zero. Under S2's consumer view that is breaking, not a minor bump. Prefer the honest non-breaking route: when beat_track returns no beats, fail the features stage (recorded failed, H3) with 'no beat grid detected (silent mix)'. Alternatively take a deliberate major bump. Document downbeat_offset as valid only when len(times) > downbeat_offset. Write the M6 conventions now (start_beat_index = first beat ≥ start_seconds, clamped; indices absent on an empty grid; the cuts_per_bar denominator). Add a silent-mix fixture.

### F4. Downbeat fit on the full mix

#### FEAT-4: Downbeat phase fit on the full-band mix lands on the snare backbeat in common patterns, and the document gives no measure of how ambiguous the choice was

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1, C2, T3, S2, S5, P2
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:185-200`, `mrw/features.py:166`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:43-47`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:50-67`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:158-164`, `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:42-50`

**Evidence.** The code computes `at_beats = np.array([float(env[max(0, f - w) : f + w + 1].max()) for f in beat_frames]); phase_means = [float(at_beats[p::4].mean()) for p in range(4)]; downbeat_offset = int(np.argmax(phase_means))` (features.py:194-198) over the full-band mix envelope (166). That envelope is the mean over 128 mel bands of positive dB flux (FEAT-1), so broadband snare and clap hits outweigh kicks, whose energy sits in a few low bands. Results from the numpy re-implementation on synthetic 120 BPM drum patterns: (a) Rock backbeat (kick on 1 and 3, snare on 2 and 4, 8th-note hats): the downbeat is chosen on beat 2 (phase means [27.4, 24.1, 29.2, 22.5], 6% margin). (b) Half-time (kick on 1, snare on 3): beat 3, 10% margin. (c) Four-on-the-floor with claps on 2 and 4: beat 2, 4.5% margin. Restricting the same envelope to mel bands below 150 Hz picks the true '1' for both the backbeat and half-time patterns. The M3 fixture marks downbeats by amplitude (`amp = 0.9 if k % 4 == 0 else 0.45`, test_m3_smoke.py:64), which is the one case the heuristic is designed for. OQ-4 accepted a phase-fit estimate but does not discuss this systematic bias, and nothing in the document reports the margin.

**Failure scenario.** Typical 4/4 pop/rock, the target material OQ-4 names, gets a downbeat_offset one beat late, so every bar index for the track is shifted by a beat. M6 snaps section boundaries 'to the nearest downbeat' and computes cuts_per_bar on that grid. For four-on-the-floor dance music the choice is close to a coin flip, yet the document carries only the generic `assumed_4_4_phase_fit` label.

**Proposal.** Fit the phase on a low-frequency rhythm envelope from the stems already on hand. Use onset_strength of the drums stem restricted to low mel bands (kick) plus the bass stem's envelope, each normalised, and fall back to the mix only if both are silent. Emit an additive `beats.downbeat_confidence`, for example (best − second best) / best phase mean at 3 dp, so consumers can tell a clear fit from a coin flip (schema 1.1.0, example updated). Add backbeat and half-time fixtures that assert the downbeat lands on the kick. Record the change under OQ-4; it is still a phase-fit estimate.

**Verifier's notes.** The described state is accurate. The phase fit uses the full-band mix envelope (features.py:166, 194-198), only `assumed_4_4_phase_fit` is emitted, and no margin is reported. The M3 fixture marks downbeats by amplitude (test_m3_smoke.py:64). My replica (e3.py) with synthetic kick, snare and hat patterns reproduces the bias for drums-only mixes: rock backbeat → beat 4 (5.6% margin), four-on-the-floor plus claps on 2 and 4 → beat 4 (4.2%), half-time → beat 3 (7.9%). However, adding one bass note on each '1' flips all three variants to the correct downbeat, with margins of 0.2–8.8%. So 'typical pop/rock lands a beat late' holds only for drum-dominated mixes. On real music, bass and chord changes on the 1 counteract it, and that cannot be verified offline. What holds robustly is that the choice is a near coin-flip in every case (margins under ~9%), and the document gives no ambiguity measure. OQ-4 accepted 'approximate' bar indices but did not discuss this. Medium, because bar indices feed M6 section snapping and cuts_per_bar.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Land the cheap honest part first: an additive `beats.downbeat_confidence` = (best − second-best)/best phase mean at 3 dp (schema 1.1.0, example updated, C2 description). Then evaluate a stems-based fit envelope (bass-stem onsets plus the drums stem below about 150 Hz) on a few real tracks before switching. In my replica the low band alone fixes the rock and half-time patterns but cannot separate four-on-the-floor without bass. Add backbeat fixtures with and without a bass line, and record the change under OQ-4.

### F5. Onset-strength normalisation saturates

#### FEAT-5: Onset strengths normalised by p98 over all envelope frames depend on onset density: channels with about 1 onset/s or fewer saturate every strength at 1.0, and the documented raw-value recovery fails

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, C2, S2, S5, P2 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:130-135`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:122-131`, `docs/reviews/001-phase0-plan.md:22-33`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:184-191`

**Evidence.** The code computes `reference = float(np.percentile(env, 98))`, floors it with `reference = max(canonical.round_ratio(reference), 0.001)`, then sets `strengths = np.clip(env[frames] / reference, 0.0, 1.0)` (features.py:130-135). A percentile over all frames tracks the onset-peak level only when peaks fill more than 2% of frames. I tested 1500 Hz bursts of random amplitude (0.05–0.9, a 25 dB spread) over a −70 dBFS floor in the re-implementation. At 0.5 and 1 onset/s every strength is exactly 1.0; at 2/s, 22% are; at 4/s, none. The repo's own M3 leading-silence fixture (vocals = 2 s silence + 3 s tone, test_m3_smoke.py:186) shows the same: the vocals channel gets p98 ≈ 0.0023, so strength_reference is 0.002, while its raw onset values are about 9.2 and 6.9. Both strengths are therefore 1.0. The schema's 'Raw strength of onset i = strengths[i] * strength_reference' (line 130) is off by roughly 4500×. The code comment blames 'whisper-quiet channels whose p98 would round to 0.000' (131-133), but the envelope does not depend on level (FEAT-1); sparsity is the cause. The 0.001 floor is not in the schema. Review 001 R-1 chose frame-p98 so one outlier accent could not compress the rest; dependence on onset density was not considered.

**Failure scenario.** Sparse channels such as a vocal stem with a few phrases per verse, a bass stem of sustained notes, or crash cymbals: every onset reads strength 1.0. R-1's motivating rule 'drum strength > 0.6' is always true, and accents cannot be told apart from ghost notes. strength_reference becomes a tiny number unrelated to the onsets.

**Proposal.** Normalise by a robust percentile of the detected onsets' own raw values, for example `reference = np.percentile(env[frames], 90)`. That is still robust to one outlier accent, which was R-1's goal, and does not depend on how many frames fall between onsets. Keep the clamp. Document the reference definition and any floor in the schema; this changes strength_reference's meaning, so bump the schema version and record the R-1 revision in OPEN_QUESTIONS (P2). Add a sparse-channel test: 0.5 onset/s with graded amplitudes must produce graded strengths.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced features.py:130-135: `reference = float(np.percentile(env, 98))`, then `max(round_ratio(reference), 0.001)` and a clip. My replica (e4.py) used 1500 Hz bursts at random amplitudes 0.05–0.9. At 0.5/s the reference is 0.079 (with a −70 dB floor) or 0.013 (no floor), and 100% of strengths are 1.0. At 1/s 86% are 1.0, at 2/s 9–10%. At 4/s none reach 1.0 and the maximum is 0.77; peak_pick's post_max=1 has no lookahead, so the picked frame often sits before the envelope peak that the p98 tracks. At 8/s 26% are 1.0 again. In the M3 leading-silence fixture vocals channel, the raw onset values are 9.18 and 6.93 and the p98 is 0.00229, so strength_reference is 0.002 and the strengths are [1.0, 1.0]; these match the finding. Strength scale therefore depends on onset density and is not monotone in it, which defeats R-1's stated goal ('drum strength > 0.6 means roughly the same thing everywhere'). Density was not considered in R-1 or review 002, so already_known=true is justified by a new argument. One sub-claim is weak. The '≈4500× recoverability' error concerns clamped values, which are unrecoverable by design (the example notes 'The 1.0 is a clamped above-reference accent'). The real defect there is that schema line 130 states the recovery formula with no 'unclamped only' caveat.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: reference = a robust percentile of the detected onsets' own raw values (e.g. p90 of env[frames]), keep the clamp, and document the reference definition and any floor. Also amend audio_features.schema.json:130 to say raw recovery holds only for strengths < 1. This changes strength_reference semantics: bump the schema version, record the R-1 revision in OPEN_QUESTIONS (P2), and trigger staleness (see FEAT-2). Tests at 0.5/s and 4/s with graded amplitudes must give graded strengths.

### F6. Stems edge cases (6-source models, clipping, downmix)

#### CRIT-4: stems.model accepts 6-source Demucs models: htdemucs_6s writes guitar/piano stems that nothing reads, and features analyses an 'other' stem that silently excludes them

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H3, S4
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:28`, `mrw/stems.py:27`, `mrw/stems.py:128-137`, `mrw/stems.py:155-158`, `mrw/features.py:289-291`, `mrw/features.py:322-325`, `mrw/models.py:137-141`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:63`, `PLAN.md:494-495`

**Evidence.** The model is free-form: mrw/config.py:28 `model: str = "htdemucs"`. mrw/stems.py:128 `for name, tensor in zip(model.sources, sources):` writes one FLAC per model source.

Every consumer uses the fixed four names:
- stems.py:27 `STEM_NAMES = ("vocals", "drums", "bass", "other")`
- the no-op check (stems.py:157)
- the features prerequisite (features.py:289-291) and loop (features.py:322 `for name in STEM_NAMES:`)
- the fixed StemFeatures model (models.py:137-141), matching the schema's 'Fixed four Demucs stems' (audio_features.schema.json:63)

The Demucs 4.0.1 README (line 219 of the sdist, whose sha256 e45a5a78... matches uv.lock) says: '`htdemucs_6s`: 6 sources version of `htdemucs`, with `piano` and `guitar` being added as sources.' PLAN.md:494-495 expects model variants to be opt-in config. Nothing validates model.sources, and audio_features.json does not record which model produced the stems.

Related to ORCH-5 (config accepts values the pipeline cannot use), but this value does run, and the output is silently different.

**Failure scenario.** With `[stems] model = "htdemucs_6s"`, stems/ contains vocals, drums, bass, other, guitar and piano. Features passes its checks and analyses other.flac, which now excludes guitar and piano. On a guitar-and-keys track, audio_features.stems.other is near-silent with few onsets. The document validates, the status is ok, and nothing flags the change; the only trace is a different stems config_hash in the manifest.

**Proposal.** - In _separate, before any work, check `set(model.sources)`. Accept exactly STEM_NAMES. For 6-source models, either fold guitar and piano into other (documented, configurable) or raise StemsError naming the supported models.
- Alternatively, make StemsConfig.model a Literal of the supported 4-source models.
- Record the separation model in audio_features as a small params block (S4).
- Add a unit test with a stub model exposing six sources.

**Verifier's notes.** The model setting is free-form (config.py:28), and stems.py:128 writes one FLAC per entry in model.sources. Everything downstream is fixed to STEM_NAMES:
- the no-op check (stems.py:155-158)
- the features prerequisite (features.py:289-291) and loop (:322)
- StemFeatures (models.py:137-141)

The Demucs 4.0.1 sdist (sha256 e45a5a78…, matching uv.lock) ships demucs/remote/htdemucs_6s.yaml, and README:219 lists piano and guitar as the added sources. With htdemucs_6s, features therefore analyses an 'other' stem that excludes guitar and piano, the two extra stems are never analysed, and nothing records the model.

This is opt-in config, so medium (not higher) is right.

A compounding effect: changing the model changes only the stems hash. The features no-op check (features.py:298-305) compares only the features hash, so an existing track's audio_features.json is not even refreshed after re-separation.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Validate at the config boundary, following the precedent at config.py:88-90. Accept only models whose sources equal STEM_NAMES (htdemucs, htdemucs_ft, hdemucs_mmi, mdx, mdx_extra, mdx_q, mdx_extra_q), so a bad value exits 2 before any weight download.
- Keep a runtime check of set(model.sources) after get_model in _separate as a backstop.
- If 6-source models are wanted, fold guitar and piano into other.flac (sum the tensors before the int16 conversion), optionally keeping the extras as untracked files.
- Record the separation model in audio_features (additive, S4).
- Add a unit test with a stub model exposing six sources.

#### CRIT-9: Stem samples beyond full scale are hard-clamped to ±1.0 with no record (Demucs's own default is to rescale), so loud masters get silently clipped stems

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/stems.py:103-106`, `mrw/stems.py:123-137`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:50-53`

**Evidence.** mrw/stems.py:123-137: after `sources = sources * ref_std + ref_mean`, each stem is written as `tensor.clamp(-1.0, 1.0).mul(32767.0).round().to(torch.int16)`. No peak or count is recorded. The stems block's warning field (manifest.schema.json:50-53) is used only for the MPS fallback.

The comment at stems.py:103 says the normalisation is 'replicated for parity with the CLI'. Demucs 4.0.1's CLI, however, rescales by default: separate.py:104 `--clip-mode` defaults to 'rescale', and audio.py:218-233 prevent_clip does `wav = wav / max(1.01 * wav.abs().max(), 1)`. I read these from the sdist, whose sha256 matches uv.lock.

Assumption (medium confidence): stems of loud masters peaking near 0 dBFS exceed ±1.0 at transients, because sources partly cancel in the mix; this is why Demucs rescales by default.

**Failure scenario.** A modern master peaking at 0 dBFS: drum and bass transients that reconstruct above full scale are flat-topped in the retained stems. This is audible if the iPad app reuses the stems (PLAN §4 stage 2), and onset strengths drop slightly. The manifest records stems as ok with no warning, so nobody knows the stems were altered.

**Proposal.** - Before clamping, compute each stem's peak and the number of samples beyond ±1.0.
- When non-zero, record it in stems.warning, e.g. 'drums: 1,204 samples clipped, peak +1.6 dBFS'.
- Optionally add `stems.clip_mode = "clamp" | "headroom"` (config, joins the hash). 'headroom' applies one fixed, recorded gain to all stems so they still sum to the mix.
- Correct the parity comment at stems.py:103.

**Verifier's notes.** The code matches the finding: stems.py:128-137 clamps to ±1.0 and records nothing.

From the Demucs 4.0.1 sdist (hash matches uv.lock):
- separate.py:104 sets --clip-mode default to 'rescale'.
- audio.py prevent_clip rescales each stem by its own peak.
- README:208-211 says output 'can need clipping, in particular due to some separation artifacts', and offers clamp as the option that keeps relative levels.

So clamping is a documented, legitimate choice. It is arguably the better one here: a per-stem rescale would move the vocal stem's absolute level, which the absolute-dBFS vocal_activity thresholds depend on.

The comment at stems.py:103 claims parity only for the normalisation, which is accurate; it needs no 'correction'.

How often real masters exceed ±1.0 cannot be checked offline, and the effect on the documents is negligible, so this is plausible at low severity.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Before clamping, count the samples beyond ±1.0 and the peak of each stem, and report any non-zero counts in the existing stems.warning field.
- Add one sentence near stems.py:103 saying that clamping (Demucs' --clip-mode clamp behaviour) is deliberate, to keep relative stem levels.
- A common-gain 'headroom' mode is optional.

#### CRIT-6: Multichannel sources are folded to stereo with ffmpeg's default matrix: the LFE is discarded, centre content drops 7.6 dB, and the downmix is not recorded

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, H2, C2
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:123-141`, `mrw/ingest.py:90-108`, `mrw/ingest.py:197-200`, `mrw/ingest.py:252-259`, `mrw/config.py:40-41`, `mrw/config.py:62`, `schemas/source.schema.json:27-41`

**Evidence.** mrw/ingest.py:132-136 (and the hash decode at :100-104) passes only `"-vn", "-ac", str(channels), "-ar", str(sample_rate)`. Multichannel input is therefore folded down by swresample's default matrix.

Measured with ffmpeg 6.1.1 (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/surround), using mrw's `-ac 2 -ar 44100 -sample_fmt s16`:
- A 5.1 file with a tone only in FC at -24.1 dBFS peak comes out at -31.7 dBFS.
- A tone only in LFE at -24.1 dBFS comes out as digital silence (-91 dB).

source.json records only media.audio.channels (ingest.py:252-259). Downstream thresholds are absolute dBFS: config.py:40-41 vocal_enter_db -35 / exit -45, and config.py:62 overlap -15. ORCH-13 covers which stream is chosen; the downmix itself is not covered by any finding.

**Failure scenario.** A 5.1 concert video (AAC or AC-3 5.1, lead vocal in the centre channel):
- The stereo working copy carries the vocal about 7.6 dB lower than a stereo master would.
- Quiet sung passages fall under the -35 dBFS vocal-activity enter threshold. Whisper's clip windows miss them, and they never appear, not even as untranscribed regions.
- The LFE's sub-bass is missing from the mix and the bass stem.
- Nothing in source.json says a downmix happened beyond 'channels: 6'.

**Proposal.** - Prefer an existing stereo stream when the file has one (fold this into ORCH-13's fix).
- Otherwise apply an explicit, documented downmix, e.g. `-af aresample=lfe_mix_level=0.5` or a fixed `pan=stereo|...` per input layout.
- Add the method to IngestConfig so it joins the ingest hash.
- Record `normalized_audio.downmix` (source layout and method) in source.json (additive minor).
- Test with a generated 5.1 fixture like the one above.

**Verifier's notes.** ingest.py:132-136 and :100-104 pass only -ac and -ar, and source.json records only channel counts.

I reproduced this with ffmpeg 6.1.1 on my own 5.1 fixtures, using mrw's flags (-ac 2, s16):

| Tone only in | Input peak | Stereo peak | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| FL | −42.1 dBFS | −49.8 dBFS | −7.7 dB |
| FC | −42.1 dBFS | −52.8 dBFS | −10.7 dB |
| LFE | −42.1 dBFS | digital silence | dropped |

The finding's '7.6 dB centre' figure is really the front-channel drop: the prior fixture (scratchpad/crit/surround/centre_only.wav) has its tone in channel 1 (FR), not FC. The real centre loss is larger.

Most of the drop comes from swresample normalising the downmix matrix to avoid clipping on s16 output. With float output, FL stays at −42.1 and FC is −45.2.

So every 5.1 source is analysed about 8–11 dB low against absolute thresholds (vocal_enter_db −35, cross-track LUFS), and quiet vocals can drop out of both vocal activity and the honesty ledger. This is not recorded anywhere. Low is kept because multichannel music input is rare.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Prefer an existing stereo stream when the file has one.
- Otherwise downmix explicitly in float with documented coefficients (BS.775 pan, with a stated LFE policy), then apply a fixed, recorded gain or a deterministic limiter before s16. Note that `-af aresample=lfe_mix_level=0.5` alone makes things worse: I measured FL −8.9 dB and FC −12.0 dB, because mixing in the LFE increases the normalisation.
- Record the source layout, method and applied gain in source.json normalized_audio (additive), and put the method in IngestConfig.
- Test with a generated 5.1 fixture.

### F7. Precision and semantics polish

#### FEAT-6: bpm_global is limited to the tempogram's discrete bins (6000/lag BPM at the 10 ms hop), with errors up to about 1.5% where the tracked grid is accurate; event times all sit on the 10 ms grid

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D3, C2, T1, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:166-171`, `mrw/features.py:204`, `mrw/features.py:134`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:38`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:147`, `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:144-148`

**Evidence.** bpm is taken verbatim from beat_track (features.py:167-170) and written as `bpm_global=canonical.round_bpm(bpm)` (204). In the pinned librosa source the estimate is `tempo_est = np.take(bpms, best_period)` (feature/rhythm.py:452) over `bin_frequencies[1:] = 60.0 * sr / (hop_length * np.arange(1.0, n_bins))` (core/convert.py:1765), with no interpolation. At sr/hop = 100 frames/s only 6000/lag BPM is representable: …117.6, 120.0, 122.4, 125.0, 127.7… near 120, and 166.7, 171.4, 176.5 near 174. In the re-implementation a 124 BPM click track gives bpm_global 125.0 (+1.0), 128 gives 127.7 and 140 gives 139.5. A least-squares fit to the tracked beats gives 123.99, 127.99 and 139.98. The M3 fixture's 120 BPM is exactly 6000/50 (test_m3_smoke.py:38, 147), so the test cannot see this. Every beat and onset time is `frames_to_time` of an integer frame (features.py:134, 171), i.e. a multiple of 0.01 s. OQ-14 justified 3 dp on the grounds that '1 ms is far below any consumer's resolution', but the actual resolution is 10 ms.

**Failure scenario.** A 124 BPM house track reports bpm_global 125.0. A renderer that free-runs a clock or LFO from this 'single representative tempo' drifts 0.8%, about 2 s over four minutes, against the audio. A 174 BPM track tracked at full tempo reports 171.4 or 176.5, about 1.5% off. The 1-dp contract implies ±0.05 BPM.

**Proposal.** When at least 3 beats exist, set bpm_global = 60 / slope of a least-squares fit of beat_times against beat index. This is unaffected by the 10 ms frame grid and matches the beat grid by construction. Otherwise keep the tempogram value, or refine it by parabolic interpolation around the peak. State in the schema description how bpm_global is derived. Optionally refine onset and beat times below the hop by parabolic interpolation on the onset envelope, or document that event times lie on the 10 ms grid. Add 124 and 133 BPM click fixtures with ±0.3 BPM tolerance.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed in the pinned source. rhythm.py takes `tempo_est = np.take(bpms, best_period)` over `60*sr/(hop*lag)` bins with no interpolation, and features.py:170/204 publishes that value verbatim. My replica (e5.py) gives 122→122.4, 124→125.0, 126→125.0, 128→127.7, 133→133.3, 140→139.5 and 90→89.6. The least-squares beat-grid tempo is 121.99, 123.99, 125.99, 127.99, 132.98, 139.98 and 90.05. Event times are frames_to_time of integer frames, i.e. multiples of 10 ms. Downgraded to low. The beat grid, which the schema makes authoritative ('Tempo drift shows up in the beat grid itself, not here'), is accurate. Nothing in the pipeline consumes bpm_global: lyrics does not, and M6 is designed around beats. Ten-millisecond event resolution is below the AV-sync tolerance in OQ-12 (50–100 ms) and comparable to one 120 fps frame. Still a cheap, worthwhile accuracy fix.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: bpm_global = 60 / slope of a least-squares fit of beat_times against beat index when there are at least 3 beats, otherwise the tempogram value. State the derivation in the schema, and add 124 and 133 BPM click fixtures (±0.3 BPM). It changes output, so pair it with a config or version event (FEAT-2).

#### FEAT-7: Series length and timeline contract drift: series carry 1 + ⌊N/441⌋ values, not the documented ⌈duration×100⌉, the M3 length check was never written, and vocal regions running to the end get end_seconds > duration_seconds

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, S5, H2, C1
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:96`, `mrw/features.py:106-108`, `mrw/features.py:252-253`, `mrw/features.py:263-267`, `PLAN.md:525`, `schemas/README.md:71`, `schemas/examples/audio_features.json:2`, `schemas/examples/audio_features.json:22`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:204-211`, `mrw/lyrics.py:495-506`

**Evidence.** rms and centroid use centred frames with zero padding (`librosa.feature.rms(..., center=True)`, features.py:96; spectral_centroid with center=True, 106-108; pad_mode is 'constant' for both in librosa 0.11). That gives 1 + N//441 values centred at 0, 10 ms, …, up to the duration. So `start_seconds=0.0` (features.py:146) is exactly right, but the count is ⌈N/441⌉ + 1 whenever N is a multiple of 441. The 20.000 s M3 fixture (N = 882000) gives 2001 values and the 242.5 s example 24,251. The docs say otherwise: PLAN.md:525 'RMS series length = ⌈duration × 100⌉', schemas/README.md:71 '24,250 points', and the example notes say '24,250 values'. No test checks length, although it is in PLAN §11's M3 smoke list (T1). In _vocal_activity, a span still active at the last frame closes at `spans.append([start, len(vocal_rms_db)])` (252-253). It is written as `end_seconds=canonical.round_seconds(e * HOP_SECONDS)` (267) = (1 + N//441)·0.01, which always exceeds N/44100 by (441 − N mod 441)/44100, a value in (0, 10 ms]. The repo's own fixture triggers it: test_m3_smoke.py:186-189 runs a tone to the end of a 5.000 s file, so the region ends at 5.01 in a document whose duration_seconds is 5.0. The assertion `abs(region["end_seconds"] - 5.0) < 0.15` (211) hides this. Lyrics clamps its own times to [0, duration] ('timeline validity is ours', lyrics.py:495-506) but uses features' regions without clamping.

**Failure scenario.** A 30 s excerpt that ends mid-chorus: vocal_activity.regions[-1].end_seconds = 30.01 while duration_seconds = 30.0. If lyrics leaves that final region uncovered, lyrics.json emits an untranscribed_region ending at 30.01, outside the timeline. A Swift consumer that pre-allocates ⌈duration × 100⌉ samples, as PLAN and the README say, is one short for every duration that is a whole number of 10 ms steps.

**Proposal.** Pass the sample count into _vocal_activity and write `min(e * HOP_SECONDS, n_samples / SAMPLE_RATE)`, then round. Fix PLAN §11, schemas/README.md and the example notes to say 'len(values) = ⌊n_samples / 441⌋ + 1 (frames centred at i·hop, the last at or before the duration)', and tell consumers to use the array length. Add the missing smoke assertions: every series has 1 + n//441 values; all channels have equal length (raise FeaturesError if a stem's sample count differs from the mix's); every region, onset and beat time is ≤ duration_seconds.

**Verifier's notes.** librosa rms and stft/centroid with center=True and pad_mode='constant' give 1 + N//441 frames (replica: N=882000 → 2001, N=882001 → 2001). That equals the documented ⌈N/441⌉ except when N % 441 == 0. Exact-10 ms durations are exactly what the synthetic fixtures and the 242.5 s example have, so PLAN.md:525, schemas/README.md:71 and the example notes are off by one only there. In `_vocal_activity`, a span still open at the end closes at `len(vocal_rms_db)` (features.py:252-253). The replica gives a region of 2.0–5.01 in the 5.000 s fixture; the test tolerance of 0.15 (test_m3_smoke.py:211) hides it. lyrics.uncovered_spans uses the region's r_end as-is (lyrics.py:345-347), so a fully uncovered final region would inherit the overshoot. No test asserts series length, although PLAN §11 lists it (T1). Low: the overshoot is at most 10 ms, the interval schema has no duration bound, timeseries are 'clamped at the ends', and consumers use the array length.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: clamp end_seconds to n_samples/SAMPLE_RATE in _vocal_activity, fix the length wording in PLAN §11, the README and the example notes, and add the length, equal-channel-length and ≤ duration assertions to the M3 smoke test.

#### TEST-11: The M3 smoke test omits the RMS-length assertion, and PLAN's ⌈duration×100⌉ disagrees with the implementation's ⌊n/441⌋+1; the beat check tolerates 10 % outliers and 25 % missed beats

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, S5
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:522-526`, `mrw/features.py:92-98`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:144-164`, `schemas/examples/audio_features.json:22`, `schemas/README.md:71`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:524-526: '...beat times within 30 ms of ground truth; RMS series length = ⌈duration × 100⌉; document byte-identical across two runs.' No test asserts any series length; test_m3_smoke.py:195 only slices `cent[:150]`. features.py:96 calls `librosa.feature.rms(y=y, frame_length=N_FFT, hop_length=HOP, center=True)`. With center=True, librosa pads frame_length//2 on each side, giving 1 + ⌊n/441⌋ frames (high confidence). The 20 s fixture therefore yields 2001 values against ⌈20×100⌉ = 2000. The schema example (duration 242.5 s, n = 10,694,250 = 24,250 × 441) has `"_note": "24,250 values in the real document."`, and schemas/README.md:71 says '24,250 points'; the implementation emits 24,251. The PLAN formula and the code agree only when n is not a multiple of 441, so the specified assertion would fail on every current fixture, since all their durations are whole multiples of 10 ms. Beats: L152 `assert len(times) >= 30` against 40 true clicks, and L155-156 require median ≤ 30 ms and `(dist <= 0.030).mean() >= 0.9`, with distance taken modulo the click period instead of pairing detections with true clicks.

**Failure scenario.** A consumer that validates or indexes series using PLAN's formula rejects every document whose duration is a whole number of 10 ms steps. A padding regression such as switching to center=False (⌊(n−2048)/441⌋+1 frames, shifting frame centres by 23 ms) changes every series length and still passes, because the VA-region checks allow ±0.15 s.

**Proposal.** Decide the contract. The implementation's ⌊duration×100⌋+1, sampling t = 0 … duration inclusive, is the natural fencepost. Fix PLAN §11, schemas/README.md:71 and the example note, and state the rule in common.schema.json's timeseries description. Assert `len(values) == n_samples // 441 + 1` for the mix and every stem (rms and centroid), and that all series in a document share one length. For beats, match each true click to its nearest detected beat: all within 30 ms (or record the 90 % tolerance) and recall ≥ 95 %.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. librosa rms and stft with center=True pad n_fft//2 on each side, giving 1 + n//441 frames (high confidence). A 20 s track therefore yields 2001 values against PLAN's ceil(20 x 100) = 2000. The 242.5 s example (n = 10,694,250 = 24,250 x 441) yields 24,251, while its note says 24,250 (schemas/examples/audio_features.json:22) and schemas/README.md:71 says '24,250 points'. No test asserts any series length. The beat check allows 25% missed and 10% off-grid beats, with modulo matching (test_m3_smoke.py:152-156). I lowered severity to low. The implementation's fencepost (samples at t = 0 to duration inclusive) is consistent with the timeseries convention (values[i] at start + i*hop), and consumers index by len(values). The drift is in illustrative docs and one smoke assertion. Review 006 recorded beats 'within one hop' in practice, so the loose tolerance is a test-strength issue only.

#### FEAT-11: mean_rms_db averages dB values, including floor-clamped frames from merged gaps, so it can understate a region's level by ~18 dB; the schema also leaves the field optional

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** C2, S1, S5
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:263-272`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:88-98`, `mrw/models.py:151-154`, `mrw.toml:3-4`

**Evidence.** `mean_rms_db=canonical.round_db(float(np.mean(vocal_rms_db[s:e])))` (features.py:268) averages dB values over the merged span, gap frames included, and those are often at the −80.0 floor. I replicated the algorithm with two 1 s regions at −20 dBFS separated by a 0.99 s gap, under the operator's current mrw.toml setting `vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`. They merge into one region with mean_rms_db −39.87, while the power mean is −21.75 dBFS. −39.87 is even below the −35 enter threshold that opened the region. The schema calls the field 'Mean vocal-stem RMS within the region, dBFS' (line 96). Also, the regions item schema has no `required` list beyond the interval's start and end, so mean_rms_db is optional in the contract. The model makes it mandatory (`mean_rms_db: float`, models.py:154). That is an S1 mismatch, and a Swift author following the schema would decode it as Double?.

**Failure scenario.** Review 007 finding 4(c) compared untranscribed spans against 'their track's all-region mean RMS'. Any region with an internal merged gap reads up to ~18 dB quieter than the singing in it, which skews exactly that comparison.

**Proposal.** Average in the power domain over active frames only, i.e. frames ≥ exit_db within the span: `10*log10(mean(10**(v/10)))`. State the definition in the schema. Add `"required": ["start_seconds", "end_seconds", "mean_rms_db"]` to the regions item, since the field is always emitted. The value change needs a staleness trigger (FEAT-2).

**Verifier's notes.** features.py:268 averages dB values over the merged span `[s:e]`, gap frames included. In a replica of the copied algorithm (e6.py), two 1 s regions at −20 dB separated by a 0.99 s floor gap, at the operator's min_gap 1.0 (mrw.toml:4), merge into one region with mean_rms_db −39.87. The power mean is −21.75. The schema's regions item has no `required` entry for mean_rms_db; jsonschema accepts a region without it. VocalRegion makes the field mandatory (models.py:154). That is a small S1 optionality mismatch. Low: the field is descriptive and nothing in the pipeline consumes it.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: a power-domain mean over active frames (≥ exit_db) within the span, the definition stated in the schema, and `required: [start_seconds, end_seconds, mean_rms_db]` added to the regions item. The value change needs a staleness event (FEAT-2).

#### FEAT-12: The vocal-activity procedure that makes regions reproducible is not documented, a single short blip can join two regions when min_gap is large, and FeaturesConfig accepts inverted or negative values

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** docs · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S4, C2, H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:243-272`, `mrw/config.py:37-46`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:76-87`, `mrw.toml:3-4`

**Evidence.** PR #6 round 3 made regions 'derivable from the document's own envelope + params', but the procedure exists only in code. Entry is `v >= enter_db` and exit is `v < exit_db` (features.py:247-249). Gaps are merged first, using the strict test `span[0] - merged[-1][1] < max_gap` (258). Spans are then dropped unless `e - s >= min_len` (271). Ends are exclusive frame indices. The schema says only 'Regions closer than this are merged' (line 85). Because merging happens before the length filter, a blip shorter than min_region can act as a stepping stone. In a replica with the current min_gap of 1.0 s, regions 1.0–3.0 and 4.45–7.0 stay separate, but one 50 ms spike at 3.5 s joins them into a single 1.0–7.0 region (mean −34.08). FeaturesConfig has no validators (config.py:40-43). Negative min_region or min_gap values would be written into params that violate the schema's `minimum: 0`, and `vocal_enter_db < vocal_exit_db` silently turns the hysteresis into toggling. LyricsConfig already validates at the config boundary (`clip_padding_seconds: float = Field(default=0.5, ge=0)`, config.py:78).

**Failure scenario.** With min_gap 1.0, a breath or bleed spike between two verses joins them across an instrumental gap. Lyrics then treats the gap as vocal activity in its clip windows, coverage and untranscribed spans. A consumer comparing regions across tracks cannot reproduce them from params without reading features.py.

**Proposal.** State the exact procedure in the vocal_activity description: frame-level hysteresis on rms_db with ≥ enter and < exit; spans closer than min_gap merged first, then spans shorter than min_region dropped; ends exclusive and clamped to the duration. Consider dropping isolated spans shorter than min_region before merging, or document why not. Add `Field(ge=0)` to both durations and a model validator requiring `vocal_enter_db >= vocal_exit_db`.

**Verifier's notes.** The procedure lives only in code: entry `v >= enter_db`, exit `v < exit_db` (features.py:247-249), merge with strict `< max_gap` (258), then the length filter (271). The schema says only 'Regions closer than this are merged' (audio_features.schema.json:85). The replica (e6.py) confirms that at min_gap 1.0 a 50 ms blip at 3.5 s joins 1.0–3.0 and 4.45–7.0 into one region, 1.0–7.0. Merging before filtering is standard VAD post-processing, though, so documenting it is the main value. FeaturesConfig has no validators apart from phase_fit_window_seconds (config.py:40-46). A negative min_gap produces a params block that fails the schema (jsonschema: '-0.5 is less than the minimum of 0'). The LyricsConfig precedent is `Field(default=0.5, ge=0)` at config.py:78.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Document the exact procedure in the vocal_activity description, and add `Field(ge=0)` to both durations plus a model validator requiring `vocal_enter_db >= vocal_exit_db` (validation only; the config_hash is unchanged). Keep merge-then-filter: dropping short spans before merging would discard short sung syllables that legitimately join phrases. State that order instead.

#### FEAT-13: The centroid's silence decisions use the unrounded float32 RMS, so they cannot be reproduced from the published rms_db and can contradict it

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, D3
- **Locations:** `mrw/features.py:101-117`, `mrw/features.py:152-157`, `mrw/features.py:327-333`

**Evidence.** `_channel_features` passes the raw float32 output of `_rms_db` to `_centroid_hz` (features.py:153-154). That function tests `rms_db <= SILENCE_FLOOR_DB + 1e-9` (109) and `silent.all()` (110), while the document publishes the values rounded by `canonical.round_db` (156). A frame at −79.996 dBFS therefore counts as valid for the centroid but is published as −80.0. vocal_activity was deliberately switched to the rounded series for exactly this reproducibility reason (features.py:327-333, PR #6 round 3).

**Failure scenario.** Take a near-silent stem whose loudest frame is −79.996 dBFS. It publishes rms_db = −80.0 everywhere but does not count as 'silent throughout'. Its centroid series therefore wanders between noise centroids instead of being the documented all-0.0. A consumer applying the schema's 'check rms_db before using centroid' rule sees data that contradicts the R-9 sentence.

**Proposal.** Round first (`rms = np.round(_rms_db(y), 2)`, or the canonical rounder) and pass the rounded series to `_centroid_hz`, and to any onset gate added for FEAT-1. Every silence decision then follows from published values.

**Verifier's notes.** `_channel_features` passes the raw float32 `_rms_db(y)` output to `_centroid_hz` (features.py:153-154), while the document publishes `canonical.round_db` values (156). `silent = rms_db <= SILENCE_FLOOR_DB + 1e-9` (109) is evaluated on unrounded float32, so a frame at −79.996 is valid for the centroid but published as −80.0. vocal_activity was deliberately moved to the rounded series for this reproducibility reason (features.py:327-333). The affected band is about 0.005 dB wide, so it breaks the whole-channel R-9 statement only if a channel's loudest frame lands there. Consumers following 'check rms_db before using centroid' are unaffected. A real but negligible inconsistency with a one-line fix.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: round once (`rms = np.array([canonical.round_db(v) for v in _rms_db(y)])` or `np.round(…, 2)`) and drive the centroid silence test, and any onset gate from FEAT-1, from the published values.

## G. Schemas and serialization

### G1. Non-finite floats are written as invalid JSON

#### ARCH-7: The canonical serializer does not reject non-finite floats: NaN or Infinity would be written as invalid JSON

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D4, C1, S3, H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/canonical.py:37`, `mrw/models.py:31`

**Evidence.** canonical.py:39 serializes with `json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, sort_keys=False)`, and json.dumps defaults to allow_nan=True. Documents reach it through models.py:31-33 (`model.model_dump(mode="json", exclude_none=True)`), and pydantic float fields accept NaN. I checked this standalone with pydantic 2.13.5 and stdlib json, without importing mrw: a model with `mean_rms_db=float('nan')` dumps in json mode as `nan`, and json.dumps writes `NaN`; `-inf` becomes `-Infinity`. Python's json.loads and jsonschema's `type: number` both accept these values, so the existing schema-validation tests would still pass. Swift's JSONDecoder rejects non-finite numbers by default (C1). Today individual stages guard case by case (features.py:221-223 clamps -inf LUFS; features.py:131-133 puts a floor under strength_reference) rather than at the single write path. M6 adds arithmetic that easily produces NaN: `on_beat_cut_ratio` for a single-shot video is 0/0, and the numpy mean of an empty array is nan.

**Failure scenario.** M6 computes `on_beat_cut_ratio = float(np.mean(np.abs(offsets) <= window))` on a static-image 'official audio' video, which has one shot and zero cuts. The result is nan, and structure.json is written with `"on_beat_cut_ratio": NaN`. The stage records ok, the double-run test is byte-identical (NaN prints the same each time), and the schema test passes. The iPad app's JSONDecoder then fails to decode the whole document.

**Proposal.** Pass `allow_nan=False` in canonical.dumps, so any non-finite value fails the stage loudly (recorded as failed, exit 1). That matches PLAN §8's 'fail loudly, no fallback masking' rule. Optionally set `model_config = ConfigDict(allow_inf_nan=False)` on a shared base model for documents, so the error names the offending field. Add a unit test that canonical.write raises on NaN and inf. Then define the empty-input conventions explicitly for M6 (ARCH-10).

**Verifier's notes.** Verified standalone on pydantic 2.13.5: model_dump(mode='json') keeps nan and inf as Python floats, and json.dumps (allow_nan defaults to True, canonical.py:39) writes NaN and -Infinity. jsonschema's 'number' type accepts them, so the schema tests would pass. There is also a path today beyond the hypothetical M6 case. TOML allows inf/nan, and the config validators accept them. `[features] vocal_exit_db = -inf` or `vocal_enter_db = nan` flows through round_db into vocal_activity.params (features.py:233-242), so audio_features.json gets -Infinity or NaN. `[video] detector_threshold = inf` is embedded unrounded in Detector.threshold (video.py:498-502); this assumes ContentDetector accepts an inf threshold, which is likely. Swift's JSONDecoder rejects these documents. Medium per 'edge-case bugs'.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** allow_nan=False in canonical.dumps alone is risky. stage_hash serializes config through canonical.dumps (config.py:131-134), so a config like `caption_budget_usd_per_run = inf` — a natural way to say 'no cap', and it works today — would raise an uncaught ValueError at video.py:329 (outside the try) and in `mrw status`. Do both: reject non-finite values at config load (allow_inf_nan=False on the config base, giving ConfigError and exit 2) and on document models, then add allow_nan=False in canonical.dumps as the backstop. Add the unit test.

#### DET-7: The serializer and models let NaN, ±Infinity and -0.0 through, and the precision contract is enforced only by convention (Detector fields written unrounded)

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D3, D4, C1, H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/canonical.py:17-39`, `mrw/models.py:31-33`, `mrw/config.py:37-46`, `mrw/config.py:85-98`, `mrw/features.py:233-242`, `mrw/video.py:497-502`, `mrw/ingest.py:209-210`, `mrw/lyrics.py:472-475`

**Evidence.** `json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, sort_keys=False)` (canonical.py:39) uses the default allow_nan=True. The helpers `round(float(x), n)` (canonical.py:17-34) pass nan and inf through and return -0.0 for tiny negatives. No model sets allow_inf_nan=False. I checked with pydantic 2.13.5 (lock: 2.13.4) in scratchpad det/probe_pydantic.py and det/probe_nan_validate.py: TOML `nan` and `inf` are accepted even by `Field(gt=0)` and `ge=0` floats; `model_dump(mode="json", exclude_none=True)` keeps them; and the canonical settings emit the bare tokens NaN, Infinity and -Infinity. Python's `json.loads` (used by lyrics.py:472-475 and by the tests) parses those tokens back. Draft 2020-12 validation of the audio_features example with bpm_global=NaN, integrated_lufs=-inf and enter_db=inf reported zero errors. So neither the pipeline nor the test suite would notice, while Swift's JSONDecoder rejects bare NaN/Infinity as invalid JSON. Config values reach documents verbatim: `round_db(fcfg.vocal_exit_db)` goes into vocal_activity.params (features.py:233-242), and `threshold=vcfg.detector_threshold, min_shot_seconds=vcfg.min_shot_seconds` (video.py:497-502) are written raw. The second is also unrounded seconds, a repeat of the PR #6 round-1 params finding. ingest.py:210 uses a bare `round(fps, 3)` instead of a helper. I traced every float field in the default-config pipeline and found no NaN source today: LUFS -inf is clamped, and the empty-list and zero-length guards are in place. This is a missing safety net, and M6's ratios (cuts_per_bar, on_beat_cut_ratio) are typical numpy 0/0 sources.

**Failure scenario.** A user writes `[features] vocal_exit_db = -inf` (a natural way to say 'never close a region') or `[video] detector_threshold = inf` ('one shot per video'). The stage succeeds and writes `"exit_db": -Infinity` into audio_features.json or `"threshold": Infinity` into video.json. mrw's own readers and the schema tests accept the file, but the Swift app cannot decode the document at all. Separately, a near-full-scale synthetic frame, with RMS within 0.005 dB of 0 dBFS, writes rms_db as `-0.0`.

**Proposal.** (1) In canonical.dumps, pass `allow_nan=False`. A non-finite value then raises inside the stage's try block and is recorded as failed. Output bytes for finite values don't change, so config_hashes don't churn. (2) Make the rounding helpers reject non-finite input and normalize negative zero, e.g. `return round(float(x), n) + 0.0`. (3) Enforce the precision contract in the models instead of at ~40 call sites: `Seconds = Annotated[float, AfterValidator(canonical.round_seconds)]`, plus `Db`, `Hz`, `Bpm` and `Ratio`, and `ConfigDict(allow_inf_nan=False)` on document models. That would have caught the Detector fields and the bare fps round. (4) Parse documents internally with a strict loader (`json.loads(text, parse_constant=_reject)`). Add tests that `dumps({'x': float('nan')})` raises and that a TOML `-inf` config is rejected at load (with DET-5). Land this before M6.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed in substance. canonical.dumps uses json.dumps' default allow_nan=True (canonical.py:39), and the rounders pass nan and ±inf through (canonical.py:17-34). A pydantic 2.13.5 probe (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/det-verify/pyd.py, pyd2.py) showed that unconstrained floats accept TOML nan and inf. gt/ge-constrained fields accept inf but REJECT nan, so the finding overstates that part. doc_dump keeps -inf, and the canonical text emits a bare `-Infinity` or `Infinity`, which an RFC-strict parse rejects. Today this is reachable only through user config on the embedded unconstrained floats: vocal_enter_db and vocal_exit_db (features.py:233-242) and detector_threshold (video.py:500). An inf in min_shot_seconds or the gap/region fields overflows in int(round(...)) and fails the stage instead. I found no computational NaN source. `Detector.min_shot_seconds` is written unrounded (video.py:501), the same class of D3 slip as PR #6 round 1. -0.0 is valid JSON. Low: the trigger needs a deliberately non-finite config value, and the consumer failure would be loud, not silent.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Ship the S subset. Use allow_nan=False in canonical.dumps. Add allow_inf_nan=False (or finite validators) on the config models together with DET-5; this is needed anyway because stage_hash also goes through canonical.dumps and would otherwise turn a non-finite config into a traceback. Route Detector.min_shot_seconds through round_seconds; the default 0.4 serializes identically, so nothing churns. Defer the Annotated precision types and the strict loader: that is a ~40-call-site refactor whose benefit is speculative before M6.

#### SCH-7: No global numeric safety net: NaN/±Infinity are written as non-JSON tokens that tests accept, and -0.0 survives rounding

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D3, D4, C1, H2
- **Locations:** `mrw/canonical.py:17-39`, `mrw/models.py:12`, `mrw/config.py:37-46`, `mrw/config.py:85-88`, `mrw/video.py:498-502`, `mrw/cli.py:28-33`

**Evidence.** canonical.py:39 `return json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, sort_keys=False) + "\n"` leaves allow_nan at its default of True. The document models are plain BaseModel (models.py:12) without `allow_inf_nan=False`. Verified with the system python (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/pydantic_probe.py case 1): model_dump(mode="json") passes NaN and -inf through, and json.dumps writes `NaN` and `-Infinity`. jsonschema's Draft202012Validator reported NO error for {"x": NaN} against {type: number, minimum: 0, maximum: 1}, so the smoke tests' schema checks cannot catch it. Config floats also accept TOML's `nan`/`inf` literals (tomllib parses them, verified), and some go straight into documents, e.g. video.py:500 `threshold=vcfg.detector_threshold`. cli.py:28-33 turns only FileNotFoundError into exit 2. The round_* helpers return -0.0 for small negatives: round(-0.0004, 3) is written as `-0.0` (verified). For example, structure.json's cut at 14.09 against the beat at 14.0904 would be written `-0.0` where the example shows 0.0. Individual call sites are guarded today (the LUFS -inf clamp, the onset sentinel, the Demucs isfinite check), but there is no global check. M6's ratios, such as cuts per bar for a zero-bar section, are the next likely NaN source.

**Failure scenario.** `[video] detector_threshold = nan` (or `[features] vocal_enter_db = -inf`) in mrw.toml loads without error. The stage writes video.json containing `"threshold": NaN` (or audio_features.json containing `"enter_db": -Infinity`). The smoke-test validator passes and the stage reports ok. The Swift app's JSONDecoder then fails on the entire document, since Foundation's default nonConformingFloatDecodingStrategy is .throw. A future M6 division by zero would fail the same way, silently.

**Proposal.** Pass `allow_nan=False` in canonical.dumps so any non-finite value fails the write and the stage is recorded as failed (H3). Add a shared `class DocModel(BaseModel): model_config = ConfigDict(allow_inf_nan=False)` for all document models and the config models, so bad TOML values become load-time errors.
