Make `_load_config` map pydantic.ValidationError and tomllib.TOMLDecodeError to exit 2. Normalise negative zero in the rounders: `r = round(float(x), n); return r + 0.0` (IEEE -0.0 + 0.0 == +0.0). Add unit tests for NaN refusal and -0.0 normalisation.

**Verifier's notes.** All mechanics verified (probe.py). model_dump(mode='json') passes nan/-inf through. json.dumps with default allow_nan writes `NaN`/`-Infinity`. Draft202012Validator reports no error for NaN against minimum 0 / maximum 1, and json.loads accepts the tokens. tomllib parses nan/-inf, and VideoConfig accepts detector_threshold=nan (min_shot_seconds' gt=0 rejects nan). round(-0.0004,3) serialises as `-0.0`. Downgraded to low. I found no computed NaN or ±inf path in normal use: LUFS has an isfinite clamp, the onset reference is floored, Demucs has an isfinite guard, coverage/flagged/confidence ratios are zero-guarded, and VA means are over non-empty spans. The remaining triggers are self-inflicted `nan`/`inf` literals in mrw.toml and a speculative future M6 division. -0.0 needs a near-zero signed value; current fields are non-negative, or rms_db would need ≈0 dBFS. The genuinely reachable bug inside the proposal is different: `_load_config` (cli.py:28-33) catches only FileNotFoundError. Any config typo (pydantic ValidationError) or malformed TOML (TOMLDecodeError) therefore exits 1 with a traceback instead of 2 (PLAN §8).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep the cheap safety nets: `allow_nan=False` in canonical.dumps, `allow_inf_nan=False` on document and config models, and `+ 0.0` in the rounders. Prioritise the exit-code fix: map pydantic.ValidationError and tomllib.TOMLDecodeError to exit 2 in `_load_config`, with a T4 test (e.g. `caption_backend = "antropic"` → exit 2).

### G2. No test enforces the contract

#### TEST-10: No test enforces the schema/serialization contract (S1, S5, D3, D4): open schemas can't catch drift, and examples, precision and canonical format are untested; canonical.py also writes NaN/Infinity and -0.0

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S1, S5, D3, D4
- **Locations:** `schemas/README.md:46-48`, `mrw/canonical.py:17-39`, `mrw/cli.py:317-334`, `mrw/config.py:131-134`, `mrw/video.py:498-502`, `schemas/common.schema.json:20-28`

**Evidence.** My script over all 7 schemas found no object with additionalProperties:false (deliberately, for `_` documentation keys, schemas/README.md:46-48). So every `_validate` helper (e.g. test_m3_smoke.py:119-130) accepts documents with extra or renamed optional keys, and JSON Schema never checks key order, which canonical.py:3 describes as 'fixed key order (pydantic field order)'. S1 lockstep holds today (my AST comparison of 40 model classes against schema property order found 0 mismatches), but only because reviewers keep it so. export-schemas (cli.py:332) covers only source and manifest, and nothing compares its output. No test validates schemas/examples/*.json; all 6 validate today under jsonschema 4.26. The precision contract isn't encoded in the schemas (common.schema.json: `"seconds": {"type": "number", "minimum": 0}`), and no test checks decimal places, which double-run tests can't see. Example: video.py:498-502 writes `threshold=vcfg.detector_threshold, min_shot_seconds=vcfg.min_shot_seconds` straight from config (harmless at the defaults), whereas features.py:229-236 rounds its params per the PR #6 D3 finding. canonical.py has no unit tests. `dumps` (L37-39) uses json.dumps defaults (allow_nan=True); with system pydantic 2.13, `model_dump(mode="json")` keeps nan/inf, so the output contains `NaN`/`Infinity`, which is invalid JSON for Swift's JSONDecoder. `round(-0.0004, 3)` is -0.0 and serializes as '-0.0'. There is no golden stage_hash test (config.py:131-134).

**Failure scenario.** All 62 tests still pass after any of these: adding an undeclared field to Shot, reordering Shot's fields (the key order Swift and LLM readers see), switching `round_db` to 3 dp, or changing `dumps` to indent=4 (both runs change identically). Jitter around zero, e.g. M6's signed cut offsets for on-beat cuts, will flip '0.0' and '-0.0' bytes. An accidental config field reorder silently mass-stales every library.

**Proposal.** Add a fast tests/test_contracts.py that needs no ffmpeg. (1) Lockstep: walk each model's model_fields alongside the schema's properties (resolving $ref) and assert identical ordered key lists; my scratch lockstep.py is a ready prototype. (2) Validate every schemas/examples/*.json. (3) An `assert_precision(doc)` walker keyed on field names (`*_seconds` 3 dp; `*_db`, `*_lufs` and rms values 2 dp; `*_hz` 1 dp; `bpm*` 1 dp; confidence/ratio/proportion/strength 3 dp), applied to the examples and to documents produced by the existing smoke tests. (4) A canonical golden text for a fixed document (non-ASCII, nesting, trailing newline); require `dumps(float('nan'))` to raise (set allow_nan=False) and make the rounders normalize -0.0 (`+ 0.0`). (5) Golden stage_hash values for the default configs, with a comment that changing them mass-stales every library.

**Verifier's notes.** I verified this with a scratchpad script (schema_check.py). No schema sets additionalProperties:false. All six examples validate under jsonschema 4.26. An AST walk of models.py against schema property order (72 object pairs, with $refs resolved) finds 0 mismatches today, so S1 holds only by review discipline. No test references canonical, the examples, or export-schemas, which covers only source and manifest (cli.py:332). common.schema.json:20-24 does not encode precision. video.py:498-502 writes the detector's threshold and min_shot_seconds unrounded, whereas features.py:229-236 rounds its params. canonical.dumps (canonical.py:37-39) uses allow_nan=True, and pydantic 2.13's model_dump(mode='json') keeps nan and inf, so NaN or Infinity would be written. No current producer emits them: LUFS is clamped and stems have an isfinite guard. round(-0.0004, 3) serializes as '-0.0'. Those two sub-points are defense-in-depth, but the core contract-test gap is real.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Do the S pieces first: the lockstep test, example validation, allow_nan=False in canonical.dumps, and -0.0 normalization in the rounders. The field-name precision walker is the M part. Also route video.py's Detector params through canonical rounding (min_shot_seconds to 3 dp), as features.py does.

#### SCH-6: S1/S5 lockstep is unenforced: no model-vs-schema test, examples never validated in CI, schemas accept undeclared keys, and export-schemas covers 2 of 5 models

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S1, S2, S5
- **Locations:** `mrw/cli.py:317-334`, `schemas/common.schema.json:6-10`, `tests/test_m0_smoke.py:77-90`, `.gitignore:7`, `PLAN.md:408`, `CLAUDE.md:42-44`

**Evidence.** export-schemas knows only two models. cli.py:329-332 has `from .models import Manifest, SourceDocument` and `for name, model in [("source", SourceDocument), ("manifest", Manifest)]:`. AudioFeaturesDocument, LyricsDocument and VideoDocument (M3-M5) were never added. The output goes to the gitignored schemas/generated/ (.gitignore:7), and no test or script compares it with anything. Even if run, pydantic's output can't be compared with the hand-drafted files. A toy model (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/pydantic_probe.py case 6) shows what it produces: `anyOf: [{...}, {type: null}]` plus `default: null` for every Optional (contradicting absent-not-null), a `title` on every property, defaulted fields such as schema_version not required, and none of the patterns or bounds. No test references schemas/examples/ or compares model_fields with schema properties (grep). The smoke tests validate produced documents, but all 45 object nodes across the seven schemas leave additionalProperties unset, so a model field missing from the schema still passes. No schema pins its own version: schema_version is only a semver pattern (common.schema.json:6-10). The 1.1.0 lyrics schema therefore accepts a document claiming 9.9.9, and nothing ties LYRICS_SCHEMA_VERSION to the schema file. PLAN.md:408 chose model-exported schemas precisely because hand-written ones 'drift from the code that writes documents'. CLAUDE.md:42-44 reversed that choice without adding a check. An AST comparison (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/lockstep.py) shows field names and order currently match for all five models. However, required/enum drift already exists (SCH-11), and all six examples validate yet are numerically inconsistent (SCH-8).

**Failure scenario.** A contributor adds `LyricsLine.syllables`, reorders flags/words, or makes `confidence` required in models.py without touching lyrics.schema.json. The whole suite still passes. Produced lyrics.json validates because extra keys are allowed, nothing compares field order, and export-schemas doesn't export LyricsDocument. The Swift consumer built from the reviewed contract never learns the field exists, or meets a key-order change in the canonical bytes. The drift is caught only if a human reviewer notices.

**Proposal.** Add a fast tests/test_schema_contract.py that needs no ffmpeg or torch. (1) For each model/schema pair, walk `Model.model_fields` in order against the resolved schema `properties`, following $ref and allOf through a local referencing.Registry. Assert identical names and order, Optional-or-default ⇔ not required, `Literal` args == `enum`, and recurse into nested models; /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/lockstep.py is a working prototype. (2) Add `"const": "<version>"` to each document schema's schema_version and assert it equals the matching *_SCHEMA_VERSION constant. (3) Validate every schemas/examples/*.json against its schema, ignoring `_` keys. (4) In the smoke tests, validate produced documents against a strict copy of each schema with `additionalProperties: false` injected at every object node; the published schemas stay open for lenient consumers. Then either extend export-schemas to all five models with normalisation (drop title/default, collapse anyOf-null) or retire it in favour of the test, and update PLAN.md §6.

**Verifier's notes.** Every premise checks out. export-schemas lists only `("source", SourceDocument), ("manifest", Manifest)` (cli.py:329-332). Its output goes to the gitignored schemas/generated/ (.gitignore:7). pydantic's export emits anyOf-null + default null + titles and omits defaulted schema_version from required (verified), so it can't be diffed with the hand-drafted files anyway. No test touches schemas/examples (grep). My own census: 45 object nodes across the seven schemas, none with additionalProperties or unevaluatedProperties. schema_version is only a pattern in every document schema. My independent AST comparison (lockstep_verify.py) agrees that names and order match for all five models and finds exactly SCH-11's four required/enum drifts. Review 003 approved export-schemas as the diff tool at M0, but it was never extended for M3-M5. Proportionate: a fast test of a few hours guarding the project's stated primary contract (S1/S5).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Two corrections to the proposal. (2) Do not add `const` to schema_version. It would make the current schema reject older-minor documents (e.g. lyrics 1.0.0 docs still in a library) that the additive policy says remain valid. Instead pin the major in the pattern (`^1\.\d+\.\d+$`), carry the current version as an annotation in each schema (e.g. `x-current-version`) that a test compares with *_SCHEMA_VERSION, and assert produced documents carry the constant. (4) Naive `additionalProperties: false` injection, or `unevaluatedProperties: false` inside the shared interval def, gives 6 false positives on the valid audio_features example (`mean_rms_db`), because VA regions apply common interval through allOf (strict_probe.py). The working recipe (strict_probe2.py): inject `unevaluatedProperties: false` at use sites (nodes with properties/$ref/allOf), skip direct allOf members, and leave common.schema.json $defs untouched. It gives 0 false positives on all six examples and catches undeclared keys in a lyrics line, a VA region, a timeseries and video_span. (1) and (3) stand as proposed.

### G3. Configurable sample rate and channels contradict the schema

#### SCH-10: Configurable ingest.sample_rate/channels contradict the schema's fixed 44100/2 and can permanently break a track

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:20-23`, `mrw/ingest.py:197-200`, `mrw/ingest.py:241-247`, `mrw/ingest.py:176-183`, `schemas/source.schema.json:33-34`, `mrw/models.py:46-51`, `mrw/features.py:86-88`, `mrw/stems.py:94-103`

**Evidence.** source.schema.json:33-34 fixes `"sample_rate": { "type": "integer", "const": 44100 }` and `"channels": { "type": "integer", "const": 2 }`. features hard-codes SAMPLE_RATE = 44100 (features.py:63; 87-88 raises '... expected 44100'), and stems requires the model's rate and channel count (stems.py:94-103). Yet IngestConfig exposes `sample_rate: int = 44100` and `channels: int = 2` with no constraint (config.py:21-22). ingest normalises the audio with them (ingest.py:197-199) and writes them into NormalizedAudio (ingest.py:243-244), whose model fields are plain `int` (models.py:48-49). Once source is ok, ingest.py:176-183 skips the run regardless of config changes, so re-ingesting cannot repair the track.

**Failure scenario.** A user sets `[ingest] sample_rate = 48000`, a natural choice for 48 kHz video sources. `mrw ingest` succeeds and writes a schema-invalid source.json (sample_rate 48000) and a 48 kHz source_audio.flac. `mrw stems` then fails with exit 1 ('... is 48000 Hz; model expects 44100'), recorded as a stage failure instead of a bad-config exit 2. Reverting the config doesn't help: `mrw ingest` reports 'already ingested — no-op' while `mrw status` shows source=stale. The track stays broken until its directory is deleted by hand.

**Proposal.** Make the schema's fixed values enforceable at both boundaries. Use `sample_rate: Literal[44100] = 44100` and `channels: Literal[2] = 2` in IngestConfig, or remove them from config as structural constants. Put the same Literals on NormalizedAudio in models.py. Make cli._load_config map pydantic.ValidationError and tomllib.TOMLDecodeError to exit 2. Optionally let ingest re-run when the stored ingest config_hash differs (see SCH-1).

**Verifier's notes.** config.py:21-22 exposes unconstrained `sample_rate: int = 44100` and `channels: int = 2`. ingest.py:197-199 normalises with them and ingest.py:243-244 records them in NormalizedAudio (plain int, models.py:48-49), but source.schema.json:33-34 fixes const 44100/2. With 48000: stems fails at stems.py:92-95 ('model expects 44100'). That is exit 1, recorded failed, where PLAN §8 says a bad config should exit 2. Reverting doesn't help: ingest.py:176-183 no-ops on source ok, while `mrw status` compares the ingest hash (cli.py:296-297) and shows source=stale. The track is unrecoverable short of deleting it. README.md:53-55 points users at mrw/config.py for config, so the knob is discoverable. Not recorded anywhere.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Use the Literal option, not removal. `Literal[44100]`/`Literal[2]` dump the same values, so the ingest config_hash is unchanged (verified: identical model_dump). Removing the fields would change the ingest subset hash, and because ingest no-ops, every existing track would then display source=stale permanently. Mirror the Literals on NormalizedAudio and map ValidationError to exit 2 in _load_config.

### G4. Manifest versioning on rewrite

#### SCH-5: Manifest rewrites keep the old on-disk schema_version, silently drop unknown keys, and crash on unknown enum values

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S2, S3, C1
- **Locations:** `mrw/library.py:40-48`, `mrw/models.py:12`, `mrw/models.py:22-28`, `mrw/models.py:340-348`, `mrw/cli.py:285-293`

**Evidence.** Every stage reads the manifest, changes one entry and writes it back. It reads with library.py:44 `return Manifest.model_validate_json(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))` and writes with library.py:48 `canonical.write(self.manifest_path(track_id), doc_dump(manifest))`. `schema_version: str = MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` (models.py:341) is only a default, so the value read from disk wins and nothing re-stamps it (grep: nothing ever assigns manifest.schema_version). The models are plain BaseModel (models.py:12), which means pydantic's default extra='ignore'. A toy model with the same pattern (pydantic 2.13; /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/pydantic_probe.py case 5) shows an unknown key is dropped on read-and-write while the on-disk version string survives. The Literal enums (Status at models.py:28, device at models.py:309) reject unknown values on read. No CLI command catches pydantic.ValidationError: cli.py:290-291 calls `lib.read_manifest(track_id)` in a loop without a guard, and the stage commands catch only PrerequisiteError and their stage errors.

**Failure scenario.** (a) A track ingested at M4 (manifest 1.2.0) is processed by the current build: `mrw video` writes documents.video.run.api_usage, a 1.3.0 field, into a file still labelled 1.2.0. After the first breaking (2.0.0) manifest change, this same path would produce 2.x-shaped manifests labelled 1.x, which a version-gated Swift decoder would decode wrongly. (b) A newer mrw adds a manifest field (1.4.0). Running any stage with this build drops that field and rewrites the file, still labelled 1.4.0. (c) One manifest containing a status or device value this build doesn't know (from a newer writer or a hand edit) makes `mrw status` abort with a traceback for the whole library. mrw's own reader breaks the S3 lenient-decoding rule it requires of consumers.

**Proposal.** In Library.write_manifest, if the on-disk version's (major, minor) is newer than MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION, refuse with PrerequisiteError ('manifest written by a newer mrw'). Otherwise set `manifest.schema_version = MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` before dumping. Give the manifest models `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="allow")` so unknown keys survive a rewrite. Turn read_manifest's ValidationError into a PrerequisiteError (exit 2), and make `mrw status` print '<track>: unreadable manifest (...)' and continue. Tests: a 1.0.0 fixture manifest is labelled 1.3.0 after `mrw features`; an unknown key survives a rewrite; an unknown status does not stop `mrw status`.

**Verifier's notes.** Behaviour verified with a mirror model (probe.py case 5). A manifest read as 1.2.0 and rewritten with a run block keeps '1.2.0', and an unknown key is dropped (plain BaseModel, extra='ignore'). Nothing assigns manifest.schema_version; grep shows only the default at models.py:341. An unknown status raises ValidationError (case 6), and cli.py:290-291 does not catch it, so `mrw status` aborts. Downgraded from medium. (a) is real for the operator's existing M2/M4-era manifests: anthropic-backend video runs write api_usage into a 1.2.0-labelled file. Under the additive-minor policy with lenient decoding that mislabel has no practical effect today. The major-bump hazard is speculative, and no consumer gates on the manifest version yet. (b) needs running an older build after a newer one; (c) needs a newer writer or a malformed hand edit. These are hygiene gaps, not normal-use failures.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Proposal is sound. Re-stamping is correct because the current model can only dump current-version fields. Re-stamp in Library.write_manifest, refuse on a newer major/minor, and map ValidationError to PrerequisiteError (exit 2). Make `mrw status` skip an unreadable manifest with a message. `extra='allow'` is optional: it also silently accepts mistyped kwargs in code, so keep it to the top-level Manifest model or merge unknown keys from the raw JSON instead.

#### DET-12: The manifest keeps the schema_version it was created with, so newer fields appear under older versions and older binaries drop fields they don't know

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S2, C1
- **Locations:** `mrw/models.py:22-26`, `mrw/models.py:340-348`, `mrw/library.py:40-48`

**Evidence.** `schema_version: str = MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` (models.py:341) is only the default for a newly created manifest. `read_manifest` restores the file's value through `Manifest.model_validate_json` (library.py:44), and `write_manifest` writes it back unchanged (library.py:46-48). A manifest created at ingest under 1.1.0 or 1.2.0 therefore keeps that version after the M5 video stage adds `run.api_usage`, a 1.3.0 field (models.py:25). Because pydantic's default is extra='ignore', a binary that predates a field drops it on its next rewrite.

**Failure scenario.** The operator's field tracks, ingested at M3/M4, now carry `"schema_version": "1.2.0"` alongside an api_usage block, which a 1.2.0 consumer doesn't know about. A decoder that gates on the version reads the wrong shape. If the operator checks out an older commit to bisect and runs any stage on such a track, it silently strips api_usage (the cost ledger) and stems.warning.

**Proposal.** In `Library.write_manifest`, stamp `manifest.schema_version = MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` when the file's version is at most the binary's (an additive minor upgrade on write). Refuse with exit 2 to rewrite a manifest whose version is newer than the binary knows. Set ConfigDict(extra='forbid') on the manifest models so an unknown field fails loudly instead of being dropped. Tests: a 1.2.0 manifest rewritten by a stage comes out as 1.3.0, and a 9.0.0 manifest is refused.

**Verifier's notes.** `schema_version: str = MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` (models.py:341) is only a construction default. read_manifest restores the stored value (library.py:44) and write_manifest writes it back unchanged (library.py:46-48). Only ingest builds a fresh Manifest (ingest.py:276-297). A manifest created at 1.1.0 or 1.2.0 therefore gains run.api_usage, a 1.3.0 field, without a version change. pydantic's default extra='ignore' also lets an older binary drop unknown fields on rewrite. Low: the fields are additive, S3 requires lenient decoders, and running old binaries against a newer library is rare. The proposal is sound; compare parsed semver, not strings.

### G5. Spec drift in schemas and examples

#### SCH-11: Specific mismatches between models.py and the hand-drafted schemas (required fields and one enum)

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S1, S3, C1
- **Locations:** `mrw/models.py:82-87`, `mrw/models.py:151-154`, `mrw/models.py:254-260`, `mrw/models.py:340-346`, `schemas/source.schema.json:84`, `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:88-98`, `schemas/lyrics.schema.json:48-61`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:7`

**Evidence.** I compared the models (parsed with ast, never imported) against the schemas with /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/lockstep.py. Field names and order match for all five document models, but there are four mismatches. (1) LyricsInput `line_count: int` and `has_timestamps: bool` are required in the model (models.py:86-87), but the schema's lyrics_input `"required": ["path", "format", "sha256"]` (source.schema.json:84) makes them optional. (2) VocalRegion `mean_rms_db: float` is required in the model (models.py:154). In the schema, the regions item gets `required` only from the allOf'd interval (start/end) and declares `"start_seconds": {}, "end_seconds": {}` as empty schemas (audio_features.schema.json:89-98). That allOf-plus-empty-schema pattern also degrades code generators, which type start/end as 'any' unless they merge allOf. (3) Manifest `created_at: str` is required in the model (models.py:346) but missing from the manifest's required list (manifest.schema.json:7). (4) lyrics flags are an enum in the schema (lyrics.schema.json:50-60) but `flags: list[str]` in the model (models.py:259), so a mistyped flag is written unchecked. All four lean the safe way (the model is stricter than the schema), so current output decodes under either.

**Failure scenario.** A Swift developer generating Codable types from the reviewed schema makes line_count, has_timestamps, mean_rms_db and created_at optional, although the producer always fills them. A producer typo such as `flags.append("low_confidnce")` passes the model and is caught only if a fixture happens to hit that path under schema validation.

**Proposal.** Tighten the schemas to match what is always written, as a documented minor bump with no producer change, since every existing document already complies. That means: lyrics_input required += line_count, has_timestamps; manifest required += created_at; for the regions item, inline `start_seconds`/`end_seconds` as `$ref` seconds with `required: [start_seconds, end_seconds, mean_rms_db]` and drop the allOf/{} construction. Type the model field as `flags: list[Literal[...]]`. Guard all of this with the SCH-6 test.

**Verifier's notes.** My own AST-vs-schema comparison (lockstep_verify.py) finds exactly these four, and nothing else beyond them: LyricsInput line_count/has_timestamps required in the model (models.py:86-87) but not in source.schema.json:84's required list; VocalRegion.mean_rms_db required in the model but absent from the regions item's required set, which comes only from the allOf'd interval with `"start_seconds": {}, "end_seconds": {}` placeholders; Manifest.created_at required in the model (models.py:346) but not in manifest.schema.json:7; lyrics flags an enum in the schema but `list[str]` at models.py:259. All lean the safe way (model stricter than schema), so the effect is only weaker generated Codable types and unchecked flag typos. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Inlining the regions item (no allOf/{} placeholders) also simplifies the strict-validation recipe in SCH-6. lyrics.json is never read back through LyricsLine, so typing flags as `list[Literal[...]]` is write-side only and safe.

#### SCH-8: The normative worked examples are internally inconsistent and differ from what the code produces

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S5, C2
- **Locations:** `schemas/examples/structure.json:84-120`, `schemas/examples/lyrics.json:13-88`, `schemas/examples/source.json:38-44`, `schemas/examples/manifest.json:33-63`, `schemas/examples/video.json:5-31`, `schemas/examples/audio_features.json:22`, `docs/reviews/002-schema-revisions.md:26-29`

**Evidence.** Review 002 (lines 26-29) declared the examples normative. All six validate (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/validate_examples.py), but /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/sch/example_consistency.py finds the following. STRUCTURE (the only spec M6 has for its numbers): the four listed intro cuts (3.104, 5.616, 8.601, 14.09; offsets -0.004 to 0.012, all within on_beat_window 0.07) contradict `{ "section_index": 0, "cuts_per_bar": 0.4, "on_beat_cut_ratio": 0.333 }`, which implies 3 cuts with 1 on-beat. The per-section counts sum to about 79 cuts and 40 on-beat, against the global 77 cuts and 0.481 (about 37). LYRICS (the honesty-ledger example): the unaligned bridge line 136.44-140.9 has no words and lies inside vocal region 136.44-158.2, yet untranscribed_regions lists only 200.48-208.91. Coverage 0.943 exceeds the 0.928 maximum implied by those two uncovered spans over 179.45 s of vocal activity. The unaligned line carries `"confidence": 0.18`, but align.py:161 sets confidence=None for unanchored lines, so the code omits it. Line confidences 0.912 and 0.641 differ from their word means of 0.914 and 0.640. SOURCE: `line_count: 42`, while lyrics has 42 lines plus 7 headers. ingest counts every non-empty line (ingest.py:149,154; test_m1_smoke.py:173), so the value would be at least 49. MANIFEST: it indexes lyrics at `"schema_version": "1.0.0"`, but the lyrics example is 1.1.0. Its api_usage usd of 0.1252 matches claude-haiku-4-5 prices (costs.py:25), while video.json names claude-opus-4-8, which would cost 0.6260. VIDEO: shot times are not derived from frames the way the code derives them (video.py:98 `canonical.round_seconds(s / fps)`). end_frame 74 gives 3.086, not 3.104. video_span ends at 242.5, but 5815 frames / 23.976 = 242.534. The representative frame at 1.552 would be snapped to 1.543 (video.py:390, 465). Motion values have 4 dp, which the code cannot emit (video.py:172-173). AUDIO_FEATURES: the `_note` says 24,250 values; the code produces 24,251.

**Failure scenario.** An M6 implementer, or an M6 golden test, reproduces structure.json's editing block and cannot match it under any bar-count convention. A prompt author who copies lyrics.json as the canonical 'honest' example teaches the LLM that an unaligned, word-less sung line does not appear in untranscribed_regions — exactly the silent omission the ledger exists to prevent. A Swift developer who treats source.lyrics_input.line_count as the number of lyric lines gets 49 from real output.

**Proposal.** Regenerate the examples from one consistent model of the track, or from a real pipeline run on a generated fixture that is then annotated by hand. Use M5's frame-derived times and 3-dp motion, list the lyrics entry at 1.1.0 in the manifest, and make the model and price match. Add the bridge span to untranscribed_regions and recompute coverage, drop confidence from the unaligned line, and compute the per-section aggregates from the listed cuts using a bar-count definition stated in structure.schema.json. Turn example_consistency.py's checks into tests/test_examples.py: sorted, non-overlapping intervals; parallel-array lengths; word means; coverage bounds; aggregate sums; matching versions and prices across examples. That makes S5 checked in CI.

**Verifier's notes.** All six examples validate (0 errors), but my own recomputation (examples_verify.py) reproduces each inconsistency. STRUCTURE: all four listed intro cuts are on-beat (offsets -0.004/+0.012/+0.002/-0.0004), against per_section[0] 0.4/0.333 (3 cuts, 1 on-beat). Per-section implied sums are about 79 cuts and 40 on-beat, against global 77 and 37. LYRICS: the unaligned 136.44-140.9 line has no words. The next line's words start at 140.9, its interpolated end. Under 1.1.0 that 4.46 s fragment must be listed, so the 0.943 coverage exceeds the 0.928 bound. The listed confidence 0.18 is something align.py:161 never emits. Word means are 0.914 and 0.640, not 0.912 and 0.641. SOURCE line_count 42 vs at least 49. MANIFEST lyrics entry 1.0.0 vs example 1.1.0; usd 0.1252 = haiku pricing while video.json names opus-4-8 (0.6260). VIDEO: frame-derived times 3.086/5.631/8.592/14.097 and span 242.534 (video.py:98); rep frames 1.543/4.338/10.427/12.262; motion at 4 dp. These are S5 violations, some introduced at specific changes: lyrics 1.1.0 sharpening (PR #10) and M5's frame-derived times. Rated low rather than medium: produced documents are correct, and the cost is reader confusion and docs drift, not wrong output.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Fix the numbers by hand (S-M). A generated fixture can't stand in for the 4-minute hypothetical track. Before M6, state the cuts_per_bar denominator in structure.schema.json (fractional bars = beats/4 is the only convention the example fits). Add a cheap tests/test_examples.py: schema validation ignoring `_` keys, sorted non-overlapping intervals, parallel-array lengths, word-mean line confidence, coverage upper bound from listed untranscribed spans, and version/price cross-references. This overlaps SCH-6 (3).

#### SCH-12: Schema descriptions written for LLM readers misstate or omit methods and units

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** docs · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** C2, H1
- **Locations:** `schemas/audio_features.schema.json:21-24`, `mrw/features.py:173-183`, `schemas/source.schema.json:89`, `schemas/video.schema.json:15-23`, `schemas/video.schema.json:52-62`, `schemas/video.schema.json:104-109`, `mrw/captions.py:133-138`

**Evidence.** tempo.confidence is described as 'Beat-tracker confidence; below ~0.5 treat the whole grid as advisory' (audio_features.schema.json:21-24). It is actually computed as regularity of the intervals between beats: features.py:180-181 `cv = float(intervals.std() / max(intervals.mean(), 1e-9))` and `confidence = max(0.0, min(1.0, 1.0 - 4.0 * cv))`. That measures steadiness, not correctness. lyrics_input.line_count has no description (source.schema.json:89), although it counts every non-empty line, including headers and LRC tags (ingest.py:149,154). detector.threshold is just `{ "type": "number" }` (video.schema.json:21), with no scale. representative_frames is documented as the midpoint or the 1/3 and 2/3 points (line 56), but the code snaps to a source frame: video.py:390 `min(int(round(t * fps)), e_frame - 1)`, then line 465. caption.tags are documented as 'Lowercase snake_case' (video.schema.json:108), but AnthropicBackend stores `[str(tag) for tag in parsed.get("tags", [])]` without normalising them (captions.py:137). The motion unit is stated only in the schema, never in the document itself.

**Failure scenario.** An LLM told 'confidence 0.97' trusts a perfectly steady grid that is at half tempo (60 instead of 120 BPM) or shifted in phase. The description promises tracker confidence, and the regularity measure scores such octave errors around 1.0. An LLM reading `"threshold": 27.0` or `"motion": {"mean": 0.004}` with no other context (C2's standard) can interpret neither. A Swift switch on caption tag 'close_up' misses 'Close-up'.

**Proposal.** Description-only updates, no version bump. tempo.confidence: '1 − 4·CV of inter-beat intervals, clamped to [0,1]; grid regularity, not correctness — octave and phase errors can score high'. line_count: 'non-empty lines in the supplied file, including header and LRC tag lines'. detector.threshold: 'PySceneDetect ContentDetector HSV content-delta threshold (default 27; higher ⇒ fewer cuts)'. time_seconds: 'time of the extracted source frame (target time snapped to the frame grid)'. Normalise tags in AnthropicBackend before caching (lowercase, non-alphanumerics → '_', strip, dedupe), or soften the claim. Consider an additive `unit` on motion so the document describes itself.

**Verifier's notes.** features.py:178-181 computes confidence as `1 - 4*CV` of inter-beat intervals, while audio_features.schema.json:21-24 calls it 'Beat-tracker confidence'. librosa's DP tracker enforces a near-constant period (tightness=100), so the metric scores near 1.0 for most grids, including octave- or phase-wrong ones. It is misleading for an LLM (C2). line_count has no description (source.schema.json:89), although ingest.py:149/154 counts every non-empty line, including headers and LRC ID tags. detector.threshold is a bare `{"type": "number"}` (video.schema.json:21). Representative times are snapped to frames (video.py:390, 465). captions.py:137 stores tags unnormalised despite 'Lowercase snake_case' (video.schema.json:108). Description-level issues: low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed, with one change: normalise tags when building the Shot (after the cache read), not only before caching. Otherwise captions already in .cache/captions keep unnormalised tags, because the cache key covers only frames, prompt_version and model.

### G6. canonical.write details

#### DET-13: canonical.write: no fsync, documents created with mode 0600, temp files never cleaned up, and the hash is computed over text rather than the bytes written

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D4
- **Locations:** `mrw/canonical.py:42-61`, `mrw/features.py:300-305`, `mrw/lyrics.py:472-475`, `mrw/ingest.py:123-141`

**Evidence.** `fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=path.parent, ...)` creates the temp file with mode 0600, and `os.replace` keeps that mode. Every document, the manifest and every caption-cache file therefore end up 0600, while the ffmpeg, soundfile, write_bytes and copyfile artifacts in the same track directory are 0644. The write is `with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f: f.write(text)` followed by `os.replace(tmp, path)`: there is no flush+fsync of the file (macOS needs F_FULLFSYNC for real durability) and no fsync of the directory. Text mode translates newlines while content_sha256 is computed from `text`, not from the bytes written; this is harmless on macOS but wrong elsewhere. A SIGKILL between mkstemp and replace leaves `.<name>.*.tmp` orphans that nothing removes, and they would break PLAN §7's `diff -r` check. The no-op checks only test `is_file()` (e.g. features.py:303) and never verify content_sha256. `_normalize_audio` lets ffmpeg write source_audio.flac in place rather than via a temp file and rename (ingest.py:123-141).

**Failure scenario.** A laptop loses power during a batch run just after `os.replace`. APFS can surface a zero-length audio_features.json whose manifest entry says ok. `mrw features T` reports 'up to date' because the file exists, and `mrw lyrics T` dies with an uncaught JSONDecodeError (features_doc is parsed before the try block, lyrics.py:473-475) instead of the document being regenerated. Separately, if a library directory is shared with another local account or process, that account can read the FLACs and JPEGs but not the JSON documents.

**Proposal.** In canonical.write: encode once (`data = text.encode('utf-8')`) and write in binary mode. Call `f.flush(); os.fsync(f.fileno())`, using `fcntl.F_FULLFSYNC` on darwin where available. Set the mode with `os.fchmod(fd, 0o666 & ~umask)`, capturing the umask once at import. Then `os.replace` and fsync the parent directory, and hash and return exactly the bytes written. At stage start, remove stale `.<name>.*.tmp` siblings. Make the no-op checks compare the file's sha256 with the entry's content_sha256 (cheap for JSON) so a torn document regenerates. Route _normalize_audio through a temp file and rename as well.

**Verifier's notes.** canonical.write (canonical.py:42-61) creates the temp file with tempfile.mkstemp, so files are 0600 and os.replace keeps that mode. It writes in text mode, does no flush or fsync of the file or the directory, and leaves `.<name>.*.tmp` orphans after a SIGKILL. The no-op checks only test is_file() (e.g. features.py:303). lyrics.py:472-475 parses source.json and audio_features.json before the try, so a torn document raises an unrecorded traceback instead of recording failed. Newline translation and hash-over-text are no-ops on POSIX, the target platform. Power-loss tearing is rare. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Worth doing: compare content_sha256 in the no-op checks (cheap), sweep stale `.<name>.*.tmp` files at stage start, and move the JSON parsing at lyrics.py:472-475 inside the try. fsync is optional for regenerable documents; F_FULLFSYNC on every write slows batch runs. Do not widen documents to 0666 & ~umask: 0600 fits the README's 'Do not publish library contents' stance. If consistent modes are wanted, tighten the FLAC and JPEG artifacts instead, as a recorded decision.

### G7. audio_features.json size

#### SCH-14: audio_features.json is about 4.4 MB per 4-minute track, roughly 2.5 times the documented size

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** performance · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** C1 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/canonical.py:37-39`, `schemas/README.md:70-72`, `docs/reviews/001-phase0-plan.md:139-143`

**Evidence.** canonical.dumps uses `indent=2`, which puts every timeseries value on its own indented line (e.g. '        -51.65,', with 8-10 spaces of indent per value). I simulated a 242.5 s document of the schema's shape (10 series of 24,251 values, about 950 onsets per channel) through the identical json.dumps call. It came to 4.40 MB, against 1.73 MB with compact separators. README.md:71 promises '~150–200 KB per series', and review 001 (line 139) accepted the document at '~1.5–2 MB', deferring a binary sidecar 'if it ever hurts'. The size was known and accepted; the new point is that the accepted figure is about 2.5 times too low.

**Failure scenario.** The iPad app decodes about 4.4 MB of JSON per track with JSONDecoder instead of about 1.7 MB, and the library grows about 2.5 times faster than planned. The size decision in review 001 rested on a figure the canonical writer doesn't produce.

**Proposal.** Have canonical.dumps keep indent=2 for objects but write arrays of scalars on one line, using a small deterministic recursive writer or post-processing of json.dumps output. That brings the document to about 1.8 MB. Alternatively, keep the format and correct README.md:71 and the review 001 figure, so the binary-sidecar decision rests on real numbers. Make the change before M7's determinism CI and the Swift consumer exist: it changes document bytes once and does not affect config hashes.

**Verifier's notes.** Reproduced (size_probe.py): a schema-shaped 242.5 s document through the identical `json.dumps(..., indent=2)` call is 4.36 MB, against 1.72 MB compact, at about 433 KB per series. README.md:71 says 150-200 KB per series and review 001:139 accepted ~1.5-2 MB. The premise is true, but the corrected figure doesn't overturn review 001's decision. The rationale stands: a one-time JSONDecoder load, separate documents keep it out of LLM context, and library growth is dominated by stems at ~40-60 MB per track (OQ-3). A custom inline-array writer on the determinism-critical serialisation path, changing every document's bytes, costs more than it returns now.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Docs only. Correct README.md:71 and PLAN §4.3 to about 430 KB per series and about 4.4 MB per 4-minute audio_features.json as written by canonical.dumps, and add a dated note under review 001's observation. Leave canonical.dumps unchanged. Revisit only with measured decode cost, through review 001's planned binary sidecar.

## H. Config, CLI and operations

### H1. Config errors are ignored or crash

#### ARCH-2: Config fails open: unknown or renamed keys are silently ignored, the committed operator mrw.toml applies to every README command, and a malformed config crashes with exit 1

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, H1, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:20`, `mrw/config.py:29`, `mrw/config.py:58`, `mrw/config.py:120`, `mrw/cli.py:28`, `mrw.toml:1`, `tests/conftest.py:3`, `README.md:48`

**Evidence.** Every config section is a plain `class XConfig(BaseModel, frozen=True)` (config.py:20, 26, 37, 49, 85, 101), which keeps pydantic's default extra='ignore'. I checked this with a standalone replica on pydantic 2.13.5 that does not import mrw: TOML with `[stems] devise = "cpu"` and a misspelled `[lyric]` section validates silently and leaves the defaults. A real case already exists. config.py:58-59 says 'Renamed from no_speech_threshold (PR #10 nit)', so any mrw.toml that still sets `[lyrics] no_speech_threshold` has been ignored since PR #10. Because extra keys never enter `stage_hash` (config.py:131-134), nothing even shows as stale. `StemsConfig.device: str = "auto"  # auto | cpu | mps` (config.py:29) is a free string. That differs from `caption_backend: Literal[...]` (config.py:92), which PR #12 round 4 moved to config-load validation, so device is only validated deep inside stems._resolve_device (stems.py:57). `_load_config` (cli.py:28-33) catches only FileNotFoundError. `Config.model_validate(tomllib.load(f))` at config.py:124-125 raises tomllib.TOMLDecodeError or pydantic ValidationError (types verified), which typer prints as a traceback with exit 1. CLAUDE.md and PLAN §8 reserve exit 2 for bad invocation. The repo also commits the operator's live config. mrw.toml:1-7 sets `device = "cpu"`, `vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0` and `language = "en"`, and tests/conftest.py:3-9 calls it 'the operator's live config and changes with field experiments'. config.load reads `Path("mrw.toml")` from the current directory by default (config.py:122), and every command in README.md:28-35 runs from the repo root.

**Failure scenario.** (a) A user follows README.md:48-50 ('set `[stems] device = "cpu"` in mrw.toml when you need it') but types `devices = "cpu"`. Separation runs on MPS under `auto`, the stem-file byte-identity guarantee silently stops applying, and the only trace is `run.device: mps` in the manifest. (b) A new user clones the repo and runs `uv run mrw lyrics <id>` on a Spanish song. The committed `language = "en"` pin forces English decoding (`language_source: pinned`), and vocal regions merge gaps under 1.0 s instead of the documented 0.2 s default (PLAN §4 stage 3). Nothing tells the user a non-default config was used. (c) A stray bracket in mrw.toml prints a Python traceback and exits 1.

**Proposal.** (1) Add a shared base, `class _Section(BaseModel): model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", frozen=True)`, for every section and for Config, and make `StemsConfig.device: Literal["auto", "cpu", "mps"]`. Forbidding extra keys changes no stage_hash, because extras were never part of the dump, so nothing goes stale. (2) In config.load, wrap TOMLDecodeError and ValidationError in a ConfigError that names the file, the key path and the allowed keys, and map it to exit 2 (ARCH-8). Add a specific hint for renamed keys (`no_speech_threshold` -> `flag_no_speech_threshold`). (3) Move the operator config out of the repo: commit `mrw.example.toml` with every key at its default, commented out; gitignore `mrw.toml`; point README at the example; and have each stage print which config file it used. (4) Add tests: an unknown key exits 2 with a clear message, and malformed TOML exits 2.

**Verifier's notes.** Verified with a standalone replica on pydantic 2.13.5 (model_config extra unset, so 'ignore'). `[stems] devise`, a `[lyric]` section and the pre-PR#10 `[lyrics] no_speech_threshold` key are all silently dropped. tomllib.TOMLDecodeError and pydantic ValidationError escape _load_config, which catches only FileNotFoundError (cli.py:28-33), so a bad config gives a typer traceback and exit 1 instead of 2. mrw.toml is git-tracked and sets device=cpu, vocal_min_gap_seconds=1.0 and language=en. config.load defaults to ./mrw.toml (config.py:122), and README usage runs from the repo root, so a fresh clone analyzes with the operator's settings. conftest.py:3-9 isolates the tests from exactly this file. I confirmed that extra='forbid' and Literal device leave stage_hash byte-identical. Re-rated to medium: the effects are recorded in the documents (vocal_activity.params, engine.language_source=pinned, run.device), and no hard constraint breaks; documents stay deterministic on MPS. It is a config-hygiene and robustness gap, not wrong output on common media.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Sound as written. Additions: (a) put allow_inf_nan=False on the shared config base. TOML accepts inf/nan and they currently pass the ge/gt validators (see ARCH-7). (b) Route `mrw status` and `models fetch` through the same ConfigError→exit 2 path, because they also call config.load.

#### ORCH-5: Misspelled config keys are silently ignored, and several fields accept values the pipeline cannot use

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, D5, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:20`, `mrw/config.py:29`, `mrw/config.py:21`, `mrw/config.py:124`, `schemas/source.schema.json:33`

**Evidence.** Every config model is declared `BaseModel, frozen=True` and keeps pydantic's default `extra="ignore"` (config.py:20,26,37,49,85,101). I checked this with a stand-alone pydantic 2.13 model of the same shape: TOML `[stems] devcie = "cpu"` and a misspelled section `[stem] retain = false` both validate silently to the defaults, while `extra="forbid"` reports `extra_forbidden ('devcie',)`. `device: str = "auto"  # auto | cpu | mps` (config.py:29) accepts any text and is only checked deep inside the stage (stems.py:47-57). That contradicts the policy stated at config.py:89-91 for caption_backend: "a typo'd backend fails at load". `sample_rate: int = 44100` and `channels: int = 2` (config.py:21-22) are unconstrained. Meanwhile source.schema.json:33-34 declares `"const": 44100` and `"const": 2`, features.py:87-88 requires 44100, and stems.py:92-101 requires the model's sample rate and channel count.

**Failure scenario.** The operator writes `[stems] devcie = "cpu"`, or the usual TOML spelling `copy-video = true`, or `[video] caption_budget_usd = 0.05`. With no message, mrw runs on auto→MPS (losing stem-file byte identity, OQ-13), references the video instead of copying it, or allows the default $0.50 per run instead of $0.05. With `[ingest] sample_rate = 48000`, ingest writes a source.json that violates the reviewed contract (jsonschema reports '44100 was expected'), and stems then fails with 'is 48000 Hz; model expects 44100'. Fixing the config does not help, because re-ingest is a no-op (ORCH-6).

**Proposal.** Declare `extra="forbid"` on every config model (`class StemsConfig(BaseModel, frozen=True, extra="forbid")`). Make `device: Literal["auto", "cpu", "mps"]`. Make `sample_rate: Literal[44100]` and `channels: Literal[2]`, or remove them from config, since the schema fixes them. Catch the resulting ValidationError in `_load_config` and exit 2 with the offending key path (see ORCH-10). None of these changes alters model_dump output, so no stage hash changes and nothing goes stale. Fast tests: a misspelled key exits 2 and names the key; `sample_rate = 48000` exits 2.

**Verifier's notes.** The config models (config.py:20,26,37,49,85,101) set only frozen=True, so extra defaults to 'ignore'. Reproduced with pydantic 2.13.5 on copies of these models: `[stems] devcie`, `[stem] retain = false`, `copy-video = true` and `caption_budget_usd = 0.05` all validate silently to the defaults. `[ingest] sample_rate = 48000` is accepted although source.schema.json:33-34 declares const 44100/2. Every TOML key the tests write is a real field, so extra='forbid' breaks no test. One overstatement: a bad device *value* already fails cleanly with exit 2 before any work (PrerequisiteError at stems.py:57), so Literal there buys consistency, not a fix. Medium rather than high: this is a missed user typo (robustness), defaults run, and the manifest records the device that actually ran.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Use extra='forbid' on all six models, including the root Config so misspelled sections are caught, and Literal for device, sample_rate and channels. Do NOT take the 'remove them from config' alternative. I verified that removing sample_rate/channels changes stage_hash(IngestConfig), which would show source=stale on every existing track forever because re-ingest is a no-op (ORCH-6). The forbid and Literal changes leave every stage hash byte-identical (also verified). Map ValidationError to exit 2 with the key path (ORCH-10).

#### SEC-10: Config typos are silently ignored, a malformed config exits 1 with a traceback, and several crash paths escape the 0/1/2 exit-code contract

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T4, H1
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:125`, `mrw/cli.py:28`, `mrw/cli.py:63`, `mrw/stems.py:259`, `mrw/cli.py:291`

**Evidence.** `Config.model_validate(tomllib.load(f))` (config.py:123-125) inherits pydantic's default `extra='ignore'`. I rebuilt the same frozen nested models under pydantic 2.13: both `[features] vocal_enter_dbfs = -30` and a misspelled section `[feature]` are accepted silently and the defaults are used. `_load_config` catches only FileNotFoundError (cli.py:28-33). So `tomllib.TOMLDecodeError` and pydantic `ValidationError` escape as typer tracebacks with exit code 1, instead of the exit 2 for bad invocation that PLAN §8 and CLAUDE.md require. That includes `caption_backend = 'antropic'`, which config.py:89-92 deliberately validates at the config boundary. The ingest command catches only IngestError (cli.py:63-69). The ZeroDivisionError and KeyError at ingest.py:209-218, and OSError from copyfile, therefore surface as tracebacks after the FLAC has already been written. The `_record(failed)` calls inside except blocks (e.g. stems.py:259-266) can themselves raise, e.g. on ENOSPC, and replace the stage's real error with an OSError traceback. `mrw status` and `mrw costs` abort on the first unreadable manifest (cli.py:291, cli.py:235).

**Failure scenario.** During OQ-5 calibration an operator writes `vocal_enter_dbfs = -30`. The key is dropped and the features config_hash doesn't change, so `mrw features T` replies 'features up to date — no-op'. The operator believes the new threshold is in effect; only `vocal_activity.params` in the document shows the default -35. Separately, a batch script that treats exit 2 as 'fix your invocation' and exit 1 as 'stage failed, retry' will retry a malformed mrw.toml forever.

**Proposal.** Set `model_config = ConfigDict(extra='forbid', frozen=True)` on every config model. Catch ValidationError and TOMLDecodeError in `_load_config` and exit 2 with a one-line message that names the offending key. Wrap ingest's post-probe logic so that any exception becomes an IngestError, with the cleanup from SEC-9. Guard `_record` in except paths so a secondary failure is reported alongside the original error rather than replacing it. Make status and costs skip unreadable manifests with a warning. Add tests: a typo'd key exits 2, and invalid TOML exits 2.

**Verifier's notes.** The config models are declared as `BaseModel, frozen=True` (config.py:20-106) and inherit pydantic's default extra='ignore'. I rebuilt equivalent models under pydantic 2.13.5: both `[features] vocal_enter_dbfs = -30` and a misspelled `[feature]` section are silently dropped and the defaults are used. `_load_config` (cli.py:28-33) catches only FileNotFoundError, so TOMLDecodeError and ValidationError escape as tracebacks with exit 1. That includes the caption_backend Literal that config.py:89-92 validates deliberately. The contract (cli.py:1, PLAN §8) assigns exit 2 to bad invocation. The ingest command catches only IngestError (cli.py:67). One overstatement: an exception raised inside the except-path `_record` is chained to the original (__context__) rather than replacing it, but the user still gets a traceback instead of the clean message. Nothing is recorded. The silent-typo trap matters most during the documented calibration workflows.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Note that extra='forbid' leaves model_dump unchanged, so the stage config hashes do not shift and nothing goes mass-stale.

#### DET-5: The config behind a document cannot be recovered: misspelled keys are silently ignored, the config file depends on the working directory, and the manifest stores only a hash

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D1, S4
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:20-128`, `mrw/config.py:120-128`, `mrw/cli.py:20-33`, `mrw/models.py:313-320`, `tests/conftest.py:3-9`

**Evidence.** No config model sets extra='forbid'; grep finds no ConfigDict or model_config anywhere in mrw/, so pydantic's default extra='ignore' applies. I checked this with pydantic 2.13.5 on a mirror model (scratchpad det/probe_cfg.py): TOML `[features] vocal_enter_dB = -30.0` and `[lyric] language = "fr"` load silently, and the features stage hash stays equal to the default. `load()` reads `Path("mrw.toml")` from the current working directory (config.py:122), while the library can live anywhere via MRW_LIBRARY (cli.py:20-22). `_load_config` catches only FileNotFoundError (cli.py:31), so a pydantic ValidationError surfaces as a traceback with exit 1 instead of exit 2. The manifest keeps only a 16-hex `config_hash` (models.py:318), so the config that produced a document cannot be recovered. tests/conftest.py:3-9 records that a working-directory mrw.toml has already flipped a determinism test. OQ-15 chose the working-directory default; the silent-typo behaviour and hash-only provenance are new.

**Failure scenario.** (1) The operator calibrates OQ-5 by writing `vocal_enter_dB = -30`. `mrw features T` replies 'features up to date — no-op' because the hash is unchanged, and the documents silently keep the -35 dB regions. (2) With MRW_LIBRARY=~/lib, running `mrw lyrics T` from ~ instead of the repo drops the repo's `[lyrics] language = "en"`. The hash differs, so the stage re-runs with auto-detection and overwrites lyrics.json. Nothing in the manifest says which config produced which document.

**Proposal.** (1) Set `model_config = ConfigDict(extra='forbid', frozen=True, allow_inf_nan=False)` on Config and every section, including the future structure section, so typos and non-finite values fail at load. Catch pydantic.ValidationError in cli._load_config and exit 2 with the field path. (2) Store the effective canonical config-subset JSON as a string (`config`) next to config_hash in each manifest entry and in the stems block. This is manifest-only, so it has no determinism cost, and it makes stale diagnoses explainable. (3) Print the config source in every stage's output ('config: ./mrw.toml' or 'built-in defaults'). (4) As an OQ-15 amendment, consider also looking for `<library>/mrw.toml` when --library or MRW_LIBRARY is set, so a library carries its own config.

**Verifier's notes.** There is no `model_config` or ConfigDict anywhere in mrw/; the config classes only pass frozen=True (config.py:20-101). A mirror-model probe on pydantic 2.13.5 (lock: 2.13.4), /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/det-verify/pyd.py, loaded `[features] vocal_enter_dB = -30.0` and an unknown `[lyric]` table silently, and the features stage hash equalled the default's, so `mrw features` no-ops. `_load_config` catches only FileNotFoundError (cli.py:31). Both pydantic ValidationError and tomllib.TOMLDecodeError (neither a FileNotFoundError subclass, verified) escape as tracebacks with exit 1, contrary to PLAN §8's exit 2 for bad invocation. Manifest entries store only a 16-hex config_hash (models.py:318). Working-directory config discovery (config.py:122) is OQ-15's decision, so scenario (2) is a known consequence. The silent typos, the wrong exit code and the hash-only provenance are new.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Lead with the S part: extra='forbid' (plus allow_inf_nan=False, see DET-7) on every config model, and catch (pydantic.ValidationError, tomllib.TOMLDecodeError) in cli._load_config, exiting 2 with the field path. model_dump output is unchanged, so config hashes do not churn, and the operator mrw.toml uses only valid keys. Embedding the canonical config subset in the manifest is a sound additive bump. Item (4) amends OQ-15 and needs a P2 resolution note.

#### TEST-15: conftest's config isolation doesn't reach module-scoped fixtures, which run against the operator's repo mrw.toml

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, T2
- **Locations:** `tests/conftest.py:1-19`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:133-141`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:91`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:113-116`, `tests/test_m2_smoke.py:92-106`, `mrw/config.py:122`, `mrw.toml:1-7`

**Evidence.** conftest.py:17-19 defines `@pytest.fixture(autouse=True) def _isolate_from_repo_config(tmp_path_factory, monkeypatch): monkeypatch.chdir(...)`, which is function-scoped. pytest instantiates higher-scoped fixtures first, so module-scoped fixtures run before the chdir, in whatever directory pytest started in. test_m3_smoke.py:133-141 `click_doc` (scope="module") calls ingest (L91) and `_run_features` (L114: `["features", track_id, "--library", str(library)]`, no --config). test_m2_smoke.py:92-106 `double_run` ingests without --config. config.py:122 resolves `candidate = path or Path("mrw.toml")` against the CWD. In CI the agent runs `uv run pytest -q` at the checkout root, whose mrw.toml sets `[features] vocal_min_gap_seconds = 1.0`, `[stems] device = "cpu"` and `[lyrics] language = "en"`. So click_doc is already computed under the operator's config today, which happens to be harmless for its current assertions. The conftest docstring (L3-9) says this exact class of leak, the PR #10 `language = "en"` incident, is now prevented for every test.

**Failure scenario.** An operator experiment in the repo mrw.toml such as `[ingest] sample_rate = 48000` makes M2's double_run ingest at 48 kHz, so every slow M2 test fails with StemsError 'model expects 44100'. `[features] phase_fit_window_seconds = 0.2` can flip test_click_track_beat_grid's downbeat assertion. Either way the results reflect operator state, not the code.

**Proposal.** Add a session-scoped autouse fixture: `with pytest.MonkeyPatch.context() as mp: mp.chdir(tmp_path_factory.mktemp('cwd')); yield`. Session scope is instantiated before module scope; keep the function-scoped fixture as well. Add a guard in pytest_runtest_setup asserting `not Path('mrw.toml').exists()`. Better still, give the CLI an explicit MRW_CONFIG environment variable and point it at an empty file for the whole session.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. conftest's chdir fixture is function-scoped (conftest.py:17-19). pytest sorts each test's fixture closure by scope and instantiates higher scopes first (high confidence), and the previous test's monkeypatch has already restored the CWD. Module fixtures therefore run in pytest's invocation directory. click_doc (test_m3_smoke.py:133-141) ingests and runs features without --config, and double_run (test_m2_smoke.py:92-106) ingests without --config. config.load resolves ./mrw.toml relative to the CWD (config.py:122). CI runs pytest at the checkout root, whose committed mrw.toml sets features.vocal_min_gap_seconds=1.0, stems.device='cpu' and lyrics.language='en'. That is harmless for today's assertions. But the docstring's guarantee (conftest.py:3-9) is false for exactly the PR #10 leak class, and an [ingest] or [features] experiment committed to mrw.toml would change what the module fixtures compute in CI.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Add a session-scoped autouse fixture: `with pytest.MonkeyPatch.context() as mp: mp.chdir(tmp_path_factory.mktemp('cwd')); yield`. Session scope is instantiated before module scope; keep the function-scoped fixture as well. Do not use the proposed pytest_runtest_setup guard: a conftest hook runs before fixture setup, so on the first test the session chdir has not happened yet and the guard would fail spuriously when pytest is run from the repo root. Put the assertion in an autouse function fixture that depends on the session fixture instead, or use an MRW_CONFIG env var pointing at an empty file.

### H2. Exit-code contract leaks

#### ORCH-10: Config, manifest and toolchain errors surface as tracebacks with exit 1 instead of a clean exit 2

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/cli.py:28`, `mrw/config.py:124`, `mrw/library.py:44`, `mrw/cli.py:233`, `mrw/cli.py:63`, `mrw/ingest.py:64`, `mrw/cli.py:249`

**Evidence.** `_load_config` catches only `FileNotFoundError` (cli.py:28-33), but config.load can raise tomllib.TOMLDecodeError and pydantic.ValidationError (config.py:124-125); I confirmed neither is a FileNotFoundError. `Library.read_manifest` calls `Manifest.model_validate_json` (library.py:44), and every caller does so outside any try: ingest.py:176, stems.py:142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317 and status (cli.py:291). `costs` calls `_json.loads` directly (cli.py:233-236). `ingest` catches only IngestError (cli.py:63-69), although probe and decode launch `ffprobe` and `ffmpeg` (ingest.py:64-75, 111), which raises FileNotFoundError when they are not on PATH, and copyfile can raise OSError. `models fetch` has no error handling at all (cli.py:249-261). Assumed Typer 0.26.8 behaviour: unhandled exceptions are re-raised, printed as a rich traceback, and the process exits 1. PLAN §8 reserves 1 for stage failure and 2 for bad invocation or missing prerequisites.

**Failure scenario.** `[stems]\ndevice = cpu` (unquoted) or `caption_backend = "Anthropic"` gives a traceback and exit 1, so a batch wrapper would count it as one track's stage failure and carry on through the whole library. A new user without ffmpeg runs `mrw ingest x.wav` and gets a FileNotFoundError traceback with exit 1. One hand-edited manifest with an invalid status makes `mrw status` and `mrw costs` crash for the entire library instead of reporting the one bad track.

**Proposal.** In `_load_config`, catch `tomllib.TOMLDecodeError`, `pydantic.ValidationError` and `OSError`, print one line and exit 2. Wrap manifest parsing in a `LibraryError` that carries the path and the first validation error, and map it to exit 2. Have `status` and `costs` print `T  <unreadable manifest: …>` and continue with the other tracks. Check `shutil.which("ffmpeg")` and `shutil.which("ffprobe")` at the start of ingest and exit 2 if either is missing. Wrap unexpected exceptions raised after the track directory is created as IngestError (exit 1). Optionally map KeyboardInterrupt to exit 130. Add fast tests for each path (T4).

**Verifier's notes.** `_load_config` catches only FileNotFoundError (cli.py:28-33). I checked that tomllib.TOMLDecodeError and pydantic.ValidationError both subclass ValueError, not FileNotFoundError. read_manifest (library.py:44) is unguarded at every caller, including status (cli.py:291). costs calls json.loads directly (cli.py:233-236). ingest catches only IngestError (cli.py:63-69), yet a missing ffprobe or ffmpeg raises FileNotFoundError from subprocess (ingest.py:54, 64, 111). models fetch has no error handling at all (cli.py:249-261). With typer 0.26.8 (uv.lock), an uncaught exception is re-raised and Python exits 1, against the exit-2 convention for bad invocation in PLAN §8 and CLAUDE.md:57. Medium is right.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Note that `shutil.which` pre-checks belong in ingest (exit 2). In video, a missing ffmpeg is currently recorded as a stage failure; map it to a PrerequisiteError before the try block.

#### ARCH-8: Each CLI command maps exceptions to exit codes on its own; a missing API key or unknown model is recorded as a stage failure only after the expensive decode work

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T4, H3, T5
- **Locations:** `mrw/cli.py:87`, `mrw/cli.py:249`, `mrw/captions.py:96`, `mrw/captions.py:133`, `mrw/video.py:420`, `mrw/video.py:440`, `mrw/video.py:557`, `mrw/costs.py:63`, `mrw/ingest.py:53`, `mrw/library.py:19`

**Evidence.** There are five sibling stage exceptions with no common base, all subclassing RuntimeError: IngestError (ingest.py:41), StemsError (stems.py:32), FeaturesError (features.py:70), LyricsError (lyrics.py:65) and VideoError (video.py:57). PrerequisiteError is defined at library.py:19 and re-exported through stems.__all__ (stems.py:29). cli.py imports it from `.stems` at line 87 but from `.library` at lines 122, 152 and 187. BudgetExceeded subclasses PrerequisiteError (video.py:61), and UnknownModelError is a ValueError (costs.py:17). Each command maps exceptions itself (cli.py:63-69, 90-97, 125-132, 156-163, 191-198), and `models fetch` (cli.py:249-261) and `costs` (cli.py:224-242) map none. A missing API key is caught too late. AnthropicBackend.__init__ raises a bare RuntimeError (captions.py:96-100), and it is constructed at video.py:440, after shot detection (:374), frame extraction (:393) and JPEG encoding (:394). The generic handler (video.py:557-579) then records `status: failed`, replacing the previous ok entry's path, content_sha256 and run, and exits 1. PLAN §8 classes missing prerequisites as exit 2. An unknown caption model follows the same path: costs.estimate raises UnknownModelError (costs.py:63-67) at video.py:420, and VideoConfig.caption_model is a free string (config.py:93). A missing ffmpeg is not caught at all: ingest._run calls `subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True)` (ingest.py:53-54), and cli.ingest catches only IngestError (cli.py:67), so the FileNotFoundError becomes a traceback with exit 1. Some error messages come out empty. `next(b.text for b in response.content if b.type == "text")` (captions.py:133) and `next(s for s in info["streams"] if ...)` (video.py:206-210) raise StopIteration, whose str() is empty, so the manifest gets `error: ""` and the CLI prints 'error: video failed: '.

**Failure scenario.** An operator sets `caption_backend = "anthropic"` but forgot to export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, on a 4-minute 1080p track that already has an ok video.json from the null backend. mrw runs the shot-detection and frame-extraction passes (about 1-2 minutes per PLAN §9) and then fails. The manifest's video entry becomes failed, so status shows video=failed even though video.json is intact, and the exit code is 1. A batch wrapper therefore treats it as a stage crash, not a setup error. Setting `caption_model = "claude-haiku-5-5"`, a current model missing from PRICES_PER_MTOK, behaves the same way.

**Proposal.** Add mrw/errors.py with `MrwError(Exception)` carrying an `exit_code`. `UsageError(MrwError)` (exit 2) is the base of PrerequisiteError, ConfigError, BudgetExceeded, MissingCredential and ToolNotFound. `StageError(MrwError)` (exit 1) carries the stage name; the five stage errors become thin subclasses, or a single class. Wrap every CLI command in one decorator that maps MrwError to its message and exit code, and turns any other exception into a one-line 'internal error' with exit 1 (traceback only with `--debug`). Move the credential and model checks into a cheap validation step that runs before any decoding. That step requires the key (or lets the SDK resolve credentials and maps `anthropic.AuthenticationError`) and requires `caption_model in PRICES_PER_MTOK`; a VideoConfig model_validator could catch the model case at config load. In a shared media helper, map FileNotFoundError for ffmpeg or ffprobe to ToolNotFound ('ffmpeg not on PATH — brew install ffmpeg'). Replace bare `next(...)` calls with `next(..., None)` and explicit messages. Add T4 tests: a missing key exits 2 and leaves the manifest entry untouched, and a missing ffmpeg exits 2.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced the run_video order: _video_stream_info (373), the full shot-detection decode (374), the frame-extraction decode (393) and JPEG encoding (394). Then costs.estimate raises UnknownModelError for an unpriced model (420), and make_backend raises a bare RuntimeError when ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is missing (440, captions.py:96-100). Both land in the generic handler (557-579), which replaces the prior ok entry with failed and exits 1. library.py:20 and PLAN §8 classify missing prerequisites as exit 2 with nothing recorded. ingest._run (ingest.py:53-54) lets FileNotFoundError escape, and cli.py:67 catches only IngestError, so a missing ffmpeg gives a traceback and exit 1. The bare next() calls at captions.py:133 and video.py:206-210 raise StopIteration, whose str() is '' (verified), so the manifest records error "". The backend is deliberately built after the budget gate so that --estimate needs no key (video.py:397-399); the fix must keep that.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do the targeted S fixes first. In run_video, before any decode, and only when caption_backend != 'null' and not estimate_only, require the model to be in PRICES_PER_MTOK and a credential to be present; otherwise raise PrerequisiteError (exit 2, manifest untouched). Catch FileNotFoundError in ingest._run, _decode_hash_and_duration and the video subprocess calls, and report a clear 'ffmpeg/ffprobe not on PATH' error with exit 2. Replace the bare next() calls with next(..., None) and explicit messages. Add T4 tests. The MrwError hierarchy and the CLI decorator are a reasonable M follow-up, not a prerequisite.

#### VID-9: Config and prerequisite errors surface only after full decode, are recorded as stage failures (exit 1, overwriting an ok entry), and `--estimate` can mutate the manifest

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/config.py:89-93`, `mrw/costs.py:63-67`, `mrw/video.py:420-425`, `mrw/captions.py:96-100`, `mrw/video.py:440`, `mrw/video.py:557-578`, `mrw/cli.py:180-181`

**Evidence.** caption_model is a free string (mrw/config.py:93). caption_backend, by contrast, is validated at load so that 'a typo'd backend fails at load, not deep inside the stage after decode work' (mrw/config.py:89-92). An unknown model shows up only as UnknownModelError, a ValueError (mrw/costs.py:63-67), raised from costs.estimate at mrw/video.py:420-425. That is after shot detection (373-375) and after full-resolution extraction and JPEG encoding (393-395). A missing key raises RuntimeError in AnthropicBackend.__init__ (mrw/captions.py:96-100), which is constructed at mrw/video.py:440, also after decode. Both errors land in `except Exception` (mrw/video.py:557-578), which records status failed over any ok entry and exits 1. PLAN §8 reserves exit 2 for bad invocation and missing prerequisites. The handler has no estimate_only guard, even though cli.py:180-181 documents --estimate as 'Print the caption cost estimate and exit without spending.' The key check also blocks replay from a warm cache, which needs no API call; the replay test has to set a fake key (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:337).

**Failure scenario.** A user with an ok null-backend video.json sets caption_backend='anthropic' and caption_model='claude-haiku-4.5' (dot instead of hyphen), then runs `mrw video T --estimate`. After minutes of decoding, UnknownModelError fires. The manifest's video entry flips from ok to failed, losing content_sha256 and run, and the exit code is 1, although video.json and frames/ are untouched. M6 would then refuse the video prerequisite. A missing ANTHROPIC_API_KEY behaves the same way, and a warm-cache library copied to a machine without a key cannot be replayed.

**Proposal.** Add a VideoConfig model_validator: when caption_backend != 'null', caption_model must be in costs.PRICES_PER_MTOK, so the error appears at load time with exit 2. For non-estimate runs with a non-null backend, check for ANTHROPIC_API_KEY at the top of run_video and raise PrerequisiteError before any decode. Better still, defer the key requirement until the first cache miss, so warm-cache replay works offline. In the except handler, when estimate_only is set, re-raise as VideoError without calling _record. Tests: a typo'd model exits 2 with the manifest unchanged; a failing --estimate leaves an ok entry ok.

**Verifier's notes.** caption_model is a free string (config.py:93), while caption_backend is a validated Literal (89-92). costs.estimate raises UnknownModelError, a ValueError (costs.py:63-67), at video.py:420-425. That happens after detection (373-375) and after full-resolution extraction and JPEG encoding (393-395). AnthropicBackend.__init__ raises RuntimeError when the key is missing (captions.py:96-100) at video.py:440. Both errors land in `except Exception` (557-578), which calls _record(failed) with no estimate_only guard. So `mrw video T --estimate` with a typo'd model overwrites an ok entry with failed, dropping path, content_sha256 and run, and exits 1. That contradicts cli.py:180-181 and PLAN §8's exit 2. Warm-cache replay also needs a key; the test sets a fake one at tests/test_m5_smoke.py:337. One correction to the proposal: a load-time VideoConfig validator would not give exit 2. _load_config (cli.py:28-33) catches only FileNotFoundError, so a pydantic ValidationError at load, including today's Literal check, escapes as a traceback with exit 1.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** (1) At the top of run_video, before any decode: if caption_backend != 'null' and caption_model is not in costs.PRICES_PER_MTOK, raise PrerequisiteError. If the validation lives in VideoConfig instead, also make _load_config map pydantic.ValidationError and tomllib.TOMLDecodeError to exit 2. (2) Construct AnthropicBackend lazily, and require ANTHROPIC_API_KEY only when the cache-aware estimate has calls > 0: raise PrerequisiteError right after the budget gate. Warm-cache replay then works without a key. (3) In the except handler, when estimate_only is set, raise VideoError without calling _record. Tests as proposed.

#### TEST-6: Failure-path gaps: config errors exit 1 with a traceback or are silently ignored, the features failure path is never executed, and several prerequisite/abort paths are untested

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T4, H1, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/cli.py:28-33`, `mrw/config.py:120-128`, `mrw/features.py:366-376`, `mrw/ingest.py:194-200`, `mrw/video.py:420-440`, `mrw/captions.py:96-100`, `mrw/cli.py:179-211`

**Evidence.** cli.py:28-33 `_load_config` catches only FileNotFoundError, and config.py:124-125 does `return Config.model_validate(tomllib.load(f))`. So a TOMLDecodeError or a pydantic ValidationError (e.g. `cpu_threads = 0`, `caption_backend = "anthropc"`) escapes the command: traceback, exit 1 (Typer/Click re-raise non-Click exceptions). The contract is exit 2 for bad invocation (PLAN §8, CLAUDE.md:57). The comment at config.py:89-91 says a typo'd backend 'fails at load'; it does, but as exit 1 with a traceback. Unknown keys are silently ignored by pydantic's default extra='ignore'; I checked with pydantic 2.13 that `model_validate({"devcie": "cpu"})` gives device='auto'. Features: the `except Exception` → status="failed" branch (features.py:368-376) runs in no test, and no test references FeaturesError; every other analysis stage has such a test. Ingest: any failure after `track_dir.mkdir` (ingest.py:194-200) leaves an orphan directory with a partial source_audio.flac and no manifest; only the pre-mkdir no-audio case is tested (test_m1_smoke.py:192-202). Video: a missing ANTHROPIC_API_KEY raises RuntimeError in AnthropicBackend.__init__ (captions.py:96-100), called from make_backend (video.py:440) after shot detection, frame extraction and JPEG encoding. It is recorded as `failed` with exit 1 rather than reported as a prerequisite error. An unpriced caption_model raises UnknownModelError at video.py:420-425 with the same result.
