Also untested: `mrw video --estimate` (cli.py:179-211, the path that promises no key and no spend), `mrw video` on an audio-only track (exit 2), lyrics with no vocal stem, `stems.device = "gpu"`, and `--lyrics` pointing at a missing file.

**Failure scenario.** A user writes `[stems] devcie = "cpu"`: it is silently ignored, so the run uses MPS and loses OQ-13's CPU stem identity. A typo like `caption_budget_usd = 0.10` silently keeps the $0.50 default budget. `cpu_threads = 0` exits 1 with a pydantic traceback, which batch tooling would count as a stage failure. Re-running features on a track whose stem is corrupt has never been shown to leave the previous audio_features.json intact.

**Proposal.** Catch `(tomllib.TOMLDecodeError, pydantic.ValidationError)` in `_load_config`, print a message and Exit(2). Add `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` to the config sections. Raise the API-key and price-table checks as PrerequisiteError before any decode work. Add a fast, parametrized tests/test_failure_paths.py: each case asserts the exit code, the manifest status (unchanged for exit 2; `failed` plus an error for exit 1), and that prior documents are byte-identical after the failed re-run. Example: features ok → plant a 48 kHz stem → re-run with a changed config → exit 1, failed, old audio_features.json untouched. Cover `--estimate` on both backends with TEST-4's socket guard active.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. _load_config (cli.py:28-33) catches only FileNotFoundError. tomllib.TOMLDecodeError and pydantic.ValidationError are both ValueError subclasses (checked with pydantic 2.13.5; 2.13.4 is locked). They escape, and Click re-raises non-Click exceptions, so the user gets a traceback and exit 1 instead of the exit 2 for bad invocation (cli.py:1, PLAN §8). Under pydantic's default extra='ignore', a typo'd `[stems] devcie` or `[video] caption_budget_usd` is silently dropped. In my re-typed models, device stayed 'auto' and the budget stayed 0.50. The features `except Exception` branch (features.py:368-376) and FeaturesError appear in no test. A missing ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (captions.py:96-100) or an unpriced model (costs.py:63-67, reached from video.py:420-425) surfaces inside run_video's try block after shot detection and frame extraction, and is recorded as `failed` with exit 1. One problem the finding missed: on `--estimate` the same except branch (video.py:555-579) writes `status: failed` to the manifest. An estimate-only run that hits an unpriced model or an ffprobe error therefore clobbers a previous `ok` entry. A grep finds no tests for --estimate, audio-only video, a missing vocal stem, device='gpu', or a missing --lyrics file.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed, plus three things. (1) Put the key and price checks at the top of run_video as PrerequisiteError. Apply the price check to every non-null backend, and the key check only when not estimate_only, since PR #12 decided --estimate never needs a key. (2) Never write to the manifest on the estimate_only path; raise without calling _record. (3) Add an ingest failure case where avg_frame_rate is '0/0'. Fraction('0/0') raises ZeroDivisionError at ingest.py:209 after track_dir.mkdir, giving a traceback and an orphan directory. extra='forbid' does not change stage hashes, because model_dump never includes extras.

### H3. Concurrent runs lose manifest updates

#### ORCH-4: Lost manifest updates: each stage reads the manifest at start and rewrites the whole file at the end, with no locking

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/library.py:46`, `mrw/stems.py:142`, `mrw/stems.py:172`, `mrw/video.py:317`, `mrw/video.py:343`, `mrw/features.py:310`, `mrw/lyrics.py:482`, `mrw/stems.py:185`

**Evidence.** Each stage reads the manifest once at the start (`manifest = library.read_manifest(track_id)`: stems.py:142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317) and then runs for minutes. At the end, `_record` changes its own field on that in-memory copy and calls `library.write_manifest(track_id, manifest)` (stems.py:172-174, features.py:310-312, lyrics.py:482-484, video.py:343-345). That call replaces the whole file atomically, with no re-read and no lock (library.py:46-48). The stems temp directory is a fixed path, and a second run deletes it: `if tmp_dir.exists(): shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir)` (stems.py:185-187). PLAN.md:100 says "`video` needs only `ingest`", so running video and stems side by side is a natural thing to do.

**Failure scenario.** Terminal 1 runs `mrw stems T` (5–10 minutes on CPU) while terminal 2 runs `mrw video T` with anthropic captions. If stems finishes first, video then writes back the copy it read at its start, so the manifest says stems=pending even though stems/ is complete. `mrw features T` refuses ('stems are not separated'), and `mrw stems T` separates all over again. If video finishes first, stems' copy resets video to pending: video.json and frames/ exist, but the entry is gone, including api_usage for paid calls, so `mrw costs` under-reports. With two accidental `mrw stems T` runs, run B deletes run A's .stems.tmp, A renames its output to stems/, B's sf.write then fails, and B records stems=failed on top of A's good result.

**Proposal.** Add `Library.update_manifest(track_id, mutate)`. It takes an exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `<track>/.manifest.lock`, re-reads manifest.json, applies only the caller's field change, writes through canonical.write, and releases the lock. Route every `_record` through it. Also add a per-stage run lock (`flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on `.stems.lock`, `.video.lock`, and so on) that exits 2 with 'stage already running for T'. Both are needed before M7 batch mode runs anything in parallel. Test: two `_record` calls made from copies read before either write must both survive.

**Verifier's notes.** Each stage reads the manifest once at the start (stems.py:142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317). `_record` then rewrites the whole file from that in-memory copy (stems.py:172-174, features.py:310-312, lyrics.py:482-484, video.py:343-345) through Library.write_manifest (library.py:46-48), an atomic replace with no re-read and no lock. Traced: with stems and video running at the same time on T, the last writer resets the other entry. stems=pending makes features refuse and stems re-separate; video=pending loses api_usage. The fixed temp directories, which a second run rmtrees (stems.py:185-187, video.py:449-454), produce the double-stems race described: good stems on disk, manifest says failed. No doc mentions locking or concurrency. Downgraded to medium: it needs two concurrent invocations on one track, and the result is a recoverable status/ledger loss, not wrong analysis. It becomes high once M7 batch mode runs stages in parallel.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Take an flock on `<track>/.manifest.lock` around re-read → apply only this stage's field → canonical.write. Add a non-blocking per-stage run lock that exits 2 with 'already running'. Route ingest's first manifest write and the ORCH-7 ledger append through the same lock. flock is advisory and reliable only on local filesystems, which suits a local library.

#### ARCH-5: Manifest updates rewrite the whole file with no lock: concurrent stages on one track silently undo each other's entries

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D4, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/stems.py:142`, `mrw/stems.py:172`, `mrw/video.py:317`, `mrw/video.py:343`, `mrw/features.py:310`, `mrw/lyrics.py:482`, `mrw/library.py:46`, `mrw/stems.py:169`, `mrw/video.py:449`

**Evidence.** Each stage reads the manifest once when it starts and writes the whole object back when it finishes. stems.py reads it at line 142 (`manifest = library.read_manifest(track_id)`) and writes it at 172-174 (`manifest.stems = state` then `library.write_manifest(track_id, manifest)`). The other stages do the same: video.py:317 then :343-345, features.py:278 then :310-312, lyrics.py:437 then :482-484. Library.write_manifest (library.py:46-48) replaces the file atomically, but there is no lock and no re-read or merge. PLAN.md:97-100 says '`video` needs only `ingest`', so running `mrw video` next to the minutes-long audio stages is the natural way to save time. The temp directories have fixed names and are wiped on entry: `.stems.tmp` at stems.py:169 (removed at :185-186) and `.frames.tmp` at video.py:449-454. The cost ledger, which must never lose recorded spend (video.py:347-349), lives in these same manifest entries.

**Failure scenario.** Terminal A runs `mrw stems T`, which takes 5-10 minutes on CPU, and terminal B starts `mrw video T`. Stems finishes first and writes stems=ok. Video finishes later and writes the copy it read at start, which puts stems back to pending. `mrw features T` now refuses with 'stems are not separated — run `mrw stems` first' even though stems/ is on disk, and `mrw stems T` separates again because its no-op check sees pending. If the two finish in the opposite order, the video entry is lost, including its `run.api_usage`, so `mrw costs` under-reports real spend. Two simultaneous `mrw video T` runs share `.frames.tmp` and can move each other's half-written frames into place. A parallel M7 batch would hit this routinely.

**Proposal.** (1) Add `Library.update_manifest(track_id, mutate)`. It takes an exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `track_dir/.manifest.lock`, re-reads the current manifest, applies only the calling stage's change (its `documents.<stage>` entry or `stems`), writes atomically and releases the lock. Route every `_record` through it. (2) Add a run lock per track and stage (`flock(LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB)` on `track_dir/.lock.<stage>`); if it is held, raise PrerequisiteError 'stage already running' (exit 2). (3) Use unique temp directories via `tempfile.mkdtemp(dir=track_dir, prefix=".stems.")`, and clean up stale temp directories when no lock holder exists. (4) Add a unit test in which two records are written from out-of-date copies and both survive. flock works on local APFS; document that concurrent runs on network-mounted libraries are not supported.

**Verifier's notes.** Traced. Each stage keeps the manifest it read at start (stems.py:142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317) and writes the whole object back at the end (stems.py:172-174, features.py:310-312, lyrics.py:482-484, video.py:343-345). write_manifest (library.py:46-48) is atomic but neither locks nor merges. With `mrw stems T` and `mrw video T` running at once, the later writer restores the other's stale block. Either stems goes back to pending (so features refuses at features.py:285 and stems re-separates), or the video entry is lost, including its api_usage. The fixed temp dirs are rmtree'd on entry (stems.py:185-186, video.py:450-454). No concurrency decision is recorded anywhere. Medium, because it needs two concurrent invocations on one track.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The fix is right for concurrency but incomplete for its own ledger goal. manifest.schema.json's api_usage description says 'partial spend never vanishes from the ledger', yet every sequential video run replaces documents.video.run.api_usage (video.py:537-551, 562-578). One case: a re-run after any [video] change replays captions from cache and records calls=0/usd=0, erasing the earlier paid run. caption_budget_usd_per_run is part of the stage hash, so even raising the budget triggers this. Another case: a successful run after a failed paid run erases the partial spend. Make the ledger append-only, for example track_dir/.cache/api_ledger.jsonl appended under the same lock, or a cumulative total, and have `mrw costs` read it. Locking: one flock on track_dir/.manifest.lock around read-merge-write, plus the per-stage run lock and mkdtemp temp dirs as proposed.

#### DET-6: Concurrent stages on one track overwrite each other's manifest entries, including the API cost ledger

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D4, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/library.py:40-48`, `mrw/stems.py:142`, `mrw/stems.py:172-174`, `mrw/features.py:278`, `mrw/features.py:310-312`, `mrw/lyrics.py:437`, `mrw/lyrics.py:482-484`, `mrw/video.py:317`, `mrw/video.py:343-345`, `PLAN.md:100`

**Evidence.** Every stage reads the manifest once at entry (stems.py:142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317). After minutes of work, its `_record` closure writes that whole in-memory object back: `manifest.documents.video = entry; library.write_manifest(track_id, manifest)` (video.py:343-345), and the same pattern at stems.py:172-174, features.py:310-312 and lyrics.py:482-484. canonical.write makes each file write atomic, but there is no lock and no re-read before writing. PLAN.md:100 says '`video` needs only `ingest`', so the DAG invites running the video stage alongside the audio chain, and M7 plans batch mode.

**Failure scenario.** Terminal 1 runs `mrw stems T` (CPU Demucs, 5–10 minutes per PLAN §9). Terminal 2 runs `mrw video T` with the anthropic backend, finishes first, and records documents.video = ok with run.api_usage for real spend. Terminal 1 then writes its stale copy of the manifest. documents.video reverts to 'pending', the api_usage entry disappears so `mrw costs` under-reports actual spend, and `mrw video T` will re-run. PR #12 round 3 required that paid caption calls never vanish from the cost ledger. If the stages finish in the other order, the stems block is lost instead, and `mrw features` refuses with 'stems are not separated' even though stems/ exists.

**Proposal.** Add `Library.update_manifest(track_id, mutate)`. It takes an exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `<track_dir>/.manifest.lock`, re-reads manifest.json, applies only the caller's change (documents.<stage> or stems), and writes through canonical.write before releasing the lock. Replace every `_record` closure with it, and use it for ingest's write too. Optionally add a per-stage lock (`.stems.lock`, `.video.lock`) so the same stage can't run twice at once on one track; two such runs would also race on `.stems.tmp` and `.frames.tmp`. Test: interleave two stages' update_manifest calls and assert both entries survive.

**Verifier's notes.** Each stage reads the manifest once (stems.py:142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317). Its `_record` closure later writes that whole snapshot back (stems.py:172-174, features.py:310-312, lyrics.py:482-484, video.py:343-345). canonical.write makes each file write atomic, but nothing locks or re-reads first. PLAN.md:100 ('`video` needs only `ingest`') makes running `mrw video T` next to `mrw stems T` a natural speed-up, and the later writer reverts the other's entry. That includes run.api_usage, which PR #12 round 3 required never to vanish (video.py:348-349), so `mrw costs` under-reports. Two runs of the same stage also share `.stems.tmp` (stems.py:169) or `.frames.tmp` (video.py:449). Medium because it needs concurrent invocations on one track. The proposed flock plus re-read-and-merge is sound and proportionate.

#### SEC-8: No per-track lock: concurrent stage runs overwrite each other's manifest updates (including API spend) and clobber shared temp directories

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D4, H3
- **Locations:** `mrw/library.py:131`, `mrw/stems.py:141`, `mrw/stems.py:172`, `mrw/stems.py:185`, `mrw/video.py:317`, `mrw/video.py:343`, `mrw/video.py:449`

**Evidence.** Every stage reads the whole manifest when it starts (stems.py:141-142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317). Minutes later, `_record` updates the stage's own entry on that in-memory snapshot and rewrites the entire file: `manifest.stems = state; library.write_manifest(track_id, manifest)` (stems.py:172-174), and the same pattern at features.py:310-312, lyrics.py:482-484 and video.py:343-345. canonical.write is atomic, but nothing makes the read-modify-write atomic and nothing locks the manifest (library.py:131-139). Temp directories have fixed names and are deleted on entry: `tmp_dir = track_dir / '.stems.tmp'` ... `if tmp_dir.exists(): shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir)` (stems.py:169, 185-187), and `.frames.tmp` (video.py:449-454). PLAN §3 draws stems and video as independent branches off ingest, so running them in parallel is a natural thing to do.

**Failure scenario.** Run `mrw stems T & mrw video T` with the anthropic backend. Video finishes first and records ok plus api_usage. Five minutes later, stems writes back the snapshot it read at start. documents.video reverts to 'pending', losing content_sha256, config_hash and api_usage, even though video.json and frames/ are on disk. `mrw costs` permanently under-reports that spend, and the next `mrw video T` recomputes everything. With two concurrent `mrw stems T` runs, the second deletes the first's `.stems.tmp` mid-separation. The first then fails on write or renames a mix of files into stems/, and the manifest ends up with whichever `_record` ran last. Two concurrent caption runs both miss the cache, and each passes its own per-run budget gate, so the same frames are paid for twice.

**Proposal.** Take an exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `<track>/.lock` for the whole stage run. Either fail fast with exit 2 and 'track busy', or wait and say so. Inside `_record`, re-read the manifest from disk while holding the lock and patch only the stage's own entry. Use per-run temp dirs, e.g. `tempfile.mkdtemp(dir=track_dir, prefix='.stems.')`, and clean up stale ones by age. Add a library-level lock for the planned batch mode. Test: run stub stems and video stages concurrently and assert both manifest entries survive.

**Verifier's notes.** Every stage snapshots the manifest when it starts (stems.py:141-142, features.py:278, lyrics.py:437, video.py:317), and its `_record` writes the whole snapshot back (stems.py:172-174, features.py:310-312, lyrics.py:482-484, video.py:343-345) through write_manifest. write_manifest is at library.py:46-48; the cited library.py:131-139 does not exist, as the file has 72 lines. grep finds no lock or flock anywhere. So `mrw stems T & mrw video T` lets stems revert documents.video to its start-time snapshot (pending), dropping content_sha256 and api_usage. The fixed temp dirs are rmtree'd on entry (stems.py:185-187, video.py:449-454). None of this is recorded. Medium: it needs concurrent runs on one track, and recovery is cheap because captions replay from the cache.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do not hold one exclusive track lock for the whole stage run: that serializes the stems and video runs that PLAN §3 draws as independent branches. Instead: (1) flock `<track>/.manifest.lock` only around a read-modify-write in `_record` that re-reads the manifest from disk and patches only the stage's own entry; (2) take a per-stage lock, e.g. `.stems.lock`, so a second concurrent run of the same stage fails fast with exit 2; (3) use mkdtemp per-run temp dirs.

### H4. Cost ledger overwritten on every re-run

#### ORCH-7: The cost ledger keeps only the latest run per stage, so re-running video erases earlier spend, including partial spend

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:537`, `mrw/video.py:562`, `mrw/costs.py:98`, `mrw/cli.py:224`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:83`, `mrw/models.py:305`

**Evidence.** Every video run replaces `manifest.documents.video` in full: `_record(DocumentEntry(status="ok", …, run=RunMetadata(…, api_usage=usage)))` (video.py:537-551), and the failure path does the same (video.py:562-578). A re-run served entirely from cache records `ApiUsage(calls=0, input_tokens=0, output_tokens=0, usd=0.0, …)` (video.py:532-536). `aggregate_ledger` sums only the current `documents[*].run.api_usage` and `stems.run.api_usage` (costs.py:98-117), and `mrw costs` relies on it (cli.py:224-242). The manifest schema promises: "Recorded on failed runs too — partial spend never vanishes from the ledger." (manifest.schema.json:83).

**Failure scenario.** Run 1 with caption_backend="anthropic" makes 50 calls and records $0.12. On a second track, BudgetExceeded tells the user to raise caption_budget_usd_per_run. Doing so changes the video config_hash for every track, so `mrw video T` re-runs, replays all 50 captions from cache and records usd=0.0. `mrw costs` now reports $0 for T. In a second case, a run fails after 20 paid calls and records a failed entry with api_usage, as designed. The retry pays for the remaining 30 and replaces the entry, so the 20 paid calls disappear, which is exactly what the PR #12 round-3 fix was meant to prevent.

**Proposal.** Make the ledger append-only. Add a list to the manifest (additive, manifest 1.4.0): `api_ledger: [{stage, started_at, status, calls, input_tokens, output_tokens, usd, estimated_usd}]`. Append one entry for every run that spends on the API, whether it succeeds or fails, under the manifest lock from ORCH-4, and never rewrite earlier entries. Keep `run.api_usage` as the per-run view. `aggregate_ledger` sums `api_ledger`, falling back to run.api_usage for manifests older than 1.4.0. Test: a failed partial run followed by a successful retry must make `mrw costs` report the sum of both.

**Verifier's notes.** video.py:537-551 (success) and 562-578 (failure) replace documents.video wholesale. A fully cached re-run records calls=0 and usd=0.0 (video.py:531-536), and aggregate_ledger sums only the current run.api_usage blocks (costs.py:98-117). A failed run's partial spend, recorded by design per PR #12 round 3 (test_m5_smoke.py:361), is therefore overwritten by the retry, and any config-driven re-run zeroes earlier spend. This contradicts manifest.schema.json:83 ('partial spend never vanishes from the ledger'), and nothing records it as accepted. Medium rather than high: only the operator's spend report is wrong. Analysis documents are unaffected and the money was spent either way.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Make the ledger append-only as proposed, appending under the ORCH-4 lock. Migration detail: on the first write to a pre-1.4.0 manifest, seed api_ledger with the existing entry's run.api_usage, or the spend recorded before the upgrade is lost at that moment. When api_ledger is present, sum only it, so nothing is counted twice.

#### SCH-4: The manifest's API cost 'ledger' is overwritten on every run, so spend is lost across a failure-and-retry or a re-run

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, C2, S2
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:343-345`, `mrw/video.py:347-363`, `mrw/video.py:531-551`, `mrw/video.py:559-575`, `mrw/costs.py:84-117`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:68-92`

**Evidence.** manifest.schema.json:83 promises 'the cost ledger entry ... Recorded on failed runs too — partial spend never vanishes from the ledger. (Added in 1.3.0.)' Every run replaces the whole entry instead: video.py:343-345 `def _record(entry: DocumentEntry) -> None: manifest.documents.video = entry`. The success path builds a new DocumentEntry with `api_usage=usage` (video.py:533, 548), taken from `_accrued_usage(estimate)`. That counts only the current backend's calls and tokens (video.py:347-363: `calls=backend.calls, input_tokens=backend.input_tokens, ...`). The failure path (559-575) likewise records only this run's partial spend. costs.aggregate_ledger (costs.py:98-107) sums only the api_usage blocks currently present in each manifest. Separately, run_metadata is described as 'Volatile provenance for the most recent successful run of the stage' (manifest.schema.json:70), yet failed video entries now carry `run` (video.py:565-575). That semantics change shipped in 1.3.0 without a description update.

**Failure scenario.** `mrw video T` with caption_backend = anthropic makes 40 paid caption calls (cost $A), then the 41st reply is not strict JSON and `json.loads` (captions.py:134) raises. The entry becomes {status: failed, run.api_usage.usd: A}. The operator re-runs: 40 captions come from the cache and 38 new paid calls cost $B. The success entry replaces the failed one, and `mrw costs` reports $B for a track that cost $A+$B. The same under-reporting happens after any config change that invalidates the cache (a new caption_prompt_version or shot threshold): the previous full spend disappears from the ledger.

**Proposal.** Make the ledger append-only with an additive manifest 1.4.0 field, e.g. a top-level `api_ledger` array of fixed-key objects {document, started_at, status, calls, input_tokens, output_tokens, usd, estimated_usd}, appended on both success and failure. Keep `run.api_usage` as 'this run' and have aggregate_ledger sum the ledger. Alternatively, carry the previous entry's cumulative totals forward whenever `_record` replaces an entry. Rewrite the run_metadata description as 'most recent run; failed runs carry run when they spent money'. Add a fail-then-retry test asserting the total equals the sum of both runs.

**Verifier's notes.** video.py:343-345: `_record` replaces `manifest.documents.video` wholesale. Both the success path (531-551) and the failure path (559-575) store only this run's backend counters (`_accrued_usage`, 347-363). costs.aggregate_ledger (costs.py:98-117) sums whatever api_usage blocks are present now. Captions are cached only after a successful parse (captions.py:134-139), so in a fail-then-retry the first run's paid calls are replayed free and its $A disappears when the success entry overwrites the failed one. That directly defeats manifest.schema.json:83, 'partial spend never vanishes from the ledger', which PR #12 round 3 added and test_m5_smoke.py:360-411 checks only within one run. run_metadata's description, 'most recent successful run' (manifest.schema.json:70), is contradicted by failed entries carrying `run` (video.py:566-575). Nothing in the docs records this. It is cost under-reporting rather than analysis output, so medium.

#### VID-7: Cost ledger is overwritten on every run: earlier spend, including partial spend from failed runs, vanishes from `mrw costs`

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** contract-violation · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, S2, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:531-551`, `mrw/video.py:561-578`, `mrw/costs.py:98-107`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:83`, `mrw/video.py:348-349`

**Evidence.** Each run replaces the whole manifest entry: on success mrw/video.py:537-551 `_record(DocumentEntry(status="ok", ..., run=RunMetadata(..., api_usage=usage)))`, on failure mrw/video.py:561-578. `usage` counts only the current run's backend calls (mrw/video.py:350-362) and is None for the null backend (mrw/video.py:531-536). aggregate_ledger sums only each entry's current run.api_usage (mrw/costs.py:98-107). Yet schemas/manifest.schema.json:83 promises 'Recorded on failed runs too — partial spend never vanishes from the ledger', and mrw/video.py:348-349 says 'paid caption calls must never vanish from the cost ledger'. The test at tests/test_m5_smoke.py:361-411 checks only the failed entry, not what happens after the next run.

**Failure scenario.** Run 1 (anthropic, 78 shots) fails at shot 40 after 39 paid calls, recording $0.11 on the failed entry. Run 2 replays 39 cached captions and pays for the other 39. The entry is replaced, so `mrw costs` shows $0.11 instead of $0.22. A later detector_threshold change produces new frames and new calls, and that run's figure again replaces the old one. Switching back to the null backend records api_usage=None, and every recorded dollar for the track disappears.

**Proposal.** Make the ledger append-only. Either add an additive manifest field, e.g. top-level `api_ledger: [{stage, started_at, status, calls, input_tokens, output_tokens, usd, estimated_usd}]` (manifest minor bump), or append lines to a library-level ledger file through the canonical writer. aggregate_ledger then sums that record. A simpler alternative is to carry cumulative totals forward whenever an entry is replaced. Test: a failed run with spend, then a successful run, then a null-backend run; `mrw costs` must equal the sum of all paid calls.

**Verifier's notes.** Both the success path (video.py:537-551) and the failure path (561-578) replace manifest.documents.video wholesale. Usage holds only this run's backend counters (350-362) and is None for the null backend (531-536). aggregate_ledger sums only the current entries (costs.py:98-107). Consequences: a failed run's partial spend disappears at the next run. A cache-replay re-run after any video config edit records calls=0 and usd=0; for example, raising caption_budget_usd_per_run changes the stage hash. Switching back to null drops the track's spend entirely. run_metadata is described as the most recent run (manifest.schema.json:70), so the per-run view is by design. But api_usage's description at line 83 promises 'partial spend never vanishes from the ledger', and `mrw costs` claims to sum recorded spend. That promise is not met, and no deliberate decision about it is recorded.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Prefer an additive manifest `api_ledger` list (manifest 1.4.0), appended on every paid run including failed ones, and keep run.api_usage as the per-run view. aggregate_ledger sums api_ledger and falls back to run.api_usage for pre-1.4.0 manifests. Test: failed run with spend, then success, then a null run; the total must equal all paid calls.

### H5. Interruption is not recorded

#### SEC-9: Ctrl-C or SIGTERM skips failure recording: paid caption spend disappears from the ledger, and an aborted copy_video is later analyzed in place of the original

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:557`, `mrw/video.py:347`, `mrw/ingest.py:194`, `mrw/ingest.py:228`, `mrw/video.py:300`

**Evidence.** Failure recording catches only `except Exception` (video.py:557, stems.py:245, features.py:368, lyrics.py:612), so a KeyboardInterrupt skips `_record`. That contradicts the comment at video.py:348-349: 'paid caption calls must never vanish from the cost ledger'. The CLI installs no SIGTERM or SIGHUP handler, so those signals kill the process with no cleanup at all. Ingest creates the track dir and writes source_audio.flac (194-199) before steps that can fail or be interrupted. One of those steps is a non-atomic, possibly multi-GB `shutil.copyfile(media_path, track_dir / f'source_video{suffix}')` (228-231); the manifest is written last. `_resolve_video_path` prefers any `source_video.*` it finds by glob over the intact original (video.py:300-302). Orphaned track dirs are never removed, because `track_ids()` simply hides directories without a manifest.

**Failure scenario.** (1) The user presses Ctrl-C two minutes into captioning a 300-shot video, after about 120 paid calls. The manifest keeps its previous entry, and `mrw costs` never sees that spend; the captions survive only in the cache. (2) With `copy_video = true`, the user aborts while 8 GB is copying, switches the setting back off and re-ingests. Ingest succeeds but leaves the truncated `source_video.mp4` in place, and `mrw video` analyzes it instead of the intact original. If the file's moov atom is at the end, the stage fails with 'ffprobe failed: moov atom not found'. With faststart, nb_frames still claims the full length: the last shot is stretched to match, and either extraction fails or motion silently reads 0.0 past the cut point. (3) Every ingest failure after mkdir leaves behind a directory with a FLAC, possibly with a partial video copy, that nothing ever cleans up.

**Proposal.** In each stage, catch BaseException in the failure path, record `failed` together with any partial api_usage, then re-raise. In the CLI entry point, install SIGTERM/SIGHUP handlers that raise KeyboardInterrupt so those paths run. Append each paid call to a per-track `.cache/captions/ledger.jsonl` at call time, so spend survives even SIGKILL, and have `mrw costs` read it. Copy the video to a temp name and rename it into place; record the copied filename and sha in source.json; prefer a copy only if its size and sha match. Wrap ingest's post-mkdir work so that a failure removes a track dir that had no manifest before. Add a test that raises KeyboardInterrupt mid-captioning.

**Verifier's notes.** Every failure path uses `except Exception` (video.py:557, stems.py:245, features.py:368, lyrics.py:612), so KeyboardInterrupt skips `_record`. That contradicts video.py:348-349: 'paid caption calls must never vanish from the cost ledger'. grep finds no signal handlers. Spend is not paid twice, because each caption is cached immediately after its call (captions.py:139), but `mrw costs` under-reports it. The ingest copy (ingest.py:228-231) is a non-atomic copyfile, and `_resolve_video_path` (video.py:300-302) prefers any `source_video.*` the glob finds. A natural way to hit this: ENOSPC during the copy surfaces as a traceback, the user turns off copy_video to save space and re-ingests, and the truncated copy is then silently analysed instead of the intact original. Orphaned directories are only clutter, since re-ingest reuses them.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** In each stage, catch BaseException, record `failed` with the partial api_usage, then re-raise the original exception (do not wrap a KeyboardInterrupt in VideoError). Map SIGTERM/SIGHUP to KeyboardInterrupt in the CLI entry point. Copy the video to a temp name and os.replace it into place. In `_resolve_video_path`, use a copy only if its size equals source.json's file.size_bytes; this is cheap and needs no schema change. The per-call ledger.jsonl is optional.

### H6. Subprocesses can hang

#### SEC-5: Ingest's PCM-hash decode deadlocks on damaged media (no subprocess has a timeout), and audio silently dropped by decode errors is never reported

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, T3, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:111`, `mrw/ingest.py:54`, `mrw/ingest.py:200`, `mrw/video.py:198`, `mrw/video.py:255`

**Evidence.** `_decode_hash_and_duration` reads stdout to EOF before it ever reads stderr: `with subprocess.Popen(cmd, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE) as proc: while chunk := proc.stdout.read(1 << 20): ...; stderr = proc.stderr.read()...` (ingest.py:111-116). Once ffmpeg fills the stderr pipe buffer it blocks, and the two processes wait on each other forever. None of mrw's subprocess calls has a timeout (ingest.py:54 and 111; video.py:198, 225, 248, 255), and neither does `scenedetect.detect` (video.py:280). I reproduced this with ffmpeg 6.1.1 on a generated 180 s, 192 kbps MP3 damaged with 1 KiB of random bytes every 4 KiB across 3 MB. The equivalent of `_normalize_audio` returns 0, because decode errors are non-fatal. At `-v error`, the PCM decode then writes 128,744 B (1,694 lines) of 'Header missing' / 'Error submitting packet to decoder'. An exact replica of the read loop hung until it was killed at 45 s; the same decode with stderr sent to a temp file finishes in about 1 s. A milder 'bad-sector' damage pattern (2 KiB zeroed every 8 KiB) produced 56,240 B of stderr. That fits in Linux's 64 KiB pipe but far exceeds macOS's default 16 KiB pipe buffer (assumption: XNU's PIPE_SIZE, which grows only for large writes; medium confidence). The damage also truncates audio silently: the damaged files decode to 127.7 s and 139.0 s of PCM instead of 180.0 s, with rc 0. `_run` throws stderr away on success (ingest.py:54-57), so source.json carries no flag, and everything after the damage is shifted on the shared timeline.

**Failure scenario.** A partly corrupted MP3 from an old rip or a truncated download makes `mrw ingest song.mp3` sit at 0% CPU forever, holding a half-built track directory; a future batch run would stall on it the same way. With lighter damage that doesn't deadlock, source_audio.flac comes out up to 52 s shorter than the original with no warning. For a music video, every audio-derived time after the damage then drifts against the frame-derived shot times.

**Proposal.** Wherever stdout is streamed, send stderr to a `tempfile.TemporaryFile()` (or drain it in a thread). Give every ffmpeg and ffprobe call a timeout scaled to media duration, e.g. max(120 s, 3x duration), mapped to IngestError/VideoError. Better: produce the FLAC and the PCM hash from a single decode (two outputs, or hash the just-written FLAC once equality is verified on fixtures), which also halves ingest decode time. Count stderr error lines and compare the normalized duration against the container duration. Surface damage in the document, e.g. an additive `normalized_audio.decode_errors` field in source.json plus a manifest warning, and exit non-zero above a threshold. Add a T3 test that builds a deterministically damaged MP3 inside the test.

**Verifier's notes.** I reproduced both halves. A generated 180 s, 192 kbps MP3 damaged with 1 KiB of random bytes every 4 KiB decodes with rc 0 at -v error. It writes 132,088 B (1,738 lines) of stderr and only 126.1 s of PCM. A replica of the read loop at ingest.py:111-116 (stdout read to EOF, then stderr) hung for more than 20 s on Linux until I killed it; the same decode with stderr sent to a file finished in 0.5 s. Milder damage (2 KiB zeroed every 8 KiB) gave 58,520 B of stderr and 137.9 s of PCM. That completes on Linux's 64 KiB pipe; whether it blocks on macOS depends on XNU pipe growth, which I could not verify (medium confidence). `_run` (ingest.py:53-58) throws stderr away on success, so source.json would show normalized_audio.duration_seconds 126.1 against media.duration_seconds 180 with no flag (an H1 gap). No subprocess call has a timeout. I downgraded to medium because it needs damaged media, which is an edge case rather than a common variant.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Minimal S-effort fix: send stderr to `tempfile.TemporaryFile()` or drain it in a reader thread, and add duration-scaled timeouts that map to IngestError/VideoError. Then add decode-error surfacing: compare the normalized duration against the container duration, add an additive `decode_errors` field and a manifest warning. Consolidating to a single decode is optional. If you do it, first verify on fixtures that audio_stream_sha256 is byte-identical to the current definition.

#### ORCH-9: The ingest decode pass can deadlock when ffmpeg writes a lot to stderr, and ffmpeg decode errors are discarded when it exits 0 (H1)

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H1, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:111`, `mrw/ingest.py:116`, `mrw/ingest.py:53`

**Evidence.** ingest.py:111-116: `with subprocess.Popen(cmd, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE) as proc: … while chunk := proc.stdout.read(1 << 20): … stderr = proc.stderr.read()…`. stderr is only read after stdout reaches end of file. `_run` (ingest.py:53-58) looks at stderr only when returncode != 0. Assumed ffmpeg behaviour (medium-high confidence): per-packet decode errors are logged at error level, and ffmpeg still exits 0 unless the failure fraction exceeds -max_error_rate (default 2/3). Repeated-line suppression only collapses identical consecutive lines. Pipe buffers are 16–64 KB (16 KB by default on macOS).

**Failure scenario.** Take a damaged MP3 or M4A (bad sectors, interrupted copy) with a few hundred corrupt frames. `_normalize_audio` succeeds: ffmpeg exits 0 and its errors are thrown away. In `_decode_hash_and_duration`, ffmpeg's alternating 'Header missing' and decode-error lines fill the stderr pipe. ffmpeg then blocks writing to stderr while mrw blocks reading stdout, so `mrw ingest` hangs forever, and a batch run would stall with it. With fewer errors, ingest completes and the track is registered as ok, with gaps or glitches in source_audio.flac and nothing in source.json or the manifest to say so.

**Proposal.** Read stderr at the same time as stdout, by sending it to a `tempfile.TemporaryFile()` or a reader thread, and add a generous timeout. Better still, compute audio_stream_sha256 and duration by streaming source_audio.flac with soundfile (`dtype="int16"`; the interleaved bytes are the same s16le), which also avoids one full decode of the original. Capture stderr on success in both passes. If it is not empty, set a deterministic flag in source.json (for example an additive `normalized_audio.flags: ["decode_errors"]`) and add a manifest warning with the truncated text. Test with a fixture whose middle bytes are zeroed.

**Verifier's notes.** The hazard is real in the code. `_decode_hash_and_duration` opens both pipes and drains stdout to EOF before reading stderr (ingest.py:111-116), the pattern the subprocess docs warn can deadlock. Both passes discard stderr when the exit code is 0 (ingest.py:53-58, 117). `_normalize_audio` uses subprocess.run, which reads both pipes, so it cannot deadlock. Whether a real damaged file actually hangs depends on ffmpeg behaviour I could not verify offline. It would have to emit more than about 16 KB (macOS pipe buffer) or 64 KB (Linux) of error-level lines: a decoder line such as 'Header missing' alternating with the CLI's decode-error line, which repeat suppression does not collapse. It would also have to exit 0 below -max_error_rate (default 2/3). I hold both with medium confidence, and the silent-glitch half likewise depends on ffmpeg exiting 0.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Minimal fix: send stderr to a TemporaryFile (or drain it in a thread) and inspect it after wait(). If you hash the FLAC via soundfile int16 instead, first add a fixture test proving byte equality with the current s16le ffmpeg pipeline, because audio_stream_sha256 is a cross-track dedup key and must keep its meaning for existing tracks. A deterministic `decode_errors` flag (additive, source 1.1.0) is reasonable.

### H7. A failed ingest leaves a half-built track directory

#### ORCH-12: A failed ingest leaves a half-built track directory that status never shows, and avg_frame_rate '0/0' crashes after the directory is created

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H2, T4
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:194`, `mrw/ingest.py:209`, `mrw/ingest.py:147`, `mrw/ingest.py:298`, `mrw/library.py:33`, `tests/test_m1_smoke.py:192`

**Evidence.** Ingest creates the track directory (ingest.py:194-195) and has ffmpeg write source_audio.flac directly into it (ingest.py:198-199). Only after that does it run the decode hash, the video probe parsing, the lyrics copy (ingest.py:147) and the video copy (ingest.py:228-231); the manifest is written last (ingest.py:298). Nothing cleans up if an exception is raised. `float(Fraction(video_stream.get("avg_frame_rate", "0/1")))` (ingest.py:209): `Fraction("0/0")` raises ZeroDivisionError (verified), and the default only covers a missing key. `track_ids()` lists only directories that contain manifest.json (library.py:33-38), so these leftovers never appear in `status`. The only failure test (test_m1_smoke.py:192-202) fails before the directory is created.

**Failure scenario.** A video whose non-cover-art stream reports avg_frame_rate '0/0' (for example a single-frame still track) raises ZeroDivisionError with a traceback (exit 1). By then library/T/source_audio.flac, and any lyrics_input or source_video copy, has already been written. There is no manifest and nothing in status, so each attempt leaves from about 25 MB to several GB of orphaned data. A copy_video run that runs out of disk space leaves a truncated source_video.* that the video stage later prefers (see ORCH-2).

**Proposal.** Build the track in a staging directory (`library/.ingest-<id>.<pid>.tmp/`) and `os.replace` it into place only on success, when the track directory does not exist yet. On any exception, remove the staging directory. Parse frame rates defensively: treat a zero denominator as unknown (fps 0.0, frame_count from nb_frames or else 0) instead of crashing. Test: force a failure after normalisation with monkeypatch and check the library contains no track directory.

**Verifier's notes.** The track directory is created and the FLAC written (ingest.py:194-199) before the decode hash, the frame-rate parse (209), the lyrics copy (223) and the video copy (228-231). The manifest is written last (298) and nothing cleans up on failure. Verified that Fraction('0/0') raises ZeroDivisionError; the default at ingest.py:209 only covers a missing key. track_ids() requires manifest.json (library.py:33-38), so leftovers are invisible to status. The only failure test fails before mkdir (test_m1_smoke.py:192-202). Two corrections: at the 0/0 crash only source_audio.flac exists, because the lyrics and video copies come later; and orphans do not accumulate per attempt, since a retry reuses and overwrites the same track_id directory. Medium: an edge-case input makes ingest crash permanently (traceback, exit 1), and a partial video copy feeds ORCH-2.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: build the track in a staging directory and os.replace it into place only on success. Treat a zero-denominator avg_frame_rate as unknown, falling back to r_frame_rate and then to nb_frames/duration. video.py:211 has the same Fraction crash; there it is inside the try block, so it is recorded as failed rather than raised as a traceback.

### H8. Tags read only from format.tags with lowercase keys

#### CRIT-2: Ingest reads tags from format.tags only, with exact lowercase keys: FLAC (uppercase keys), Ogg/Opus (tags on the stream) and MKV/WebM (ARTIST) lose title and artist

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H1, C2, T3
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:202-205`, `mrw/ingest.py:267-269`, `mrw/ingest.py:279`, `mrw/cli.py:48`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:13-15`

**Evidence.** mrw/ingest.py:202-205: `tags_raw = fmt.get("tags", {}) or {}` ... `if tags_raw.get("title") or tags_raw.get("artist"):`. Only format-level tags are read, and only exact lowercase keys match. The manifest title then falls back to the filename (ingest.py:267-269 `title or (tags.title if tags and tags.title else None) or media_path.stem`) and the artist is dropped (:279). The CLI has --title but no --artist (cli.py:48).

Verified with system ffprobe 6.1.1 on files I generated (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit/tags):
- FLAC written with TITLE/ARTIST: format.tags keeps the case, {'TITLE': ..., 'ARTIST': ...}.
- Ogg Vorbis and Opus: format.tags is None; the tags appear only under streams[0].tags, in either case.
- MKV and WebM: the artist is reported as 'ARTIST' even when ffmpeg wrote 'artist'.
- MP3, M4A/MP4, WAV and AIFF: lowercase keys, so these work.

Assumption (high confidence): common FLAC taggers (Picard, foobar2000, Mp3tag, XLD) write uppercase Vorbis-comment keys. SCH's coverage notes mentioned the FLAC case as unverified; the Ogg/Opus and Matroska cases are new.

**Failure scenario.** A ripped FLAC tagged TITLE='Neon Reverie', ARTIST='X' is ingested:
- source.json has no media.tags.
- The manifest title is the filename stem (e.g. '01 - neon_reverie_master') and the artist is absent.

An .opus file loses both fields. An .mkv or .webm music video loses its artist. `mrw status` shows the filename, and no downstream consumer (LLM, iPad app) receives the artist.

**Proposal.** - Build the tag dict case-insensitively from format.tags merged with the selected audio stream's tags; the stream tags are needed for Ogg/Opus. Example: lowercase every key from `audio_stream.get('tags')` and `fmt.get('tags')`, with format-level values winning.
- Add an --artist option.
- Add tests with ffmpeg-generated FLAC (uppercase keys), Ogg, Opus and MKV fixtures (T3).

Existing tracks keep their stale tags until re-ingest can refresh source.json (DET-4/ORCH-6).

**Verifier's notes.** ingest.py:202-205 reads only fmt['tags'] and only exact lowercase keys.

I re-verified with ffprobe 6.1.1 on files I generated (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/d3582791-7c7b-5d18-bdbf-52b78033257a/scratchpad/crit-verify/tags):
- FLAC whose raw Vorbis comments are 'TITLE=' / 'ARTIST=' (I confirmed this by parsing the metadata block): format.tags is {'TITLE','ARTIST'}, with case preserved.
- Ogg Vorbis and Opus: format.tags is None; the tags appear only in streams[0].tags.
- MKV and WebM: format.tags has 'title' but 'ARTIST'.
- MP3, M4A, MP4 and WAV: lowercase keys, so these work.

Result: FLAC tagged with the conventional uppercase keys, and Ogg/Opus, lose title and artist; MKV/WebM lose the artist. The title falls back to the filename stem (ingest.py:267-269), the artist is absent (:279), and the CLI has no --artist (cli.py:48). The impact is limited to advisory metadata, so medium stands.

The proposal has a flaw. I generated an MKV whose audio stream has title='English Stereo' (a track name) and whose format tags have no title. Merging stream tags with format tags winning would set the manifest title to 'English Stereo'.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Lowercase the keys of format.tags.
- Fall back to the selected audio stream's tags only when format_name == 'ogg' (covers .ogg and .opus, where Vorbis comments live on the logical stream). Never take a stream-level 'title' from Matroska or MP4: those are track names.
- Add --artist with the same precedence as --title.
- Add ffmpeg-generated fixtures (-metadata TITLE=… for FLAC; Ogg; Opus; MKV with a track-name title).

Existing tracks keep their old tags, because ingest is a no-op once registered (ingest.py:176-183).

### H9. Untrusted strings echoed raw to the terminal

#### SEC-14: Untrusted media tags and filenames are printed raw to the terminal (escape-sequence injection)

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:267`, `mrw/cli.py:72`, `mrw/cli.py:75`, `mrw/cli.py:314`

**Evidence.** `resolved_title = (title or (tags.title if tags and tags.title else None) or media_path.stem)` (ingest.py:267-269) copies the container's title tag, or the filename, verbatim into manifest.title. The CLI prints it without sanitizing: `typer.echo(f'ingested {result.track_id} ({result.title}) [{kind}]')` (cli.py:75, and similarly at 72). `mrw status` prints every track's title on every run (314). Error messages also echo raw ffmpeg stderr and file paths (cli.py:68, via ingest.py:57). JSON documents escape control characters, but terminals act on them.

**Failure scenario.** An MP3 whose ID3 title is 'ESC]0;pwned BEL ESC[2J ...' clears the screen and retitles the terminal window on `mrw ingest`, and again on every later `mrw status`. On terminals that honour them, OSC 8 hyperlinks and OSC 52 clipboard writes can plant misleading links or clipboard contents. Terminal escape handling has also had RCE-class bugs (e.g. iTerm2 CVE-2019-9535). Source-agnostic ingest accepts files from anywhere by design.

**Proposal.** Add a single `_safe(s)` helper that replaces C0/C1 control characters (except tab) with U+FFFD or hex escapes. Use it for every echo of untrusted strings: titles, artists, filenames, ffmpeg stderr and exception text. Optionally also strip control characters from manifest title/artist at ingest, while keeping the raw tags JSON-escaped in source.json.

**Verifier's notes.** The title is the CLI title, the container tag or the filename stem (ingest.py:267-269). It is echoed raw (cli.py:72, 75) and again on every `mrw status` (cli.py:314). ffprobe's JSON writer escapes control bytes as \u001b, json.loads restores them, and click.echo strips only CSI sequences, and only when the stream is not a TTY. So escape sequences reach the terminal. The JSON documents are safe, because json.dumps escapes C0 controls. Realistic impact on Terminal.app or iTerm2 is screen clearing or title and link spoofing. Low severity, S effort.

### H10. Performance

#### SEC-12: Whole-file float32 decodes with avoidable copies: Demucs peaks near 18 GB on a 1-hour input, and there is no duration guard

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** performance · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium
- **Locations:** `mrw/stems.py:91`, `mrw/stems.py:96`, `mrw/stems.py:111`, `mrw/stems.py:124`, `mrw/features.py:315`, `mrw/lyrics.py:133`

**Evidence.** In `_separate`, `data, sample_rate = sf.read(flac_path, dtype='float32', always_2d=True)` (stems.py:91) is followed by `wav = torch.from_numpy(data.T.copy())` (96), which keeps both copies alive. `wav = (wav - ref_mean) / ref_std` (111) and `sources = sources * ref_std + ref_mean` (124) are both out-of-place. In demucs 4.0.1 apply.py (read at the locked tag), `out = th.zeros(batch, len(model.sources), channels, length)` (223) allocates the full output. For a BagOfModels such as htdemucs it then does `estimates = 0.` ... `estimates += out` (183-194), which creates a second full-size tensor. Estimate for a 1-hour 44.1 kHz stereo input (N = 158.8 M samples):
- data 1.27 GB + wav 1.27 GB + ref 0.64 GB
- separated output 5.08 GB, 10.2 GB while the bag sum runs
- the denormalize line then creates two more 5.08 GB tensors
That gives a peak of about 18 GB (about 3 GB at 10 min, 1.2-1.5 GB at 4 min). Features decodes every channel whole (features.py:86-89, 315-343) and keeps the mix stereo until LUFS runs at the end. librosa's rms (frames, then abs2) and its STFT transients at hop 441 are each about 3 GB per channel at 1 hour (assumption based on librosa 0.11's implementation; medium confidence). `_detection_window_audio` decodes the entire vocal stem (1.9 GB at 1 hour) just to use at most 30 s (lyrics.py:133-142).

**Failure scenario.** `mrw stems` on a 60-minute DJ mix or concert recording is accepted with no check. After 30+ minutes of separation, the denormalize step pushes a 16 GB Mac into swap or the out-of-memory dialog. Either the run is lost and recorded as failed, or the machine stalls.

**Proposal.** Apply these cheap fixes. They perform the same IEEE operations in the same order, so output should be bit-identical; confirm with the existing CPU double-run test.
- Build wav with `torch.from_numpy(np.ascontiguousarray(data.T))`, then `del data`.
- Normalize in place with `wav.sub_(ref_mean).div_(ref_std)`, then `del ref`.
- Denormalize in place with `sources.mul_(ref_std).add_(ref_mean)`.
Together these remove about 10 GB of transients at 1 hour. In features, compute LUFS first and then drop the stereo array. In lyrics, read only the needed slices via `sf.SoundFile(...).seek()/read()`. Add `ingest.max_duration_seconds` (e.g. 1200 s, overridable), or at least a warning showing the estimated peak memory, and document memory scaling next to PLAN §9's timings.

**Verifier's notes.** The mechanism is confirmed. stems.py:91/96 keep both the soundfile array and its transposed copy alive, and stems.py:111/124 are out-of-place. In the demucs 4.0.1 apply.py source, the full output is allocated with `th.zeros(batch, len(model.sources), channels, length)`, and for the BagOfModels that htdemucs resolves to, `estimates = 0.` followed by `estimates += out` creates a second full-size tensor. librosa 0.11.0 rms materializes `util.abs2(util.frame(y, 2048, 441))`, about 2.9 GB per channel per hour. So about 18 GB peak for a 60-minute input is consistent. For in-scope inputs (a 4-10 minute song) the peak is about 1.2-3 GB, so it bites only on hour-long inputs to a song workbench. The in-place fixes are cheap.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Make the in-place changes (`np.ascontiguousarray(data.T)` then `del data`; `wav.sub_(ref_mean).div_(ref_std)`; `sources.mul_(ref_std).add_(ref_mean)`). To prove bit-identity, compare against stems produced by the current code: the double-run test compares new code with new code and cannot show this. Prefer a CLI duration warning over a new IngestConfig field, because per the config-evolution policy a new field mass-stales source for every track. In lyrics, read only the needed slices with sf.SoundFile seek/read.

#### CRIT-11: Clipped transcription pays a full 30 s Whisper encoder pass for every vocal clip, multiplying lyrics runtime on phrase-structured vocals

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** performance · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium
- **Locations:** `mrw/lyrics.py:111-123`, `mrw/lyrics.py:190-199`, `mrw/lyrics.py:160-162`, `mrw/config.py:76-78`, `PLAN.md:470`

**Evidence.** clips_from_regions (mrw/lyrics.py:111-123) merges padded windows only when they overlap (`s, e = max(0.0, s - padding), e + padding`; `if clips and s <= clips[-1][1]`). Vocal regions more than 2x0.5 s apart therefore become separate clips. The clips go to clip_timestamps (lyrics.py:196-199) on a single-threaded model (lyrics.py:160-162 `cpu_threads=1`).

In faster-whisper 1.2.1 (wheel sha256 79a66ad5... matching uv.lock; transcribe.py generate_segments), each clip is processed as follows:
- `segment_size = min(nb_max_frames, content_frames - seek, seek_clip_end - seek)`
- `segment = pad_or_trim(segment)`
- `encoder_output = self.encode(segment)`

So every clip costs at least one full 30 s encoder pass, however short it is. PLAN.md:470 budgets about 20–60 s for Whisper.

**Failure scenario.** A 240 s track needs about 8 encoder windows unclipped. Suppose its vocal activity forms 40 clips, i.e. phrases separated by gaps over 1 s (an assumption about typical material). The clipped run then needs at least 40 full encoder passes plus per-clip decoding and word alignment: about 5 times the transcription time on one CPU thread. Across an M7 batch of 100 tracks this adds hours. Clip count and lyrics duration are not recorded, so the slowdown is invisible.

**Proposal.** - Record clip count and stage duration in the manifest run metadata, and measure them on the field tracks.
- Then pack neighbouring clips into one window while the packed span stays ≤ 30 s and the gap stays below a new `lyrics.clip_merge_gap_seconds` (joins the lyrics hash).
- Re-check the review-007 conditioning result before changing the default.

**Verifier's notes.** The mechanism is confirmed in faster-whisper 1.2.1 (wheel sha256 79a66ad5…, matching uv.lock). In generate_segments, each window is cut at seek_clip_end, padded to 30 s by pad_or_trim(), and passed to self.encode(). Every clip therefore costs at least one full encoder pass.

The size of the slowdown depends on how many clips real material produces, which nobody has measured; 40 clips is an assumption.

The claim that lyrics duration is not recorded is wrong: run_lyrics writes manifest documents.lyrics.run.duration_seconds (lyrics.py:595-600). Only the clip count is missing.

Clipping was a deliberate accuracy fix (007 finding 4). Plausible, low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** - Measure before changing anything: log or print the clip count, and compare the existing documents.lyrics.run.duration_seconds with clip_to_vocal_activity on and off on the 007 field tracks.
- Packing clips into shared windows would put silence back inside the windows, which is exactly the failure mechanism 007 finding 4 identified. Any packing therefore needs a config knob (which joins the lyrics hash) and re-validation on that field track before it becomes the default.

## I. Tests and CI

### I1. No CI check blocks a merge

#### TEST-1: No deterministic CI gate: the tests run only inside the LLM review agent, and the job's status ignores pytest's result

- **Severity:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** ci · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, T2, T4, T5
- **Locations:** `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:8-10`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:16`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:57-91`, `docs/reviews/005-m2-stems.md:30-45`, `docs/reviews/006-m3-features.md:89-92`, `docs/reviews/007-m4-lyrics.md:70-84`

**Evidence.** claude-review.yml is the only workflow (`ls .github/workflows`). The tests are one line of the agent's prompt: L70-71 "2. Run the test suite: `uv sync`, then `uv run pytest -q`. Report the exact result in your summary." The job's conclusion comes from claude-code-action (the agent finished, hit the turn cap, or timed out), never from pytest's exit code. Nothing in the repo sets up a required check; branch protection can't be checked statically. Triggers: L8-10 `pull_request: types: [opened, synchronize]` has no `push` to main, so main is never tested. L16 `if: github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository` means fork PRs run no tests at all. Review 005:30-35 records that the action "skips itself" when a PR changes the workflow file, so those PRs run zero tests and infra changes land on main untested. Review 007:70-84: M4 reached main unreviewed through infra commit 2db7fc8, and no test ran there. The signal is unreliable in both directions: a run failed at the 50-turn cap (006:89-92), and a clean run posted no summary (007:103-106). Review 005:41-45 kept the slow tests in this job at a test time of about 2 min, adding "Revisit when M3+ grows the suite". Since then the slow suite has grown from 6 Demucs separations (~81 s of audio) to 9 (~146 s at 1 thread), plus 4 Whisper runs and 7 video-stage runs, all inside the same 20-min/50-turn budget. No re-measurement is recorded.

**Failure scenario.** A PR breaks test_features_double_run_byte_identity, a D1 regression. The agent sees `1 failed` and then either reports it, misreports it, or runs out of turns first. In every case the 'Claude PR Review' check is green, or red for an unrelated reason. Merge isn't blocked, and main is never re-tested afterwards.

**Proposal.** Add .github/workflows/tests.yml. Trigger it on `pull_request` (forks included, since it needs no secrets) and on `push: branches: [main]`, with `permissions: contents: read`. Jobs: `fast` (`uv sync --locked`, then `uv run --no-sync pytest -m 'not slow' -ra`, optionally `ruff check`); `slow` (cached models, `pytest -m slow`); later `e2e-determinism` (TEST-7). Make `fast` and `slow` required status checks. Change step 2 of the review prompt to read results with `gh pr checks` / `gh run view` instead of running the tests; this also lets TEST-13 drop `uv sync:*` / `uv run:*` from the allowlist. Land it on main between review cycles (review 005 note 1).

**Verifier's notes.** I checked this myself. .github/workflows/claude-review.yml is the only workflow. The tests exist only as step 2 of the agent prompt (L70-71: `uv sync`, then `uv run pytest -q`. Report the exact result...`). A failing pytest is therefore just a Bash tool result inside the agent session. The job's conclusion follows the session (the turn-cap failures in 005:36-40 and 006:89-91), never pytest's exit code. The only triggers are pull_request opened/synchronize (L8-10): there is no push to main and not even `reopened`. L16 skips fork PRs entirely, and 005:30-35 records that the action skips itself on PRs that touch the workflow. 007:103-106 shows a green run that posted no summary, i.e. no visible test evidence, being accepted as a clean pass. The growth arithmetic is correct. At M2 the slow tests separated 30+30+6+6+6+3 = 81 s of audio in 6 runs. Since then m3 added 30 s and m4 added 15+20 s, giving 146 s in 9 runs, plus 4 lyrics-stage Whisper runs and 7 video-stage runs. Review 005 note 3 kept the tests in this job with 'Revisit when M3+ grows the suite'. This finding is that revisit with new evidence, not a duplicate. Branch protection cannot be checked statically.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound, with three adjustments. (a) The review job runs concurrently with the new tests job, so a plain `gh pr checks` will usually show `pending`. Either have the agent run `gh pr checks <n> --watch --required`, bounded by the existing 20-min timeout, or drop test reporting from the prompt and let the required checks speak. (b) Making `fast` and `slow` required is a branch-protection or ruleset setting, not a repo file. Record the decision in a review note (P1) so it can be audited. (c) Add `reopened` to the tests trigger, and use a per-ref concurrency group with cancel-in-progress.

#### ARCH-3: No deterministic CI gate: the tests that protect determinism run only as a prompt instruction inside the LLM review job, and there is no lint or type checking

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** ci · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, T2, T5, S1, P3
- **Locations:** `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:8`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:70`, `pyproject.toml:25`, `mrw/cli.py:317`, `docs/reviews/005-m2-stems.md:36`, `docs/reviews/007-m4-lyrics.md:72`, `PLAN.md:440`

**Evidence.** The only workflow is .github/workflows/claude-review.yml. It runs on `pull_request: types: [opened, synchronize]` (lines 8-10) for same-repo PRs only (line 16). Running the tests is step 2 of a natural-language prompt: 'Run the test suite: `uv sync`, then `uv run pytest -q`. Report the exact result in your summary.' (lines 70-71). A failing pytest cannot fail the job, because the check reflects the action run, not the tests. `uv sync` (rather than `uv sync --locked`) can also re-resolve dependencies. The review record shows this harness skipping its output: docs/reviews/005-m2-stems.md:36-40 ('hit `error_max_turns` at 25 without posting anything'), 006-m3-features.md:148-150 (round 4 'failed at the 50-turn cap') and 007-m4-lyrics.md:103-106 ('clean — but silent'). Nothing runs on pushes to main, and 007-m4-lyrics.md:72-80 records M4 landing there unreviewed via 2db7fc8. There is no lint, format or type-check configuration: pyproject.toml has only pytest and uv sections, the dev group is pytest and jsonschema (pyproject.toml:25-29), and there is no pre-commit config. CI runs only on ubuntu x86_64, while the target is macOS arm64 (CLAUDE.md:27-28), and 007:88-95 documents an architecture-dependent 'passes locally, fails on CI' divergence. The S1 rule that models stay in field-order lockstep with the schemas (CLAUDE.md:42-43) is enforced only by review. My static check found all 40 model/schema object pairs in matching order and all six examples valid today, but no test checks this, and `export-schemas` writes only source and manifest (cli.py:329-334). There is also no end-to-end double run, which PLAN.md:440-441 calls for ('CI check (once implemented): analyze a fixture twice, diff -r'). Double-run tests exist per stage only: m0 source, m2 stems (slow), m3 features, m4 lyrics, m5 video (slow). A type checker would already flag real issues, for example stems.py:203-208 passing a plain `str` device into `RunMetadata.device: Literal['cpu','mps']` (models.py:309).

**Failure scenario.** A PR adds an unrounded float to a document. Its review run hits the turn cap before running pytest (as happened in review 005), or the reviewer's comment is ignored. The PR still shows a passing check and merges, and byte identity regresses without any failing check. A commit pushed directly to main (the 2db7fc8 pattern) is never tested at all. The Python 3.13 install failure (ARCH-4) is also invisible, because CI only runs ubuntu with system Python 3.12.

**Proposal.** Add `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, triggered on push to main and on pull_request. It needs no secrets, so it also runs for fork PRs. Job `fast` runs on ubuntu-latest and on macos-14 (arm64, CPU only): `uv sync --locked`, `uv run ruff check`, `uv run ruff format --check`, `uv run pyright` in basic mode (pyright understands pydantic models), and `uv run pytest -m "not slow"`. Job `slow` runs nightly and on main, with model weights cached as today: `pytest -m slow` plus a new `test_e2e_determinism`. That test runs ingest -> stems (cpu) -> features -> lyrics -> video twice into two library roots and compares every file except manifest.json (PLAN §7.5 / M7). Add `tests/test_schema_lockstep.py`: compare each model's `model_fields` order with its schema's `properties` order for every pair, and validate schemas/examples/*. Make `ci/fast` a required status check and keep the Claude review advisory. For ruff, select E, F, I, UP, B and SIM, and ignore PLC0415, because the lazy imports are deliberate (T5). Optionally add a pre-commit hook that runs ruff.

**Verifier's notes.** claude-review.yml is the only workflow. pytest runs only as step 2 of an LLM prompt (lines 70-71), so a failing test cannot fail the check. The workflow runs for same-repo PRs only (line 16), has no push-to-main trigger, uses `uv sync` without --locked, and runs on ubuntu only. pyproject.toml has no lint or type configuration. The review record confirms runs that hit the turn cap and posted nothing: 005:36-40 and 006:89-91. The finding's '006:148-150' citation is wrong because that file has 92 lines. The record also shows the unreviewed 2db7fc8 landing (007:72-84). My static check agrees that lockstep holds today: 41 model/schema pairs are in field order and all six examples validate, so the proposed lockstep test would pass immediately. It is partly mitigated: tests do run on every same-repo PR, and 005:41-45 records a deliberate decision to keep the slow tests in the review job. The gate is unenforced and unreliable rather than absent, so medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Lead with the S piece: a ci.yml on push and pull_request that runs `uv sync --locked` and `uv run pytest -q` (with ffmpeg, espeak-ng and the weight caches) as a required status check. Keep the slow Demucs/Whisper CPU double-runs on PRs; review 005 recorded them as the neural-path determinism evidence at about 2 minutes cached, so do not move them to nightly-only. Add the schema-lockstep/examples test. The macos-14 job, ruff and the end-to-end double-run are good follow-ups. pyright is optional: the cited stems.py:203-208 str→Literal mismatch is a typing nit, because _resolve_device only ever returns 'cpu' or 'mps'.

### I2. Smoke tests weaker than PLAN §11

#### TEST-8: The M5 smoke test is much weaker than PLAN §11 and the deviation is unrecorded: 2 cuts not 10, ±3.6 frames not 1, colour checks that accept ΔE≈83 instead of ΔE<5, no moving scene

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:534-539`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-203`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:238-258`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:72-89`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:536-539: 'generated video with 10 hard cuts between solid-color scenes → exactly 10 boundaries within 1 frame; each shot's top palette swatch within ΔE < 5 of the known color; static scene motion ≈ 0, moving-gradient scene motion > 0.' The fixture (test_m5_smoke.py:188-195) is red 3 s | green 3 s | blue 5 s, i.e. 2 cuts. L240-242 allow ±0.15 s, which is 3.6 frames at 24 fps. L253-256 check only shot 0: `assert r > 150 and g < 100 and b < 100`. With my own sRGB→CIELab script, (151,99,99) is ΔE76 ≈ 83 from pure red, and the green and blue shots are never checked. L257-258 check motion only for shot 0, and the stage fixture has no moving content; the moving case exists only as a synthetic unit test (L92-104). The palette unit test's ±30-per-channel tolerance (L81-86) allows ΔE76 ≈ 19–20. There is no M5 review record or OQ note: docs/reviews stops at 007, and a grep finds no rationale.

**Failure scenario.** Any of these passes the M5 suite: a palette regression that returns a desaturated or wrong-hue swatch that still clears r>150, g<100, b<100; a 2-frame boundary error in frame indexing; a broken motion pass that reports zero motion for every shot.

**Proposal.** Build the fixture PLAN describes: 11 solid-colour shots of 12–30 frames each (at least min_shot_seconds), with colours far apart in Lab, plus one moving-gradient or moving-box shot (lavfi `testsrc2`, or `color` + `overlay` driven by a time expression). Assert len(shots)==11; `abs(end_frame - true_cut_frame) <= 1` for every cut; ΔE76 < 5 between palette[0] and the known colour for every solid shot, computing Lab in the test with an independent sRGB→Lab formula rather than cv2; motion.mean < 1e-3 on static shots and above a floor on the moving shot. Tighten the unit test to ΔE < 5. If ΔE < 5 is unattainable after the yuv420p round trip, record the accepted tolerance in a review note (P1).

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed against PLAN.md:534-539.
- The fixture (test_m5_smoke.py:187-203) has 2 cuts.
- Boundaries are allowed +/-0.15 s, which is 3.6 frames at 24 fps (L240-242).
- Only shot 0's palette is checked, with `r > 150 and g < 100 and b < 100` (L253-256). My independent sRGB-to-CIELab script gives dE76 = 83.3 for (151,99,99) and up to 85.1 for (151,0,99) against pure red. The green and blue shots are never checked.
- Motion is checked only for static shot 0 (L257-258). The moving case exists only as the synthetic motion_stats unit test (L92-104), so a _motion_pass, pair_times or shot_motion integration bug that returns all zeros would pass.
- The unit palette test's +/-30 per channel admits dE76 of about 19.4 for red and 20.4 for blue.
No review record exists (docs/reviews ends at 007) and no OQ note records the deviation (T1, P1). ContentDetector is exact on hard solid-colour cuts, so PLAN's 1-frame bound is attainable.

#### VID-15: M5 smoke test is weaker than PLAN §11 and blind to real-content failure modes; replay test has no network guard

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, T2, T3, T4
- **Locations:** `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:240-242`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:253-258`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:320-357`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:45-48`, `PLAN.md:536-539`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:536-539 specifies 10 hard cuts detected 'within 1 frame', a top palette swatch 'within ΔE < 5' of the known colour, and 'moving-gradient scene motion > 0'. tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195 instead generates 2 cuts between flat solid colours. Lines 240-242 allow ±0.15 s (3.6 frames). Lines 253-256 check only `r > 150 and g < 100 and b < 100`. Line 258 checks only static motion; moving motion is tested only on a synthetic array (lines 92-104), never through the stage. Flat solid-colour scenes cannot reveal VID-1 (band averages of a uniform frame are unchanged) or VID-3 (Farnebäck gives about 0 on flat frames). There are no rotated, start-offset, VFR or audio-tail fixtures (VID-2, VID-4, VID-11). The replay test (lines 320-357) relies on usage counters, so a determinism regression would turn it into a real HTTP call with the fake key. The module-level ffmpeg skipif (lines 45-48) also skips the pure cost and cache tests.

**Failure scenario.** VID-1, VID-2, VID-3, VID-4 and VID-11 all pass a green suite. A future change that perturbs frame bytes makes the 'zero API calls' replay test attempt a live network call instead of failing hermetically.

**Proposal.** Implement the PLAN §11 smoke test as written: testsrc- or noise-textured scenes, 10 cuts, exact-frame boundary assertions, a ΔE<5 check through the stage path on a two-colour frame, and a moving-pattern scene. Add generated fixtures for rotation (`-display_rotation 90`), a start offset (`-itsoffset 0.5` with fps_mode passthrough), VFR (a 30→15 fps concat) and an MKV with an audio tail. In the replay and budget tests, monkeypatch anthropic.Anthropic to raise so that zero API calls is enforced. Move the ffmpeg skipif onto only the tests that need ffmpeg.

**Verifier's notes.** PLAN.md:536-539 asks for 10 cuts detected within 1 frame, a top swatch within ΔE<5 for each shot, and moving-gradient motion > 0. The test instead uses 2 cuts (tests/test_m5_smoke.py:187-195) with ±0.15 s tolerance, about 3.6 frames at 24 fps (240-242), and checks an r/g/b threshold on shot 0 only (253-256). Through the stage it checks only static motion (258); moving motion is tested only on a synthetic array (92-104). No OQ or review note relaxes PLAN §11 (T1, P2). Solid-colour scenes cannot reveal VID-1 or VID-3, and there are no rotation, offset, VFR or audio-tail fixtures. In the replay test, a cache miss would build the real client with the fake key and attempt a live HTTP call; the test would still fail, but not hermetically. The module-level pytestmark also skips the pure cost and cache tests when ffmpeg is absent.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. PLAN's own spec uses solid-colour scenes, so add the textured and two-colour cases needed for VID-1 and VID-3 alongside the 10-cut test, not instead of it. Guard the network by monkeypatching anthropic.Anthropic to raise in the replay and budget tests, and move the ffmpeg skipif onto only the tests that need ffmpeg.

#### TEST-9: The M4 smoke test deviates from PLAN §11: the aligned path never runs on real audio, the overlap check is 3 of 5 hand-picked words, and the music-only check is vacuous

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, H1
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:527-533`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:437-454`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:506-548`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:457-486`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:333-367`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:529-533 requires: the alignment path 'produces monotonically increasing word times covering ≥ 90 % of words; transcription path on the same audio yields ≥ 80 % token overlap; a music-only fixture yields empty words + untranscribed_regions covering the vocal-activity span.' No slow test ingests with --lyrics: `_run_chain` (L437-454) passes only the media file, so aligned mode never meets real Whisper output. Aligned mode is tested only with a stubbed engine (L333-367), and that test asserts neither word monotonicity nor word coverage. The transcription check (L539-541) is `hits = sum(w in text for w in ("quick", "brown", "fox", "lazy", "dog"))` with `assert hits >= 3`: 3 of 5 chosen words from a 9-token sentence, so as little as 33 % overlap passes. The degenerate test (L457-486) uses a click-only bed with no vocal activity, which makes 'untranscribed_regions covering the vocal-activity span' vacuous. Loosening the hallucination count is recorded (review 007, round 1); the other deviations are not. The schema invariant on words, 'Sorted, monotone, within the line span' (lyrics.schema.json), is asserted by no test.

**Failure scenario.** A regression in `_pair_words` or `align_lines` that only shows up with real Whisper word streams (merged or split tokens, punctuation-bearing words, non-monotone times after clamping) ships green. So does a transcription-quality drop from 9/9 words to 3/9.

**Proposal.** Add a slow test_espeak_aligned_path: ingest the same espeak mix with --lyrics (the sentence split over 2 lines) and assert mode=aligned, at least 90 % of supplied words carry timings, and word times are non-decreasing across the document and inside each line span. In the transcription test, compute token overlap |tokens ∩ truth| / |truth| ≥ 0.8, or record a calibrated threshold. Add a fast stubbed test: fabricated vocal tones give vocal activity, the engine returns nothing, and the document must have lines == [], untranscribed_regions equal to the VA regions, and coverage 0.0.
